# Decisions
Status: **Final** or **Proposed**. Date 2026-10-28 unless noted.

1. **GitHub is the shared source of truth.** Reason: laptop and Codespaces must share work. Consequence: all state lives in Git, not sessions. — Final (stated by user)
2. **Windows 11 and Codespaces are development environments; production is a separate Linux server.** Consequence: no assumption of shared files/env; deploys separate. — Final
3. **Phase 1 begins with Kotak Neo market-data integration.** — Final
4. **NIFTY 50 is the initial underlying; initial expiries are weekly and current-monthly.** — Final
5. **Web dashboard is a core product requirement.** — Final
6. **Backend manages broker credentials and connectivity; frontend consumes normalized backend data.** Reason: keep secrets out of browser. — Final
7. **Advanced analytics deferred until reliable data collection is established.** — Final
8. **Backend Python, frontend React + TypeScript.** Reason: stated in brief (Python/TypeScript standards). Framework (e.g. FastAPI, Vite) not chosen. — Proposed
9. **Intended layout `backend/ frontend/ tests/ docs/`.** — Proposed
10. **Claude Code settings:** no `.claude/settings.json`, no subagents or MCP servers added. Reason: not needed yet. — Proposed
11. **Server-first architecture: GitHub stores source; the Linux server runs production and pulls approved commits to deploy automatically.** Reason: server is the real runtime; pull model avoids exposing the server to CI. Consequences: server needs a deploy agent, rollback, read-only deploy key; production config/secrets stay on the server. — Proposed (user-stated direction; details unconfirmed)
12. **"Approved commit" = reviewed merge to `main` or an owner-created release tag.** — Proposed
13. **Build locally first; GitHub is touched only after the local build is done, then push.** Reason: user's sequencing choice. Consequences: no remote/push/Codespaces work until the user says so; the Codespaces and server-deploy parts of the workflow stay documented but dormant. — Final (stated by user, 2026-10-28)
14. **Kotak Neo SDK: `kotakneoapi` (repo Kotak-Neo/kotak-neo-python), v3.0.x, Python ≥3.10; SFeed via `create_websocket()`.** `kotak-neo-api-v2` is retired. — Final (verified from SDK source v3.0.13, 2026-10-28)
15. **TOTP: the TOTP secret is stored in the backend `.env` and codes are generated with pyotp for automatic re-login.** Consequence: whoever has the `.env` can log in; `.env` must never leave the machine/server, is git-ignored, and is never logged. — Final (user choice, 2026-10-28)
16. **Monthly expiry roll: when the current calendar month has no remaining listed expiry, use next month's last listed expiry, labelled "next month".** — Final (user choice)
17. **Storage: snapshots as JSON (+CSV export), live ticks as JSONL per session; a stdlib SQLite file indexes sessions/snapshots only.** No DB server, no extra dependency. — Final (user choice)
18. **Snapshots come from `option_chain()` (which also provides contract tokens); the scrip master is not needed for Phase 1 discovery.** — Proposed
19. **Stack: FastAPI + pydantic-settings backend; React + TS + Vite + Tailwind, Radix primitives, TanStack Table/Virtual/Query, Zustand, Apache ECharts frontend.** — Final (approved plan)
20. **Dependencies are installed per machine (Windows laptop, later server); never copied between OSes.** — Final
21. **Verified live `option_chain()` shape (2026-10-28, nse_fo NIFTY) differs from the SDK docs; parser accepts both.** Observed: `inst{neoSymbol,symbol,optType,strkPrc,exp,moneyness(lowercase itm/atm/otm)}`, `quote{ltp,o,h,l,c,pc,vol}`, `oi{cur,prev,chg,chgPct}`, plus `spot{ltp,prevClose}`, `common_data{mktLot,...}`; 81 strikes per side for count=40 (±40 around ATM, step 50). Values are strings. — Final (observed)
22. **Kotak's `oi.chg`/`oi.chgPct` are NOT open-interest change** (they equal LTP − previous close and its %, in 158 of 162 contracts). OI change is computed as `cur − prev` (percentage only when `prev > 0`); the source fields are never used. Also `quote.c` equals `quote.pc` for every contract and `future.prevClose` is implausible; neither is used for display. — Final (observed; re-verify if Kotak changes the API)
23. **Underlying price comes from the chain response's `spot` block and is shown only as a labelled snapshot value ("not live").** — Final
24. **One broker feed, many browsers:** a single `StreamManager` owns the one Kotak SFeed connection; browsers receive batched, normalized updates from `/ws/market-data` (250 ms coalescing, bounded per-client queues, client limit). The browser never connects to Kotak. — Final
25. **Stream start sequence:** connect, subscribe market status (`subscribe_exchange`), small test subscription (index + ATM CE/PE), wait for first data (10 s), then the full universe (weekly + monthly chains, deduplicated, index included) in chunks of 200. No data during the test is reported (`no_data`) and does not block. SDK's 3000-token limit applies (~325 used). — Final
26. **Reconnect policy:** the SDK reconnects by itself (5 attempts); when it gives up, our supervisor retries with exponential backoff 2 s, 4 s, ... capped at 60 s, at most 8 attempts (a session that lived 60 s resets the budget), then state FAILED until a manual start. Auth failure triggers one re-login. Start is idempotent (never a second ingestion process). — Final
27. **Connection state, subscription, message flow and freshness are separate:** states stopped/connecting/subscribing/waiting_for_data/streaming/reconnecting/auth_failed/failed; freshness fresh/stale/market_closed/none derived from the age of the last market-data message (default stale after 30 s). A connected socket never implies fresh data. — Final
28. **Market status comes only from the exchange feed** (`SFeedMarketStatus`: open codes 1 and 4, closed code 2; others shown as "other"). Trading hours and holidays are never assumed; status resets to Unknown when the stream stops. — Final
29. **Stream values: wire prices of 0 become missing; volume and OI keep 0.** Exchange timestamps are NOT derived: `last_trade_time`/`last_update_time` are carried as raw provider integers (unit and epoch unverified, audit F5). Exact repeats are dropped and counted as duplicates. — Final
30. **UI overlay rule (superseded in part by #33):** live values overlay the snapshot rows, matched by instrument token with row-level subscriptions; stale streams dim the live cells.
31. **Snapshot lookup prefers the newest non-empty snapshot** so a failed-parse (empty) snapshot cannot hide a good one (audit F3). — Final
32. **No mock or demo mode.** The application uses only real Kotak Neo data. Backend credentials are always required (the backend will not start without them). Automated tests replay real captured Kotak responses from `backend/tests/fixtures` (credential-free), never invented numbers. Old demo rows already in `data/index.sqlite3` are hidden, not deleted. — Final (user instruction, 2026-10-28)
33. **Snapshot is the base, the stream updates it.** On start the backend makes sure the weekly and monthly snapshots are current (fetches new ones if older than 5 minutes), subscribes their instruments, and every value that can follow the stream does: LTP, volume, OI, OI change (streamed OI minus snapshot previous-day OI), totals, the ATM strike (nearest strike to the live NIFTY price) and the spot. A streamed OI of 0 never replaces a positive snapshot OI (unverified semantics). With the market closed, everything shows the real snapshot, labelled as the last session. — Final
34. **Streaming starts automatically with the backend** (`STREAM_AUTOSTART=true` by default); Stop/Start buttons remain. One login is shared (`ensure_login`) so startup never double-signs-in. — Final

## Open questions
- Python and Node versions on the Windows laptop and production server.
- Meaning of SFeed `last_trade_time` / `last_update_time` (epoch units, exchange vs server time): `scripts\stream-check.ps1` prints each raw value interpreted as Unix seconds and as seconds since 1980-01-01 so the right reading can be identified during market hours.
- Whether streamed `open_interest` is populated for options and matches the snapshot's OI; whether zero means 'none yet'.
- What the index feed (`SFeedIndex`) sends for the subscribed name `Nifty 50` (key and `close_price` semantics).
- Whether the first messages after subscribe arrive when the market is closed.
- Whether `quote.c` (equal to prev close during/after session) becomes a distinct settlement price later; whether `oi.prev` is the previous trading day's OI.
- Backend/frontend frameworks, package managers, formatter/linter choices.
- Storage technology for snapshots/history.
- Kotak Neo SDK version, auth flow (incl. TOTP), rate limits, streaming limits.
- Hosting details of the production server (OS, resources, process manager such as systemd/Docker).
- Exact approval rule for auto-deploy (main merge vs tag; required CI checks; signed commits?).
- Where builds happen (on server vs elsewhere) given low resources; polling interval; rollback/health-check definition.
- Notification channel for deploy success/failure.
- The local folder was empty and not a Git repo at initialization; GitHub remote to be confirmed.

## D35 — Analysis dashboard from stored snapshots (2026-10-28)
- Widget dashboard at `/analysis` (react-grid-layout v2 + tree-shaken ECharts, lazy-loaded). One `DashProvider` merges snapshot rows with live ticks (throttled 400 ms), applies expiry/strike-range/CE-PE filters; every widget reads that one state so charts, table and key levels agree.
- Time series use only real stored snapshots via `GET /api/history/observations` (universe ATM ± N; premium = sum of LTP; straddle; totals). No candles, no interpolation; <2 snapshots → empty state; all-identical → flat-line note (captured while market closed).
- OI change = current − previous-day OI computed locally (Kotak `oi.chg` is price change). OI/volume are units, lot size shown in Key levels.
- Max pain: candidate settlements are listed strikes; strikes with missing OI excluded and counted.

## D36 — Scheduled market-hours sampling (2026-10-28)
- `SnapshotSampler` (backend, background task) captures the weekly + monthly chains every `SAMPLER_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default 60, min 30) while the NSE session is on: Mon–Fri 09:15–15:30 IST. Same read-only REST path as "Refresh snapshot"; no orders.
- Exchange-feed "closed" received today blocks sampling (weekday holidays); a "closed" from an earlier day is ignored so it cannot block the morning. Failures back off (interval ×2 up to 5 min) and are shown on the Analysis page; the loop never dies.
- Nothing is sampled outside the session, so history never fills with repeats of closing values. Status: `GET /api/sampler/status`. Cost: see D37 (the original ~40 MB/day estimate was wrong; measured 86 MB/day uncompressed).
- History observations are cached per immutable snapshot id (limit now up to 2000).

## D37 — Compressed snapshots, repeat-skipping, disk guard (2026-10-28)
- Audit measured 230 KB per capture (both expiries; normalized + raw files) = ~86 MB/day at 60 s, about twice the earlier estimate. Now snapshots are written gzip (`.json.gz`, `.raw.json.gz`): ~36 KB per capture, ~13 MB/day. Older plain `.json` files are still read; nothing existing was rewritten or deleted.
- The scheduled sampler skips a chain whose spot, prices, OI and volume equal the last stored one for that expiry (holidays, stream down, after close). Manual "Refresh snapshot" always stores.
- Sampling pauses when free disk is under `SAMPLER_MIN_FREE_GB` (default 2). No automatic deletion; retention/compaction remains a user-approved task.
- Open: control endpoints trust any loopback client; add authentication before any reverse-proxy deployment.

## D38 — Checkpoint 1 audit fixes: market status per segment, "live" only when fresh (2026-10-28)
- Evidence (PC logs 2026-10-28): stream connected, 325 tokens subscribed and 325 acknowledged, test subscription received data; each connect also delivered ten market-status messages with codes 1 (open) and 2 (closed) interleaved. The manager kept only the last message, so the market state depended on arrival order and could show "open", making a quiet feed read as "stale".
- Fix: status is kept per exchange segment (`market_segments`); the market state is NSE F&O, else NSE cash, else unknown. Other exchanges are never borrowed. The segment is now logged. Which segment names Kotak sends is unverified until market hours (`scripts\stream-check.ps1`).
- Fix: the spot price (header, Analysis toolbar, Key levels) is called live only when the stream's effective state is "streaming" (fresh data), not merely connected. Stale, market-closed or waiting streams fall back to the labelled snapshot.
- Stored snapshots are real Kotak data. The four identical ones were all captured after the 15:30 close (spot 22,698.85 each time), so identical values are expected, not a storage bug. Two earlier demo snapshots and two empty (parser-bug) ones remain hidden.
- Open: `oi.prev` is treated as previous-day OI but that is unverified; `exchange_timestamp` is null in every stored snapshot, so history timestamps are local receipt times; sessions are never marked ended when the app is killed.

## D39 — Observation store and OI intelligence (Checkpoints 2-3, 2026-10-28)
- `data/observations.sqlite3` (WAL, one writer) keeps compact observations across restarts: `samples` (one per chain fetch: received time, provider time if any, spot, previous close, lot, source, content fingerprint), `contracts` (only when values changed: LTP in paise, OI, broker previous-day OI, volume; NULL = missing, never 0) and `events` (app start/stop, stream state changes, subscription acknowledgements, per-segment market status, sampler failures). A fetch identical to the previous one is stored as an 'unchanged' sample with no contract rows, so replay can tell "no change" from "no data". Existing stored snapshots are imported once at start-up (idempotent, real data only). Nothing is deleted automatically; size is visible at `GET /api/observations/status`. Estimated ~3-4 MB/day at 60 s (to be measured in market hours).
- Not recorded yet, because the verified data model does not carry them: bid/ask, quote quantities, IV, provider timestamps (`exchange_timestamp` is empty in every stored snapshot), stream-tick observations. These need the live feed to be inspected first.
- OI change is always "now minus an explicit baseline", chosen by the user: previous distinct observation, a selected IST time, session open, previous session (our own stored data), or the broker's previous-day field (meaning unverified). Never extrapolated; a missing baseline gives "Insufficient data", a zero baseline gives no percentage.
- Labels are neutral: OI increased / decreased / unchanged / insufficient data; price-and-OI shows only "OI up, premium down"-style observations. No buildup/writing/bullish/bearish language, and the highest-OI strike is never called support or resistance. Heatmap colours are neutral (blue above baseline, amber below).
- Endpoints: `GET /api/oi/change`, `GET /api/oi/heatmap`, `GET /api/observations/status`. Widgets (Add widget menu): Intraday OI change, OI change heatmap, OI concentration, Price and OI.
- Verified: hand-calculated unit tests for every baseline, concentration, heatmap, missing/zero baselines; real captured chain through the API. Unverified until market hours: that OI really changes between fetches, and the meaning of the broker's previous-day OI field.

## D40 — Collapsible side menu and a minimal top bar (2026-10-28)
- Side menu is a 56 px rail that opens over the page (never pushing it) on hover, touch or keyboard focus, and closes after a choice. "Keep menu open" pins it beside the page (remembered in this browser). The page keeps its full width. Mobile keeps the Menu button.
- Top bar keeps the product name, the NIFTY level and one status pill (problems first, then warnings, then Live / stored-data state). The signal list, selected expiries, last snapshot time, Refresh and theme moved to Settings (System status, Theme). The pill opens Settings.

## D41 — Refresh-only development workflow (2026-10-28)
- One start file, `NSE Universe.cmd`: starts the app if it is not running, otherwise only opens the browser. The separate Start/Stop files are gone (`scripts\stop.ps1` remains for a full shutdown).
- Backend runs with `APP_RELOAD=1` (uvicorn reload on `backend\app` changes, 1.5 s delay); every restart signs in to Kotak again. Frontend is the Vite dev server (hot updates). `scripts\watch.ps1` installs packages when `requirements*.txt` or `package-lock.json` change and nudges the backend to reload.
- Offline checks no longer run on every start (use `scripts\check.ps1` or `launch.ps1 -Checks`). An instance started by the old launcher is replaced once, automatically.
- Untested here: the PowerShell scripts could not be run in the Linux workspace; the backend reload itself was tested (a touched file restarted the app and it answered).

## D42 — Prediction Intelligence: Milestones 1-2 (2026-10-28)
- Two separate forecasters (T+1, T+5) for NIFTY 50; research only, never trades. Specifications in `docs/prediction/` (10 documents). Code in `backend/app/prediction/`: NSE trading calendar (2026 holidays read from NSE circulars CMTR/71775 and CMTR/72260; other years refused), label formulas, point-in-time guard (unknown publication time = unavailable), immutable per-horizon forecast ledger (SQLite triggers + content hash; outcomes attachable only after the target session closes), purged walk-forward splits and metrics.
- Not built: any data adapter, feature pipeline, model, dashboard workspace. No NIFTY price history exists in the project; a source needs the user's approval (docs/prediction/overview.md, decisions 1-6).
- Reasoning on sources: NSE terms ban systematic automated collection; Reuters has no public feed; Moneycontrol has no official feed; ET RSS terms unverified; GDELT terms are open. Option-chain features are prospective (history from 2026-10-28).

## D43 — Prediction decisions and the price-history downloader (2026-10-28)
- User decisions: cutoff 08:30 IST next morning; price history from Yahoo via yfinance (unofficial, personal use); news from official announcements + GDELT + Economic Times RSS (ET terms unverified, headline/link/time only); building later (Milestone 8).
- Built: `HistoryStore` (immutable first-seen bars, `bar_changes` log, run log), Yahoo adapter with a symbol registry (only NIFTY 50's close time is marked verified; everything else is stored but not usable as a feature until checked), `HistoryService` (automatic first download and a daily run after 18:30 IST, per-symbol failure isolation, 1 h retry, 1 request/s), API `GET /api/prediction/history/status` and `POST /api/prediction/history/refresh` (local only), Settings panel "Prediction data". `yfinance` is in `requirements-research.txt` (PC/Codespaces only, not on the server).
- Not testable from Claude's workspace: neither it nor its PC link can reach Yahoo, so the first real download happens on the user's PC; failures show in Settings and logs.
- Verified on the live feed: Kotak segment names (nse_cm, nse_fo, ...), confirming the per-segment market-status fix (D38).

## D44 — Milestone 3 audit: price history accepted for research, baselines recorded (2026-10-28)
- NIFTY 50 history (4,689 bars from 2007-09-17) matches the NSE-circular 2026 calendar exactly; invalid vendor bars are skipped, never repaired.
- Naive baselines (climatology, always-up, previous-direction) are barely better than a coin on Brier/log loss; models must beat them in the same purged walk-forward folds. Results in `docs/prediction/data-audit-m3.md`, reproducible with `research/m3_audit.py`.
- The 63.8% positive opening-gap share is unverified against official NSE opens; no gap model is trusted until checked.
- Non-NIFTY series stay unusable as features until close times are verified (`timing_verified`).

## D45 — First model and Prediction page (2026-10-28)
- `logit-tech` v1: ridge logistic regression on 13 price-only NIFTY 50 features (`tech-v1`), T+1 and T+5 direction, evaluated by purged expanding walk-forward against the training up-frequency. "Better" requires a 95% block-bootstrap interval of the paired Brier gain to exclude zero.
- Result on real data (2012-2026, 3,500 predictions per horizon): no detectable edge for either horizon (T+1 gain +0.0007, interval -0.0006..+0.0021; T+5 gain -0.0007, interval -0.0021..+0.0006). The page states this verdict rather than hiding it.
- Forecasts are recorded in the immutable ledger with cutoff = reference session close (15:30 IST) because only price data through that close is used; outcomes attach automatically after the target close.
- Next: add India VIX and verified global/overnight inputs, then test whether they change the verdict.

## D46 — Model comparison protocol and UI theme (2026-10-28)
- Candidates: logit-tech, logit-small (5 a-priori features), boost-tech (depth-1/2 boosted trees), ensemble (mean of tuned logit-tech and boost-tech). Hyper-parameters are tuned ONLY on the earlier half of the purged walk-forward folds (2012-01 to 2019-03); every verdict comes from the untouched later half (2019-03 to 2026-04). The headline model is the best on tuning, not on holdout.
- Verdicts: "better" requires the Bonferroni-adjusted (8 comparisons) block-bootstrap interval of the Brier gain to exclude zero; "promising" passes only the plain 95% test; otherwise "no edge shown" / "worse". Results (holdout): T+1 boost-tech gain +0.0017 (95% +0.0001..+0.0035) = promising, unconfirmed; every other model/horizon shows no edge or is worse (T+5 logit-tech and ensemble worse than the base rate).
- The ledger records every model's forecast, so the forward test decides whether "promising" is real.
- Results compute in a background thread and are cached in data/prediction_report.json (keyed by last session, model and feature version).
- UI: new vibrant theme (violet/cyan accent, glass panels, gradient headings, SVG nav icons) via design tokens in index.css; dark default, light kept.

## D47 — Separate overnight (T+1) and broad (T+5) models, calibration, calls (2026-10-28)
- Timing rule: every outside series is used only through "the last bar dated on or before the reference session", complete by 08:30 IST next morning. Verified on real history (research/timing_audit.py): US bars dated t correlate +0.38 with NIFTY's next opening gap, bars dated t+1 only +0.10. All series now `timing_verified`.
- T+1 (t1-v1): NIFTY last session, India VIX, US/Europe/Asia closes, Brent, gold, DXY, USD/INR, US 10y. T+5 (t5-v1): adds trend/vol context, 8 sector relative strengths and breadth, global 5-20 day trends. T+5+flows: adds NSE participant positions, evaluated on its own history once >= 1,500 sessions exist.
- Protocol: earlier half of purged walk-forward folds tunes hyper-parameters and fits a Platt calibration; later half is an untouched holdout; verdicts use a Bonferroni-adjusted bootstrap. "Calls" (all base models agree and the ensemble is in the top quarter of tuning-period strength) are scored separately; otherwise "no call".
- Result on real data (holdout 2018-09 to 2025-12, 1,750 predictions): T+1 all four models beat the base rate (Brier gain +0.013 to +0.015, strict test passed, hit rate 60-61%); confident calls (41% of days) right 67.7% vs 52.9% always-up. Leakage sanity: delaying the overnight data by one day removes the edge. T+5 broad: no detectable edge (ensemble worse).
- Recording: forecasts are recorded only 08:35-09:10 IST on the target day, after the overnight data is complete (US session complete after 03:00 IST); otherwise the page shows a preview. History refresh also runs at 07:30 IST.

## D48 — Server-first deployment kit (2026-10-28)
- `deploy/` holds a repeatable bootstrap and a pull-based updater: a timer checks GitHub every minute (`git ls-remote`), builds a new release next to the running one, checks `/api/health`, switches the `current` symlink, and rolls back by itself on failure. Production does not depend on GitHub Actions.
- Public exposure requires authentication (CLAUDE.md rule): Caddy HTTPS with a mandatory login in front of everything; the backend listens on 127.0.0.1:8010 only; ALLOWED_ORIGINS is the public origin.
- Resource and data rules: 1 GB swap file, systemd memory limits, journald cap, nightly integrity-checked SQLite backups, disk monitor (70/80/90 %), no automatic deletion of market data; deploys never touch the data directory.
- Existing services on the VPS (an earlier nse-tradingdesk) are left untouched; the script refuses to continue if ports 80/443/8010 are taken.
- Not done by me: logging in to the VPS, DuckDNS registration, filling broker credentials (they stay on the server). I could not verify the kit on the real server; it is tested here for the update/rollback logic and backend startup only.

## D49 — Events and news inputs (2026-10-29)
- Scheduled events (`backend/app/prediction/reference/events.json`): FOMC 2026-27 (federalreserve.gov calendar), RBI MPC 2026-27 (RBI press release 2025-2026/2306), US election day, India CPI (MoSPI). Each has a source; release clock times that the source does not state are marked unconfirmed.
- Events are shown as event risk beside each forecast. For T+1 only, a directional call is withheld when a high-impact event falls between the reference close and the target close. This is a precaution; there are too few past occurrences to validate it. T+5 lists events but is not withheld.
- News: GDELT DOC API daily tone and coverage share for five themes, collected twice a day and stored append-only (`news.sqlite3`). GDELT serves daily series for only the last three months, so there is no multi-year history: the series builds forward and is NOT a model input yet. Economic Times RSS (terms unverified) and NSE announcements (automated access refused) are not used.

## D50 — US data calendar, FII/DII cash window (2026-10-29)
- Trading Economics is not used: its terms could not be read and its calendar/API is an account/paid product. The US 3-star releases come from the official BLS schedule (CPI and jobs report, 2026-27); FOMC from the Fed; RBI from RBI. Event times the source does not name a zone for are marked unconfirmed.
- Events remain risk flags, not model inputs: too few past occurrences exist to validate them as features.
- FII/DII cash: NSE's report feed refuses automated clients (HTTP 403) and the collector does not work around that. Data enters through `POST /api/prediction/flows/cash/import` from the CSV downloaded from NSE's report; every row must satisfy buy - sell = net. A rolling window keeps only the newest 7 sessions and deletes older cash rows on each import (user-approved retention for this table).
- NSE participant-wise F&O files: pruned to the newest 7 sessions per file type after the user approved it (2026-10-29), with a verified full backup of the database written first to `data/backups/` (kept) and then a rolling window on every run. Consequence: the optional T+5 model with NSE positions cannot be trained; the position tables remain as context.

## D51 — Daily data without gaps (2026-10-29)
- The owner asked for a browser-style User-Agent to fetch NSE's FII/DII feed; the tool runner refused that as bot-protection circumvention and it was NOT built. FII/DII cash stays imported through a scheduled task that reads NSE's page in a real browser on the owner's PC (18:47 IST weekdays) or the CSV import box.
- NSDL's public FPI page (no stated restriction on automated access) is fetched once each evening by the app with an honest identifier: foreign equity activity (stock-exchange route and sub-total), validated (purchases - sales = net), rolling 7 sessions. It measures a different thing from NSE's FII figure and is shown separately.
- History refresh retries every 10 minutes between 07:30 and 09:10 IST while any overnight series (S&P, VIX, Brent, dollar index, USD/INR, US 10y) is older than the last weekday, so the forecast window gets complete data whenever the source has it.

## D52 — Intraday analysis page and command center (2026-10-29)
- The Analysis page is now **Intraday analysis**; the Market Sentiment Command Center sits above the unchanged widgets ([intraday](intraday.md)).
- Built on the existing stores only (observations, market history, flows, events); no new provider, no new dependency. One small append-only `intraday.sqlite3` holds score snapshots so the provisional rules can be evaluated later (no schema change to the existing databases).
- Rules-based, versioned, provisional scoring; correlated price measures form one group; missing groups lower coverage and are never zero; no probabilities anywhere until a validated intraday model exists.
- VWAP is reported as undefined for the NIFTY 50 index rather than approximated. Breadth and intraday sectors are shown as not available.
- The feed's previous close is cross-checked against the stored close of the previous trading session; a mismatch above 0.05 % uses the stored close and shows a warning.
- The master spec asks for the PC as the runtime and no external server; the VPS deployment (D48) was authorised earlier by the owner and is left as it is. Both run the same code.

## D53 — Prediction models are walled off from intraday data (2026-10-29)
- **Finding (audit):** 12 daily bars for 28 Oct (S&P, Dow, Nasdaq, Brent, WTI, gold, dollar index, VIX, US 10y, USD/INR, DAX, FTSE) had been stored at 15:15 UTC, mid-session, and 4 Asian bars for 29 Oct at 02:03 UTC. The bar store never overwrites, so partial intraday bars would have stood as final data feeding today's T+1 forecast.
- **Rules now enforced:** (1) a downloaded bar is stored only once its session has finished (`complete_after`: exchange close + 30 min; futures/FX series use 17:00 New York or end of the UTC day); (2) a bar stored too early is excluded from every read, and replaced by its final bar through the append-only `bar_corrections` table (the original row is kept, nothing deleted); (3) series whose newest bar is held back count as missing, so the overnight retry and the stale-data checks fire and the forecast is withheld rather than made on partial data; (4) the models are trained and evaluated only outside 09:15-15:45 IST on trading days, a cached report is still served; (5) a test fails the build if anything under `app/prediction` imports live, broker, stream, observation-store, API or intraday code, or if the intraday package imports the model code.
- Data flow is one-way: intraday code reads stored daily bars; the models never read intraday samples, option chains, live ticks or the intraday score ledger.
- News tone (GDELT) is collected but is not a model input (D49), so its 12-hourly schedule is not restricted.

## D54 — Data and storage plan for the 15 GB server (2026-10-29)
- Plan in [storage-plan.md](storage-plan.md), sized from measured data: a full session is about 16 MB of raw snapshot files, 5.5 MB in the observation database and 1.4 MB as a compressed archive (about 5 GB a year if nothing is removed). The server is the production collector, so the budget is the 15 GB SSD, not the PC.
- After 15:45 IST each finished session is validated, archived (verified by checksum and row counts), summarised (including the close-of-day option chain, permanent) and given a manifest; after 19:00 the databases are backed up (the server's own timer does this; the app skips its copy there) and regenerable leftovers are tidied. Daily T+1/T+5 models retrain on each new final price bar, never during 09:15-15:45.
- Deletion: only regenerable files (old exports, stray `*.tmp`, expired backup folders) are removed automatically. Removing snapshot files (raw payloads, normalized) is implemented with safety checks but **disabled** (`SNAPSHOT_RAW_KEEP_DAYS=0`, `SNAPSHOT_NORMALIZED_KEEP_DAYS=0`) until the owner approves limits. The recommended limits are 14 days (raw) and 30 days (normalized); the observation database and archives are kept.
- The intraday data is not an input of the daily models and cannot be (D53); it is retained because Kotak provides no historical option chains and it is cheap when archived.

## D55 — Intraday regime measure and the staged intraday-ML plan (2026-10-29)
- The one-minute efficiency ratio called a +171 point, 1.6x-normal climb "sideways". The regime now uses a z-score of the move against the 60-session daily volatility and the 5-minute path efficiency.
- Scenarios shown only when supported; the others are listed as ruled out with a reason.
- Intraday ML is staged by labelled sessions (5 experimental, 20 paper-test, 60 eligible for "validated"), and a labelled feature/label dataset is built every night from day one ([intraday-ml-plan](intraday-ml-plan.md)). No model is trained yet; no probability is shown.

## D56 — Market breadth, heavyweights, volume and sector analysis: plan (2026-10-29)
- Plan in [market-breadth-plan](market-breadth-plan.md); nothing built. Breadth (0.15) and sector (0.10) score groups stay "not available" until it is.
- Feasibility checked against Kotak's documentation: up to 3,000 subscribed tokens per connection, a scrip master and quotes API; our ticks already carry previous close and cumulative volume. Everything else (token coverage of NIFTY 500, tick rate, server load, weights/constituent sources and terms) is verified in a market-hours capture before any code depends on it.
- "Is the move real" is answered by breadth (NIFTY 50 and 500), equal- vs cap-weighted return, heavyweight contribution, and relative volume; thresholds are provisional and will be checked against the labelled dataset.

## D57 — Market breadth, heavyweights, volume and sectors built (2026-10-29)
- New `backend/app/market/` (pure `metrics.py`, `sources.py`, append-only `store.py` -> `data/market.sqlite3`, `service.py`). Constituent lists from NSE archive CSVs, bhavcopy for the volume baseline (>=10 sessions), tokens from the Kotak scrip master, weights only from an official import (never invented). Ticks for these tokens go to an internal book and never touch option-chain freshness or broadcasts.
- Breadth and sector evidence feed the intraday score only when coverage >= 0.7 (breadth) or >= 6 sectors (sectors); otherwise missing, not zero. Move quality is a provisional rule; linear relative volume can show thin but never confirm strong.
- Status: tested on real NSE file lines, hand calculations and labelled stand-in stock prices. Unverified against live Kotak stock ticks. Flag `BREADTH_ENABLED`.

## D58 — Futures flow/CVD, top-5 heavyweight depth, estimated weights, own volume profile, retention enabled (2026-10-29)
Owner direction (29 Oct): breadth for NIFTY 500 is only the advance/decline count (aggregate, not per-stock detail) to protect the 1 GB server; do everything autonomously; use Kotak depth for volume/CVD; index volume from the current-month future; keep only the data the ML needs and delete the rest.
- **NIFTY 500 load:** only aggregates are stored (counts per minute); per-stock rows are stored for the NIFTY 50 only; ticks for these stocks never reach browsers.
- **Constituent lists:** NSE archive CSVs checked daily (cheap, one file each); a change in membership is recorded in `list_changes` and shown in the sources.
- **Depth:** the five largest NIFTY 50 weights and the NIFTY current-month future are subscribed with market depth (best bid/ask and quantities). The future is chosen from the F&O scrip master by the contract month of the app's own current-monthly option expiry.
- **Flow (`market/flow.py`):** each volume increase is classified by the quote rule (at/above ask = buy, at/below bid = sell), then the tick rule; volume before the first push is left unclassified. CVD, buy/sell split, order-book imbalance and a 30-minute price-vs-CVD divergence. Always labelled *estimated*.
- **Weights:** official import wins; otherwise weights are **estimated from our own minute recordings** (index change regressed on the 50 stock changes, non-negative, accepted only if it reproduces the index within 0.03 points). Until then the five are chosen by previous-session traded value and labelled a liquidity proxy. No keyless official stock-weight feed could be verified from here (NSE hosts are not reachable from the build environment).
- **Volume reference:** profile = median share of the day's volume by minute from our own recorded sessions (needs 5 sessions; futures and pooled NIFTY 50 stocks). Until then the linear reference stays and cannot confirm "strong". Futures baseline = their own recorded daily volume (Kotak has no history API that we use).
- **Technical read:** SMA20/50, Wilder RSI14 and ATR14, 20-day range and volume against the 20-day average, from NSE daily bars (bhavcopy open/high/low/close now stored in `daily`; the 60-session backfill runs automatically). The 50-day trend is shown only with 50 sessions.
- **Retention (owner-approved in principle):** `SNAPSHOT_RAW_KEEP_DAYS=7`, `SNAPSHOT_NORMALIZED_KEEP_DAYS=14`. Only days with a verified archive are pruned; observation DB, archives, EOD summaries, the nightly ML dataset and `market.sqlite3` (small aggregate recordings) are kept.
- **Not verified live:** Kotak depth message fields on real data, the F&O scrip-master column names, tick rate and load on the server. First market-hours session decides.

## D59 — Official NIFTY 50 weights from NSE's index tracker (2026-10-29)
- The owner pointed to https://www.nseindia.com/index-tracker/NIFTY%2050. The page is rendered by script, so the weights were read from the page's own `getConstituents` call in the owner's browser pane (not scraped by the server, no bot-protection bypass): 50 stocks, `weightage` per stock, as of 29 Oct 2026 15:30 IST, sum 100.01, symbol set identical to the page's constituent list. Saved as `backend/app/market/seed/nifty50_weights_2026-10-29.csv`; the service imports it as **official** weights whenever none are stored.
- Free-float weights move with price, so official weights are **rolled forward** to the newest stored close (`w x close_now / close_at_weights_date`, renormalised); skipped when a close is missing or a ratio looks like a corporate action. Weights older than 60 days are flagged stale (the index is reviewed in March and September). To refresh: replace the seed file or `POST /api/market/weights/import`.
- The server cannot call NSE's API itself (it needs a browser session), so the regression estimate remains the automatic fallback only when no official weights exist.
- Verification targets for the first market-hours session (page values at the 29 Oct close): NIFTY 50 advance 39 / decline 11, close 22,936.75, 3,444.82 lakh shares traded; top contributors KOTAKBANK, ADANIPORTS, ETERNAL, INFY, BHARTIARTL. Compare `/api/market/summary` with the page after the close.

## D60 — Intraday engine brief: phased build, price structure first (2026-10-29)
- The owner's brief (modules A-N) is built in phases; status per module is in docs/intraday-engine-status.md and is updated with each increment. Greeks/IV, prediction, strategy and backtesting were out of scope and are now requested; order placement stays out.
- Phase 2 built: `intraday/price_structure.py` (opening range, breakout state machine, quality score, swing structure) and `market/vwap.py` (day VWAP of the NIFTY future from the exchange's average traded price; the index has no volume). Rules are defined in the module docstrings and replay deterministically from stored minute samples. The quality score is explicitly not a probability and is untested as a predictor until sessions accumulate.
- A breakout call is given only with fresh spot samples (<= 180 s old); otherwise the widget says HISTORICAL and shows no status.

## D61 — Implied volatility, Greeks and stock participation (2026-10-29)
- `intraday/volatility.py`: Black-76; the forward comes from put-call parity at the strike nearest spot (market implied), so no dividend or carry assumption is needed; only the discount factor uses an assumed rate (`RISK_FREE_RATE`, default 6 %, shown on the card). T runs to 15:30 IST on the expiry date in 365-day years; nothing is computed inside 15 minutes of expiry. IV above 300 % or outside no-arbitrage bounds is rejected. IV change compares the ATM IV of earlier stored samples of the same session. Greeks: delta wrt the forward, theta per calendar day, vega per volatility point.
- `market/participation.py`: share above day VWAP (exchange average traded price per stock), above/below the stock's own 15-minute range (from recorded samples), breadth momentum and divergence, with missing stocks counted as missing.
- Not yet compared with a broker or exchange reference IV; the card says so.

## D62 — Positioning detail, term structure, barrier labels and the evaluation gate (2026-10-29)
- `intraday/positioning.py`: window PCRs (ATM +/-2/5/10, all), OI-change PCR only when the call OI change is positive, four-way premium/OI reading against the sample 15 minutes earlier (changes under 0.5 % count as unchanged; the readings are labelled tentative), concentration and its shift. `volatility.term_structure`: ATM IV of expiries sampled within 5 minutes of the main one.
- `intraday/labels.py`: barrier = k (0.5) x daily sd x sqrt(h/375) x spot, daily sd from earlier sessions only; first sampled touch decides; a touch after a gap over 3 minutes is `ambiguous`, uncovered horizons are `missing`; neither is resolved for either side.
- `intraday/evaluate.py`: whole-session walk-forward (expanding window, so no horizon crosses the split), baselines fitted on training sessions only, log loss, Brier, balanced accuracy, calibration (ECE). Gate (tightened by the audit, D64): probabilities are shown only with >= 60 sessions, the model beating every baseline in >= 60 % of test sessions, a one-sided sign test p <= 0.05 with a mean log-loss gain >= 0.005, and ECE <= 0.05; with 5-59 sessions the label is EXPERIMENTAL MODEL: PROBABILITIES NOT VALIDATED, with fewer it says insufficient. Overlapping minute samples are not independent, so sessions (not rows) are what the gate counts.
- No probability is produced for the dashboard yet. Until the evaluation passes there is nothing to show; the research card reports exactly that.

## D63 — if-2 dataset, replay, setups, paper testing (2026-10-29)
- `FEATURE_VERSION` is now `if-2` (enrich.py adds implied volatility, futures VWAP/CVD/depth, breadth and breakout state read as-of each sample, max age 3 minutes; missing stays missing). Older sessions are rebuilt under the new version at the next end-of-day run (`features_missing_days`); rows are append-only and keyed by version.
- Replay rebuilds the dashboard from samples stored at or before the chosen time with no live ticks and no writes; outcomes after that time are returned separately.
- Setups never carry a probability. Position size comes from the loss limit and the stop distance. Costs: futures STT 0.05 % sell side (in force from 1 April 2026), other charges are configurable assumptions to be replaced from the broker contract note.
- Paper rules use signal on completed minutes, fill on the next sample, gaps suffered at the sample price, and no conclusion under 30 trades.
- Feature development stops here; the next step is a strict audit (see docs/audit-2026-10-29.md).

## D64 — Audit of the intraday analysis and the fixes it forced (2026-10-29)
- Full report in docs/audit-2026-10-29.md. Fifteen defects fixed, each with a failing-then-passing regression test; the most important were a look-ahead in replay (institutional flows), repainting swings and flickering breakout statuses, wrong LIVE labels on derived blocks, and a probability gate that could pass on a hair-thin edge.
- Rules adopted: one data-status vocabulary for every derived block; a replay or evaluation may read only information published before the moment analysed; validated probabilities need 60 sessions and a significance test; heavy read endpoints take a fixed parameter menu and run one at a time.

## D65 — Swing engine: backtest research page and automatic paper trading (2026-11-05)
- The user's institutional swing engine (spec PDF, ledger and engine code supplied) was rebuilt in `research/swing` and reproduces the ledger's headline statistics (see [swing-backtest](swing-backtest.md)). Honest read: before costs about +80-95 % over 2023-2026, after 0.15 % per side about +40-50 % against NIFTY +32 %; profit sits in a handful of trades; fragile to the minimum score. Not proof of an edge.
- Website: new **Swing trading** page (Paper trading, Backtest, Backtest trades, Robustness, Rules & limits). Backtest tabs read `data/swing/report.json`, built on the PC (`scripts\swing-refresh.ps1`) and copied to the server; they are never computed on the server.
- **Paper trading runs on the server, automatically, after 17:00 IST** (`backend/app/swing`, standard library only, no pandas: the server rule is no heavy libraries). Each run settles the session that just closed (judges last night's orders on today's bar, applies Day-1 cuts and trailing stops), scans the Chartink list, applies the market and sector gates and queues orders for the next session. Paper only: no broker call, no order code.
- One rule set: `app/swing/indicators.py` and `engine.py` are ports of the research code, proven equal by parity tests (every scorer field at many bars on random and 1,455 real stock-days with 0 differences; the engine reproduces the research backtest trade for trade on 30 synthetic stocks).
- Storage (tier 1, tiny, kept forever): `data/swing_paper.sqlite3`, append-only (triggers), one transaction per session, point-in-time Chartink list kept gzip-compressed, so survivorship bias can be measured out of sample. Roughly 10 KB per session (about 2.5 MB a year). Covered by `deploy/backup.sh` (every `*.sqlite3`).
- Safe by construction: refuses to act on incomplete data (below 90 % of the list, or any held/queued stock missing), idempotent per session, catches up missed sessions in order, drops today's bar before 16:00 IST. Yahoo currently answers 429 to long browser user-agents, so the fetcher sends `Mozilla/5.0`; if that changes the job fails safe and the page shows the book as stale.
- Forward-test record starts when the server first runs. Judge it only after months, never from a few sessions.

## D66 — Swing paper trading uses the Kotak feed for live prices, in shadow mode first (2026-11-05)
- The backend already holds a Kotak feed, so the swing paper trader reads live price, day open/high/low and volume from it for the stocks
  it cares about (queued orders and open positions only, about a dozen) instead of waiting for end-of-day bars. It pulls the feed's latest
  values every 2 seconds; it adds no hook on the tick path and cannot disturb the feed. New subscriptions go through the existing
  "internal" path (kept off the browser feed), `StreamManager.add_internal_tokens`, and are repeated on every reconnect.
- Live decisions are **shadow-only** until verified: shown on the Swing page and logged once a day per kind and stock (`live_events`,
  append-only), never applied to the book. Reason: the forward record is immutable and the live path cannot be exercised before a market
  session; a wrong live fill would stay in it for good. Switching to live execution is a separate, tested step after a session's shadow
  events have been compared with the end-of-day settlement.
- Stops are not moved intraday: that would add same-day stop-outs the backtest does not have. Targets (1.8R, 2.5R) are shown and logged.
- The live volume rule is a straight-line pace against the full-day minimum; it is an approximation and is documented as such.

## D67 — Overnight bars of 24-hour series are provisional until New York midnight (2026-11-05)
- Server evidence (read-only, 5 Nov): T+1 for 3 Nov was a preview (BRENT, DXY late), 4 Nov had no morning run at all (3 Nov DXY bar missing), 5 Nov DXY had only a live-tick row. Yahoo keeps adding the evening session to the bar of a US-dated future, FX or yield until New York midnight (about 10:30 IST, after the 09:10 window); closes stored at 17:00-22:00 New York differed from the final ones by up to 0.9% (Brent) and 2.8% (WTI).
- Rule: `final_after` (yahoo.py) = New York midnight + 30 min for those series; `complete_after` (17:00) now only means "usable provisionally". Before final, the bar is never stored as history; the newest row with sane prices is kept in `provisional_bars` (a working copy, replaced each refresh) and the morning forecast may use it. A row mixed with a live tick (open above high) is dropped and the series stays stale.
- Forecasts made with a provisional input are recorded as usual but flagged: `data_quality.provisional_series`, dataset_version suffix `+provisional`, and the day-log reason. Stored bars taken before final now count as premature and are replaced by append-only corrections when the final bar arrives (existing mechanism; nothing deleted).

## D68 — Futures flow and Sectors move to Future Lab; CVD and book-imbalance audit (2026-11-05)
- "Futures flow and top-5 heavyweights" and "Sectors" are no longer on the Intraday Booster (Analysis) page. They are the Future Lab tools "Future Flow & Heavyweights" and "Future Sectors" (same cards, same `/api/intraday/summary` data and cache).
- Live audit of the estimated order flow on the server, 09:15-09:17 IST (read-only, two snapshots 45 s apart): buy + sell + unclassified rose by exactly the rise in traded volume for the futures and all five heavyweights (conservation holds); the quote rule classified 59-92% of volume, which also shows the bid/ask order is right (best level first); data was fresh (last push under 1 s old).
- Found and fixed: (1) `classified_share` was 1.0 while CVD covered only 55% of HDFCBANK's day volume (volume before the first push, i.e. the opening auction or a backend restart, is never classified); the page now shows `coverage_of_day_volume` and the time it is counted from. (2) Nothing showed whether the figures were live: they now carry `age_seconds` and `stale` (no push for 120 s). (3) The book imbalance kept its last value forever if depth pushes stopped; it is now None after 60 s, also in the stored minute samples. (4) After a backend restart the futures note read "not looked up yet" although the contract was known; it now reports the contract, and the no-flow message says flow starts with the first push after 09:15.
- Not changed, to state plainly: CVD is an estimate (no trade tape with the aggressor side exists, so it can be checked for consistency, not against ground truth); the quote rule uses the book as pushed after the trade; `depth_imbalance_avg` averages per push, not per unit of time; research feature `fut_cvd_share` divides CVD by total volume, so it is biased low whenever coverage is below 1 (kept as defined, since models and tests use it).

## D69 — Three "Planned" tools built from data that already exists (2026-11-05)
- Why they were planned: each placeholder named something that was not built (an endpoint, a classification, a rule engine). Market hours do not change that; a planned tool opens only when its data and rule exist. The tool registry (`frontend/src/lib/tools.ts`) says what each one needs.
- Built now, from stored data only: **IV - HV** (`GET /api/intraday/hv`: HV 10/20/30/60 = sample stdev of daily log returns x sqrt(252) from final NIFTY 50 closes, checked against an independent calculation on the server's real closes: HV20 16.01% both ways; set beside the command center's ATM IV), **IV - Intraday** (`GET /api/intraday/iv-series`: ATM IV for each stored snapshot of the latest session, same model and inputs as the command center, thinned to 90 points, newest always kept), **Future Price vs OI** (frontend only, from `/api/futures/series`: the standard four-way price/OI reading per 15 minutes and for the session; a value not recorded stays missing).
- Still planned, and why: **IV Grid** (needs IV stored per strike over time, which is not stored), **OI Crossover** (no agreed rule), **Option Triggers** (needs a server-side rule engine and a delivery channel), **Future Heatmap** (futures of more than NIFTY: out of scope), **Future Sentiment Cycle** (no validated rule set). None of these is faked.

## D70 — Smart OI price candles: one-minute index bars from the live feed (2026-11-05)
- Cause of "no wicks, gaps between candles" on the Smart OI chart: the price candles were cut from the option-chain snapshots' index level (about one a minute). A 3-minute candle therefore had 3 points: its open was the first sample, its close the last one (about 2 minutes in), so the next candle opened a minute later than the previous close (the gap) and its high/low were only the highest/lowest of 3 points (almost no wicks). TradingView uses every tick.
- Fix: the market service reads the live NIFTY 50 index tick about once a second and stores one bar per finished session minute (`index1m` samples: open, high, low, close, ticks; written once, never rewritten; no bar for a minute without a fresh tick). `GET /api/market/index-bars` serves the newest day; the Smart OI chart builds each candle from the bars in its slot (open of the first, close of the last, extremes of all) and falls back to the stored samples where no bars exist, saying so in the chart title and footer.
- Limits stated: highs and lows are the extremes of about-one-second reads, not the exchange's tick tape, so they can still be a little narrower than TradingView; history before 5 Nov cannot be rebuilt (no intraday index series was stored), so older days keep the sampled candles.

## D71 — Swing section audit, 5 Nov 2026 (server read-only, then fixes)
- Found on the server: the paper book had processed one session only (3 Nov, run by hand at 01:36 IST on the 5th), so its queued orders came from the 3 Nov scan and were meant for 4 Nov. The live panel nevertheless checked them against 5 Nov prices and logged three "would buy" events (IIFLCAPS, ARIS, ANANDRATHI); those say nothing about the book, because the next run settles the orders on their own day first (the catch-up replays 4 Nov, then 5 Nov, in order, with no look-ahead). Fix: the live layer checks queued orders only when the book's last scan is the trading day before today (`queue_state`); otherwise the rows read "Not checked: queued for an earlier session" with a note saying how many sessions behind, no entry event is logged, and entry events already logged from such a queue are not shown (they stay in the append-only table). Events now record the scan session the order came from.
- Volume: the live volume rule is a straight line (day volume so far against 1.25 x the 20-day average, scaled by the share of the session elapsed), and trading volume is heaviest at the open, so the opening minutes flatter it (IIFLCAPS "triggered" at 09:16:23 on a pace floored at 5% of the session). Volume is now not judged before 09:45 IST (state "too early to judge volume"). The wording is explicit that the backtest rule uses the full-day volume, which is only known at the close, so a live "triggered" is provisional; "volume too low" now says what is short (GCSL: price through the trigger, volume at 26% of the pace needed).
- The backtest tabs returned 404 on the server (no data/swing/report.json there). A copy of the last built report now ships with the release (`backend/app/swing/report.json`, 215 KB); a report in the data folder still takes precedence. Rebuilt reports are committed the same way, or copied to the data folder.
- Checked and fine: a partial same-day bar is dropped before 16:00 IST; the sector gate flags in the scan are trend results, not fetch failures; gap-skip, trigger and volume numbers on the page agree with the stored orders (trigger = pivot x 1.003, no-chase limit = pivot x 1.025, minimum volume = 1.25 x SMA20).

## D72 — A day with no prices at the source must not become a permanent decision (2026-11-05)
- Found before tonight's catch-up run: Yahoo's 4 Nov rows for four of the seven queued stocks (SPECTRUM, ARIS, GCSL, HCLTECH) have no close or volume, so the fetcher dropped them. Settlement would then have written "skipped: no price bar for the session" for those orders (append-only, permanent) and, for held stocks, skipped the stop test without a trace.
- Rules now: (1) a held or queued stock with no bar for a session being settled stops the pass (`data_unavailable`, nothing committed for that day, retried every 15 minutes); (2) before stopping, the bar is read from NSE's own full bhavcopy for that day (one file per session, all equities; same unadjusted day values as Yahoo, checked on ANANDRATHI and KRISHANA), and the scan row says which bars came from NSE. A stock that really did not trade that day (suspended) has no bhavcopy row either and will hold the pass until someone looks at it.
- Why ltp crossing the trigger today did not open positions: the live layer is shadow-only (the book changes only at the end-of-day settlement), the queued orders were meant for 4 Nov, and an entry also needs the day's volume to reach 1.25 x the 20-day average. On the real 4 Nov bars: KRISHANA and ARIS meet every rule (entries at 237.14 and 148.19); ANANDRATHI (volume 50% of needed), IIFLCAPS (58%) and GCSL (87%) fail on volume, SPECTRUM gapped above its limit, HCLTECH never reached its trigger.

## D73 — Phone layout, PWA install and light theme (2026-11-05)
- **Overlaps on a phone** (OI-by-strike chips over the chart, Smart OI notes over the lower charts): the widgets lived in fixed-height boxes sized for a desktop font. When buttons wrapped, a note was long or the phone's font size was raised, the content outgrew the box and the chart (minimum 120 px) was drawn over it. Widgets on the phone layout (Analysis, below 820 px) and on tool pages now take the height of their content; every chart keeps the height it was designed for (`--chart-h`, `.widget-stacked .echart-box`); the option-chain table keeps a fixed box because it scrolls. Smart OI has explicit chart heights, and its long reading notes sit behind a "How to read this chart" disclosure. Tool pages put half-width widgets two to a row on wide screens.
- **Header**: one compact row on a phone (menu icon, logo, NIFTY level and change, status, theme); the wordmark and the "NIFTY 50 / Live" caption return from 520 px.
- **Strategy chain table**: rows are top-aligned (the LTP cell holds a hidden buy/sell row, which made LTP sit above the other cells) and on touch screens that hidden row is removed until a row is selected.
- **PWA "This app cannot be installed"**: the manifest, service worker and icons were behind the Caddy login (401, checked 5 Nov) and a browser fetches them without it, so Chrome found no manifest. With the owner's approval (5 Nov) the Caddy template exempts exactly those static files (manifest, sw.js, the icons and logos: no data) via `@private not path ...` and `basic_auth @private`; `src/test/pwa.test.ts` keeps the list in step with `public/` and forbids `/api`, `/ws`, `/` and wildcards in it. A deploy does not touch Caddy's config: `deploy/apply-caddyfile.sh <domain>` validates, keeps a backup, reloads and prints what is public.
- **Light theme**: Vega board colours and day-high/low shading used fixed colours (grey dotted line, emerald-400 text) that washed out on white; they now use the theme tokens. Muted text and borders are darker in light (`--muted` #475467, `--rule` #d3d9e4) and dark declares `color-scheme: dark` so native controls match.

## D74 — Session and timeline controls on the price boards (2026-11-05)
- Price vs OI, Multi Strike, Multi-Straddle and Vega Analysis drew their x-axis over the whole trading day (09:15 to the close), so during the session the lines filled only the left part of the chart. Each board now has a **Session** selector (every stored trading day with in-session samples, newest first; an earlier day is requested up to its own close with `until`) and a **Timeline** (Fit = first to newest sample plus a little room for the value tags, the default; Full day; last 2 h, 1 h, 30 min). The calculation is `sessionWindow` in `lib/analytics.ts` (hand-checked in `analytics.test.ts`). The axis only changes what is shown: the data and the fixed strikes of a session are unchanged.
- The Analysis time-series widgets already fitted the axis to the data while the session runs (`fitMax`), so they were left as they are. Vega Analysis also says what each axis is.

## D75 — IV Grid built; IV vs HV chart; full-size OI legend (2026-11-05)
- **IV Grid** (`GET /api/intraday/iv-grid`, tool page `ivgrid`): implied volatility by strike (ATM +- 10 of the newest snapshot, fixed all session) and stored snapshot, a cell being the IV of the out-of-the-money option of that strike (average at ATM). It is computed from the stored prices per snapshot, so nothing new is stored; missing prices stay empty cells. It was "planned" because no per-strike IV series existed; computing it on demand removes that need.
- **IV vs HV**: the page now has the latest session's ATM IV with the 10/20/30/60-day HV as dashed lines, and the 20-day HV history with the ATM IV at the close of each stored session (`iv_by_session` in the HV report: nearest expiry with at least 2 days left, named). IV exists only for sessions whose chains were stored (since 28 Oct); earlier days show HV only.
- **Full-size OI chart**: the legend wrapped onto a second row over the axis and the ATM label; it now scrolls on one line and the plot starts lower.

## D76 — One scrollbar; dates and times on the paper book (2026-11-05)
- **Two scrollbars** (Swing page): `sr-only` table captions are `position: absolute`; inside the scrolling `<main>` (not a positioning context) they were placed against the page, so the document grew to 2122 px in an 800 px window and the page scrolled as well as `<main>`. `<main>` is now `relative`; measured document height equals the window. Applies to every page with screen-reader-only text.
- **Timestamps**: the paper book is settled from daily bars after the close, so it knows the *day* of a fill, not the minute. Tables now show, under each entry, exit, decision and queued order: the day, when the paper job recorded that day (`booked`, IST), and, only where the live layer was watching (from 5 Nov), the exact moment the Kotak feed saw the rule met (`live trigger` / `live stop hit`, to the second). Nothing is guessed: no live sighting means no time. Catch-up passes record several days at once, so those share one `booked` time. Live shadow decisions now carry their date.

## D77 — Vega Analysis rebuilt as level, replay and change views (2026-11-06)
- The owner asked for the kind of analysis a public vega page shows. Its definitions were read from the page itself (labels, units, the explanatory text); no code or data was taken. Our version uses only our stored one-minute samples and our own Black-76 engine (`chainGreeks`, vega = rupees of price per volatility point per unit; at-the-money vega is about 10 with four days to expiry, the same scale as the page).
- **Level view** (`lib/vegaAnalysis.ts`): average call vega and average put vega over a strike window (selected strike +- 0 to 5 strikes, default the newest ATM +- 1), per 1/3/5/15-minute candle (last sample of the candle), with price, CE - PE bars, daily range and running-average overlays, a replay slider with Play and speed, and six cards: CE vega, PE vega, average, skew, regime and rank. Percentile = share of the stored history of that window below the current value plus half of those equal. Regime is our own rule (lowest third low, highest third high) and is shown only with at least 100 stored samples over two sessions; before that the cards say there is not enough history instead of inventing a rank. The public page's "volume-weighted" overlay is not built (no per-contract traded volume per sample in this series).
- **Change view**: sum of vega over the calls from the open's ATM upwards and the puts from it downwards (5/10/15/21 strikes per side; the server limits one query to 45 strikes, so 25-40 are not offered), minus the same strikes' vega at a chosen baseline sample; a strike counts only if it had a vega at both moments. Optional normalisation (percent of the baseline sum), synthetic future and selected-strike overlays, a latest-first table that starts with the zero baseline row, statistics and a plain-language reading.
- The old Vega board (OI-weighted and Greek-sum views) was removed; session and timeline controls of D74 apply.

## D78 — Gamma Exposure rebuilt (2026-11-06)
- Built from the definitions a public gamma page publishes in its own explanatory text (no code or data taken), on our own chain and samples. **GEX per strike** = gamma x open interest (units) x spot^2 x 1%, in rupees crore per 1% move, gamma from Black-76 with each contract's own implied volatility; calls positive, puts negative (the market assumed long calls, short puts: a convention, stated on the page; dealer positions are not published), net = call + put, absolute = call + |put|. This is the convention the old widget already used.
- **Levels**: call wall = largest call GEX at or above spot; put wall = largest put GEX at or below spot; peak gamma = largest absolute GEX; max pain from open interest; **gamma flip** = the spot level where aggregate net GEX changes sign, found by re-pricing every contract's gamma at trial spot levels (its own volatility fixed, +-8%) and bisecting the bracket nearest to spot (hand-checked: puts and calls of equal size cross at sqrt(K1 x K2) x e^(-sigma^2 t / 2)). Regime: sign of the aggregate, "near the flip" within 0.2%. Corridor: the nearest of put wall, call wall, peak, flip and max pain on each side of spot.
- **Page**: seven level cards; regime banner; regime / corridor / position against the flip; GEX profile by strike (combined, calls, puts; level lines; absolute line); gamma density (Gaussian-smoothed call, put and total concentration, peaks and intersection, extra-smooth option); spot, walls, peak, flip, net GEX and its change per minute through the session (21 strikes around the open ATM, 1/3/5/15-minute candles, session and timeline controls); text readings; top-10 strike table with types; absolute and net bars.
- **Not built, on purpose**: the public page's execution playbook (long and short triggers, targets, invalidation) and its proprietary "PT levels": their derivation is not published and inventing one would be fabricating a signal; the page says levels only. No volume overlay (no per-strike volume series) and no separate volatility-skew panel (the Volatility Skew tool exists). The whole-chain profile and the 21-strike session series differ in scope, and the page says so.

## D79 — IV tools: IV / HV / IVP chart, IV − HV bars, IV Grid removed (2026-11-06)
- **IV Grid removed** at the user's request (tool, page, `/api/intraday/iv-grid`, `IntradayService.iv_grid`, tests, types).
- **IV / HV / IVP Chart** (new tool `iv-hvp`, `GET /api/intraday/iv-chart?hv=10|20|30|60`): daily India VIX (the implied-volatility series), rolling HV of NIFTY and the NIFTY close, on dates present in both stored daily histories. IVR = (IV − low) ÷ (high − low) × 100 and IVP = share of the range's days below today's IV, over a 3M / 6M / 1Y range. A range that is not full (or has under 80% of its days) gives no rank, never a shorter-range rank.
- **Why India VIX:** our own ATM IV exists only for the few sessions whose chains were stored, so a year of IV, IVR and IVP cannot come from it. The page says the series is India VIX (NSE's 30-day figure), not one expiry's ATM IV. No future-price history is held, so the price line is the NIFTY daily close. The reference's separate "RV" line was not built (it would duplicate HV).
- **IV − HV** page: daily IV − HV bars with the NIFTY line and an HV window selector added above the existing table and session charts.
- **IV − Intraday** (`GET /api/intraday/iv-series?expiry&session&tf&max_points`, `GET /api/intraday/iv-sessions`): up to five expiries on one chart, a stored-session picker, 1/3/5/15-minute candles (the last stored snapshot of each candle, counted from the open), the synthetic future (put-call-parity forward) on a second axis, and a Term structure view built from each chosen expiry's last computable ATM IV (the page says expiries may be sampled at different minutes). Live data comes from the same one-minute stored chains; there is no separate live mode.
- **Volatility Skew** (`GET /api/intraday/iv-skew`, tool page `ivskew`, new `skew_metrics` in `volatility.py`): cards ATM IV, 25Δ skew and 10Δ skew (put IV − call IV at the nearest delta, accepted only within 0.1 / 0.05 of the target), 25Δ butterfly ((put wing + call wing) / 2 − ATM IV), skew slope (least-squares IV points per 1% moneyness over the OTM options) and term slope (ATM IV far − near per 30 days, across expiries stored within 5 minutes). Views: Smile, Term, Table. A figure with no contract near its target delta is missing, not zero. Hand-checked in `test_skew_metrics_match_the_hand_calculation`.
- The Analysis dashboard's old `ivSkew` widget is unchanged.

## D80 — Future Lab: Price vs OI rebuilt; Heatmap and Sentiment Cycle removed (2026-11-06)
- **Removed** at the user's request: Future Heatmap and Future Sentiment Cycle (both were planned placeholders).
- **Future Price vs OI**: timeframe 1 / 3 / 5 / 15 minutes (default 15), counts of long buildup / short covering / short buildup / long unwinding, a bullish-vs-bearish window bar (bullish = long buildup or short covering; windows with no change or reading are excluded; our reading: 55% or more bullish is Bullish, 45% or less Bearish), the price (left) and open interest (right, crore) chart with Price / OI toggles, and the table newest first. Same stored one-minute futures samples and the same classification rule as before. There is no Replay or Historical mode: the futures series API serves the latest session only.
- **RV line added (same day, at the user's request):** the reference does not say how its RV is computed, so ours is labelled "RV (range)": Parkinson volatility sqrt(mean(ln(H/L)^2) / (4 ln 2) x 252) over the HV window, from each daily high and low (a window with any bad bar is missing). It is not claimed to match the reference's RV.

## D81 — Audit fixes: daily-history close run, chart date axes, skew term view, one rate constant (2026-11-06)
- **Daily history missed each day's close bar (server, found 6 Nov):** `HistoryService.due()` treated any successful run on the same IST date as "done", so a successful 07:30-09:10 morning run blocked the 18:30 run. 5 Nov's NIFTY / India VIX bars were therefore not fetched in the evening (server stored through 4 Nov). Now the evening run is due whenever the last good run was before today's 18:30 IST (regression test in `test_history.py`). The 5 Nov bar arrives with the next morning run or, from now on, every evening.
- **Chart axes (IV / HV / IVP and IV − HV):** the x-axis hid labels by overlap, so the last label shown was 29 Oct although data ran to 4 Nov, and end labels collided with the y-axis ticks. Labels are now counted back from the newest day (always labelled), formatted "5 Nov 26", with end alignment and more bottom room. The chart now shows exactly the chosen range (3M 63 / 6M 126 / 1Y 252 trading days; it used to show at least 126 whatever you chose), and IV − HV has the same range buttons.
- **Volatility Skew term view** listed expiries of "today", so it was empty after midnight; it now uses the chain's own session.
- **Risk-free rate:** the frontend repeated 0.06 in four places; it is now one constant (`lib/assumptions.ts`) that must equal the backend `DEFAULT_RATE`. Note the backend `RISK_FREE_RATE` setting is not wired into `IntradayService` (it uses `DEFAULT_RATE`); both are 6%.

## D82 — Numeric audit against independent code and StockMojo, 5 Nov 2026 session (read-only; 6 Nov)
Method: the last stored chain of the 9 Nov expiry (15:38 IST, spot 24 536.60) and the stored futures minutes were read from the server (read-only), recomputed with independent Python, and run through our own frontend code. StockMojo values were read from its pages for the same session. No data was changed.
- **ATM IV engine:** independent Black-76 gives ATM IV 15.865% (r = 0) / 15.875% (r = 6%) at strike 24 550 with F = K + C − P = 24 536.85; the service returned 15.861% (parity forward 24 536.46, r = 6%). Agreement within 0.015 points. Call IV and put IV are equal at the parity forward, as they must be.
- **Versus StockMojo (15:40, 9 Nov expiry): theirs 15.44%, ours 15.86%.** With the same call price and time, their number is reproduced to 0.005 points by the call alone at their displayed "future" 24 545.75 (15.435%); the put at that forward would give 16.30%. So the model and the time to expiry are the same; the difference is the forward and using one side instead of the call-put average. Our own futures price at that minute was 24 579 to 24 581, so their "future" is neither our futures nor spot (24 536.6). We keep the parity forward (it makes call and put agree) and do not tune to match. The 23 Nov expiry shows the same sign: ours 13.69% against their daily 13.34%.
- **Futures price and OI versus StockMojo's table (15:21 to 15:39):** price within 0.0 to 6 points, OI within about 0.1% at each 3-minute step (their sampling second differs). Their 15:40 row (price 24 588, OI 12 641 590, a drop of 2.2 lakh) is the exchange's closing print; our last stored minute is 15:39, so that print is not in our series.
- **Gamma exposure:** independent code on the same chain gives calls 41 573 crore, puts −33 293 crore, net +8 280 crore (gamma with respect to the forward, OI in units, spot² × 1%); our frontend code gives 41 576, −33 325 and +8 251 (0.01%, 0.1%, 0.35% apart; it uses a chain-wide parity forward and skips 25 contracts with no solvable price). OI is confirmed to be in units (141.8 million call units = 2.18 million lots of 65 for this expiry). ATM call vega 10.24 per point matches F × φ(d1) × √T ÷ 100.
- **India VIX as IV on the daily pages:** a different quantity from StockMojo's daily ATM IV (theirs 13.34% on 5 Nov; India VIX was 15.19% on 4 Nov). Labelled as India VIX on our pages.
- **Known limits, not changed:** (1) the "ATM IV at the close by session" series on the IV − HV page picks the nearest expiry with at least 2 days left, so it can switch between the weekly and the monthly from one day to the next (3 Nov monthly 13.70, 4 and 5 Nov weekly 14.86, 15.86); the expiry is named per point. (2) No chain is stored for the 17 Nov expiry that StockMojo lists. (3) The closing print after 15:39 is not captured. (4) The assumed 6% rate and the 252-day year are assumptions shown on the pages.

## D83 — HV and RV conventions corrected against StockMojo's IV / HV / IVP page (2026-11-06)
- **What was wrong (my miss):** D82 compared ATM IV, GEX, vega and futures but left HV, RV, IVR and IVP unchecked, and the RV I had added (Parkinson range volatility) was a guess. StockMojo's numbers for 5 Nov (HV range 1 Month): IV 13.34, HV 16.83, RV 17.75, IVR 26.06, IVP 63.89; ours showed HV 16.01, RV 13.38.
- **Reverse-engineered on our own stored bars (5 Nov close 24 536.60 added to the stored bars through 4 Nov):** over the last **21** daily log returns, **HV = population standard deviation (divide by n) × √252 = 16.83** and **RV = root mean square of the returns (no mean removed) × √252 = 17.75**: both reproduce their values exactly. Our old HV (20 returns, divide by n − 1) gave 17.20 on the same data and Parkinson gave 13.63.
- **Changed:** HV now divides by n; RV is the root-mean-square realised volatility (Parkinson removed); the chart's HV windows are 10 days, 1M (21) and 3M (63) with 1M the default. Hand-checked tests: returns 0.0198026 and −0.0098523 give HV 23.54 and RV 24.83.
- **Most of the visible gap was one missing day:** our stored daily bars end on 4 Nov (D81: the 18:30 run did not fire on 5 Nov). 5 Nov's +2.40% return is inside the window of StockMojo's figures and not in ours.
- **Not reproducible, by data not by formula:** their IV is a daily at-the-money IV (13.34 on 5 Nov) and their IVR / IVP are ranks of that series over a year. We store option chains for only about 7 sessions, so our IVR and IVP rank India VIX instead and will not match theirs. This is stated on the page.


## D84 — Latest session shown as a flagged provisional day (2026-11-06)
- **Why the charts ended on 4 Nov:** Yahoo publishes a day's NIFTY and India VIX bar the next morning. On 5 Nov evening its daily row had Open/High/Low but a NaN close (checked read-only on the server; a manual refresh was run at the user's request and correctly stored nothing, `incomplete: 1` per symbol). The bar of 4 Nov reached us at 08:00 IST on 5 Nov. The D81 scheduler fix does not change this; Yahoo's data is the limit.
- **Change:** `IntradayService._provisional_bar` takes the newest stored session that is newer than the last daily bar and uses our own index feed's last in-session level as its close (never stored as history, replaced by the real bar when it arrives, not used if the gap exceeds 5 days or the date is in the future). `iv_chart` and `hv` add it so HV, RV and the NIFTY line reach the latest session; India VIX has no source for that day and stays empty (a gap, not a copy). The pages say the day is provisional. With 5 Nov's close the HV (1M) and RV match StockMojo's 16.83 and 17.75 (D83).

## D85 — Gamma and vega audit against StockMojo, chart layout fixes (2026-11-06)
- **Gamma scale:** StockMojo shows 25 050 as net 30 406.7 and absolute 30 968.8 crore (header net 6.09 lakh crore, absolute 37.70 lakh crore); ours is 437 and 445 crore (whole chain: net +8 251, calls 41 576). Their call and put parts at that strike (30 687.7 and 281.1) are 69.6 and 70.3 times ours, so the strike-level shape agrees and the scale does not. A bound shows ours is the possible one: GEX per 1% = gamma x OI x spot² x 0.01 = (change in delta for a 1% move, at most 1) x OI x spot, so a strike's GEX cannot exceed its open-interest notional. Our 25 050 call OI is 1 543 750 units (about ₹3 800 crore of notional): ours (437) is inside it, theirs (30 687) is 8 times over; the whole call side is ₹3.48 lakh crore of notional against their 21.9 lakh crore. A factor of about 65 (the lot size, applied to an open interest that is already in units) fits. OI in the chain is in units: 141.8 million call units for the expiry, and the futures OI from the same feed agrees with StockMojo's futures table.
