- **Vega change:** our page reproduces its own figure (21 strikes a side, baseline 09:15: call +45.78, put −42.02, checked with the same stored 5 Nov prices). StockMojo's call +24.19 and put −33.89 cannot be reproduced by any symmetric band around the first-minute ATM (10 strikes gives +21.3 and −23.7, 15 gives +35.3 and −34.7); their call and put are asymmetric, so their strike window, or their IV and vega model, differs. Not tuned to match.
- **CE − PE vega at one strike is about 0 after the forward is fit (0.0000 from 13:30):** the chain forward comes from put-call parity, so call and put of the ATM strike get the same implied volatility and vega. The early-session spread (−1.51 at 09:15) is stale first-minute prices. It is real but says little for a single strike; use a strike window.
- **Layout:** right-axis numbers no longer sit under the latest-value tags (tags first, axis numbers after: shared `timeLinesOption`); the GEX profile's level labels are horizontal and staggered over four rows and the absolute line is no longer smoothed (the spline overshot between strikes); the session chart has separate panels with titles, readable tooltips (the raw 16-digit numbers are gone) and leaves levels more than 4% from spot off the price panel instead of squashing it.

## D86 — IV − HV: our own ATM IV beside India VIX; integrity sweep of stored data (2026-11-06)
- **What the user saw:** the IV − HV bars end on 4 Nov with an empty 5 Nov, and the Sep–Oct bars are mostly positive where StockMojo's are mostly negative. Both come from the series used: our bars are India VIX minus HV, StockMojo's are a daily at-the-money IV minus HV (their 5 Nov: IV 13.34, HV 16.83, IV − HV −3.49; consistent with the HV we reproduce). India VIX for 5 Nov is not published by Yahoo yet (close NaN), so it is left empty.
- **Added:** `own_iv` in `/api/intraday/iv-chart`: our ATM IV of the farthest stored expiry with 5 or more days left (the monthly; `iv_history(far=True)` keeps one maturity from day to day) for each session whose option chain we stored, plotted as diamonds on both IV charts ("ATM IV (ours, monthly)" and "ATM IV − HV (ours, monthly)"). For 5 Nov: ATM IV 13.69% (23 Nov expiry) against HV 16.83 gives −3.14 (StockMojo −3.49; the 0.35-point IV gap is the forward-price convention measured in D82). It exists only from 28 Oct, when chain storage began; earlier days are VIX only and say so.
- **Integrity sweep (read-only, server, 6 Nov):** daily bars, 25 symbols: no impossible OHLC, no duplicate dates, no gap above 6 days in the Indian series; 4 Nov is the newest bar (5 Nov unpublished). One NIFTY bar on a Saturday (1 Feb 2025, the Budget-day session: real). **Five 24-hour series (BRENT, DXY, GOLD, USDINR, WTI) have a final bar dated Sunday 1 Nov 2026**: Yahoo's DST-shifted stamps; flagged by the history status; not used by the IV, vega, gamma or futures pages; stored bars are immutable, so correcting them needs approval. Option samples: 9 sessions stored; one sample a minute with 0 to 6 gaps of 3.5 to 8 minutes a session (5 Nov: 13:15, 8.1 minutes), no spot jump above 0.6% a minute; charts draw across such gaps. Option-chain exposure totals now say how many strikes they cover.

## D87 — Section-by-section numeric audit, 6 Nov 2026 (read-only on the server; every figure below was recomputed with separate code)
- **IV tools:** skew cards (25Δ skew −0.756, 10Δ −0.461, butterfly +0.581, slope +0.1147 per 1% moneyness) match an independent recomputation exactly; IVR and IVP over 3M, 6M and 1Y (71.07 / 80.95, 52.16 / 70.63, 32.23 / 69.05 on 4 Nov) match the frontend library; HV (1M) 16.83 and RV 17.75 for 5 Nov match StockMojo (D83); term slope −4.654 per 30 days equals (13.69 − 15.86) / 14 × 30.
- **Gamma:** net GEX at spot +8 251.2 crore, flip 24 428.9, call wall 25 000, put wall 24 400, peak 24 500 equal an independent re-pricing (own implied volatility per contract, bisection on re-priced net GEX). Vega change over 21 strikes a side from four baselines (09:15, 09:20, 09:30, 10:00) agrees with independent code to 0.05 (D85).
- **Futures:** on all 385 minute rows of 5 Nov: basis = future − spot, basis % , OI change = OI − previous-session-close OI (13 829 205), fair basis = spot (e^(rT) − 1) and carry = ln(future / spot) / T have no violation; no missing values; no gap above 2.5 minutes; no OI jump above 1.5 lakh a minute.
- **Option chain:** all 81 strikes: OI change = OI − previous OI and its percent hold; PCR (all strikes) 1.0304, ATM ± 10 PCR 0.6822 and max pain 24 350 equal independent values. The chain's previous-day OI is the exchange's end-of-day figure and differs by about 0.3% from our own last live minute of the previous day for 111 of 138 contracts (the exchange adjusts OI after the close); the rule (current minus previous-day OI) uses the exchange figure.
- **Corrections to my own earlier work:** my independent IV checks in D82 used a 15:30 expiry close; the application correctly uses 15:40, the derivatives close since 3 Aug 2026 (`app/domain/session.py`), a 10-minute difference worth about 0.015 IV points at 4 days. The conclusions of D82 stand.
- **Bugs fixed in this pass:** an unknown data age was shown as "0 s ago" in the health list (now says the age is unknown); option-chain exposure totals silently skipped strikes (now state how many strikes they cover); the persistent cross-checks are in `tests/unit/test_data_integrity.py` (every solved IV re-prices its market price with an independent Black-76; forward, ATM strike and ATM IV follow from the chain; skew measures follow from the rows; time to expiry runs to the session close).
- **Not audited with independent recomputation yet:** Prediction, Swing paper trading, Strategy builder payoff maths, Portfolio, Intraday scores and ML, Market breadth and sector pages. Those rely on their own tests and the earlier swing audit.

## D88 — Audit continued: market breadth and swing paper book (6 Nov 2026, read-only, independent recomputation)
- **Market breadth (NIFTY 50, 5 Nov):** advancing 39, declining 11, equal-weight return 2.0207, cap-weight return 2.4523, sum of contributions 2.4525 and every stock's contribution (weight × change ÷ 100) agree with the app. Top-5 and top-10 share (0.5277 and 0.7333) and stocks needed for half (5) agree once the definition is read from the code: the sum of the largest same-direction contributions divided by the net move, not a share of the absolute total (my first comparison used the wrong denominator; no bug). Sector rows (15) agree for equal-weight return, cap-weight return, contribution and stock count; relative strength is the sector's cap-weight return minus the index change (4.636 − 2.404 = 2.232 for Auto). Three single-stock sectors (Capital Goods, Telecommunication, Construction) have no sector return by rule (`min_covered = 2`): missing, not a one-stock average presented as a sector.
- **Swing paper book:** equity = cash + invested; invested = sum of open-position values; return % = (equity ÷ capital − 1); unrealised P&L and percent per open position; trade gross P&L, costs (0.15% each side) and net; closed-trade totals (net −3 234.38, costs 1 196.89, wins 0); the equity curve; and cash (capital − cost of open positions + realised net − entry costs on the open positions, 599.15) all reconcile to the paisa. R multiple is the gross price move divided by the entry risk ((exit − fill) ÷ (fill − initial stop), −0.2224 and −0.0705): it excludes costs, which are reported separately.
- **Still not independently re-checked:** Prediction forecasts, Strategy builder payoff maths and Portfolio, intraday sentiment scores and ML. These rest on their unit tests and the earlier swing audit.

## D89 — Server Management Centre (2026-11-06, branch wip/server-management, not deployed)
- **What:** System menu "Data clean-up" is now "Server Management Centre" (`/server`, 14 tabs). The existing clean-up is unchanged and sits inside Maintenance; `/cleanup` still works. Full description, security model, one-time server setup, verification and rollback: [server-management](server-management.md).
- **Own database** `server_mgmt.sqlite3` (WAL) rather than the trading stores: it is bounded by retention and prunable; the trading data is never touched by it.
- **Collection is server-side and independent of the browser:** a metric sample every 300 s and a daily storage snapshot at 18:45 IST (catch-up after downtime), one scan at a time, one daily row per IST date (unique index), failed scans stored as failed rows without numbers. A page load never scans.
- **Growth is only between complete snapshots of consecutive days taken with the same method**; otherwise "—". Forecast needs 5 comparable days, uses the 7-day average, and is withheld for zero or negative growth or erratic changes.
- **No generic shell, no new dependency** (/proc and the standard library). Changes need loopback + Origin check + `X-NSE-Action` header; destructive ones also confirmation with a preview. The web process cannot restart services (`NoNewPrivileges`, `ProtectSystem=strict`): a restart is a request file read by a root-owned helper installed once at `/usr/local/sbin/nse-mgmt-helper` (never run from the app-owned release folder), which restarts only `nse-universe`.
- **Defaults chosen for accuracy:** exclusions are empty (everything counts toward storage totals); release rollback is not offered from the website.
- **Found while testing:** a bearer token after "Authorization:" was left visible by the first redaction order (fixed, tested); Windows junctions would have been followed by the scan (fixed, tested).
- **Not verified:** anything on the production host (see the document); symlink/hard-link tests are skipped on Windows.
## D90 — Audit continued: strategy payoffs, prediction ledger, intraday scores (6 Nov 2026)
- **Strategy builder (expiry payoff):** `frontend/src/test/strategy-property.test.ts` generates 150 random strategies (1 to 5 legs, calls and puts, bought and sold, some legs already exited with booked P&L) and compares the exact analysis (maximum profit and loss, unlimited profit or loss, breakevens) with a plain scan of the expiry payoff on a 1-point grid. All agree; every reported breakeven sits on a zero and every sign change on the grid has a reported breakeven. Portfolio and the live strategy P&L use the same leg functions.
- **Prediction ledger (read-only on the server):** all 8 T+1 forecasts (four models for each of 29 Oct and 30 Oct) keep their stored hash, were written before the target session opened (03:05 and 03:07 UTC, before 09:15 IST) and their outcomes were attached after it (+0.5073% and +1.6818%, equal to the close-to-close return from the daily bars, direction correct). **Gap found:** no forecast exists for the reference sessions 2 Nov, 3 Nov and 4 Nov (so none for 3, 4 and 5 Nov); this is the problem recorded earlier whose fix (D67, provisional overnight bars) has not yet been seen to work in a live morning. It is not a calculation fault, and nothing is backfilled (a forecast written after the fact would not be a forecast). The next morning run (07:30 to 09:10 IST on 6 Nov) is the test: if it does not write one, the cause is in the scheduler or the data wait, not the maths.
- **Intraday sentiment scores:** all 4 328 stored scores recompute exactly: coverage = sum of the weights of the available components, each contribution = weight × component score, score = sum of contributions ÷ coverage, every component score within [−1, 1]; no row lacks a score while coverage is 0.5 or more; the label bands (±0.2, ±0.5, with the high-volatility override) hold.
- **Audit status:** every numeric area has now been recomputed with separate code at least once (IV tools, gamma, vega, futures, option chain, breadth, swing book, strategy payoffs, prediction ledger, intraday scores). What this is not: a line-by-line read of every file. Layout and wording of each page were checked only where reported. Open items: forecast gap above; weekend-dated bars of five 24-hour series (D86).

## D91 — A stopped-out leg came back to life and was stopped again, six times (6 Nov 2026)
- **What happened (server data, read-only):** the 24550 sold-CE leg of the straddle (entry 160.70, 30% stop at 208.90) was closed by the server six times at 09:50, 10:07, 12:13 (twice, 14 s apart), 13:09, 14:04 and 15:34 IST, at 209.10, 209.05, 208.95, 210.70, 226.00, 367.00 and 474.75, with no re-entry event in the server's log and the entry price still 160.70 (a real re-entry would have reset it to the live price). Between the closes the leg was open again. The web server's access log shows an accepted `PUT /api/strategies/running` from a browser (an Android phone, later the desktop) in the seconds before each of the next closes.
- **Cause:** the server accepted any browser write whose revision number matched, and replaced the whole leg list with what the browser sent. A browser holding an older copy of the legs (leg still open) but the current revision number therefore re-opened a leg the server had closed; with the market already above the stop, the server closed it again at the next check. Each cycle booked a new loss at that moment's price (the last one −₹20,413 instead of about −₹3,133 at the real stop), and the old copy kept coming back.
- **Fixes (three layers):** (1) the server now refuses to re-open a closed leg from a copy that merely never saw the exit: a closed leg stays closed unless the incoming leg was really entered again (a re-entry changes the entry price and counts a cycle), has a new leg id (a template or saved strategy opened) or the editor asked for a fresh start (the Reset button now sends a new `rearmAt`); every refusal is written to the server events ("Ignored an edit that would have re-opened …"). (2) The browser binds a write to the revision its payload was built on, not the revision at the moment of sending, so a write built before the browser adopted the server's exit is refused with a conflict. (3) The browser no longer runs its own stop and target checks on live prices until it has fetched the server's copy (before that it holds only the old copy kept in the browser, which can be hours behind): on opening the desktop page at 15:34 that old copy closed yesterday's already-closed straddle again at 709.30 and re-created it in Saved (the 'restore' at 10:04:37 UTC).
- **Not proven:** the exact browser sequence that sent a stale leg list with the current revision. The logs prove the writes and their times, not the browser state; (2) closes the one path found in the code, and (1) makes any such write harmless whatever its origin. One strategy slot is shared by every device that opens the builder: two devices with different strategies in the builder still overwrite each other's slot (the revision check turns the later one into a conflict and the page then takes the server's copy).
- Tests: `test_a_closed_leg_can_only_be_reopened_by_a_real_re_entry_or_a_fresh_start`; the existing service test that accepted such an overwrite now asserts the leg stays closed.
- **Correction made (6 Nov 2026, approved by the user):** the running strategy's CE leg was restored from the last close (474.75, −₹20,413) to its first stop (209.10, −₹3,146 = −(209.10 − 160.70) × 65), through the application's own `PUT /api/strategies/running` at the current revision (rev 75), after checking the leg still had exactly the state described above. The server's event log still lists all seven closes, and the leg's own log says what was corrected. The saved copy "Straddle -range bound-06/10/2026" was not touched; the PE leg is unchanged and open.

## D92 — Strategy builder re-audit; live Volatility Skew (6 Nov 2026)
- **Strategy builder:** the rule engines were compared mechanically. `backend/tests/fixtures/strategy_parity.json` holds 300 generated strategies (legs, live prices, leg stop, target, trailing stop, re-entries, strategy stop loss, target, lock and trail); the server code reproduces it (`test_strategy_parity.py`, which fails if the file is out of date) and the browser code gives the identical legs, event counts and rule state (`strategy-parity.test.ts`). A second pass over the same prices changes nothing except a permitted re-entry (a stop with re-entries left, one per pass); a fired strategy rule never fires again and nothing re-enters after it. So a rule repeating comes from the document sync fixed in D91, not from the rules. D91's guard now covers the other things an old copy could undo: a leg with fewer cycles than the server's (a re-entry taken back) and a trailed stop sent back at its pre-trail level (a trailing stop only tightens). A stop the user types to another level, a real re-entry, a new leg id and the Reset button are not blocked.
- **Volatility Skew is live again:** the page read only the stored chain (a sample a minute, refreshed every 60 s), while the old skew widget recomputed from the live chain on every tick. While the stream is fresh the smile and the 25 and 10 delta skew, butterfly and slope are now recomputed from the live chain prices on every tick (same Black-76 contract solving as the old widget, `lib/skew.ts`, same definitions and the same hand-worked numbers as the backend, `skew-lib.test.ts`); the header says "live chain, updating with every tick" or "stored chain". When the stream is not live (market closed, stale), and always for the term view and term slope (other expiries), the stored chain is used and its age is shown. The deltas of the live path come from the browser's contract Greeks (delta with respect to spot); the stored path uses the backend's: they differ in the third decimal, so a contract exactly at the 25 or 10 delta boundary may be picked on one and not the other.

## D93 — Swing page: cooldown labelling, live open positions, exit levels; Strategy portfolio statistics (6 Nov 2026)
- **Why ARIS and IIFLCAPS were queued again after a day-1 cut: it follows the rules.** The scan (`make_orders`) lists every stock that passes the score and the NIFTY and sector gates and knows nothing of the book; the 3-session cooldown is applied when the order is judged (`settle_session`: skipped when `session ordinal − ordinal of the last exit < 3`). This is also how the research backtest behaves, and the paper engine is tested trade for trade against it, so the queue was not changed. ARIS (exit session 2) and IIFLCAPS (exit session 3) are session 4's orders: 4 − 2 = 2 and 4 − 3 = 1, both below 3, so both will be skipped on 6 Nov (ARIS for one session, IIFLCAPS for two). The page misled by showing them as ordinary orders; the orders table now has a "Next session" column: "Will be skipped: cooldown, N more sessions" (from the book's own trades and the scan's session number) or "Can fill", with the live price and distance to the trigger.
- **Open positions now use the live feed:** price now (the feed's last trade, marked live, stale with its age, or "settled close" when there is no live price), the settled close beside it (the figure the paper book uses: NSE's closing price is an average of the last half hour, so it differs from the last trade by a little), unrealised P&L at the live price with the settled figure under it, the current stop and its distance, both exit levels with a reached tick (1.8R: stop to breakeven + 0.2%; 2.5R: the trailing stop starts; R = fill − initial stop), the R reached and the rupee risk, and a price line (stop, entry, 1.8R, 2.5R, price). There is no fixed profit target by design (trend-following exit by trailing stop) and the page says so. A "Live equity (provisional)" figure (cash + positions at live price) sits with the book figures. The live prices panel and every live figure carry a "provisional" mark: the paper book still follows the end-of-day settlement.
- **Strategy portfolio:** a Performance panel from the closed strategies' booked P&L only: net P&L, win rate, expectancy (win rate × average win + loss rate × average loss = net ÷ count), profit factor, average win and loss with payoff, maximum drawdown and current drawdown (rupees from the highest cumulative P&L reached; percentages only against a capital the user types in, kept in this browser), best and worst strategy, longest win and loss streaks and the current streak, recovery factor, best and worst day, and a cumulative P&L curve with a drawdown panel; the closed table gains a cumulative column. Hand-worked tests in `portfolio-stats.test.ts`, `swing-positions.test.tsx`. With fewer than 10 closed strategies the page says the figures are not statistically meaningful.

## D94 — The stopped leg came back again on 9 Nov, after D91's guard (10 Nov 2026)
- **What the server shows (read-only):** the 24900 short straddle of 9 Nov: the PE leg was stopped by the server at 09:41:06 IST (107.25) and again at 10:42:28 (106.20), both with entry 81.55 and no re-entry. The CE leg was exited by hand at 09:59:54 (+₹920); in the page the CE leg then showed "EXITED" with the red text "2026-11-09 is not a listed expiry for this instrument" (the 9 Nov expiry had passed when the screenshot was taken on 10 Nov, which is only the validation message). The server's own event log never printed "Ignored an old copy", so the guard of D91 was never reached by the write that re-opened the leg.
- **Cause, from the saved strategy's revisions and the access log:** the phone and the desktop held the same strategy under different leg ids. At 09:59:55 the phone saved the strategy and wrote the running copy straight after its manual CE exit: its copy still had the PE leg open (it had not taken the server's exit of 09:41, 18 minutes earlier), under ids (`…ch-0`, `…ch-1`) that were not the server's. D91's guard matched legs by id only, so it did not recognise the same PE leg and let it be re-opened; the server stopped it again at 10:42. Three things made this possible: (1) the guard keyed on leg ids; (2) a page that has anything unsent never took the server's exits, and the unsent test compared a document that included the live-feed keys, which come and go with the stream, so an idle phone could look permanently "edited"; (3) the server wrote its exits only to the running copy, never to the saved strategy the page had open, so the saved copy always kept the leg open and re-opening it on any device brought the leg back.
- **Fixes:** (a) the server matches an incoming leg to its own by id, else by contract, side and entry premium, so another device's ids no longer bypass the guard; a deliberate new document (a strategy opened from Saved, a template, New, a changed expiry, Reset, Exit all) sends a new `rearmAt`, which the server accepts as a fresh start. (b) The page now takes the server's exits at once, even with an edit waiting (`adoptExits`, matched the same way; a leg entered again here has another premium and is left alone), and its unsent-edit test ignores the live-feed keys. (c) The server writes the stops it executes back to the saved strategy the page has open (`loaded`), as the page already did for its own exits. Tests: id-independent guard and write-back (`test_strategy_automation.py`), `adoptExits` (`strategy.test.ts`).
- **Not proven / limits:** that the phone's stale copy came from the changing keys is an inference (the logs show the writes, not the page state). I did not add a page-level test of the two-device race. Two devices that deliberately hold different strategies in the one running slot still overwrite each other (the later is refused as a conflict and the page takes the server's copy).
- **Correction made (10 Nov 2026, approved by the user):** the closed record "Expiry straddle-09/2026" booked +₹6,217.25 because the re-opened PE leg was carried to the expiry price 0.05. It now books the rule result: PE stopped at 107.25 (−(107.25 − 81.55) × 65 = −₹1,670.50) plus the CE exited by hand at 107.50 (+₹919.75) = −₹750.75. Done through the application's own `PUT /api/strategies/closed` after checking the record still had exactly the state described; the record carries a `correction` note and each leg's log says what was corrected, and the earlier version stays in the revision history (create 9 Nov 16:20 IST, update 10 Nov).

## D95 — No chart animation; charts fit phones; the new weekly with no stored session (10 Nov 2026)
- **Animation off everywhere:** the shared chart wrapper (`EChart.tsx`) now forces `animation: false` and zero animation durations on every update, so a chart redrawn on each tick (the live Volatility Skew, price boards) no longer keeps sliding and can be read. This replaces the per-chart settings, which several charts lacked.
- **Charts fit narrow screens:** below 560 px of container width `fitNarrow` caps the numeric grid margins (left 46, right 66), hides a right axis whose numbers had been pushed out beside the latest-value tags (the tags and the tooltip carry the values) and shrinks the tags' font. The Vega charts used a fixed 132 px right margin that left the plot about half the phone width. Wide containers are unchanged. Checked at 375 px in the harness (Vega change chart fills the width); tests in `expiry-fallback.test.tsx`.
- **"No session samples stored yet" on the holiday (10 Nov):** the weekly expiry had rolled to 17 Nov, which the sampler only stores after hours until its own first session (on 9 Nov the weekly was the expiring 9 Nov contract and the monthly 23 Nov), so the Vega, Gamma, Smart OI and other tool pages, which default to the weekly, correctly found no session for it. The 23 Nov monthly holds the full 9 Nov session. `/api/expiries` now reports `last_session` (expiry → latest session with at least 10 stored in-session samples), and the tool pages open on the monthly, with a visible note and the last session's date, whenever the weekly has none; picking Weekly yourself is never overridden. The sampler is not changed: storing the next weekly earlier would add storage and was not asked for.
- **Correction to the previous paragraph (10 Nov 2026 was an exchange holiday, found from the server):** the weekly 17 Nov and monthly 23 Nov each had 13 to 16 "in-session" samples on 10 Nov (09:07 to 09:27 IST): the sampler runs on a holiday, stores a few samples and then the same chain flagged unchanged (12 of 14), all at the last close 25 044.65. The session counters counted them, so (1) the boards chose 10 Nov, the holiday, as the latest session (flat, or "no session samples" at 09:16 with one sample); (2) `last_session` named 10 Nov; and (3) worst, the IV charts added 10 Nov as a provisional day with the unchanged close, injecting a zero-return day into HV and RV (the 21-day HV read 14.99 on that day instead of 15.47 on 9 Nov). Now a day counts as a session only with changed samples: the observation store counts changed samples only (`unchanged = 0`; every real session so far has none flagged), the IV session list and provisional day need at least 10 of them (`MIN_SESSION_SAMPLES`), and the boards (`sessionDays`) skip a day that has any unchanged sample and fewer than 10 changed ones, so on a holiday, and in the first minutes after a new weekly starts, they show the last real session (9 Nov) or the monthly's. Tests: `test_an_exchange_holiday_of_stale_samples_is_not_a_session`, `sessionDays on an exchange holiday`. The full backend suite passes (767).

## D96 — Payoff estimate counts closed legs; hide the chain; intraday / overnight holding (10 Nov 2026)
- **Blue curve above the green one:** the "On target date (model)" curve summed only the legs still open, while the "On expiry" curve (correctly) also carries the booked P&L of legs already closed by a stop or target. After a stop-out the estimate was therefore lifted by exactly the loss that leg had booked, and sat above the green zone, which a sold option cannot do. `theoreticalPnl` already handled closed legs; the page now passes it every enabled leg (`mAll`), used for the curve, the what-if figure and the scenario grid. Greeks and the stop/target markers still use open legs only. Test: a stopped short call plus an open put differ from the put alone by exactly the booked loss.
- **Option chain hide/show** button (remembered in this browser): the chain collapses to its header bar and the payoff chart gets the full width.
- **Holding rule (intraday / overnight / manual):** a document field `hold = {mode, closeAt}` (default overnight, 15:20 IST, customisable, must be inside 09:15-15:40) and a server-stamped `holdFrom`. Intraday closes every open leg at the first close time after the rule started (weekends skipped); overnight leaves legs to expiry and closes them at the close time on their expiry day (not if armed after it); manual never closes by the clock. Closes use the live price, reason `strategy`, no re-entry; a leg without a live price is retried each second, never guessed. Runs on the server every second (`apply_hold`, mirrored by `applyHold` in the browser for the no-server fallback) and needs the Auto stop & target switch on. Closing by hand (leg exit, Exit all) is unchanged. Changing the rule restarts its clock. Known limit: no holiday calendar, so a holiday after the armed day closes at the next live moment.

## D97 — Options intelligence programme, step 1: delta-interpolated skew, metric registry, methodology panels (10 Nov 2026)
- **Scope:** the request (IV engine, momentum, term structure, GEX universes, regime classifier, demand analyser, backtests, central dashboard) is a multi-step programme. Step 1 is the foundation; the rest follows in later steps, each with its own methodology panel.
- **Existing calculation modified:** 25Δ/10Δ wing IVs were the nearest listed contract within a delta tolerance; they are now found by linear interpolation in delta between the two bracketing out-of-the-money contracts (exact on a hit, no extrapolation, missing when outside the listed range). Changed identically in `skew_metrics`/`iv_at_delta` (backend) and `skewMeasures`/`ivAtDelta` (browser). Skew keeps its sign (put − call); the requested call − put figure is its exact negation and is shown and stored beside it (`call_minus_put_25d/10d`). Added: 10Δ butterfly, 10Δ and 25Δ call/put IV. Hand-calculated tests on both sides were rewritten with the interpolation arithmetic.
- **Metric registry** (`frontend/src/lib/metricRegistry.ts`) holds formula, inputs, steps, sign, readings, assumptions and the computing function for iv_skew, iv_butterfly, iv_slope, term_slope; `MethodologyPanel` renders it (closed by default) at the bottom of the Volatility Skew section with a worked example produced by `ivAtDelta` on the data on screen, and a freshness block. Limit: the registry is in the browser beside the live calculation; the server's `skew_metrics` is kept equal by shared hand-calculated cases, not by reading the registry.

## D98 — Options intelligence programme: sections, regime, backtest (10 Nov 2026)
Scope, definitions, modified calculations, limits and verification steps are in [options-intelligence](options-intelligence.md). Choices worth keeping: no new storage (derived metrics computed from stored chains and cached in memory); one GEX calculation (`gammaAnalysis.ts`) reused for the fixed, dynamic and full universes; regime = transparent thresholds with confidence shown as completeness and agreement, no probabilities; backtests are chronological, fixed-threshold, thinned, net of a stated cost, and say "insufficient sample" below 20 out-of-sample events; GEX, flip and term-slope backtests are listed as not testable until their history is stored server-side.

## D99 — GEX history reconstruction, backtest performance and derived metrics (10 Nov 2026)
GEX and gamma-flip history are rebuilt on demand from stored chains (`gex_history.py`) and cached per stored sample in memory; no table was added. Measured cold cost for 60 sessions is about 1 minute in a background job with progress; if restarts make that a problem, the proposed `derived_metrics` table (about 12 MB a year) is documented in [options-intelligence](options-intelligence.md) but not built. IV inversion uses a safeguarded Newton solver with the same results as bisection. The regime now includes the GEX-price relationship from one named GEX universe, with explicit equal weights.

## D100 — Swing live view: trade-for-trade stocks get a token; "triggered" is provisional (10 Nov 2026)
`parse_scrip_master` keeps only "-EQ" rows, so a stock in the trade-for-trade segment (row "-BE"), such as SWARAJ appears to be, had no token and no live price. The swing live view now uses `parse_scrip_master_any_series` (EQ preferred, then BE, BZ, SM, ST, SZ); a stock still missing says it is not in the Kotak cash file. Not verified against the real Kotak instrument file (needs the login): if SWARAJ is absent under every series it is suspended or renamed and the note says so. The live panel's "Triggered: would be bought" is only the live reading of the entry rule; the paper book books every buy at the evening run (after 17:00 IST) on the full day (price through the trigger and volume at least 1.25 x its 20-day average). Wording now says so; live intraday fills are not enabled (the paper record is permanent, see live.py).

## D101 — Live paper ledger for the swing book (10 Nov 2026)
A separate SQLite file (`swing_live_fills.sqlite3`, a few KB a day) holds provisional intraday paper fills. Entry: the order reads "triggered" (fresh price, after 09:45, volume on pace) on two consecutive polls, feed streaming, queue current, no official holding or cooldown, a free slot of five; fill at `max(day open, trigger)`, sized by the shared `position_size` from the ledger's own equity. Exits: the stop check reuses `check_position` (initial stop, or the official trailing stop once the official book holds the stock). After each evening settlement `LiveLedger.settled` marks each fill confirmed or rejected with the official reason, voids a rejected one at its last live price, and follows official same-day or later closes. The official tables are never written (tested by comparing the official snapshot before and after). Costs and `risk_pct` come from the same `Config`. Limits: live trailing stops between evenings follow the official stop only; the day-1 close rules apply only at the evening run; behaviour with real ticks has not been observed yet.

## D102 — NIFTY 50 Market Intelligence dashboard and the new Home (10 Nov 2026)
A master aggregation layer (`app/intelligence`) reads the existing services in process (market flow and breadth, the intraday command centre, futures series, skew, IV momentum, strike positioning, rebuilt GEX, VIX/HV/RV history), fuses six engines with configurable weights (futures 25, options 20, GEX 15, heavyweight 15, volatility 10, structure 15), and serves one summary. Unavailable engines are left out of the weighted average, never scored neutral; below 3 engines or 55 % coverage the dashboard says INSUFFICIENT DATA. Confidence is separate (coverage x agreement x freshness). Scenarios are heuristic scores, not probabilities. A new `intelligence.sqlite3` keeps one row per recorded minute for the timeline and alerts (about 0.2 MB a session) because the fused score has no history elsewhere; nothing is back-filled. The Home page now opens on the compact market strip, five start links and the searchable tool list; every existing page stays. Full map and limits: [market-intelligence-audit](market-intelligence-audit.md).

## D103 — Sentiment engine: evidence overlap correction, honest confidence, validation framework (10 Nov 2026)
The command-centre score was removed from the structure engine (it is built from price, VWAP, breadth, PCR and OI, so it double counted them) and the six engines became five evidence groups that each own their inputs: derivatives positioning, order-flow proxies, volatility, market structure, participation. Terms built from the same data share a *family* and are averaged into one reading; a term that also reads price carries weight 0.5 and says so. Default weights 30/20/10/20/20, configurable, labelled "Provisional weights – not yet statistically calibrated." Confidence no longer derives from the size of the score: agreement is 1 − 2 × min(bullish share, bearish share), and confidence is capped at 40 when 25 % or more of the weight opposes the rest (the old agreement rule scored a perfectly split set as unanimous when the score was near zero). CVD is labelled a proxy everywhere with a tooltip, GEX as modelled exposure with its methodology documented, and India VIX as "Last Daily Close" with its date and never an intraday input. A chronological walk-forward validation (no look-ahead, layout-aware, interval-reported, 30-call minimum) reads the stored timeline; it has no real history to run on yet. Rationale and full audit: [market-intelligence-audit](market-intelligence-audit.md). Not decided: any change to the weights (they stay provisional until validated on real sessions).

## D104 — Heavyweight Intelligence engine: proxy CVD, normalised aggregate, participation, divergence (11 Nov 2026)
Audited first: the top-five selection from current weights, per-stock quote/tick CVD trackers, the five depth subscriptions and a per-minute `heavy5` sample already existed. Added without a new table, subscription or worker: weights provenance (company, previous weight, effective date, staleness flag), VWAP/bid/ask exposure, the extended sample (price, VWAP, weight, normalised CVD, methodology tag), and `intelligence/heavyweights.py` (rolling 5/15/30-minute readings from stored samples only, momentum, acceleration, contribution, tanh-normalised index-weighted aggregate valid only with 3+ stocks and 60 % of the weight, participation classes, seven NIFTY-versus-heavyweight signals). Everything is labelled CVD PROXY; genuine CVD is unavailable (no aggressor-side trade tape) and is documented as needing a licensed tick-by-tick feed. CVD readings form one scoring family in the order-flow group and breadth stays price-only in the participation group, so nothing is counted twice; a group's available share is now measured by family. Not verified with live market data. See [market-intelligence-audit](market-intelligence-audit.md).

## D105 — Prediction desk: why days went unrecorded and inputs looked stale, and what changed (11 Nov 2026)
Symptoms reported: T+5 always near 57 %, global inputs "stale", the forward ledger and day log full of "not recorded", news not fresh. Findings and changes (nothing could be checked on the server; causes are from the code and the Yahoo responses, and are listed as such):
1. **T+5 at about 57 % is the result, not a fault.** The stored evaluation shows no T+5 model beating the always-up base rate on the holdout (Brier skill -1.2 % to +0.3 %, hit rate 58.5-59.0 % against a 58.8 % base); the calibrator therefore pulls every forecast back to the base rate. T+1 does have a small edge (skill about 5-6 %). The page now says this on the T+5 card. No model was changed to "look more active": a probability that moves without skill would be worse than a flat one.
2. **Holidays and the cutoff date.** The models used world-market bars dated on or before the reference session. After an Indian holiday (10 Nov) that left them a world session behind. Rule now (feature versions `t1-v2` / `t5-v2`): bars dated on or before the day before the NEXT session, i.e. unchanged on ordinary days and weekends, and including the world sessions that traded while India was shut. Freshness now asks for the latest *US trading day* (NYSE calendar for 2026-27, `prediction/usmarket.py`), so a US holiday (Thanksgiving, Good Friday...) no longer blocks a morning, while a genuinely late series still does. The page lists every overnight series with its newest bar and whether it is final, provisional or late.
3. **Why forecasts went unrecorded.** (a) A training started by a data refresh before the window could swallow the 08:35 forced run, which then returned a stale preview, and the day was marked done anyway. A forced run now waits and trains again, and the day is done only when forecasts are really in the ledger (the ledger, not memory, decides; it is also retried every four minutes until 09:10). (b) The day log read in-memory state, so a restart turned a recorded day into "report not computed"; it now reads the ledger and the saved report, and names the report it did have. (c) A refresh that succeeded for 24 of 25 symbols ended the day's attempts; with the calendar the schedule now also checks the data (NIFTY 50's last completed session; the overnight series' US trading day) and retries every 10 minutes in the morning, hourly otherwise, remembering its last attempt across restarts. Not found, and not claimed: the exact cause of each past missed day (server logs were not available).
4. **News.** The daily GDELT tone is a daily value (at best yesterday's complete day), so it cannot be made "live". Added hourly latest headlines per theme (GDELT article list: title, link, site, time seen; no article text; the same approved source), stored append-only, shown with their fetch time; both news schedules now survive restarts. News is still not a model input.
Old forecasts, ledger rows and day-log rows are untouched (append-only); the feature-version change retrains the models once.

Menus: Options Lab and Future Lab are one **FNO Tools** menu (the Option Chain is its first group; futures groups renamed "Futures price" / "Futures OI" so names do not clash); **Swing trading** is a main section beside Market Intelligence; Strategy Builder keeps the builder and the portfolio.
