Dark theme unchanged. Checked in the browser at desktop and 375 px width without a backend; chart pages in daylight were not looked at with live data.

## D107 — FII/DII cash: why 6 Nov was missed, and the fix (11 Nov 2026)
Cause (found in the code; the server's logs were not available to confirm the evening's exact events): the "newest session stored" check for FII/DII cash took the newest session of ALL rows in the cash table, and the NSDL foreign-investor rows (categories "FPI equity ...", fetched once a day) live in the same table. NSDL had 6 Nov by the evening, so the session looked stored, automatic checks stopped ("Everything for it is stored") and NSE's FII/DII figures for that day were never fetched. NSE's feed shows only the latest session, so the day could not be fetched later either; it stays missing until a CSV is imported. Fix: "cash" now means NSE's FII/DII rows only (NSDL is tracked separately, `fpi_nsdl_last`), so the schedule keeps checking, and recent sessions without NSE figures are listed (`cash_gaps`) and flagged on the page with the way to fill them. Is the collection robust? Better, not robust: it depends on one unofficial NSE endpoint that keeps one day, one evening window of retries per missed day is all the feed allows, and NSDL has the same one-day property (2 Nov has no NSDL value). Removing that fragility needs a source with history (NSE's FII/DII report CSV downloaded by hand, or a licensed feed); that is the user's decision. The "NEW" tool badges were removed from the menus, tool tabs and home page.

## D108 — Audit of 11 Nov 2026: production fixes, intraday ML in Market Intelligence, daylight colours (11 Nov 2026)
Full report: [audit-2026-11-11](audit-2026-11-11.md). Changes: mypy and ruff clean (53 and 19 findings fixed); the option-chain endpoint no longer blocks the event loop; the snapshot index uses WAL; the NSE calendar is parsed once; a cache keyed by changing values (intraday slow results) is bounded (it grew for as long as the server ran); secondary pages are code-split (entry chunk about 330 KB, was about 610 KB). The intraday ML work is a research pipeline (labelled features per minute, walk-forward test of a logistic model against baselines, a gate that withholds every probability); it now writes a stored digest once per session day after 15:45 IST and Market Intelligence shows it (what it does, stage, last evaluation, gate reasons) as research only, outside the score. No performance figure is claimed: no evaluation result existed that this audit could read. Daylight theme re-tuned to the dark theme's vibrancy (cool paper page, deep-navy ink, same hues). Unverified: everything on the live server; the proxy login in front of the control endpoints should be checked there.

## D109 — Live Market Intelligence analytical corrections (11 Nov 2026)
1. **CVD acceleration** now compares two consecutive, non-overlapping windows of equal length (current 5 min and the 5 min before it): current and previous CVD, rate per minute each, change in rate, acceleration (change in rate per minute). The old "momentum" subtracted a 5-minute rate from a 15-minute rate (different durations) and is removed for futures and heavyweights (heavyweights: this 5-minute score less the previous one, acceleration = that per minute). 15 and 30 minute CVD stay as separate context. Unavailable with a reason when three stored points, continuity or classified volume are missing.
2. **VWAP**: every VWAP figure is the NIFTY future against its own exchange average traded price (it always was in the maths; the labels in the heavyweight panel said "NIFTY vs VWAP"). Spot VWAP is UNAVAILABLE (the index has no traded volume) and spot is never compared with the futures VWAP.
3. **GEX validation** (real captured 28 Oct chain, `test_live_corrections.py`): strike gamma equals an independent Black-76 gamma (to 1e-12); exposure uses OI in units, no lot multiplier, x spot^2 x 1 % / 10^7; calls +, puts - (reversing it negates the profile and moves no zero). Findings: (a) the net total uses only strikes with both a call and a put exposure, but the flip used every contract: 6 points apart on that chain; both are now reported; (b) a 10 % disagreement about who holds the OI (calls x0.9 / puts x1.1 and the reverse) moves the flip by about 160 points (0.7 % of spot), so the flip is labelled UNSTABLE when its range exceeds 0.25 % of spot, it has several sign changes, it is more than 1 % from spot, or the profile is flat there. An unstable flip counts at a quarter of its weight in the score. Distance is shown in points and percent with the range. No corrected flip is invented. The Nov 11 snapshot itself was not available to this session (local data ends 29 Oct; no server access), so the cause of that day's large distance could not be reproduced; the diagnostics will show it live.
4. **Weights**: effective date, load time ("verified" = when the set was loaded from its source, not a re-check against NSE), source, valid constituents; stored weights are never called live.
5. **Confidence renamed EVIDENCE QUALITY** (same calculation; API key `confidence` kept, `evidence_quality` added); explained as freshness, completeness and agreement, never a probability.
6. **Observations**: five readings are detected and explained without reversal language (NIFTY up with negative heavyweight CVD; up with improving 5-minute CVD; futures OI down while price rises; futures versus futures VWAP; positive modelled GEX with a distant flip).

## D110 — Volatility intelligence: components, sign convention, interpretation, configurable scoring (11 Nov 2026)
Scoring numbers are unchanged by default (`mode: legacy`, scales 0.5 / 0.6 / 0.4, group weight 10): the reported readings (ATM IV -0.27 pts; 25-delta CE IV change less PE IV change -0.71 pts) reproduce by hand as +29.6, -94.4 and a group score of -32.4, and the verdict is CONFLICTING because the two components disagree. One sign convention: risk reversal = CE IV - PE IV, skew = PE IV - CE IV = -risk reversal, risk-reversal change = CE IV change - PE IV change (the old "momentum"), positive = calls strengthening = bullish; a table that breaks the identity is flagged. Added (`intelligence/voleval.py`, shown in the Volatility block only): CE and PE IV now and their changes, risk reversal now and at the previous close, ATM, 25-delta and 10-delta separately, which leg moved (put IV expansion versus call IV contraction), per-component contribution, bullish / bearish / conflicting evidence, six interpretation lines and a final explanation with the actual readings. Expansion and contraction are reported as changes in the level of risk, never as a direction. Configurable through `INTELLIGENCE_VOLATILITY` (JSON: mode `legacy` or `context`, atm_scale, atm_factor, skew_scale, thresholds); `context` scores ATM IV only when it agrees with the price move about fear (price up with IV down +, price down with IV up -, otherwise 0), so IV falling during a decline is not bullish. Default stays legacy because the weights were not to be changed blindly; whether to switch is a decision for the stored-session validation. Not validated on the 11 Nov snapshot itself: it was not available to this session, so the reported readings were used as inputs.

## D111 — Volatility score review: distinct evidence, thresholds, mode comparison (11 Nov 2026)
Defaults unchanged (mode legacy, weights, scales). Verified and tested: only the 25-delta risk-reversal change and the ATM IV change are scored; the 10-delta risk reversal, the skew (the same number with its sign reversed), the previous-close risk reversal and the volatility-versus-price divergence are listed with contribution 0 and the reason (the 10-delta move cannot change the score; the divergence is built from the same inputs and is interpretation only). The risk-reversal change is blind to a parallel shift of the smile, so it does not repeat the ATM move; in practice both rise in a sell-off, so they are documented as correlated. Found and fixed: a 0.1-point risk-reversal change scored -24 (tanh) and counted as evidence. Now a change below the materiality threshold (ATM 0.2 pts, risk reversal 0.2 pts) is not counted as evidence and does not set the verdict, and the explanation says so; the score values are unchanged. An optional dead zone in the score itself (`noise_pts`, default off) is available but changes scores, so it waits for validation. A modest ATM move (0.2 to 0.3 pts) is now labelled "modest", which makes the 11 Nov reading (-0.27) consistent between label and evidence. All four price/ATM IV combinations and all four single-leg moves (put and call IV expansion and contraction) are tested in both modes. The timeline now stores the three volatility inputs, and `compare_vol_modes` (also in `/api/intelligence/validation`) recomputes legacy and context scores from them on the same chronological sessions; it reports only after 20 sessions with the inputs, describes with intervals and never switches the default. Known limit: the fusion step still counts a group whose two components conflict by its net score (here -32) when it measures agreement between groups; changing that is a scoring change and needs validation first.

## D112 — "Preview, not recorded" shown after a successful recording; recording status on the page (11 Nov 2026)
Found in the code: the forecast card decided "recorded" only from the moment of computing. Outside 08:35-09:10 IST every computation returned "preview: forecasts are recorded between 08:35 and 09:10", even when that morning's forecast was already locked in the ledger. Any later retrain (a data refresh, a restart, a deploy, Compute now) therefore overwrote the saved report with previews, so the page and its "Forecast locked in ledger" chip said "not recorded" for the rest of the day while the ledger held the forecast. Now a forecast that exists in the ledger is always shown as locked (its stored numbers, never a recomputed preview). The page also states the recording status in words (before the window, waiting for named series, ready, recorded, missed with the reason) with the time of the last automatic check. A day that really missed the window stays missed: it is never recorded afterwards, by design (a forecast is only valid if locked before the outcome). Not verified on the server: whether a given past morning was recorded depends on the server's data and logs, which this session cannot read; the ledger table and the day log on the page are the record. Avoid deploying between 08:30 and 09:15 IST: a restart in the window delays that morning's run.

## D113 — Server check of the prediction pipeline, and four defects found there (11 Nov 2026)
Read-only inspection of the server (ledger, day log, history runs, logs). Result: the morning of 11 Nov worked end to end: history refreshed from 07:30 IST, models trained 08:36, all four T+1 and four T+5 forecasts locked at 08:37 (cutoff 08:30), day logged "recorded". The earlier misses (3-9 Nov) were: BRENT and DXY not yet in (3 Nov), the NIFTY close bar missing at the time of the run (4-6 Nov, "report not computed from the X close"), all overnight series missing on 9 Nov; the fixes of D105 address these and the days since have been recorded. Defects found and fixed here: (1) "13 bars differ from what is stored" was Yahoo's 32-bit float rounding (for example FTSE 10829.2598 against 10829.2998), logged again every 10 minutes: bars now agree within 0.02 % or half a cent, and a real revision is logged once, not every run; (2) the history refresh ran every 10 minutes until 09:10 because a provisional 24-hour bar (BRENT, DXY, 10-year yield, gold, WTI are final only at 11:00 IST) counted as missing: a provisional bar now counts as present; (3) in market hours, whenever the saved report's data stamp no longer matched (any restart, deploy or refreshed bar), the page showed only "models are trained after the market closes", hiding the forecast recorded that morning: the last report is now shown, labelled; (4) Sunday 8 Nov (Muhurat session) was listed as an FII/DII gap: special sessions are excluded. 6 Nov FII/DII cash remains a real gap (NSE's feed keeps one day; import the CSV). FII/DII cash is not a model input; participant positions feed only the T5 flows variant.

## D114 — Prediction audit: three more defects fixed, known limits listed (11 Nov 2026)
Fixed: (1) the post-mortem showed no "inputs that pushed it" because only the logistic model stores them and the ensemble row is shown: rows now borrow the logit model's drivers for the same forecast day; (2) the "forward results so far" tile summed the four models (2 days showed as 8): it now counts days; (3) when the newest NIFTY close is not the previous session's, the morning run no longer trains and treats an older close as the morning forecast, the recording status says "waiting for the NIFTY 50 close of X", and the card says so instead of the misleading "recorded between 08:35 and 09:10". Verified on the server: ledger outcomes equal the labels (3 forward days stored, 8 outcomes attached). Known limits, not fixed (each needs data we do not have or a modelling decision): (a) training uses the final daily bar of 24-hour series (Brent, WTI, gold, dollar index, 10-year yield: final at New York midnight, about 10:30 IST next day) while the live forecast at 08:30 uses the provisional bar, so train and live differ by about two hours of thin evening trading; (b) the probability calibrator is fitted on tuning-period predictions of models trained on less data than the live model; (c) the ledger id does not contain the feature version, so rows made under t1-v1 and t1-v2 share model version 3 in the scoreboard; (d) after_refresh retrains whenever any input changes, including provisional closes, so the morning can retrain repeatedly (about 84 s each on the server); (e) the T+5 models show no skill over the base rate on the holdout, by the page's own test, and 3 forward days are far too few to say anything.

## D115 — ATM Rolling Straddle tool (FNO Tools > Price tools, 11 Nov 2026)
The existing ATM Straddle chart draws the straddle of whichever strike is ATM at each sample, so it jumps at every roll and part of each move is a different contract. New tool `/tools/rolling-straddle` (frontend only; it reads the stored one-minute strike series the other price tools already use, ATM +-10 strikes, nothing new is collected or stored). `lib/rollingStraddle.ts` (pure, hand-calculated tests) separates: the rolling premium of the held strike under a roll rule (nearest ATM at every sample, or roll only when spot is 1 or 2 strike-gaps away: fewer rolls), the fixed opening-strike straddle, a roll-adjusted premium that adds up only same-contract changes (a roll makes no jump: the pure premium path of a rolled short straddle), roll credits (new strike's premium minus old strike's at the roll), implied move (premium as a percent of spot), the break-even band (held strike +- premium), day open/high/low/time-average, change from open with and without rolls, the last-hour change of the roll-adjusted premium, NIFTY's move against the opening premium, every roll in a table, and a plain description that never makes a trade call. A missing price is a gap, a roll with a missing price has no credit and is counted; nothing is filled. Not verified live: built and tested on fixtures; check the chart on the server in market hours.


## D116 — Straddle Decay Intelligence (Market Intelligence page, 11 Nov 2026)
Question: why do NIFTY ATM straddle premiums not decay as expected, and how much of the move is theta, vega, delta, gamma or vol repricing. New `GET /api/intelligence/straddle` (+ `/straddle/validation`) and a panel on the Market Intelligence page, separate from the sentiment score and the fusion (no weight, group or default changed; `app.state.straddle`, `intelligence/straddle.py` pure, `straddle_service.py` reader). It reuses the existing Black-76 code in `intraday/volatility.py` (price, implied vol, Greeks, parity forward, `iv_at_delta`, `surface`), `fusion.gex_state` for GEX, and the stored one-minute chain samples (expiry and strike identity preserved there); nothing new is collected or stored.
Attribution: for one leg between two samples, Black-76 repricing over three factors (time, forward, implied vol) by Shapley (all 6 orderings); delta = delta(start) x dF, gamma = the forward's Shapley effect minus the delta term; residual = market change minus the four terms. Each end's vol is solved from that end's own price, so the residual is solver error, not an "IV impact"; vega is the vol factor's own effect, not the remainder after theta. A window is the sum of one-sample steps (gamma is realised convexity). CE, PE and combined are shown separately. Fixed opening strike and rolling ATM are kept apart (rolling adds only same-contract changes).
Decay efficiency = actual decay / theta decay x 100; classes efficient (>= 75), slower (20-75), none (-10-20), expansion (< -10) are working thresholds, not validated. The shortfall reason names the largest positive offset (vega, gamma, delta). Realised vol over 5/15/30/60 min and since open on trading minutes versus quoted implied vol; GEX is shown as context with the explicit note that it guarantees nothing. The final reading answers the six questions and a "favourable / mixed / unfavourable" tally, labelled a tally and not a forecast.
Validation: prediction made at each 30-minute window start from time alone (forward and vol held) against the actual change of the same contracts, over stored chronological sessions; no fitting, so all windows are out of sample, and "sufficient" needs 20 sessions and 100 windows, else the page says Insufficient. Even then it checks decay arithmetic, not a forecast. Missing prices or unsolvable vols skip the step and are counted; opening = first stored sample, not the exchange open. Tests: 25 backend (isolated-factor identities, hand-calculated efficiency, synthetic flat/vol-up/whipsaw sessions) and 10 frontend. Not verified on real stored sessions yet.

## D117 — Straddle Decay Intelligence, phase 2: buyer and seller environments, recording, alerts, validation (11 Nov 2026)
Extends D116 to the full brief; still separate from the sentiment score (no weight, group or default changed; Market Intelligence is only read through `summary()`). New page `/market-intelligence/straddle` (twelve sections in the requested order); the Market Intelligence page keeps a compact card with the two scores and a link. Audit and reuse plan: `docs/straddle-intelligence.md`.
Engine: the Shapley attribution now carves out vanna (dF x dVol x d2P/dFdVol) and volga (half d2P/dVol2 x dVol2) from the vol and forward effects (totals unchanged, residual is still solver error only); the vega is split into a parallel part and a skew part (vega x (own vol change - average vol change)); the decay shortfall is given in points, rupees per lot (65) and percent of theta, with named causes (parallel IV expansion, skew, price movement, gamma, vanna and volga, unexplained). A non-positive expected theta gives no efficiency and says so. Added: premium and IV changes over 5/15/30/60 minutes, high/low, opening and current ATM strike, fixed-minus-rolling difference, 10/25-delta call and put IV, butterflies, the six call/put IV regimes (described, never read as buying or selling), which side retains premium and why, realised-vol acceleration (last 15 minutes against the 15 before), a regime label (volatility compression / expansion / directional acceleration) from IV change, realised acceleration and 30-minute path efficiency, GEX walls, session GEX path and flip crossings, and a gamma-configuration reading with its modelled-exposure caveat.
Environments (`straddle_env.py`): independent 0-100 seller and buyer scores, 50 + 50 x sum(w x v)/sum(w available), each component in [-1, +1] with weight, reason and source; a missing input is dropped and lowers coverage (under half: no score). Seller weights: decay efficiency 20, ATM IV trend 12, realised vs implied 12, movement vs break-even 10, gamma 8, order flow 8, heavyweight 8, call/put IV 6, positioning 6, GEX stability 5, skew 5. Buyer: IV expansion 10, call/put IV momentum 6, realised acceleration 12, direction 12, premium justified by movement 12, gamma 8, premium expansion 8, futures CVD 7, heavyweight 6, VWAP 5, GEX migration 5, Smart OI 5, skew 4. Risk flags (sudden IV expansion, rising realised vol, directional acceleration, asymmetric tail) cap the seller class. Short straddle, short strangle, call-side and put-side risk, and four buyer types are separate sub-assessments; rising IV alone never scores a buyer without realised movement. Weights and break points are provisional.
Recording: `straddle.sqlite3`, one row per market minute (inputs, premiums, IV, attribution totals, efficiency, both scores, regime, +-2 strike premium ladder; about 1.2 KB, 0.45 MB a day) from the day it deployed, nothing back-filled; 12 alert kinds with persistence checks, thresholds and a 10-minute cooldown. Validation: recorded minutes against what followed at 15/30/60 minutes and end of session, by score bucket, with non-overlapping picks, a short/long ATM straddle premium proxy net of an assumed 4 x 0.5 point cost, direction accuracy, worst adverse excursion, drawdown, expiry vs other days, earlier vs later sessions; Insufficient until 20 sessions and 30 picks per bucket.
Not verified: scores against real forward outcomes (no history yet); live behaviour of the new recorder in market hours until the server has run it.

## D118 — Order-flow figures survive a restart of the backend (11 Nov 2026)
Symptom: the Heavyweight Intelligence strip showed the 15- and 30-minute aggregates (and the 5-minute change and acceleration) as unavailable, and the "session" CVD covered 0.6% of the day's volume. Cause: the flow trackers (NIFTY future and the five heavyweights) keep cumulative buy/sell volume in memory; every restart (each deploy) started them from zero, the rolling windows are differences of stored cumulative values and were correctly refused across a reset, so each window stayed unavailable for its own length after every restart, and the session figures silently began at the restart. Fix: `FlowTracker.restore()` continues today's cumulative buy/sell from the last stored minute of the same IST day (`flow.last_cumulative`), the first live push stays a baseline so the volume traded while the backend was down is not classified (it stays missing, coverage shows it), and `quote_rule_share` is computed over the volume seen since the run began because the restored part has no stored split. A latent weakness found while testing: the futures rolling windows compared only their two end samples, so a restart in the middle could pass as "ok"; `rolling.reset_inside` now checks every stored sample in the window. Heavyweight windows already truncated at the last reset and need no change. A restart still loses the volume that trades while the backend is down (about a minute); nothing is guessed. Verified by synthetic formula checks (hand-calculated) and on the server after deploy; the first restart after this deploy cannot be a test of the fix itself.
Second finding, same day, after the fix was live (all six trackers logged `flow_restored`; session and 5-minute windows were available straight after the restart): the cash segment's market status changed to code 8 at 15:15 IST and to code 10 at 15:20 IST, and the cash volume counters of all five heavyweights restarted at 15:20 (HDFCBANK 53.7M -> 2.1M), so the trackers reset by design (volume below the previous volume = a new counter run) and the rolling windows are unavailable until enough new minutes exist. That is the data, not a bug; what was wrong is that a tracker that has seen only a sliver of the day's volume still produced a "session" CVD (+99.9 from 16 units of volume; -21 from 0.6% of the day in the screenshot that started this). `heavyweights.assess` now requires `coverage_of_day_volume >= 0.5` for the session reading (not reported = not blocked, like the classified share) and says why in `session.status`. Not established: what NSE codes 8 and 10 mean for the volume counter (they coincide with the discontinuity; treated as observed, not explained).

## D119 — Chart legends and unregistered ECharts parts (11 Nov 2026)
Reported on the ATM Rolling Straddle chart: the NIFTY 50 line (hidden by default) never showed and legend clicks did not stick. Causes, all in the shared `components/EChart.tsx`: (1) a builder that sets `legend.selected` (the price tools through `timeLinesOption`, and the HV lines of the IV chart) sent the defaults again with every live refresh, so a click lasted only until the next data update; the wrapper now remembers legend choices (`keepLegend`), and when the page itself changes a default (an external control) its intent wins. (2) Audit of every series type, component and feature the app's options use against what was registered: `scatter` (the roll markers of the rolling straddle, never drawn), `markPoint` (the delta diamonds of the IV skew chart), `graphic` (the text corners of two charts) and the `LabelLayout` and `LegacyGridContainLabel` features (overlap-free latest-value tags, `containLabel` grids in two tool widgets) were all silently ignored by ECharts; all are now registered. `src/test/echart-registration.test.ts` renders each part server-side and fails if one is missing, and tests the legend memory (the tests fail on the previous wrapper). Not changed: the other 31 chart users build ordinary line/bar/candlestick/heatmap options that were already registered.

## D120 — Hover freeze, light-theme contrast, Market Intelligence layout, "unavailable" rows (11 Nov 2026)
1. Rolling straddle chart froze on hover. Cause: a regression from D119. Registering the scatter series made the roll markers join the axis tooltip, and `timeLinesOption`'s formatter assumed every series was one of its lines (`by.get(name)!.fmt`), so it threw on every mouse move. Fixed in the formatter (a roll now shows as "Roll 25,150 -> 25,200") and, for every chart, `EChart` wraps all tooltip formatters (`safeTooltips`) so one bad series can only blank a tooltip, never break hovering (logged once). Measured on the live page: 1.8 ms per hover, 0 errors.
2. Light theme. Measured contrast on the real page: green and red figures were 3.3:1 and 4.1:1 on the inset panels, secondary text 5.8:1, and a legend entry the user switched off (PCR, NIFTY 50) used ECharts' default #ccc. Tokens now: up #067a54, down #c81e3e, warn #a14a08, info #165fc2 (all 5:1 or better on white and on the panels), muted #46546e (7:1+); faded utility classes (`text-muted/70`, `/50`, `text-paper/90`) map to the full tokens on the light theme; every legend gets `--legend-off` (readable on both themes) unless it sets its own colour. Dark theme colours are unchanged except the new `--legend-off`.
3. Market Intelligence layout. The alert centre had 30 items (2,584 px) beside a 392 px divergences panel, so the grid stretched and left a large gap; the page was about 8,700 px. The alert list now scrolls (440 px, keyboard-focusable) with a count and a severity filter, side-by-side panels align to the top, interpretation and "readings side by side" share a row, a sticky section navigator (jump links, current section highlighted) is added, and the data-quality table is rebuilt (aligned columns, source times in IST, a count summary, a "show only what is not fully available" switch). Page height about 6,650 px.
4. "Unavailable" rows. At the time of the screenshot (about 18:05 IST) the live trackers had nothing, so session CVD, VWAP and heavyweight CVD said UNAVAILABLE although the stored minutes of the day exist. These capabilities now fall back to the last stored minute and say so (PARTIAL, with its IST time and the figure: session CVD %, the future's average traded price against its last price); they are UNAVAILABLE only when nothing is live and nothing is stored. The rows that cannot exist now say why: `feed` (aggressor-side CVD for heavyweights, option trade side, spot VWAP: the feed carries no such data), `not_subscribed` (rollover needs the next-month future, stock futures: nothing subscribes to them yet). Not done: subscribing to the next-month future and stock futures (a stream, sampler and storage change), so rollover and stock-futures analytics stay unavailable until that is built.

## D121 — Swing: the live-style rules on 1-minute candles, run in shadow (11 Nov 2026)
Why: if the swing engine shows an edge it should be traded live with the SAME conditions, but the tested engine decides on daily bars (fills simulated on the day's open/high/low/volume, the volume rule uses the full day's volume, Day-1 cuts use the day's close). Agreed with the user (11 Nov): the live version is a different strategy variant, so it runs in shadow beside the official book and must earn its own evidence; real orders stay out of scope until separately authorised.
Rules (all on completed 1-minute candles, fills at the NEXT candle's open, never at the close it was decided on): entry on a candle that closes between 09:30 and 15:00 at or above the trigger (pivot x 1.003) and at most 1% above it, day open at or below the no-chase limit, day volume at least the minimum volume (1.25 x 20-day average) scaled by the expected share of the day (the app's recorded stock volume profile; a straight line, not judged before 09:45, when no profile exists), a free slot (5), 3-session cooldown, and the fill cancelled if the next open is more than 1% above the trigger. Stop: a candle that closes at or below the stop in force exits at the next open. No broker-side stop (user decision). Breakeven at +1.8R and trail from +2.5R (stop = highest of itself, fill x 1.002, highest 1-minute close - 2.5 x ATR14 of the signal day) apply intraday on 1-minute closes and take effect from the next candle; the official trail's EMA10 leg is not used. Day 1: on the candle closing at 15:20, exit if the close is below the pivot or in the bottom 35% of the day's range so far.
Build: `swing/minute_rules.py` (pure rules), `minute_store.py` (`data/swing_minute.sqlite3`: bars, fills, append-only events, daily comparisons; about 0.2 MB a session), `minute_shadow.py` (candles built from the 2-second polls `SwingLive` already makes, so no new subscription or feed hook; open shadow positions also keep their stocks subscribed), `GET /api/swing/minute`, a panel on the Swing page (Paper trading tab). After each evening settlement `MinuteShadow.settled` compares, per order and per exit, what the official book did with what the shadow book did (fill gap, orders only one book filled, exit gaps). It never writes to the official book, the live ledger or a broker.
Known limits, stated on the page: a candle's high and low come from polled prices (every ~2 s), so a spike shorter than a poll is missed; a minute without a poll has no candle and nothing is filled in; the volume profile is the recorded stock profile once it exists, else a straight line; the shadow book's ATR for the trail is the signal-day value; history cannot validate these rules (no minute history), so the evidence is forward only (the official engine takes about 120 trades a year). Go/no-go criteria for real money are to be agreed before the first real order.

## D122 — Swing rule version swing-v2: volume requirement 2.0 x the 20-day average (11 Nov 2026)
Why: day-1 exits are 46.5% of the research backtest's trades (217 of 467; cost about Rs 14.2 L against Rs 23.7 L earned by the others) and nothing known before entry predicts them (20 signal-close and entry-open features, univariate AUC 0.44-0.54; a gradient-boosting model fitted on 2023-24 scored 0.50 on 2025-26). The only rule lever that moved the result was the volume requirement. Sweeps on the default backtest, net of 0.15% per side: 1.0x +22%, 1.25x (spec) +49%, 1.5x +52%, 2x +96%, 3x +108%, monotone, profit factor 1.18 -> 1.35 -> 1.49, trades 467 -> 399 -> 324. Robustness: at 2x the result is positive at min scores 62, 65, 68 and (+80%) 70 (the spec's 1.25x is negative at 62 and 70), under random tie orders, and in every year at score 65 (+45/+6/+12/+33); at 3x also in every year. Other levers (gap limit 1.5%, wider ATR stop, narrower distance window) were non-monotone or fragile and were not adopted.
Decision: 2x (not 3x) as a moderate step: it removes the fragility of the spec setting without leaning on the extreme end of one sweep. `Config.vol_mult` = 2.0 and `RULE_VERSION` = "swing-v2": every committed session is stamped with its version, the 7 sessions already recorded stay swing-v1 and must not be pooled with v2 results. Applies from the next evening scan: orders already queued (for 12 Nov) carry their stored minimum volume (1.25x) and are settled under it; the first v2 orders are queued on the evening of 12 Nov for 13 Nov. The live-rules shadow book and the live ledger read each order's minimum volume, so they follow automatically.
Caveat (the main one): the backtest tests the entry day's FULL-day volume, which cannot be known when the trigger fires, and a day with 2-3x volume is partly a day on which the stock already moved. The gain is therefore overstated by an unknown amount; the live version only ever knows the volume so far. The research code (`research/swing`) keeps the spec's 1.25 as its default so the published reconstruction stays reproducible. Evidence that counts is forward: the shadow book stores every candle's price and cumulative volume, so volume-pace thresholds can be replayed offline once enough sessions exist.

## D123 — The Swing "Rules & limits" tab is written from the engine's own parameters (11 Nov 2026)
Why: the tab described the original rules in prose and had drifted (volume requirement, live layers, exact score points, Day-1 reasons were missing or stale). Decision: the tab now documents every step (daily timeline, universe and safeguards, gates, trend template, the four score pillars with exact points, order levels, entry skip reasons, sizing, exits, live view, live ledger, 1-minute shadow rules, rule-version history, terms) and renders its numbers from `/api/swing/paper` `config.params` and `config.live`, which the backend builds from `Config`, the signal-window constants and the shadow-rule constants. A changed parameter therefore changes the page; only the score point values and gate tolerances (in `indicators.py`) are prose and must be edited with the code.

## D124 — Participant-flow analysis on the FII/DII Summary page (11 Nov 2026)
Added `lib/participantFlow.ts` (pure functions) and `components/ParticipantAnalysis.tsx` above the existing participant open-interest chart. It reads only the stored NSE participant OI/volume rows (now requested with limit 30): readout of what changed, one card per group (net, long share, change, rank among stored sessions, streak), a per-group table of long/short/net and their changes for index and stock futures and index calls/puts, comparison against the previous session, 5 sessions (1 week) or 10 sessions (2 weeks), and charts of legs, FII option bias (calls - puts) and long share. Move labels (long build-up, long unwinding, short build-up, short covering) go by the bigger of the two leg changes, under 1% of the book is "little changed"; futures labels lean bullish/bearish, option labels only name the leg. Positions are contracts, not rupees, and not a signal; missing columns stay missing; ranks are among the stored sessions only (7 on the server today), so "lowest/highest" claims widen as history accrues.

## D125 — Smart OI chart: stalled NIFTY quote and silent index bars are flagged, not drawn (11 Nov 2026)
Evidence (11 Nov 2026, from the server's stored data): the chain samples' spot was exactly 25,130.00 in 14 consecutive samples (15:15-15:29), then 25,255.05 at the 15:30 close; the live one-minute index bars had one read each (n=1, 25,130.00) at 15:15-15:17, none at 15:18-15:19, then resumed at 25,528 and slid to 25,255.05 by 15:29. The two index sources disagree during 15:15-15:29, and the option prices (put-call parity forward about 25,180 throughout) did not move with the 25,528 bars. The stall is upstream (the index quote itself); our chart drew the frozen samples as flat dashes and the resumed bars as a +1.6% gap. Decision: identical spot in 5+ consecutive samples (STALL_MIN_SAMPLES) and bars built from fewer than 10 reads (THIN_BAR_READS) are not drawn as candles (OI and volume bars are unaffected); a drawn candle opening more than 0.5% from the previous drawn close after missing candles is reported as an unconfirmed jump; a banner states all three with the numbers. Nothing is filled in or smoothed. Which source is right for 15:15-15:30 is not established.

## D126 — Strategy builder "Price chart" tab: the built strategy as a price through the day (11 Nov 2026)
New tab beside Positions/Greeks/Scenarios (`components/StrategyTimeChart.tsx`, pure maths in `lib/strategyTimeline.ts`). From the stored one-minute strike prices (`/api/history/strike-series` for exactly the strikes of the ticked, still-open legs of the selected expiry; no backend change) it draws the strategy's combined price (price to buy back for a net-credit structure, position value for a debit one) or its rupee P&L, against NIFTY spot and future, with: true Future VWAP (futures volume per minute), an activity-weighted average of the strategy (weights = futures volume per minute, because option volume per strike is not stored; labelled so, never called the strategy's VWAP), time average, SMA 14, EMA 50, Bollinger 20/2, entry or break-even line, the strategy stop and target from the rules, summary tiles (high, low, from open, z-score, percentile, max drawdown) and a split of the P&L change into spot-driven and other. The split uses an empirical delta from a 5-sample regression over the shown range (compared with the model Greek delta in the readout); it is an estimate, and curvature, decay, volatility and noise all fall in "other". Missing prices stay blank; averages restart each IST day; ranges: today (or the newest stored session) and the last 3 sessions.

## D127 — Strategy VWAP built from the legs' stored traded volume (11 Nov 2026)
Correction to D126: option volume per strike IS stored (every snapshot keeps each contract's cumulative day volume); it was not exposed. `/api/history/strike-series` now returns `ce_volume`/`pe_volume` (cumulative, per strike). The Price chart's "Activity-weighted avg" (futures-volume weights) is replaced by a true Strategy VWAP: each leg's VWAP since the day's first sample = Σ price x interval volume / Σ interval volume (interval volume = rise of the cumulative counter between samples, priced at the last price of the closing sample; the first sample of a day carries the volume traded since the open; a falling counter adds nothing), combined like the strategy value (signed, lot-weighted; shown as cost to close for a credit structure; also in rupee P&L terms). Null until every leg has traded. Limits: last-traded prices, about one-minute samples, so intra-minute trades are priced at the interval-closing price.

## D128 — Strategy builder: every listed expiry, and strategies across expiries (calendar, diagonal, weekly + monthly) (11 Nov 2026)
Data: the sampler still collects only the weekly and the monthly (stored chains, stream, history). `GET /api/option-chain/on-demand?expiry=` fetches the chain of ANY expiry the broker lists, now, and does not store it (no snapshot, no observation row, so the disk cannot grow); reused 10 s per expiry, one fetch at a time per expiry, two broker calls at once at most. The builder offers every listed expiry (first four as buttons, the rest in a dropdown) and keeps the legs when the shown expiry changes: a leg carries its own expiry, price (from its own expiry's chain), implied forward/carry and market volatility (one `Book` per expiry, `lib/strategyBook.ts`). Prices of non-collected expiries come from the 15 s fetch, so their legs have no stream price, no price-history chart and no server-side stop/target watching (stated in the page; the server skips legs without a live key).
Maths (`lib/multiExpiry.ts`): with legs on several expiries there is no single expiry payoff, so the curve is read AT THE CLOSE OF A CHOSEN LEG EXPIRY (default the earliest). A leg expired by then is worth its intrinsic value at the axis level (earlier-expiring legs are treated as settled at that same level: a stated simplification, since a leg really settles at NIFTY on its own expiry day); a leg still alive is valued by Black-76 with its own expiry, own forward and its market implied volatility held constant. The curve is exact only for legs that have expired; otherwise max profit/loss, breakevens, POP and the curve are labelled model estimates. Unlimited sides follow the net signed call quantity. A strategy with one expiry uses the unchanged exact analysis; a test shows the curve path reproduces it to the cent. Calendar, straddle calendar, double calendar and diagonal (call/put) templates; a strategy is filed (saved, closed, sent to the server) under its LAST leg expiry. The Price chart merges the samples of several collected expiries on the first expiry's time axis (nearest sample within 90 s, else blank).
Not built: payoff at a date between expiries along a path (needs a NIFTY-level distribution at the earlier expiry), margin, calendar-specific Greeks tables beyond the per-leg ones.

## D129 — Smart OI: switchable lower-right chart (11 Nov 2026)
The third (lower right) Smart OI chart has two views: PCR (OI and volume PCR, default) and one combined volume view: cumulative put and call volume traded today (units, same strikes in every candle) on the left axis, and the put-minus-call difference on the right axis (cumulative line; per-candle bars hidden until chosen in the legend, green when puts traded more). Built from the stored cumulative contract volumes already in the series; a missing volume stays blank. Candles now carry `ceVol`/`peVol`.

The /analysis page itself and its route stay; it is still linked from Market Intelligence ("Open the intraday analysis"). The planned-tool rendering code stays for future use; no tool is planned today.

## D131 — Straddle decay intelligence audit: decay efficiency noise floor, labelling, chart/card consistency (12 Nov 2026)
Findings (live server, 12 Nov, 09:33-09:45 IST): (1) "Fixed 25200 at NIFTY 25,338" is by design, not hard-coded: the fixed position is the strike nearest NIFTY at the first usable sample of the session (opening spot 25,175 -> 25,200), held all day; the page now says "Fixed 25200 · ATM at open", names the opening time and spot, and shows today's ATM strike and the rolling alternative. (2) The seller/buyer scores swung 30 <-> 77 within a minute (stored rows 03:47-04:13 UTC: 77.5, 75.9, 50.9, 30.6, 51.8 ... 31.4, 32.3, 53.9 ... 64.4, 43.1). Cause: the decay-efficiency component divided the actual premium change by the window's theta decay, which early in the session (and on a 5-day expiry) is 0.03-0.75 points while last-traded prices move 1-4 points per tick; stored efficiencies were +8,625 %, +1,548 %, -271 %, -1,032 %, +246 %, -497 %, and its sign flipped whenever the straddle ticked across its opening value (weight 20 of the seller score, 8 of the buyer score, plus the climate tally). Fix: below 3 points of expected decay the ratio is not computed (status "low_signal", reason shown, component unavailable); from 3 to 8 points it is computed and the two components are scaled by confidence = (expected - 3) / 5 toward neutral; full weight at 8 points or more. The working floor is a provisional engineering value like the other weights (not validated); it is a noise rule, so it does not change what a healthy reading means. (3) Top cards showed the live computation while the history chart showed the last recorded minute (59/49 against 59/48): the chart now ends with the current reading when it is newer than the last recorded minute. (4) The "no clear call/put IV pattern" text on a component that was unavailable because the 30-minute window did not exist yet now says so. Not changed: scores still renormalise over available inputs (coverage shown), so they move when an input appears (coverage 0.64 -> 0.86 at 09:19 on 12 Nov).

## D132 — Straddle decay page: a plain-language "Today at a glance" block (12 Nov 2026)
Why: the page was twelve dense tables; the Fixed/Rolling buttons only change the sections below, so clicking them left the scores (computed once on the server) unchanged and looked broken. New `StraddleGlance` above the detailed sections: seller and buyer gauges with zone bars, the 15-minute-ago marker and a note when a risk flag holds the label below the zone; a plain-words reading; Fixed against Rolling side by side (open, now, change, and a seller's result per lot before costs) with a change-since-open chart of both and the share of its opening price each side of the fixed strike keeps; diverging bars for what pushes each score up or down; grouped bars of what moved the premium (time, volatility, NIFTY, curvature, other) for both positions; the score history ending with the current reading; jump links and a glossary. The detailed sections stay, each with a one-line plain explanation and larger text. Presentation only: no calculation changed, and the low-signal rule (D131) now also carries the lot size so rupee results show early in the session.

## D133 — Straddle glance charts follow the chosen position (12 Nov 2026)
Feedback on D132: after clicking Rolling ATM nothing visible changed (the comparison cards and charts always showed both, and the scores do not depend on the position), so the control looked dead. Now a "Following" control sits at the top of the glance; the followed position's line and bars are bold and the other is faded; the call/put chart shows the followed position's call and put at the open and now (for Rolling: the first sample's ATM strike against today's, flagged as different strikes); the note states which position is followed; and a "How are these charts drawn?" section explains the fixed line (strike chosen at the open, minus its opening value), the rolling line (same-contract changes only, no jump at a strike change) and why they differ (the fixed strike goes in the money as NIFTY moves). Scores stay position-independent and say so.

## D134 — Market Intelligence page: readable on a phone, fewer walls of text, heavyweight chart fixes (12 Nov 2026)
Seen on a phone: the data-quality table was cut off on the right (min width 44 rem inside a scroller), the sources table likewise, the heavyweight table needed a 52 rem scroll, the heavyweight line chart's legend wrapped onto its axis labels, and the bar chart's value labels collided with the stock names. Changes: tables with `stack-rows` become one card per row below 768 px (labels from data-label); the data-quality block is collapsed by default with its counts always visible; the heavyweight table has a card list for phones; the line chart has a one-line legend with short names, short time labels and room above the plot; the bar chart has a symmetric axis so labels never reach the names. The "valid" wording is now explicit (5 of 5 heavyweights valid for the live 5, 15 and 30 minute readings) and an amber note states in words why the whole-day aggregate is not shown (which stocks were tracked for only part of the day, the weight they carry against the 60% rule; volume traded while the app was down is never guessed). Not changed, and still genuinely unavailable: genuine aggressor-side CVD, spot-index VWAP and option trade-side data (not in the broker feed), rollover and individual stock futures (not subscribed: a feed subscription project, not a display fix). Known: every deploy during market hours restarts the app, and a restart loses the volume that traded while it was down, so deploying mid-session degrades the heavyweight whole-day CVD; today HDFCBANK and BHARTIARTL show only about 18% of the day's volume.

## D135 — Flow trackers no longer wipe the session on a volume-counter fall; a wiped session is rebuilt from the stored minutes (12 Nov 2026)
Evidence: stored heavyweight minutes show HDFCBANK at 11.34 M of 11.84 M traded volume classified at 10:25 IST and 53 k a minute later (BHARTIARTL 1.95 M -> 12 k), while ICICIBANK, RELIANCE and LT continued, around an app restart. Cause: `FlowTracker.update` called `reset()` whenever a cumulative volume figure was below the baseline, so one late or foreign figure after the first push of a restart wiped everything just restored. Fix: a figure within 2 % below the last one is ignored (late or out-of-order, never a basis for a delta); a larger fall is a counter restart and only re-baselines (the flow already classified is real and is kept; the volume that traded while the counter was off stays unclassified, as after a backend restart); a new day still starts clean. `last_cumulative` (the restore after a restart) now returns the observed total, the sum of the increases over the stored minutes with a fall counted as a fresh start, which equals the newest sample when nothing fell and repairs sessions already wiped (replayed on the real 12 Nov rows: HDFCBANK 3.73 M -> 15.06 M of 15.70 M traded, BHARTIARTL 0.70 M -> 2.64 M of 2.77 M). Nothing is estimated. The inputs the broker feed does not carry (aggressor side, spot VWAP, option trade side) and the unsubscribed ones (rollover, stock futures) remain unavailable and are labelled.

## D136 — Index VWAP estimate, stock futures and rollover (12 Nov 2026)
Checked against the Kotak Neo SDK's feed models (`neo_api_client/websocket/feed/models.py`): a stock or futures quote carries price, OHLC, average trade price, last trade time and quantity, total buy/sell order quantity, volume, total traded value, open interest, circuit limits and five depth levels; the index message carries only price, OHLC and change. There is no aggressor flag and no trade tape, so genuine aggressor-side CVD (stocks and options) stays unavailable; the index has no volume, so a true spot VWAP does not exist. Two items that had been labelled "feed" or "not subscribed" are now built:
(1) Index VWAP ESTIMATE (`market/futures.py`): previous index close x the index-weight-weighted mean of (stock day VWAP / stock previous close) over the NIFTY 50 constituents that have a fresh average trade price (at least 30 stocks and 80 % of the weight, else unavailable with the reason). Labelled an estimate everywhere, never mixed with the futures VWAP, shown as a pulse card and the `spot_vwap` capability (AVAILABLE at 95 % of the weight or more, else PARTIAL).
(2) Stock futures and rollover: the current- and next-month futures of the NIFTY 50 stocks (found in the F&O scrip master by trading symbol, e.g. RELIANCE26NOVFUT) and the NIFTY next-month future are subscribed as touch-line tokens (about 100 against the SDK limit of 3000; 824 were acknowledged before). Per stock: price change, open interest, OI change against the FIRST reading of the session (the feed has no previous-day OI; the first stored sample survives restarts), buildup label by the usual price/OI rule, basis against cash, rollover = next-month share of open interest by value, roll cost. NIFTY: next-month share of open interest (units) and roll cost. Stored every fifth minute as `stkfut` (about 0.3 MB a session, about 70 MB a year; to be added to the storage plan). Page: a "Stock futures and rollover" section with tiles, an OI-change chart, a table and phone cards. Still unavailable (the feed has no such field): aggressor-side CVD for heavyweights and options.

Verified live on 12 Nov 2026 after the deploy: the scrip-master lookup found 50 of 50 current-month and 50 next-month stock futures and the NIFTY next-month future; all 50 stock futures and the NIFTY pair streamed; the index VWAP estimate (25,287 from 50 stocks, 100 % of the weight) agrees with the futures VWAP less the basis (25,326 less about 40) and with the mean of the day's one-minute index closes (25,300). Because the stock futures were added mid-session, their open interest changes are measured from the first reading this app had (about 13:01 IST) and the page says so with that time; from the next session the baseline is the stored 09:15 sample. The index previous close is cached so one missing index tick does not blank the estimate.

## D137 — Strategy price chart showed no strategy line for real strategies (12 Nov 2026)
Cause: a builder leg already has a `key` field (its live-feed key, "nse_fo|<token>", set whenever the leg is in a collected expiry). The chart's series lookup (added with the multi-expiry merge) read `leg.key` as the key into the stored strike maps, found nothing and returned no price at every sample: no strategy line, VWAP or averages, empty tiles and an empty spot-driven split, only spot and the future's VWAP. Tests and my own checks used legs without a feed key, so they passed. Fix: the merged-series key is a separate field (`seriesKey`), with a regression test using legs that carry feed keys (lib and component). Layout: one-line scrolling legends, no axis names that collided with the legend, room above the plot, and a caption instead of an overlapping title on the split chart.

## D138 — Turn watch: what changed in the last 20 minutes (12 Nov 2026)
Why: on 12 Nov NIFTY fell about 130 points from 10:45 to 12:15 and the dashboard never turned bearish. At 10:45 the fused score was +41 ("Bullish trend", because Positioning reads standing OI levels), then it drifted to about zero ("Sideways") while Structure and Participation fell to -38 and -30. The score is a level and the alerts are threshold crossings between two minutes, so a slow turn is averaged away. What: `intelligence/turn.py` compares each engine's 5-minute average with the same average 20 minutes earlier, from the stored per-minute timeline only (no new data). Signals: both flow engines (Structure, Participation) down or up by 12+ points; fused score slide of 20+ points; breadth (3 of 5 engines moving together, context only, never sets the lean). A signal shows only when present in at least 4 of the last 6 minutes, and is marked "price has not confirmed yet" when NIFTY moved under 0.15% in the same 20 minutes. A "Turn watch" alert is raised once when a flow or score signal switches on. Shown on the Market Intelligence page under the sentiment strip, with the 20-minute change per engine. Evidence on the two stored sessions (in-sample, so not proof): the flow-down signal switched on at 11:05 on 12 Nov (NIFTY 25,322; low 25,217) but also at 10:19 that morning (price then rose 20 points), and at 10:09 on 11 Nov (price then rose); on 11 Nov it also fired 12:31 and 15:05 with price lower after. Only two full sessions are stored, so the thresholds are provisional, the page says so, and nothing here is called predictive. Not done: a Smart OI divergence rule (put-minus-call day-change gap against price): the stored observation history holds only two full sessions and Smart OI is computed in the browser, so it needs the OI day-change totals recorded in the timeline first (next step, so a record builds).

D138 follow-up: the timeline now also stores the call and put open-interest day-change totals every minute (`ce_oi_chg`, `pe_oi_chg`, against previous-day OI, from the options panel), so a record of the put-minus-call gap builds from the next session. A gap signal exists in `turn.py` but is context only and uncalibrated: it needs 60 recorded minutes and fires when the 20-minute gap move is at least 2x today's median. It never sets the lean or raises an alert until enough sessions are stored to test it (the 2x figure is a placeholder, not a tuned value). Also fixed: the lean read "mixed" when only context signals were present; it now reads "none".

## D139 — A strategy closed by its own close rule now moves from Saved to Closed (12 Nov 2026)
Cause: only the Exit all button filed a strategy under Closed. The server's intraday close (15:20) and the profit rules exit every leg inside the saved strategy and stop there, so a fully closed straddle stayed in Saved with "booked ₹3,019, open ₹0". Fix (browser, so it uses the same Saved/Closed storage and sync as Exit all): `isFinished(legs)` is true when every ticked leg is out, at least one was closed by the strategy itself (reason "strategy": the clock close or a profit rule) and no stopped leg can still re-enter. The open strategy is then filed exactly as Exit all does, and any other saved strategy in that state (closed on the server while no page was open) is filed the next time the list loads, with the P&L the legs booked and their exit prices (id `auto-<name>-<date>`, so it is filed once). Strategies closed only by a stop or target, or with a leg still open, stay in Saved. Not changed: the server does not move it itself, because a browser holding an older cached copy would bring a server-side delete back; the move happens when the page is next opened, which is where it belongs in the Saved and Closed lists.

## D140 — Day theme is the default and the primary design (12 Nov 2026)
The day theme was a tint of the dark one: a blue-grey page under flat white cards with barely visible edges, which read as dull and washed out. Rebuilt as its own design (tokens and rules in `frontend/src/index.css`): near-white page (#f1f4f9), white cards with a hairline and a two-layer soft shadow, 12 px card and 8 px control corners, deep ink text (17:1), muted text 6.6:1 on white, direction and accent colours at 4.9:1 or better on the page and inset panels (brass, the ATM/second-series colour, darkened to 4.8:1 on white because it is also used for text). Controls are white with a visible edge, a lift and a pressed state; selected chips are filled; table heads are tinted with a firm line; inputs have a visible focus ring. The app now opens in the day theme when no choice is saved (the browser bar colour follows); a saved night choice is kept, and the night theme is unchanged. Checked in the browser at phone and desktop width on Market Intelligence, Intraday analysis, Swing, Smart OI (charts, tooltips) and the Strategy builder; no hard-coded dark colours were found in the components (colours come from tokens). Not done: a systematic pass over every page; pages are checked as they are touched.

D140 follow-up (12 Nov 2026): all 54 pages (15 main pages and 37 tool pages, plus the option chain) were swept in the browser at phone width with a script that, for every visible text node, computes its contrast against the real background (tinted and colour-mixed backgrounds included) and lists dark panels and sideways overflow. Result: no sideways overflow, no dark panels left over, and the only text under 4.5:1 were disabled buttons (exempt) and four real ones, now fixed: the "Projected profit" banner in the strategy builder (white on a faded green; and white text on a pale panel when there was no estimate), the "price has not confirmed yet" chip, faded warning text, and the amber chip text. The Index tools pages (contributors, weightage, breadth, sectors, movers) were bare tables on the page; they now sit in a card like every other page. Desktop width was checked by eye on a sample (option chain, gamma, portfolio, server, volatility skew, FII/DII, overview, index tools). Not covered by the sweep: text drawn inside charts (it takes its colours from the same tokens) and states that need user input (dialogs, an open strategy with many legs).

## D141 — Market Intelligence layout: sticky strip, empty cells, a printed object (12 Nov 2026)
(1) The section strip stuck below the page's own padding, so scrolled content showed through a gap above and beside it; it now sticks flush under the top bar and spans the full width (`-top-*`, `-mx-*` matching the padding of `main`; checked at phone and desktop width: strip top = bar bottom). (2) Cards in two-column rows left blank space: the five engine cards sat in three columns (an empty cell), the alert list made its row as tall as every alert, and the stat tiles used a fixed column count. Engine cards now fill the row (3 + 2 wide ones), the alert list takes the height of the divergences panel beside it and scrolls, paired cards stretch to equal height, and stat tiles use a flowing `.tiles` layout in which a short last row stretches across the width. A script checks every grid on the page for an unfilled last row: none left at 1440 px. ## D142 — Swing page separated into what each test is (12 Nov 2026)
The Swing page had grown five things under one "Paper trading" tab (the official book, the live price watch, the live paper ledger and the 1-minute shadow book stacked on one page) plus three backtest tabs, with no statement of which counts. There are five ways the same rules are run: A official paper book (daily bar, evening run, counts), B live price watch (display only), C live paper ledger (live prices, two fresh readings, own equity, reconciled each evening), D 1-minute candle shadow book (candle closes, filled at the next open, compared each evening), E backtest (the past, before and after costs). `lib/swingModes.ts` holds that list (prices read, when it decides, fill, effect on the official book) and the page shows it at the top (folded on a phone) with a button that opens each one. The tabs are now Official paper book | Live tests (shadow: B, C, D as sub-tabs) | Backtest (history: Results, Trades, Robustness) | Rules and limits, each starting with a one-line banner saying whether the result is official, a shadow test or history. No rule, calculation or data changed; only the page layout and wording.

## D143 — Two paper books, kept apart; the live one is a full portfolio page (12 Nov 2026)
Decision with the user: there are two modes, the official paper book (daily bars, evening run) and the live paper book (live prices on completed 1-minute candles), each with its own portfolio. The Live tab is now one page like the official book (no A/B/C sections): figures, portfolio analytics (return on capital, win rate, profit factor, expectancy and average R, average win/loss, max drawdown, costs, average hold, best/worst), an equity curve, open positions with stop in force, distance to the stop and the 1.8R and 2.5R targets, the watched orders with the time of each reading, every closed trade with entry and exit date and time, hold, R and net P&L, a dated event log, and the evening comparison with the official book. Every time on the page carries its date (IST). Backend: `minute_analytics.py` (pure, hand-calculated tests), an `equity` table (one snapshot per minute of market hours; recorded from this deploy, nothing back-filled, so the curve and the drawdown say "since <first recorded minute>"), position levels and closed-trade R in the view.
ANANDRATHI on 12 Nov: the official book really did hold it (86 shares bought 05 Nov at 2,320.00, still open with the stop at 2,235.57), so its new order was skipped by the official rule "already holding this stock". The live book never held it and its own reading was "below the trigger" at 09:30, so it did not buy it either; the two books were already independent (the live book only takes the same queued candidate orders). The comparison now says so in words ("the official book bought it earlier; the live book is separate and decides on its own") and gives the live book's last reading inside the entry window instead of the last reading of the day (which was always "outside window" after 15:00).
Removed from the page (backend untouched): the "Live price watch" panel (the official tab already shows live prices for its orders and positions) and the "Live paper ledger" (it voided its fills when the official book skipped an order, which tied it to the official book). The ledger is still recording on the server (setting swing_live_fills_enabled) and can be switched off; its panel and tests were deleted, not weakened.

## D144 — The scan hands the same candidates to both books; each then works alone (12 Nov 2026)
Decision with the user: after the market closes the scan reads the Chartink list and queues candidate orders once; both paper books receive that same list, and from then on each works only on its own rules and its own portfolio. Before this, the live book read its candidates from the official book's order table (the undecided queued orders), so the two shared a table even though no position, cash or decision was shared. Now `SwingService.on_candidates` hands the queued candidates to the live book after each committed scan, and the live book keeps its own copy in its own store (`queue` table in `swing_minute.sqlite3`; the first copy of a stock wins, a repeat hand-over adds nothing). `SwingLive` gives the live book only that queue and judges whether it is current by that queue's own scan date; it never reads the official orders, positions or decisions (it still streams prices for the official positions, for the official tab's live view, and the evening comparison still reads both books afterwards without changing either). One-time hand-over on the first start after this change: when the live queue is empty, the candidates the scan had already queued are copied once so tonight's session is not lost; after that the official table is not read again. Tests: a faithful copy including the tuple field, no rewrite on repeat, the live book is given its own candidates not the official ones, a stale own-queue is never acted on, and the one-time hand-over.

## D145 — Market Intelligence redesigned: a reading first, five tabs (12 Nov 2026)
The page was one long scroll (about 5,400 px) of twelve sections with the same visual weight, so the answer to "what is the market doing and can I trust it" was buried under jargon. Now it opens on an Overview whose first card is a plain-language reading written only from fields the summary already carries (`lib/miVerdict.ts`, tested by hand): the direction and score with a marker on a -100 to +100 line; a headline sentence with the trust level; three answers (how far can I trust it, what changed in 20 minutes, what kind of market is it); which evidence groups point each way, strongest first; and what would change the view, taken from the scenarios (nothing is shown when there are no scenarios; with no reading it says "No reading" instead of a direction). Below it: the turn watch, "Evidence at a glance" (one bar per evidence group from bearish to bullish, tagged stale, partial or no reading, never drawn as zero) and the key numbers. The rest moved into tabs: Evidence (the five groups, heavyweights, stock futures, straddle, intraday ML), Signals and alerts (divergences, the alert centre with its count, scenarios, interpretation), Timeline, and Data and method (the detailed quality figures, detail panels, data-quality table, overlap audit, sources). The tab shows in the address (#evidence) so a reload or a link opens the same tab; the strip is sticky under the bar. No calculation, endpoint or stored value changed. The old jump-link strip was removed. One wording rule: a breadth-only signal is described as context ("no sustained turn; 3 of 5 engines improving together"), never as a turn, matching how the turn watch treats it. Tests: the verdict logic by hand, the overview and tab behaviour, and the 33 existing page tests moved to the tab that holds what they check.

## D146 — Straddle Intelligence page redesigned the same way (12 Nov 2026)
Same problem as Market Intelligence: one panel of twelve numbered sections plus a glance block, with the answer to "is it a good time to sell or buy premium" mixed in with the detail. Now five tabs, one question each: Overview (the reading), Today's premium (the followed straddle against the open, fixed against rolling, what moved the premium, the tracker and the theta/vega/gamma attribution), Why these scores (the overall interpretation, buyer and seller environments with every component, IV and skew, implied against realised volatility, GEX, options positioning, why each score is what it is), Alerts and checks (live alerts and the historical validation, still computed only when opened), Method (calculation notes, data sources and the glossary). The Overview opens with a reading card written only from fields the summary already carries (`lib/straddleVerdict.ts`, tested by hand): the service's own plain-words comparison as the headline, both gauges, where the fixed and the rolling straddle stand (opening, now, change, with the roll-adjusted change for the rolling one) in words, what holds the scores back (risk flags, a held label, stale or stored data), up to three things that would change the picture, and the decay climate; with no environment it says so and shows nothing in its place. The score history sits under it. The tab shows in the address (#why), the stale/live chips stay on every tab, and the section numbers were dropped from the titles (a tab replaces them). No calculation, endpoint or stored value changed.

D145 follow-up: the straddle seller/buyer summary card moved from the Evidence tab to the Market Intelligence Overview (under the evidence bars), at the user's request; it still links to the full Straddle page.

## D147 — "Move other leg's SL to cost" in the strategy builder (12 Nov 2026)
A switch next to Auto stop and target (off by default). When on and a leg is stopped out, the stop of every other open leg on the SAME side (buy or sell) moves to that leg's own entry premium, so a profitable leg cannot turn into a loss. Rules, all decided so that nothing can misfire: it is set off only by a stop (not a target, not the clock close, not a strategy rule); a leg is moved only if it is in profit at that moment (a stop at the entry of a leg already at or below cost would exit it at once, so such a leg keeps its own stop and the event says "not in profit (price against entry)"; a leg with no live price likewise says so); a stop is only ever tightened (one already at the entry or on the profit side stays); the moved stop is a trailed one (it may sit at the entry) and the stop typed by the user is remembered (`stopBase`); a leg with no stop gets one at cost, and a trail set on it runs in the same pass so a repeat pass over the same prices changes nothing; the exit is labelled "Stop at cost"; a stop typed by hand clears the mark; the "stop must be below the entry" flag no longer fires for a trailed or cost stop. It applies to stops hit from now on (nothing is moved retroactively), is not reverted if the stopped leg later re-enters, and needs Auto stop and target on. The server applies the same rule every second, even with no page open: the switch is part of the running document (`moveToCost`), like the hold rule, and is not stored with named saved strategies. The browser (`moveStopsToCost`) and the server (`move_stops_to_cost`) were written as twins and are held to each other by the 300-case parity fixture (half the cases run with the switch on; compared fields now include `costMoved`) plus hand-calculated tests with the same numbers on both sides, a page test (off by default, on moves the stop, label shown, exits at cost with no loss), a test that the switch is taken from and sent to the server, and a service test with no browser. While writing the parity check a real gap showed up (a leg given its first stop by this rule could only start trailing one pass later, so a second pass over the same prices changed something) and was fixed.

## D148 — Prediction and intraday ML removed (13 Nov 2026)
Decision with the user: "we don't need" the Prediction section and the Intraday ML section, nor their data. Removed: the /prediction page and its visuals; the T+1/T+5 forecast system (`research`, `ledger`, `models`, `evaluation`, `features`, `features_multi`, `daylog`) and its routes (`/api/prediction/summary`, `/summary/recompute`); the events/news panel with the news collector (`news_service`, `news_store`, `/prediction/news/*`, `/prediction/events`, `news_autofetch`) and the news row in Diagnostics; the intraday ML research (`dataset`, `enrich`, `evaluate`, `mldigest`, `IntradayService.research/ml_digest/_ml_status`, `/api/intraday/research`, the nightly feature-row build in the end-of-day job and its `feature_rows` table definition, the Market Intelligence "Intraday ML" panel and its capability, the Command Center ML stat, research card and ML block); their tests (whole files or the tests that used them) and docs (`docs/prediction/`, `docs/intraday-ml-plan.md`, the CLAUDE.md section). Kept on purpose, because other features read them: the daily price history (volatility/IV charts, the Command Center global and sector cards, the end-of-day job), NSE institutional flows (the FII/DII pages), the events calendar (Command Center), the trading calendar and the NSE/Yahoo sources, with their routes (still under `/api/prediction/` for history, flows and cash import); the Settings panels were renamed "Daily price history" and "NSE institutional flows". Not removed in code: the global-series downloads in the history service (the Command Center uses them). Stored data on the server (forecast ledger, day log, report and run files, the ML digest, the news database, and the feature-row table inside intraday.sqlite3) is deleted separately and only after explicit confirmation of the exact list, since that cannot be undone.

The daily-history service now downloads only NIFTY 50 and India VIX. Removed with it: the S&P 500 / USD/INR "Global markets" group of the intraday score (weight 0.10; the remaining weights are renormalised by the existing rule, coverage is measured against the new total), the Global markets health row, the "Global conditions?" question, the Command Center global and sector-index tables (that card is now "Institutional flows"; stock-based sector participation from NIFTY 500 stays), the 07:30 overnight-bar morning run and its 10-minute retries, and `usmarket.py`. Also removed: the leftover "Intraday ML model" health row. Stored rows of the removed series in `market_history.sqlite3` are deleted on the server only after approval.

## D150 — Day theme polish: depth, size, weight (13 Nov 2026)
The user found the day theme poor next to the night theme. A scripted check showed no contrast failures or overflow, so the problem was design, not defects: pale cards on a pale page, inset tiles that were grey patches, thin 10-11 px grey text, pastel notices and a faint gauge. Changes, all in `frontend/src/index.css` unless noted: a cooler page grey and firmer card edges; inset tiles get an inset ring; darker secondary and data text (muted 8:1 on white); small text one step larger (10→11, 11→12, xs 12.75, body 14.5 px); heavier headings; coloured notices get a 4 px edge in their own colour and a stronger tint; table rows lightly banded; the Market Intelligence verdict card takes a tone edge and wash and its gauge a red-to-green track with a filled segment (`Verdict.tsx`); Command Center tiles wrap their sub-line instead of cutting it with an ellipsis. Night theme is unchanged. Checked in the browser on Home, Market Intelligence, Straddle, Swing, Strategy and Analysis at 1100 and 1280 px.

## D151 — Tool menus redesigned: one A-Z list, three recent tools, filter box (13 Nov 2026)
The FNO Tools and Analyse drop-downs were grouped (Live chain, OI tools, ...) in a three-column grid that was hard to use on a phone. Each menu is now one alphabetical list with no group headings, led by the three most recently opened tools (newest first, kept in the browser's local storage only, recorded when a tool page is opened) and a filter box. On a desktop the list flows down two or three columns and the panel slides left if it would leave the screen; on a phone it is one column with larger rows. The Home page still shows the groups. Code: `components/ToolMenu.tsx`, `lib/recentTools.ts`, `components/TopNav.tsx`; tests in `shell.test.tsx`.

## D152 — Chart layout on phones: no collisions, less dead space (13 Nov 2026)
The user's phone screenshots showed legends over the top axis label, date labels printed over each other, corner readouts lying on the lines or the legend, ATM and Spot labels overlapping, end-of-line tags cut at the edge and a wide empty margin beside the plot. ECharts wraps a long legend onto several rows but reserves no room for them, so the fix is central, in `components/EChart.tsx` (`fitLayout`, applied to every chart): the legend's rows are counted (text measured on a canvas) and every grid that would start inside the legend band is moved down with its bottom kept; text readouts (`graphic`) are stacked under the legend and pulled inside the chart; axis labels that would overlap are dropped and a narrow time or value axis gets fewer ticks; the right margin is raised to the width of an end label when the chart gave it less, and lowered to 54 px on a phone when it only holds the tags. Chart-specific: Portfolio peak and worst labels move beside the point when it is at the right end; the volatility smile shares one label when ATM and spot are within three strikes. Page text: the Strategy builder's name box no longer collapses on a phone and the option chain hides the Greek columns below 640 px so it fits without sideways scrolling; tile sub-lines wrap instead of ending in an ellipsis. Verified with a script that reads every chart's legend and plot rectangles in the browser (no collisions on 40 chart pages at 375 px) and unit tests in `chart-layout.test.ts`.

## D153 — Production hardening pass (13 Nov 2026)
Reviewed what already protected production (pull-based deploy with health check and automatic rollback, HTTPS with a login in front of everything, nightly integrity-checked database backups, storage monitor) and closed what was missing. Added: a watchdog that checks the backend, broker login, live feed, sampler, disk, backups and last deploy every 5 minutes and shows problems as a strip on every page (optional ntfy/Telegram push, off until chosen); the running release in `/api/health`; a restore check proved on the real 12 Nov backup; an off-server copy (`scripts/pull-backups.ps1`, first copy taken); a gzip check in the nightly backup; (a GitHub Actions workflow was added and then removed the same day at the user's request: no GitHub workflows, everything is server-based); `.gitattributes` so server scripts stay LF. Decided not to enforce `ruff format` (about 140 files would change) and not to gate deploys on any CI (the server's import check, build, health check and automatic rollback are the gate; the offline checks run on the development machine with scripts\check.ps1). Left for the user, with a script and instructions: SSH hardening (password and root login are still allowed, no firewall, no fail2ban) because a wrong step can lock them out, and choosing a push channel. Not done: cross-checking Yahoo closes against an NSE source, and verifying the live stream in market hours (the market was closed; the watchdog now raises an alert if the feed or sampler stops during a session).

## D154 — Robustness for the long run (13 Nov 2026)
Looked for things that would break quietly with time.
