Found and handled: (1) the NSE holiday list ends on 31 Dec 2026 and after that the app silently treats every weekday as a session (wrong on holidays): the watchdog now warns 60 days ahead and goes critical 14 days ahead, and `docs/maintenance.md` says what to add from the official circular (not guessed: only an official source is acceptable); (2) Python dependencies were ranges, so a rebuild could pull untested releases: `backend/requirements.lock` now pins the 64 packages the working server runs, `deploy/update.sh` installs with it and runs `pip check` (verified on the server with a dry run); (3) NSE lot-size changes: the app compares the stored chain's lot size with the cost model and shows an alert; (4) daily-history and NSE-flow staleness, a failed Yahoo download and a blocked NSE download now raise alerts instead of showing only on a diagnostics page; (5) a kernel update waiting for a reboot (flagged after a week, to be done on a weekend); (6) a runbook of every alert and every recurring duty. Already sound and left alone: systemd restarts on failure, NTP is synchronised, timers and services are enabled for boot, Caddy renews certificates by itself, `package-lock.json` makes frontend builds reproducible, events calendar runs to Dec 2027. Not done: the 2027 holiday list itself (not published yet; a person must add it), and a scheduled weekly run of `pull-backups.ps1` on the PC (a Task Scheduler entry is a change to the user's computer).

## D155 — Holidays can be updated in Settings (13 Nov 2026)
The verified NSE holiday list ends on 31 Dec 2026 and had to be extended by editing a JSON file in the code. Settings now has an "Exchange holidays" panel: paste the holiday lines of NSE's circular (several date layouts are understood, serial numbers and weekday words are skipped, weekends are skipped, every line that cannot be read is reported), review the rows, name the circular, tick a confirmation when extending the covered period, Save. The entries are kept in `data/holidays_user.json` and merged over the NSE circular list, which stays read-only and verified; the merged calendar is updated in place so every part of the app (sessions, expected data, the browser's holiday list, the watchdog) sees it at once and the warning clears. Refused with a reason: a weekend holiday, a date already in the circular list, a date outside the covered period, a duplicate, no source named, an extension with no new holiday or without the confirmation, a period ending before the verified list or more than about two years ahead. Only the server itself can save (the same local-control rule as other settings; the website login protects it from outside). The file is copied by the nightly backup and `scripts/pull-backups.ps1`. A damaged file is ignored, leaving the verified list alone.

## D156 — Live India VIX from the broker's index feed (13 Nov 2026)
- The stream now also subscribes the index `nse_cm|India VIX` (Kotak subscribes indices by name), as an internal best-effort token like the breadth stocks: it is kept off the browser feed and never counts as option-feed activity, so a feed that does not serve it cannot disturb the option stream or the watchdog.
- `MarketService.vix_live()` reads only the live tick (value, previous close, change, age, `fresh` within 2 minutes); nothing is carried over from the daily history. The pulse card shows "India VIX (live)" while fresh, otherwise the labelled daily close as before. The intraday score does not use it: it is NSE's 30-day figure, not an expiry's ATM IV.
- Not built: a stored intraday VIX line (the observation schema has no VIX column; add one only after the live value is proven in market hours).
- Checked live on the server (13 Nov, 10:15 IST): Kotak serves it, delivered as "INDIA VIX" (capitals) with the previous close; the stream matches an index to its internal key ignoring case.

## D157 — Blank pages after an update, and speed (13 Nov 2026)
- **Finding:** switching pages sometimes showed a blank screen until a reload. Every push deploys a new build whose files carry new hashed names, and the server kept only the newest build's files. A tab (or the installed app) opened before the deploy still asked for the old names; Caddy answered a missing file with the index page (`try_files {path} /index.html`), so the browser could not load the page, and the app had no error boundary, so React removed everything. The service worker could also store that index page under a script name.
- **Fix, four layers:** (1) `update.sh` copies the previous release's built `assets/` into the new build (content-hashed names never clash; files older than 14 days are dropped), so an open tab keeps working across deploys. (2) An error boundary around every page (the menu stays) and around the whole app, with a plain message, Reload and Try again; a failed page file after an update reloads the page once (not again within 30 s, so a real fault cannot loop). (3) Caddy: `/assets/*` is a plain 404 when missing and is cached for a year (`immutable`, only when the file exists); `index.html`, `sw.js` and the rest are `no-cache`. (4) The service worker (v2) stores only real files and never an HTML answer.
- **Speed:** the other pages are fetched quietly while the browser is idle after the first screen (skipped on a data-saver or 2G connection), so a menu click is instant. Timed every parameterless GET on the server: 61 endpoints, two slow ones: the Data clean-up overview (22 s) and straddle validation (5 s), plus the storage status (7 s, D156-era fix). All three walk every stored file or session; their results are reused for two minutes (five for the validation), overlapping calls share one build (one CPU core), disk free space is always read fresh, and a clean-up run drops the cached overview. Not changed: the first call after a restart still pays the full cost.
- **Caddy change needs one command on the server** (a normal deploy does not touch Caddy's config): `sudo bash /opt/nse-universe/current/deploy/apply-caddyfile.sh <domain>`.


## D158 (13 Nov 2026): straddle charts show every stored minute
The straddle service thinned a session to 240 points, so a full day (about 385 one-minute samples) was drawn at every second minute. The cap is now 400 (the IV series default too, was 90), so nothing is thinned; the charts use every stored sample. Both lines of the fixed-against-rolling chart are drawn equally thick; the followed one is solid, the other faded. Cost: the straddle summary reads about twice the snapshots (cached 20 s).


## D159 (13 Nov 2026): payoff chart shows the full shape of a bounded strategy
The payoff y-axis clipped a side at 3x the other, so an iron fly whose maximum profit was over 3x its maximum loss had its peak cut off. A payoff that is flat at both ends (iron fly, condor, spreads) now shows its real peak and trough; only a side that keeps running is still clipped. Choosing a different strategy (strikes, sides, types) also resets the zoom to the profit-zone view instead of keeping the previous zoom.


## D160 (13 Nov 2026): a closed trade is never filed twice
The builder files any working strategy whose legs are all out as a new Closed trade. The working copy is kept in the browser without the "opened closed trade" mark, so a closed trade opened to look at and then restored (refresh, page switch) came back as plain finished legs and was filed again under a new name ("Strategy 13 Nov, 17:51" and "21:56" copied "Straddle - neutral 13/11/2026", +3,660 each). Before filing, the legs are now compared (side, type, strike, expiry, entry premium, exit price) with every closed trade; a match is shown as that closed trade instead. The two duplicate records already stored are NOT removed by this change (stored data is only deleted with approval).


## D161 (13 Nov 2026): payoff curve samples the strikes
The payoff chart was drawn from a uniform grid (about 7 points apart for a wide window). An expiry payoff only bends at the strikes, so when a short strike (an iron fly peak at 25150) fell between two grid points the peak was cut and drawn as a small bend instead of a sharp tip. The strikes are now always sampled, so the peak and every kink are exact. Option prices were checked against the stored chain: put-call parity gives a forward of about 25151 across strikes, and volume and open interest kept updating until 15:39, so the prices are real last-traded values, not closed-market leftovers.


## D162 (14 Nov 2026): replay on a tool page, one shared clock
Smart OI, Multi OI & Volume, Gamma Exposure, Vega Analysis, ATM Straddle Chart and ATM Rolling Straddle have a Replay button (`tool.replay`, `components/dashboard/ReplayBar.tsx`). It is OFF until pressed, so the page is live or last-session as before; after the close it plays the stored session from the first sample, one stored sample per step (speed 1x = 1 per second), like it looked live. Design: the page still loads the whole day once; a replay time (`ReplayContext`) hides everything after it. `DashProvider` cuts the stored history (`lib/replay.ts` `clipObservations`) and replaces the latest chain with the stored snapshot of the replay time (`/api/snapshots/{id}`, at most about one request a second), with no live overlay; per-strike series (`useClipped`/`clipSeries`) are cut where each widget loads them. Vega's own scrubber was removed in favour of the shared bar. Limits stated on the page: steps are the stored samples (about a minute apart, no ticks, no open-high-low-close inside a minute); a minute that was not recorded is not filled in; engine scores exist only for the days they were recorded. Not yet on the replay: the Straddle Intelligence page (its scores come from the server per session), the IV and futures tools.


## D163 (14 Nov 2026): replay no longer flashes "Loading stored samples"
During a replay the ATM moves with each step, so the rolling straddle (and the Vega, Gamma and strike boards) asked for a different strike set each time and every new request showed "Loading stored samples" in the middle of the play. The strike-series queries now keep the previous series on screen while a new one loads (`placeholderData: keepPreviousData`), and during a replay the rolling straddle holds its strike set (41 strikes) on the session opening ATM, so it asks once.


## D164 (14 Nov 2026): Smart OI hover follows by time position, not by series
The three Smart OI charts are meant to show one hover (pointing at a time in one marks that time in the others). They shared an ECharts chart group, which copies the hovered series and data index to the other charts. With put and call volume switched off in the volume chart, the series it reported did not exist or had no point at that index in the candle chart, so that chart showed nothing. Reproduced in a real browser with the real chart code and synthetic data. `EChart` has a new `hoverGroup` prop (`lib/hoverSync.ts`): the category index under the pointer is mapped to each other chart (convertFromPixel / convertToPixel) and each shows its tooltip there, whichever series are on. Used by the three Smart OI charts; other charts keep `group`. Zoom and legend are no longer linked between those three (nothing there used it). A first attempt that removed the shared hover was a misreading and was reverted.


## D165 (14 Nov 2026): every linked chart group uses the position-based hover
D164 fixed the shared hover for the Smart OI charts. The same ECharts `connect` was behind every chart group in the app (price boards, multi-strike and straddle boards, rolling straddle, Vega, Straddle Intelligence, Strategy time chart): hovering one chart copied the hovered series and data index to the others, which show nothing when that series is switched off in a legend or is not theirs. `EChart` no longer calls `echarts.connect`. For `group` (and `hoverGroup`) charts it links by x position (a category index or a time/number value, so charts with different ranges still match), and for `group` it also applies zoom to the others and switches a legend item of the same name on or off in the others, as `connect` did. Checked in a real browser on three grouped time-axis charts built with the real option builder: hover follows with legend lines off, from any chart, after a zoom; zoom and legend toggles link. Logic and tests in `lib/hoverSync.ts`, `test/hover-sync.test.ts`.


## D166 (14 Nov 2026): Smart OI volume panel is readable and its tooltip shows the volume
The bottom panel of the Smart OI chart (stacked put and call volume per candle) was small and gave no numbers on hover. It now has a taller plot (19% of the chart instead of 11%), a legend of its own (Put volume, Call volume, which also switches one side off), the title "Volume per candle: put (green) + call (red)", and the chart tooltip adds the volume of the hovered candle: put, call, total, the put share, put minus call, and the day's totals so far (a dash when the figure was not stored). Volume of a candle = the change in the cumulative day volume between the last samples of two candles; the first candle is everything since the open. Checked in a real browser with generated data; test `smart-oi-volume.test.ts`.


## D167 (14 Nov 2026): GEX chart level labels no longer overlap
The level labels above the GEX profile (Spot, Call wall, Put wall, Peak, Flip) were staggered over four rows by taking the label number modulo four. With five levels the fifth wrapped onto the first row, and when two levels were equal (a call wall that is also the peak) or close, their labels overlapped, most visibly on a phone; the top row also ran into the legend. Each label now has a row of its own (`labelRowOffset`, 13 px apart) and the chart's top margin grows with the number of labels (`labelRowsTop`: 34 px for the legend plus the rows), for the profile and the density chart. Not yet looked at on a phone in a real browser.


## D168 (15 Nov 2026): replay on the Straddle Intelligence page
The page's numbers (tracker, attribution, IV and skew, realised against implied, regime, climate) are computed on the server over a whole session, so a replay needs the analysis as it stood at each minute, not a cut of the finished one. `GET /api/intelligence/straddle` takes `as_of` (a time with a zone): the service reads only the samples stored up to that minute, runs the same analysis with that moment as "now" (never live, so no buyer/seller environments), and cuts the recorded score history and alerts at it. Answers are kept per stored minute (48 at most) and a replay never touches the live minute's cache or the premium ladder that `record` keeps. One analysis takes the server about 2.5 to 3 s on a full day (measured 12 and 13 Nov), so the page keeps one request in flight and asks for the newest replay time next; the header says which minute the analysis is "as of". Same Replay button as the tool pages (off until pressed, plays from the first sample, steps are the stored samples). The recorded scores in the history chart replay too, for the days they were recorded (from 11 Nov); the large buyer/seller gauges stay "live only" as on any stored session. Tests: `test_straddle_service.py` (as_of cuts the samples, the history and the alerts; cache and live state untouched), `straddle-replay.test.tsx`.


## D169 (15 Nov 2026): Hide info on every chart; Smart OI volume legend no longer overlaps its title
On a phone the card that follows the pointer over a chart (its tooltip) can cover the whole chart. Every `EChart` now has a small "Hide info" / "Show info" button in its top-right corner (`lib/chartInfo.ts`). It sets `tooltip.showContent` (the card goes, the crosshair that marks the position stays), is one choice for all charts, and is remembered in this browser (`nse.charts.hideInfo`). Two ECharts details found in a real browser: `showContent` has to be set both ways (a merged option that merely lacks the key leaves the old `false` in force), and switching it back on needs the tooltip component rebuilt (`replaceMerge: tooltip`, only when the choice changes, so a live refresh does not close an open card); otherwise the card stays blank. The Smart OI volume panel's legend was beside its title and overlapped it on a narrow chart; it now sits on its own line under the title (title 66.5%, legend 70%, bars 76% to 91% of the chart height). Checked on a 380 px wide viewport with generated data.


## D170 (15 Nov 2026): the hide-info switch is one button per page, beside its heading
D169 put a "Hide info" button on every chart; on a phone those buttons covered chart titles and there were dozens of them. The per-chart buttons are gone. `components/ChartInfoToggle.tsx` is a single "Hide chart info / Show chart info" button placed beside the heading of each page that has charts (tool pages, Intraday analysis, Market Intelligence, Straddle Intelligence, Strategy builder, Swing, Portfolio, Home, Server Management). It is still one choice for every chart (`lib/chartInfo.ts`, kept in this browser, so all buttons show the same state); the card that follows the pointer is not drawn and the crosshair stays. The ECharts details of D169 (set `showContent` both ways, rebuild the tooltip component when the choice changes) are unchanged. Pages with charts that are not in the list have no button; the choice made on another page still applies to their charts.


## D171 (15 Nov 2026): charts use the width they have
On a phone the plots stopped well short of the right edge. Two causes. Charts with latest-value tags (the coloured values at the end of each line) kept a column of about 55 px at the right for them, empty between the tags; and charts with nothing at the right edge (the portfolio charts) kept a 66 px margin. `fitNarrow` (narrow containers, under 560 px) now moves the tags into the legend text ("Rolling ATM straddle  299.99") when the chart has a legend, and caps the right margin at 14 px when nothing is drawn at the right of the plot (54 with tags, 66 with a visible right axis, as before); a tag that is drawn above or beside its point no longer claims a column in `fitLayout` either. `legendBox` counts the legend rows from the text as drawn, so a legend that grew with the values still gets its room. The portfolio chart's latest-value tag is now drawn above its dot, ending at it (so its margin is 24 px, not 78), and the Peak label is not drawn when the latest point is the peak. Wide charts are unchanged except the portfolio. Checked in a real browser at 400 px and at desktop width with the real option builders and generated data; not yet seen on the live pages.


## D172 (16 Nov 2026): global markets, economic calendar and news (Analyse menu)
Three new Analyse pages: Global Markets, Economic Calendar, News. Backend module `app/globalmkts` with its own store `global.sqlite3` (bars, latest quote per symbol, runs, events, event revisions, news), started with the app and switched off by `globalmkts_enabled`. Rules:
- **Quotes** come from Yahoo Finance one-minute bars through the unofficial `yfinance` package (the same dependency as the daily history): US index futures (ES, NQ, YM, RTY), cash indices and VIX, US 10-year yield, dollar index, USD/INR, gold, silver, Brent, Nikkei, Hang Seng, KOSPI, Shanghai. Polled every 60 s in Indian market hours and every 300 s otherwise; a full two-session refresh every 6 h. Bars are stored once and never deleted automatically (about 1,440 minutes a day for the futures; sizes to be measured and added to the storage plan).
- **Change is computed here** from a stored reference close, never copied from the source: US index futures from the 16:00 New York cash close, the other symbols from the previous session's last bar. Status is live (bar at most 8 min old), stale (should be trading but not updating) or closed, by rule-based exchange hours. A futures contract roll can cause a one-off jump (known risk, flagged on the page).
- **Calendar**: the project's official event list plus the Forex Factory public weekly JSON (unofficial, labelled). Only high impact (three-star) events are shown, grouped Today, Tomorrow, Rest of this week, Next week, Later this month, in IST, with no filters. A moved time or changed forecast is kept as a revision.
- **News**: public RSS of Economic Times (India), BBC and CNBC (global): headline, short summary and link only, polled every 10 minutes.
- **Moneycontrol is not used.** With an honest client identity its pages answer 403 and the server's address is redirected to a login-consent page; bot protection is not worked around. Alternatives if wanted: the user exports or pastes the calendar from their own browser and we import it, or a licensed data API.
- **Not done**: GIFT Nifty (no source connected yet; needs a check of the Kotak instrument list or NSE IX); real delay of the futures to be measured when CME reopens.
Tests replay real captured Yahoo, RSS and Forex Factory payloads in `backend/tests/fixtures/global`; the Yahoo bars are trimmed to the reference session and a sample of the latest session.

**Addendum to D172 (16 Nov 2026): optional local Moneycontrol export.** If `economic_calendar.json` exists in the data directory it is merged into the calendar store (read at start and whenever it changes, checked every 5 minutes); if not, the Forex Factory and official sources run alone. Nothing in the program fetches Moneycontrol; the file is the user's own export. Layout: `{"date": "YYYY-MM-DD", "events": [{"time_ist", "country_name"/"country_code", "event", "impact" 1-3, "actual", "previous", "consensus"}]}`; "-" is missing. A release in the file replaces the same release (same country, within 5 minutes, shared keyword) from the other sources in the view. A broken file is reported on the page and ignored.

**Addendum to D172 (16 Nov 2026): fresher news.** News feeds are read every 2 minutes in Indian market hours (weekdays 09:00-15:45 IST) and every 10 minutes otherwise, with conditional requests (ETag / If-Modified-Since) so an unchanged feed costs one small response. Headlines carry topic chips (NIFTY, RBI, Fed, Crude, Gold, Rupee, Inflation, Earnings, FII) found by whole words in the title and summary; a "market-moving topics only" switch keeps those. The page re-asks every minute and marks headlines stored after the browser's last visit as New (kept in localStorage). Real feed lag (publisher time to our store time) is to be measured on the server.

**Addendum to D172 (16 Nov 2026): biquote / MQL5 calendar for India and US.** `https://biquote.io/api/calendar` is a free, keyless API that its own documentation (llms.txt, OpenAPI) offers to machine clients; its terms allow storing and displaying the data but not re-exposing the raw feed. Its `robots.txt` disallows `/api/` for search crawlers; this is a documented API used by an identified client (`NSEUniverse/1.0`), one request every 15 minutes. It is the MQL5 calendar as carried by a MetaTrader broker feed: third-party and unofficial, not an official source, and labelled "biquote / MQL5 (third-party feed)". Events go straight into the calendar store (not through `economic_calendar.json`, which stays for the user's own export). Request: India and US, importance medium and above (the API's importance is a minimum, so `high,medium` is rejected with 400), yesterday to 60 days ahead. Priority when several sources carry the same release: local export, then biquote, then the rest; the winner keeps the highest importance any source gave it (MQL5 rates the RBI decision medium, the official list high). Readings: null stays missing, percent gets %, thousands and millions K and M; a non-exact time is shown all-day. Cross-checks on 15 Nov: its RBI decision (4 Dec 10:00 IST) and Fed decision (9 Dec 19:00 UTC) agree with the official list; India CPI of 12 Nov carries an actual of 6.08%.

**Addendum to D172 (16 Nov 2026): India two-star releases are shown.** The calendar page lists three-star events plus India's two-star (medium) releases, drawn with two stars, because MQL5 rates most Indian releases (WPI, GDP) medium and the page would otherwise carry almost nothing for India. Medium events of other countries stay out.

**Addendum to D172 (16 Nov 2026): "Coming up" and readings on every event.** A sixth calendar section, Coming up, lists events from the end of the month to 45 days ahead (the next RBI and Fed decisions show weeks early). Every event shows Previous, Forecast and Actual in fixed boxes: a missing value shows a dash (or Pending for the actual of an event that has not happened), never a zero; a released actual is compared with the forecast as above, below or in line (a plain comparison, not good or bad).

**Addendum to D172 (16 Nov 2026): GIFT Nifty, clean-up, chart links, calendar filter.**
- **GIFT Nifty** is the near-month NIFTY index future at NSE IX. Kotak Neo's API has no NSE IX segment (its client knows nse_cm, bse_cm, nse_fo, bse_fo, cde_fo, mcx_fo; the app may show GIFT Nifty from another feed, which was not checked on the live account) and Yahoo has no ticker for it, so the quote comes from the NSE IX website's own data endpoint (`/api/derivatives-watch`), read every 60 s while it trades (06:30-15:40 and 16:35-02:45 IST, Monday to Friday) and every 5 minutes otherwise. It is an unofficial use of the exchange's website feed, labelled as such. Change is computed here from the contract's own previous close (the feed's change column agrees: 25266 - 25171.5 = 94.5). The contract rolls to the next expiry after expiry day; the card shows the expiry. Holidays are not known: on an NSE IX holiday it would show stale.
- **Clean-up (approved by the user on 16 Nov):** calendar events are deleted when their day (IST) has passed, with their revisions; news older than 48 hours is deleted; neither is stored again if a feed still carries it. It runs at start and every hour, and writes a `cleanup` run with the counts and the database size. Price bars (the global board's one-minute prices) are not deleted: they are the reference for the next session's change and the 24-hour charts. Retention for bars is open (see storage-plan).
- **View chart** on every global card opens the symbol's full chart at its original source in a new tab (Yahoo's chart page for Yahoo symbols, the NSE IX quote page for GIFT Nifty).
- **Calendar filter**: All, India or US (client side); the next-event card follows it.

**Addendum to D172 (16 Nov 2026): price-bar retention and robustness (approved by the user on 16 Nov).** One-minute price bars of the global board older than 7 days are deleted by the hourly clean-up (the latest quote of each symbol stays; 7 days covers a long weekend plus the previous session that each change is measured from). This replaces the earlier note that bars are kept with retention open. Each job (quotes, GIFT Nifty, news, calendar feeds, biquote, local file, clean-up) now runs in its own loop with its own timing: a slow or failing job never delays or resets the others, failures back off (2 minutes doubling to 15), a job loop that dies is restarted by a supervisor, and a damaged `global.sqlite3` is moved aside (kept) and recreated. After a large delete the file is compacted. `GET /api/global/health` reports each job as ok, stale (no success within its allowance: quotes and GIFT 20 min, news 40 min, biquote 45 min, calendar feeds 13 h, clean-up 3 h), failing (3 failures in a row) or starting, plus database size, row counts and retention; stale or failing jobs also appear in the server alert list (`/api/ops/alerts`).

**Fix (16 Nov 2026): change figure lost on cash indices.** A poll of today's bars alone (a cash index on a weekend, or a restart in mid-session) cannot see the previous session, so the stored quote lost its reference close and the card showed "No reference close". The stored reference of the same session is now kept. Comparison with Moneycontrol on 14 Nov: its Dow, S&P 500 and Nasdaq rows are CFDs derived from futures, so they differ from the cash index closes shown here by the futures basis; its GIFT Nifty row is also marked derived (CFD) and differs from the NSE IX last trade by a few points; reference close and day range agree with NSE IX.

**Addendum to D172 (16 Nov 2026): official closes for cash indices.** Checked against Yahoo's own chart data on 16 Nov: futures, rates, FX and commodities matched exactly, but the cash indices (S&P 500, Nasdaq, Dow, VIX, Nikkei, KOSPI, Shanghai) differed by 1-14 points because the last one-minute bar is taken before the closing auction, while Yahoo's page shows the official close (Moneycontrol's S&P 500 and Nasdaq rows equal those official closes). A closed cash index now shows its official daily close and the previous official close (Yahoo daily bars, re-read every 30 minutes); while it trades, the one-minute quote stands. Futures keep the 16:00 New York cash close as their reference (Yahoo's page measures a future from its settlement price, so a future's change can differ from Yahoo's page by design). Moneycontrol's Dow row (52,306.75) matches neither the cash close (52,347.55) nor our futures and is a CFD price, so its source cannot be matched.

**Addendum to D172 (16 Nov 2026): charts open in the page.** Yahoo's full-screen chart page (`/chart/...`) loaded only partly on a phone (a blank chart for a future on a weekend, a half-empty page). "View chart" now opens our own chart over the page (full screen on a phone): 1D, 3D or 7D of the stored one-minute prices (at most 720 points sent, the line breaks across closed-market gaps, the previous close drawn as a dashed line, times in IST), with an "Open at source" link; each card also has a "Source" link, now to Yahoo's quote page (`/quote/...`, which loads reliably and carries its own chart) or the NSE IX quote page for GIFT Nifty. The series endpoint accepts up to 168 hours (the bar retention).

**Addendum to D172 (16 Nov 2026): heads-up reminders and news.** A "Heads up" strip under the alert strip on every page (`GET /api/global/headsup`, rules in `app/globalmkts/headsup.py`). Events: only market-moving catalysts (India's releases, rate decisions, FOMC and the Fed chair, CPI, PCE, PPI, payrolls, unemployment, GDP, retail sales, ISM PMIs, trade balance; not every three-star item, ADP excluded). A reminder starts 3 days before, comes again at 2 days, then every 4 hours in the last day (buckets of 4 hours) with a final one in the last hour; each moment is a "phase" with its own key, so a reminder the user closed stays closed (kept 7 days in the browser) until the next phase begins, without timers. It disappears when the event starts. At most 8 reminders. News: a headline stored in the last 45 minutes, published in the last 3 hours and about a market-moving topic (NIFTY, RBI, Fed, crude, gold, rupee, inflation, earnings, FII), newest first, at most 3; closing a headline keeps it closed. Nothing is sent outside the app: a phone push would use the server's existing optional ntfy/Telegram setup in `/etc/nse-universe/notify.env` and is not built (it needs the user's topic or token).

**Addendum to D172 (16 Nov 2026): source delay is not "stale".** Measured on the server at 02:18 UTC on 16 Nov, Yahoo's data was behind real time by about 9-10 minutes for CME and ICE futures (S&P, Nasdaq, Dow, Russell, 10-year note, gold, silver, Brent, dollar index; Yahoo's own page says "CME - Delayed Quote"), 14 minutes for Nikkei, Hang Seng and Shanghai, 19 for KOSPI, and none for USD/INR; GIFT Nifty from NSE IX was under a minute. The 8-minute "live" limit therefore flagged every future as stale while the market was open. Each symbol now has its normal source delay (`delay_min`: 10 futures and dollar index, 15 Nikkei, Hang Seng and Shanghai, 20 KOSPI, 0 others) added to the 8-minute limit, and the card says "Live · 10 min delay". The delays of the US cash indices and VIX were not measured (markets closed) and are set to 0: if Yahoo delays them they will show stale in US hours and need a value.
USD/INR (`delay_min=180`): Yahoo writes a bar for it only when the price moves, and overnight the gaps reached 98 minutes and more, so it is called stale only after 3 hours without a bar; the card then says just "Live".

## D173 (16 Nov 2026): forward test of the seller favourable score (paper)
New Analyse page "Forward Test" and `app/forward/`: whenever the seller score of an expiry reaches 60, simulated short positions are opened and tracked with 11 exit rules, to see in which market regimes each works. Paper only: no order is sent (order placement stays out of scope). Rules approved by the user on 16 Nov 2026:
- **Trigger:** the seller score of the weekly expiry (cohort weekly) and of the monthly expiry (cohort monthly), read from the straddle engine's live analysis each minute. Every re-crossing counts: after a position is opened the trigger re-arms once the score has been below 60 for 30 minutes, at most 3 triggers a day per cohort. Entries 09:30-14:30 IST on weekdays, at the last traded prices of the newest stored sample (less than 5 minutes old); a minute without prices keeps the trigger armed.
- **Structures:** the at-the-money short straddle and the short strangle (call 50 points above, put 50 below the ATM strike), one lot (65), per trigger and cohort: up to 4 positions.
- **Exit rules on the same entry:** no stop, leg stops of 30, 40, 50, 60, 70 and 80% above each leg's entry price, a 40% leg stop that moves the other leg's stop to its entry price, and combined-premium stops of +15, +25 and +40%. All close at 15:20 IST the same day.
- **Mechanics:** a stop is checked against each stored sample and filled at that sample's price (no bid/ask, no slippage; a touch between samples is missed: on 16 Nov the 40% put stop the server's tick data caught at 10:34:38 showed first in the minute samples at 10:46). Results are net of estimated charges (brokerage 20 per order, STT 0.1% on premium sold, exchange 0.0356%, SEBI, stamp, GST). State is kept per position in `forward.sqlite3`, so a restart changes nothing; positions are settled from the stored samples at 15:20 (or at the next start if the app was down).
- **Context recorded for regime analysis:** regime label, seller and buyer scores, days to expiry, weekday, order-flow score, directional ratio, implied minus realised vol, IV, climate, and after the close the day's range, net move and whether it was a trend day. The page breaks every exit rule down by regime, time of entry, days to expiry, weekday, seller score band, order flow, vol gap, directional ratio and kind of day; a row with fewer than 20 positions is marked too few and nothing is called validated.
- **Limits:** intraday only (nothing about overnight holds, gaps or expiry-day gamma); last traded prices; about 20 to 50 positions a month at first, so regime cells need months. Data is kept (small: a few dozen rows a day); deleting it needs approval.

## D174 (16 Nov 2026): every leg event shows its date and time
The strategy builder showed times without dates ("10:34:38 Stop: ...") and had no time at all for the entry, the stop and target being set, or a trailing stop's move. A leg now keeps `enteredAt`, `stopSetAt` and `targetSetAt` (ISO times; the browser sets them when the entry premium is taken from the market or first entered, when a stop or target is typed or changed, and on a re-entry or roll; the server sets them when the trailing stop or "Move SL to cost" moves a stop and on a re-entry). Each trailing-stop move is written in the leg's log, which was not logged before. Log lines are stamped "16 Nov, 10:34:38" (IST) and shown from their own `at`, so older lines that carry only a time also show their date. Under each leg: Entered, Stop set or moved, Target set, Exited with price and reason, then the last three history lines (earlier ones folded). A leg saved before this change shows the strategy's start time as its entry, marked "(strategy last started)", not used when it is later than the leg's exit, or "time not recorded". The new fields are not part of the server/browser parity fixture (`strategy_parity.json` compares prices, exits and event counts, which did not change).

## D175 (16 Nov 2026): buyer and seller scores in the top bar
The straddle engine's recorded buyer and seller environment scores sit beside the NIFTY price as one small bordered chip, buyer above seller ("B 38" over "S 73"; a number of 60 or more is green, under 45 muted), linking to the straddle intelligence page. A tooltip gives the expiry, the recording time, each score's change over 15 minutes, the regime and that it is not a trade signal. It reads `GET /api/intelligence/straddle/scores` (the newest recorded row: no analysis, refreshed every 30 s) and shows nothing when no minute was recorded in the last 15 minutes (outside market hours) or the engine is off, so the bar is unchanged then. Checked in a browser at 320, 360, 411 and 1,200 px wide: nothing overflows.

**Addendum (16 Nov 2026): read marks on the News page.** Each headline has Mark read / Mark unread; opening it (click, middle click or Enter on the link) marks it read; read headlines are dimmed, lose their New badge and count out of "N unread of M"; "Mark all as read" and "Hide read" are on the page. Marks are kept in this browser only (`nse-universe.news-read`, headline id to time), dropped after 7 days (the server keeps headlines 48 hours). Opening a link from the browser's context menu ("open in new tab") is not seen by the page, so use the button for those. Not shared between devices.

**Addendum to D173 (16 Nov 2026): the Forward Test page is a portfolio.** The first version was a table of all exit rules with a heat map and was hard to read. The page now works like the Strategy portfolio: filters on top (structure: straddle, strangle or both; expiry: all, weekly or monthly; stop loss: no stop, 30 to 80%, 40% with the other leg to cost, combined +15, +25, +40%), and everything below follows the selection: portfolio statistics (trades, win and loss rate, net P&L, max and current drawdown, highest and lowest win, highest and lowest loss, longest win and loss streaks and the current one, average win and loss, profit factor, expectancy, recovery factor, stop-hit rate, average dip while open, best and worst day), the equity, per-trade and drawdown chart of the Strategy portfolio, the open positions marked for that rule, the closed trades (what was sold, exit reason and time, net and cumulative), the rules compared (click a row to select it) and the breakdown by regime and other conditions for the selected rule. `GET /api/forward/overview` takes `structure`, `cohort`, `rule` and `dim` and returns `portfolio` (all closed trades of the rule), `rules`, `breakdown`, `open`. Statistics reuse `portfolioStats`; trades are ordered by the time they were opened, so a straddle and a strangle opened together follow one another in the streaks.

**Addendum to D172 (16 Nov 2026): why the board differed from Investing.com at the US open.** At 20:12 IST on 16 Nov, Investing.com showed US Tech 100 at 27,428 (-1.85%) and our Nasdaq 100 futures card 27,666 (-1.33%). Two causes, both by design and now stated on the board's notes: (1) timing: Yahoo's futures are about 10 minutes behind (its own NQ=F data stopped at 14:32 UTC when it was 14:42) and the card was 13 minutes old at 14:29, at the open when the index fell about 130 points in 3 minutes; Investing's CFD is real time; (2) instrument: Investing's "US Tech 100" is a CFD on the cash index (previous close 27,943.7), ours is the Nasdaq 100 future (previous close 28,038.25 at 16:00 New York), the future trading about 60-95 points above the cash index, so their changes differ too. The board now also has the Nasdaq 100 cash index (^NDX, real time on Yahoo) to compare like with like; US 500 (7,494) and US 30 (52,234) on Investing correspond to our S&P 500 and Dow cash cards, not to the futures cards. VIX's delay was measured at about 15 minutes (set to 15).

**Addendum to D173 (17 Nov 2026): a "Today" panel.** The user could not tell whether the forward test was running while the seller score was above 60: the page showed only settled trades, and positions open until 15:20 were further down. The test was running (17 Nov: weekly positions opened 09:30 IST, the first minute of the entry window, monthly at 09:57 when its score reached 60). The page now opens with a Today panel (positions open now, and for each expiry the last seller score read, its trades today with their times, and in words what it waits for: a score of 60, the 30 minutes below 60 before the next trade, the entry window, or the cap of 3) and the Open now panel sits above the statistics. `GET /api/forward/overview` returns `today`.

**Addendum to D173 (17 Nov 2026): forward positions open in the Strategy builder.** Clicking an open position (Open now) or a closed trade (Closed trades) puts it into the Strategy builder as the strategy being worked on and opens the builder (the hand-over the Strategy portfolio uses, `nse.builder.working.v1`): two sold legs at the test's strikes and entry premiums, one lot, entered at the trigger time; the stop-loss rule selected on the page becomes the legs' stops (30 to 80% above each entry premium, tick-rounded), "Move SL to cost" for the 40% + cost rule, or a strategy stop loss in rupees for a combined rule (percent of the premium times the lot); a closed trade (or a leg a stop has already closed) carries its exit prices, exit times and booked P&L, the leg a stop caught marked as a stop. An open position's payload now includes the exit prices of closed legs. Positions of past days open with their (expired) expiry.

**Fix (17 Nov 2026): a forward position opens in the Strategy builder as a temporary view.** The first hand-over wrote the position as the builder's working copy, but on load the builder takes the server's running strategy (the user's own) over the browser copy, so the builder showed the user's saved strategy and not the position. The position is now handed over in `sessionStorage` (`nse.builder.temp.v1`, this tab only) and opened as `/strategy?temp=forward`. In that mode the builder takes its legs, stops, rules and times from the document, does not read the server's running strategy, sends nothing to it, does not touch the working copy kept in this browser, does not run the automatic exits, skips the "expired expiry" reset (a past trade keeps its expiry) and shows a banner "Temporary view of <name>: not saved, the server is not watching it, your own strategy is exactly as you left it. Use Save to keep a copy", with "Back to my strategy" (clears the document and reloads the builder as it was). Edits in the temporary view are not saved anywhere unless the user uses Save.

## D176 (17 Nov 2026): a second forward test, with Move SL to cost
The forward test only tried plain stop losses. A separate page and test, "Forward Test · SL to cost" (`family=cost`), runs on the same entries: leg stops of 30, 40, 50, 60, 70 and 80% above each entry premium, and when one leg's stop fires the other leg's stop moves to its own entry premium. The rule follows the Strategy builder's: only a leg that is in profit at that moment (below its entry price, being sold) gets its stop moved, no price at that moment means no move, and a leg that is not in profit keeps its original stop. The moved stop closes the leg when its price comes back to the entry ("stop at cost"). The two tests are separate pages with their own filters, statistics and portfolios; the cost page also puts each rule beside the plain stop of the same percentage on the same trades ("same stop, no move" and "gain from SL to cost" = the difference). The plain page no longer lists the old single "40% + cost" chip (it is now one of the six on the cost page). Positions already stored (open and settled) get the new rules by replaying the stored prices from their entry (`COST_RULE_VERSION`, once per version, also recomputing the earlier 40% version under the builder's rule); each leg's own exit time is kept so a position opens in the builder with the right times. `GET /api/forward/overview` takes `family=plain|cost`.

**Fix (17 Nov 2026): a strategy the server closed at 15:20 was filed under Closed at 15:56 "at stale prices".** The server's intraday close did fire at 15:20:00 IST (the put closed at 0.60, +Rs 3,286; the call had been stopped at 10:17, -Rs 3,978; net -Rs 692), but the Strategy builder files a finished strategy under Closed when the page next loads, and it stamped that with the time of filing (15:56) and the stored chain's staleness, so the list read "closed 15:56, at stale prices". A strategy whose legs are all already out is now closed at its last leg's exit time, with the exit time as its snapshot time and not marked stale (no price is taken when filing it). The closed record already filed for 17 Nov keeps its old time and flag.

## D177 (17 Nov 2026): days-to-expiry filter on both forward pages
Both forward pages (plain and SL to cost) have a "Days to expiry (trading days)" filter: All, 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5+ DTE, each chip showing how many positions it holds. DTE is counted in trading days from the entry session to the expiry (sessions after the entry day up to and including the expiry, holidays from `TradingCalendar`; weekdays only outside its coverage), so a Tuesday weekly reads Wed 4, Thu 3, Fri 2, Mon 1, Tue 0; monthly entries are mostly 5+. Stored `context["dte"]` is calendar days, so the value is derived from `session` and `expiry` and works on old trades too. `GET /api/forward/overview?dte=all|0..4|5plus` filters the statistics, equity, closed trades, rule comparison, breakdown and open positions together; the breakdown's "days to expiry" dimension now uses the same trading-day buckets. Chip counts follow the structure and expiry filters, not the DTE one. Tests: `test_trading_days_to_expiry_and_the_filter`, overview/API cases, `forward-cost.test.tsx`.

## D178 (17 Nov 2026): the straddle decay premium series runs to the last sample on expiry day
The Black-76 model (forward, implied vol, Greeks, attribution) refuses samples closer than 15 minutes to the 15:40 session close, so on expiry day every series went empty from 15:25 and "Now" showed the 15:24 price as if it were current, while the option chain showed the settlement (25300 call 87.25 against 133.9 at 15:24). The premium series, the spot line, the ATM strike and the tracker now use the stored last traded prices of those later samples; implied vol, Greeks, attribution and the "implied move" reading still use model points only and stay empty there (never filled). The Now card on Today's premium shows the time of the sample it reads. Not fixed: the index quote itself can stall (it repeated 25,413 from about 15:15 to the close while the options priced about 25,434), so the NIFTY line and the ATM strike derived from it can lag; that is a feed issue and stays flagged by the existing stall notice.

## D179 (17 Nov 2026): index feed glitches no longer draw wicks; raw numbers removed from interpretation text
On expiry day the Kotak index quote froze at 25,413 from 15:15 to 15:27 (option put-call parity put the index near 25,455 at 15:20, futures confirmed), then sent single reads of 25,284 and 25,580 inside one minute, which drew a 300-point wick on the Smart OI chart (the one-minute bar builder took every read). `IndexBarBuilder` now holds a read more than 0.15% from the last accepted level until the next read confirms it; an unconfirmed read is dropped and counted in the bar's `x`. Nothing is invented. Also fixed: `PCR 0.759547016959292` and `flip 25395.945629000897` printed raw in the intelligence summary and alerts. Not changed: the stored 15:20 bar of 17 Nov (editing stored data needs an explicit request); the stalled index still feeds the chain's stored spot and the ATM strike during a freeze (a cross-check against futures is open); unmapped swing stocks are labelled with the default sector CNXINFRA (235 of 467 backtest trades), a strategy decision for the owner.

## D180 (17 Nov 2026): index bars are cross-checked against the future; the straddle ATM strike follows the options when the index freezes; page smoke audit
- **Bars vs the future:** `/api/market/index-bars` adds `off` (points) to a one-minute bar whose close, or whose high or low, is far from where the NIFTY future (a separate feed) says the index was, measured against the basis of the neighbouring minutes (median of ±15 minutes; 40 points for the close, 80 for a high or low; the opening minutes are skipped). The Smart OI page leaves such bars out of the price candles and says which. Nothing is repaired or deleted: the stored 15:20 bar of 17 Nov (open 25,284.35, high 25,580.2 against a close of 25,450.85) stays in the database and is simply not drawn, along with the 15:21, 15:22, 15:25 and 15:26 bars whose closes or ranges the future and put-call parity contradict (checked on the captured data: 15:20, 15:21, 15:22, 15:25, 15:26 flagged, the other 362 bars not). This replaces editing the stored row.
- **Frozen index and the straddle:** the at-the-money strike of the straddle decay engine uses the level the option prices imply (parity forward discounted by the assumed rate) when the stored index level is more than 30 points from it (`atm_reference`). The stored spot series itself is shown as stored.
- **Not covered:** the option chain table's ATM label (it uses the live stream quote) and the strike window of the sampler still follow the index quote.
- **Smoke audit:** `src/test/audit-smoke.test.tsx` (skipped unless `AUDIT_DIR` holds captured server payloads) renders all 58 pages and opens every tab and toggle against them. On the captured 17 Nov payloads all pages render; on the payload from before the D178 fix it reproduces the straddle crash and passes after the fix.

## D181 (17 Nov 2026): a frozen index quote is replaced at the source; number audit
- **Why it was left out of D180:** it was scope, not a technical limit. The ATM strike of stored observations, the strike window and the chain table label all start from the broker's index level or its ATM label in the snapshot, so a fix at one place would leave the others wrong. It is now fixed where the snapshot is made.
- **Change:** `SnapshotService._checked` (`app/domain/spot_check.py`) compares the broker's index level with the level the chain's own put-call parity implies (median of the 5 strikes nearest the quote, discounted by e^(-rT); expiries up to 8 days away only). More than 30 points apart: `underlying_ltp` becomes the implied level, the broker's value is kept in `underlying_ltp_broker`, `spot_note` explains, and the broker's `ATM` label is ignored for that snapshot (stored observations in `services/history.py`, the strike window in `services/universe.py`). The raw payload on disk is unchanged. The Option Chain page marks ATM at the strike nearest the implied level and shows the note. Setting `chain_spot_check` (default on; off in the test fixtures that replay captures at another wall-clock time).
- **Verified numbers (captured payloads of 17 Nov 2026, independent Python recomputation):** chain OI totals, OI change = OI - previous-day OI for all 162 contracts, put-call parity per strike, observations (straddle = call + put, ATM nearest the index, order, gaps), `/oi/change` totals against the stored observation, `/history/oi-series` totals, PCR and spot at all 168 matching times, futures basis and fair basis, max pain (25,400 on 81 and on 21 strikes), IV/HV/RV on 750 days (recomputed 15.71 and 14.08), swing report (467 trades: wins, win rate, profit factor, win/loss ratio, each trade's return and R). No discrepancy except those listed in D179 and D180.
- **Still limited:** deep in-the-money contracts with no trades show an LTP of 0.0 or an old print on the chain (shown as sent by the broker). Not recomputed independently: Greeks and GEX (Black-76 model values), IV surface, the sentiment and straddle scores (weights are provisional), Yahoo-sourced global data.

## D182 (17 Nov 2026): model values and scores verified independently; global reference closes fixed
Independent re-computation on the captured server payloads of 17 Nov 2026 (scripts kept out of the repository: a second Black-76 pricer built by numerical integration of the lognormal forward, bisection, finite differences and the Breeden-Litzenberger identity; nothing imported from the app):
- **Implied vol:** 80 fixed-strike points through the session (09:15 to 15:19) matched the app within 0.0033 vol points (T to the 15:40 close confirmed, forward = parity median of the five nearest strikes, r = 6%).
- **Greeks:** the near-expiry straddle gamma 0.012222 and theta -328.37 a day equal the closed-form values (0.012222 and -328.35) at 15:24:35; the finite-difference theta over one minute differs by 1.7% because T was 15 minutes.
- **GEX and gamma flip:** at 12:24:41 and 14:49:01 IST the net GEX equals the app's to 0.003% and 0.001% (crore per 1%: -48,064 and +4,629), the absolute GEX to 0.001%, and the gamma flip to 0.015 points (25,520.26 and 25,427.22). The `ce` and `pe` totals count every strike with an exposure while net and absolute count only strikes with both sides, as documented (so they differ by 0.4 to 0.5% from a both-sided sum).
- **ATM IV and 25-delta skew** at 12:00, 12:24 and 14:49 IST: ATM IV is the mean of the call and put IV (23.224, 23.908, 31.069 match); the skew (1.305, 1.455, 0.326) matches to three decimals.
- **Realised vol:** the 5, 15, 30, 60 minute and since-open windows (0.00, 8.15, 6.79, 7.65, 8.22) match exactly, 375 x 252 trading minutes.
- **Scores:** the sentiment fusion (33.8) reconciles to the decimal from the five engine scores, the family weights and the coverage rule (engine scores, fractions, coverage 0.767, agreement 1.0, freshness 0.261, confidence 25.0); the term "OI added: puts minus calls" = 100 tanh(0.08887 / 0.05) = 94.4. The seller (75.6) and buyer (36.4) straddle scores reconcile as 50 + 50 x sum(weight x value) / sum(available weight); coverage 0.92 and 0.89; the break-even move 231.8 = sqrt(2 x 328.37 / 0.012222). What the arithmetic cannot show: whether the provisional weights and the tanh scales are right (they are still uncalibrated).
- **Global data (Yahoo):** change = price - prev_close and change % match on all 20 rows. Found and fixed: the **reference close drifted during the day**. A cash index used the last stored one-minute bar of the previous session, so the S&P 500 reference went from 7420.19 (the 20:59 UTC bar) to 7414.21 a few polls later, and Yahoo's own previous close is 7416.59 (Nasdaq 100 and Dow the same: 27,204.77 / 27,186.11 against 27,197.15). Now a cash index trading keeps its live one-minute price, but its change is measured from the previous official daily close; and for the futures a stored reference whose bar is later (nearer the 16:00 New York close) is kept instead of an earlier bar found by a later poll. A future's reference stays the 16:00 New York cash-close bar (7441.50 on 16 Nov) by design, not Yahoo's settlement (7439.00).
- **Not a defect, noted:** Yahoo revises its recent one-minute bars afterwards (the 16:41 UTC S&P bar read 7321.08 when polled and 7317.79 an hour later), so a stored price can differ from Yahoo's chart by a few points for the same minute.

## D183 (18 Nov 2026): a closed strategy in the Strategy builder shows a booked result
Opening a closed trade whose every leg had been closed (the 17 Nov straddle) showed statistics of a live position: "Max profit -₹692" in green, "Reward : Risk -1.00 : 1", "POP 0%", "Breakevens none", the stale-price warning on a result made of booked exits, and on the chart the current-price tag printed over the SD labels (on expiry day the ±1 SD lines are a few points apart). Now, when every enabled leg has exited, the first tile reads "Realised P&L (all legs closed)", Max profit, Max loss, Reward : Risk, POP and Breakevens show a dash (or N/A) because there is no range of outcomes left; Max profit/Max loss take their colour from their sign (a negative "max profit" is red); Reward : Risk is N/A unless the best outcome is a profit; the "stale stored prices" note appears only when some leg has no exit price; the SD labels are not drawn when the ±1 SD band is under 5% of the visible range (the lines stay, the tooltip still says where the level is). Tests: `strategy-page.test.tsx` (closed trade opened as a booked result), `chartOptions.test.ts` (crowded SD labels).

## D184 (18 Nov 2026): economic events keep their outcome for two days
The calendar kept only today and later, and asked biquote only from today, so an event's outcome was never visible the next day and a late actual after midnight was not refreshed. Now: events stay for two days after their date (IST) with their actual, forecast and previous, then `cleanup` (hourly) deletes them with their revisions (`EVENT_KEEP_D = 2`); older events are not stored again. The biquote request starts two days back, so the last two days keep being refreshed. `/api/calendar` has a new last section "Past 2 days: outcomes" (newest first; today's events stay in Today). While an event due in the last six hours has no actual, the biquote calendar is read every 2 minutes instead of every 15 (`awaiting_actual`), so the outcome appears within about two minutes of the release. Everything is automatic. Missing stays missing: a past event without an actual says so. Tests: `test_the_last_two_days_keep_their_actual_outcomes_and_older_events_go` and the updated retention tests, `global-pages.test.tsx`.

## D185 (18 Nov 2026): a new minute reaches the page within about 10 seconds
The Smart OI page was about 50 s behind: the sampler counted 60 s from the end of each capture (so its phase was arbitrary, 09:36:29 for the 09:36 sample, and it drifted and sometimes skipped a minute), and the pages polled every 15 or 30 s out of step with it, so a sample could wait 30 s or more after being stored. Now a one-minute sampler fires 3 s into every wall-clock minute (a failed sample keeps its back-off), its loop checks every second instead of every 5, the futures minute sample is taken within 5 s of the minute (was 20 s), and the pages that read these once-a-minute series (the chain and history on the Analysis page, premium decay, OI series, Smart OI, OI change, heatmap, index bars, futures) refetch at 8 s past each minute (`minuteAligned`, `src/lib/refresh.ts`) instead of every 15 or 30 s. A sample is stored 3-6 s into the minute (Kotak's chain call takes 1-3 s) and the page shows it by about 8-10 s, and one fetch a minute replaces two. Tests: `test_sampler.py` (wall-clock alignment), `refresh.test.ts`.

## D186 (18 Nov 2026): Update on every strategy in the builder
The Update button (Positions header, next to Exit all) existed only for a strategy loaded from the Saved list, so a single-leg strategy that was running or built from the chain (never saved) had no way to commit an edited stop. It is now there for every strategy with legs (not in a temporary view or an opened closed trade): a saved strategy keeps the old behaviour (disabled until it differs from its saved copy, a dot marks unsaved changes, it overwrites the same entry); a strategy that is not saved shows the same button with a dot, and one click saves it as shown (under the name typed in the name box, or a dated name) so that later edits are saved with Update. Same for one leg or ten. Test: `strategy-page.test.tsx` "Update for every strategy".

## D187 (18 Nov 2026): Exit all closes on the first click and files one record
Reported 18 Nov: the first Exit all on a running one-leg strategy filed a closed copy but the live strategy stayed, the second click closed it and filed a second copy ("Short based on smart OI - 181/2026", +₹1,190 at 09:50 and +₹1,261 at 09:51). Cause: the page's clear-the-strategy write to `PUT /api/strategies/running` carries the revision it last saw, and the server refused it (409) whenever the revision had moved at all. The revision moves on every write from any open page (the running document had reached rev 460 with no rules and no rule state), so with two pages or devices open the refusal was frequent; on a 409 the page adopts the server's copy, which still had the open leg, so the strategy came back. Fix: the document keeps `legsRev`, the revision of the last change to the legs (a stop, target, hold close, re-entry, or a write that changed them); a write is refused only when `legsRev` is newer than the writer's revision, so a rule-state change or another page's identical write no longer refuses it, while an exit the server made still does (an exit cannot be undone). And a second Exit all on the same position (same sides, strikes, expiries and entry premiums) within 15 minutes replaces the first closed record instead of adding a copy. Tests: `test_a_rule_state_change_on_the_server_is_not_a_conflict_but_a_leg_change_is`, `strategy-page.test.tsx` "Exit all on the same position twice files one closed record". Not changed: the two existing duplicate records stay in Closed until deleted (the one at 09:50 is the earlier copy of the same trade); deleting stored records is the owner's call.

## D188 (18 Nov 2026): closed strategies live on the Strategy portfolio page
On a phone the Strategy builder's Closed pop-up list ran off the right edge of the screen, so the delete cross could not be reached. The list is moved to the Strategy portfolio page: each closed trade has visible Open and Delete buttons under its name (tall enough to tap, inside the first column so a horizontally scrolled table cannot hide them), and a two-step "Delete all closed". Delete removes the record from the server and from this browser's copy (otherwise the builder's start-up sync would upload the browser copy again) and reads the list again. Open hands the id to the builder (`nse.builder.open-closed.v1` in session storage, read once) which opens it as a closed trade. The builder keeps a "Closed (n)" link to `/portfolio#closed` in place of the pop-up. Tests: `strategy-adjust.test.tsx` "Strategy portfolio: closed strategies", `strategy-page.test.tsx`.

## D189 (18 Nov 2026): a capture with holes is asked for again; a failed sample is retried after 10 s
Gaps in the Smart OI per-candle bars and dips in the OI totals traced to two causes on the capture side. (1) A snapshot in which the broker returned a near-ATM contract without its open interest (10:25 and 10:27 IST on 18 Nov: call OI 73.5 M, 65.3 M, 73.9 M in three consecutive minutes; 20 and 19 strikes used instead of 21) is stored as it came, so every total dipped and the candle's volume change went negative, which the chart leaves blank. Now `_capture_one` checks the contracts within 10 strikes of the index (`app/domain/chain_quality.py`: no OI, volume or last price) and asks the broker again up to 2 times, 1.5 s apart, keeping the most complete answer; nothing is filled in, and a chain that stays incomplete is stored as it is with a log line. (2) After a failed sample the sampler waited double the interval (120 s), a whole missing minute; it now retries after 10 s, then 20, 40, ... up to 5 minutes. The server-side collectors (sampler, stream, futures minute samples, index bars) run without any page open; the pages only read. Stored samples with a hole (10:25, 10:27) are left as they are. Tests: `test_sampler.py` (retry of a chain missing OI, quick retry after a failure).

## D190 (18 Nov 2026): the surprise of a release against its forecast
Requested: BEAT / MISS / IN-LINE badges for economic events, an Actual / Consensus / Previous layout and filter tabs. The feed itself already existed (biquote for India and the US, the SQLite events store, two days of outcomes, 2-minute polling after a release, D184), so the requested standalone service writing `economic_calendar.json` was not added: a second store of the same events would drift from the first. The surprise is computed in `calendar_feed.surprise` and served as `outcome` on each event of `/api/calendar`. Readings are compared as numbers in their unit (a % against a count is not compared; K, M, B, T are read). BEAT or MISS where higher is better; HOT or COOLING for inflation (CPI, WPI, PPI, PCE: higher than forecast is hot, red, not a beat); unemployment and claims are reversed (higher is a miss); releases that are neither good nor bad (rate decisions, minutes, speeches) show only ABOVE or BELOW with no colour. No forecast or no actual: no badge (Reported, or Pending). The badge shows the difference in the forecast's own units (+0.3%, -20K, -0.4M); the actual is green or red by the outcome. The page has "All events / Reported outcomes / Upcoming" tabs next to the existing All / India / US buttons. A plain comparison of two numbers, not a statement of what the release does to the market. Tests: `test_the_surprise_compares_actual_with_forecast_and_knows_which_way_is_good`, `global-pages.test.tsx`.

## D191 (18 Nov 2026): actual values for the other countries' releases
The UK CPI y/y (12:30 IST) showed "Time passed, no actual value in the feed". The row came only from the Forex Factory public feed, which carries no actual values, and the biquote calendar (which does) was asked for India and the US only. Now a second biquote request covers the UK, Euro area, Japan, China, Australia, Canada, Switzerland and New Zealand at high importance (30 days ahead, two back), run in the same poll (every 15 minutes, every 2 minutes while a due release has no actual); a failure of that request is recorded and does not hide the India and US result. The two sources name one release differently ("CPI y/y" and "Inflation Rate YoY"), so "inflation" now counts as the same word as "cpi" when matching, and biquote's row replaces the feed's. Verified on the real biquote response of 18 Nov (UK Inflation Rate YoY: actual 3.6, forecast 3.7, previous 3.5), kept as the fixture `biquote_world_18nov2026.json`. Not done: a country or a release that biquote does not list stays without an actual (no number is invented).

## D192 (18 Nov 2026): the closing session has no index; the chart says so and uses the future
The gap in the NIFTY candles every day at 15:15–15:30 is not random. The exchange feed switches the cash segment at exactly 15:15:00 IST (`market_status` nse_cm "other", code 8; then 10 at 15:20 and 11 at 15:25; "closed" at 15:30:00), and from that moment the index quote stops moving (14 identical reads on 18 Nov, 15:15–15:28), then comes back with wild reads (a 15:20 bar from 25,284 to 25,580 against a close of 25,451 and a future near 25,215) and settles at the closing value at 15:29. Seen the same way on 11, 17 and 18 Nov. Where it goes silent is NOT established: the first version of this note said the exchange does not publish the index then, but the owner sees the live price moving at 15:15, so the cause may be the broker's forwarding or our own handling (`LiveBook.update` drops a tick identical to the stored one without refreshing its receive time, so a repeated value looks like silence to the bar builder). What the stored data does show: no index tick reached the server 15:15–15:19 (the minute samples fell back to the stored snapshot), ticks resumed at 15:20 with values up to 80 points from the future-implied level, and the stored chain's own index level stayed at 25,220.45 until 15:28. D193 adds a per-minute account of the index ticks (count, distinct values, first and last) between 15:10 and 15:35 IST to settle it. No index price is invented meanwhile. What changed: (1) for the minutes of 15:15–15:30 in which the index bar is missing, silent (a few reads) or contradicted by the future (a tighter allowance there: 25 points for the close, 40 for a high or low), `/api/market/index-bars` adds stand-in bars (`proxy: true`) from the NIFTY future minus the basis it had in the 15 minutes before 15:15 (median of future minus index over the trusted bars); (2) the Smart OI chart draws those as grey outlined candles, never as index candles, and a separate "Closing session (15:15–15:30)" note replaces the "feed problem" wording for them; (3) the constant at 15:29 and after is the closing value and is no longer reported as a stall. The basis is an estimate (a few points on 18 Nov: the stand-in 15:29 read 25,222.5 against the real close 25,227.95). Minutes with neither an index bar nor a future price stay empty. Tests: `test_the_closing_session_gets_stand_in_bars_from_the_future_minus_its_basis`, `option-tools.test.ts`.

## D193 (18 Nov 2026): where the index goes silent at 15:15
`app/stream/index_watch.py`: from 15:10 to 15:35 IST the server logs once a minute (`index_ticks_minute`: ticks received, repeats included, distinct values, first and last value) for the NIFTY index key. Read tomorrow after 15:35 with `journalctl -u nse-universe | grep index_ticks_minute`: ticks every second with changing values mean the feed is fine and the loss is downstream (our handling); ticks with one repeated value mean the broker forwards a frozen value; no ticks mean nothing reaches the server. Diagnostic only, no behaviour change.

## D194 (18 Nov 2026): the broker's index level freezes while the options keep trading
Owner's challenge: OI, volume, PE-CE and the other OI charts kept updating through 15:15–15:30, so the feed is not down. Checked on the stored one-minute observations of 18 Nov: from 15:15:04 to 15:28:04 call and put OI, volumes and the ATM option prices change every minute, but the index level in the same broker response (`spot.ltp` of the option chain) stays at 25,220.45 and the ATM strike stays 25,200, while the option prices themselves imply about 25,243 (25,200 + 188.1 - 144.2); at 15:29:04 the level jumps to the close, 25,227.95. So the fault is confined to the index quote (the REST chain's index field, and no index tick on our server 15:15-15:19), not the option data, which is why every option-based chart is complete and only the NIFTY candles had a gap. Two changes: (1) the parity check on the chain's index level (D181, D178) now replaces it when it is more than 15 points from the option-implied level (was 30; the 18 Nov gap was 20-27 points, below the old limit, so the ATM strike stayed on 25,200); (2) `LiveBook.update` now moves the stored tick's receive time on an exact repeat (a repeat was dropped without that, so an index whose value did not change looked silent to the bar builder and the minute samples). Whether the stream's index ticks arrive during 15:15-15:19 is still settled by the D193 log tomorrow. Tests: `test_spot_check.py`, `test_straddle_decay.py`, `test_stream_book.py`.

**Correction to D194 and D181 (18 Nov 2026, evening).** The claim that the option prices implied "about 25,243" at 15:15 forgot the carry: the parity forward of the 23 Nov expiry sits about 21 points above the index (5 days at 6%), so the implied index level was about 25,222, within 2 to 5 points of the frozen 25,220.45. The broker's frozen level was therefore NOT far from the true level, the ATM strike 25,200 was right, and the "20 to 27 points" that justified lowering the tolerance from 30 to 15 points did not exist. The tolerance is back at 30 points (chain level, straddle ATM). What stays true: the level did not move for 14 minutes (not possible for a live index), the one-minute bars had no reads 15:15-15:19, and the bars of 15:20 and 15:21 were wild (25,327 and 25,284 against a future-implied level near 25,222). The repeated-tick receive-time fix and the D193 tick log are unaffected. `scripts/day_check.py` now discounts the forward.

## D195 (18 Nov 2026): managed-trade messages in the heads-up strip
Owner's request: the Heads up strip should also tell what happens to the trades the app manages. `GET /api/headsup/trades` (`app/headsup_trades.py`, pure functions) turns what the server already stores into messages: the running strategy's `serverEvents` (stop, target, trailing stop, stop to cost, strategy stop/target, profit lock, auto close) and the swing book (entry triggered, waiting for volume, entry signal, not chased, position entered, Day-1 exits, stop, stop raised, 1.8R/2.5R levels reached). Only today's events of the last 6 hours, newest first, at most 12; each has a stable key, so closing the strip hides it for good and a new event shows again. Nothing is computed or guessed: the text is the server's own. Tests use the real 17 Nov events (`tests/fixtures/headsup/trades_17nov2026.json`). Limits: the swing minute messages exist only while the shadow engine runs; "stop in force reached" and level messages follow the live book and are shown while the state holds (6 h window not applied to them beyond the key).

## D196 (18 Nov 2026): today's high and low under each strategy leg's LTP
The Strategy builder's leg table shows "H x · L y" under the LTP: today's high and low of that contract, from the same stored quote as the LTP (each leg in its own expiry's chain). A value the quote does not carry shows a dash (never zero). It is the day's range, not the range since the leg was taken; a since-entry range would need the stored minute history of each contract and is not built.

## D197 (18 Nov 2026): FII / DII cash page as cards on a phone
On screens under 640 px the cash table (seven columns, numbers running into each other) is replaced by one card per session: date and total net in the header, then FII net, DII net, FPI equity (NSDL), FII buy and FII sell in a three-column grid. Same figures and tones as the table (the table stays from 640 px up); a missing value is a dash and a total needs both FII and DII.

## D198 (19 Nov 2026): phone top bar, NIFTY label above the price
On a phone the label "NIFTY" sat in front of the price and pushed the figures wide. It now stands above the price in a small column (label, level, change), left-aligned, with a slightly larger level; from 520 px up the layout is unchanged. Pushed during market hours at the owner's explicit instruction (frontend only; the deploy restart costs about a minute of samples).

## D199 (19 Nov 2026): the top-bar scores are the live calculation
The top-bar chip (B/S) showed the last RECORDED minute while the Market Intelligence card showed the live calculation, so they differed (09:19 IST: chip 56/43, card 53/45, and the live read 59/43 a minute later). `latest_scores` now returns the live calculation on the newest stored sample, the same 20 s analysis the page uses (`source: "live"`), and falls back to the newest recorded minute (`source: "recorded"`) only when no live calculation exists; chip and card poll every 15 s. Limit: the engine reads stored samples (one per minute, about 3 s into the minute), so the scores move when a sample is stored, not on every index tick.

`fmtTimeIST` already ends in " IST"; six places added it again ("Now (10:03:04 IST IST)" on the straddle page, the India VIX time, the futures, index, IV and snapshot lines). The duplicate is removed; the formatter is the one place that adds the zone.

## D201 (19 Nov 2026): outcomes of releases MQL5 rates medium; text releases named as such
Australia's Employment Change and Unemployment Rate (19 Nov, 06:00 IST) stayed without an actual: Forex Factory (high) has no actuals, and biquote rates them medium, below the high-only request for the other countries. The world request is now at medium importance, and `keep_matched_medium` keeps a medium row only when it is the single best match (shared name words, then time) of a high row another source stores, so "Full-Time Employment Change" and "Participation Rate" do not appear as new releases. Checked on the real biquote answer for 19 Nov (Employment Change −43.5K vs forecast 19, Unemployment Rate 4.9% vs 4.8%, MISS). FOMC Minutes, speeches, meetings and statements never carry a figure: the row says "a statement, not a figure" instead of "no actual value in the feed". Limit: the medium row is taken only after Forex Factory's high row is stored (the next biquote poll, at most 15 minutes), and a release that neither source lists at high stays unseen.

## D202 (19 Nov 2026): India ADRs on the Global markets page
A new group "India ADRs and ETF (US-listed)": HDFC Bank (HDB), ICICI Bank (IBN), Infosys (INFY), Wipro (WIT), Dr. Reddy's (RDY), MakeMyTrip (MMYT) and the iShares MSCI India ETF (INDA), from the same Yahoo one-minute feed and the same machinery as the US cash indices (official close once the market is closed, change against the previous official close, stale and closed flags from the bar age). Tickers were checked on real data of 18 Nov 2026; Tata Motors, WNS and Yatra no longer exist on Yahoo and were left out, SIFY trades too thinly (149 bars in a session) and EPI is a duplicate of INDA. Dollars, not rupees: an ADR is not the NSE price (the ADR ratio and the rupee move it), and no NSE-implied premium is computed, so nothing is claimed beyond the dollar change. Updates: every minute during the NSE window and now also during the US session (09:30-16:00 New York, 20:00-02:30 IST in winter), every five minutes otherwise.
