## D203 (19 Nov 2026): straddle environment reads the premium path, not only the decay ratio
Cause (found on the server at 13:43 IST): the seller score showed 69 while the fixed 25150 straddle was +49.95 points (+16.1%) above its 309.35 open. The decay-efficiency component divides by expected theta; with four days to expiry that is about 1.4 points an hour, under the 2-point floor, so it was "low signal" and dropped out, and nothing else in the score looked at what the premium did. Change: two new components from the stored cumulative series, the fixed-strike and the roll-adjusted premium change as a percent of the opening premium (60% since open, 40% last 60 minutes; seller weight 14 and 8, theta weight 20 to 8; buyer gets the fixed path at weight 8); a stop-zone flag `premium_up` at +15% of the open (class capped at Neutral) and +30% (capped at Unfavourable); the class label is the median of this minute and the two recorded minutes before it (the number stays raw). The same-day 19 Nov real server read is the fixture (`straddle_19nov2026_1343.json`): fixed +16.15%, rolling +2.09% since open, hand-calculated values -0.884 and -0.062. All scale, zone and weight values are provisional, not validated. Existing flat-market test threshold moved from 75 to 70 because the premium paths (-2.1% in that synthetic session) add a modest +0.18 each. Not done: a per-component `window` field (the windows are in each component's text) and days-to-expiry weighting.

## D204 (19 Nov 2026): decay is read on the at-the-money straddle; the opening strike is a reference; a managed straddle is added (corrects D203)
Cause: at 13:57 NIFTY was 235 points above the 25150 strike held since the open, so that straddle's +43 points was mostly delta (net delta about +0.7), not decay, and its theta was no longer an at-the-money theta. D203 had wrongly put that fixed path into the seller score (weight 14) and its stop-zone flag; both now read the roll-adjusted at-the-money straddle (`prem_rolling`, weight 14, flag `premium_up` at +15% / +30% of its opening premium, same-contract steps only). The fixed strike stays on the page as "Reference only" with its distance, share of the premium and net delta once it is 100 points from NIFTY (`reference_status`). The question answers ("Is the straddle decaying normally?", IV, gamma) now use the rolling ATM. New managed straddle (`managed_held`, `managed_book`): the opening straddle is closed and re-struck to the nearest strike whenever NIFTY is 100 points from the held one, each re-strike booking entry minus exit from the stored last traded prices (before costs and slippage); shown as a card and as a line in the premium-change chart (premium-change sign, above zero = seller behind). The page now follows the rolling ATM first. Tests: reference status and managed book hand-calculated (sold 300, closed 330 = -30; new pair sold 280, now 260 = +20; total -10 = -650 rupees per lot of 65), an end-to-end trending session, the 19 Nov real server read for the premium path (+2.09% rolling, 6.45 points over a 309.35 open). 100 points, 15%/30% zones, the 60/40 blend and the weights are provisional, not validated. Not done: a replay of a stored real session through `managed_book` (only synthetic chains and the real premium path are tested), managed-straddle Greeks attribution, and weighting by days to expiry.

## D205 (19 Nov 2026): the 15:15 silence is NSE's closing auction; a moving index quote is never replaced (corrects D181, D192, D193, D194)
Cause, from the live watch of 15:10-15:33 IST and the stored data of 19 Nov: the whole CASH segment pauses at 15:15, not only the index. The exchange feed's own status messages for `nse_cm` were code 8 at 15:15:00 ("continuous trading closed, closing auction starting soon", Kotak SDK `MarketStatusCode`), 10 at 15:20 ("closing auction started"), 11 at 15:25 ("market orders restricted") and closed at 15:30; the F&O segments sent no status at all and kept trading (future, option prices, OI and volume changed every minute). Index ticks: about 580 a minute until 15:14, 2 in the 15:15 minute, none 15:16-15:19, about 100 a minute from 15:20 (first values 130 points above the future, settling by 15:23, close 25,514.3 at 15:29). The 50-stock breadth and the heavyweight prices and volumes were identical from 15:15 to 15:20, the heavyweight cash volume counters restarted low at 15:21 (HDFCBANK 22.8M to 1.1M, the auction session's own volume) and returned to the day's total at 15:29 (24.08M). The tick account counts at the first point where our server receives the feed, so the broker side and our handling are cleared for the silent minutes: nothing was sent. The sources describing the schedule (a 20-minute session 15:15-15:35: transition and price calculation 15:15-15:20, order entry 15:20-15:30, matching) are a news summary of NSE's page (NSE's own page timed out); a Kotak article gives 15:30-15:40 from 7 Dec 2026, so the exchange's schedule is NOT confirmed from NSE itself; today's status codes match the 15:15 version. D192's wording ("the exchange does not publish the index") is replaced by this finding; how the index value is computed during the auction is not known.
Second finding, a real bug of ours (D178/D181/D194 parity check): between 09:15 and 09:47 IST the check replaced the broker's index quote 32 times, with a level up to 66 points BELOW the live index (average about 35 below; the broker quote followed the live bars within about 25 points). The options' forward was really 10 to 66 points below the index that morning (five strikes agreed to within one point, so it is not noise), and the check treated that as a frozen quote. At 09:15:02 the live index was 25,194 and we stored 25,128, so the opening strike (and the "Fixed" straddle) became 25150 instead of 25200. Fix: a quote is replaced only when it is FROZEN (the same value in three captures at least 90 seconds apart: `SnapshotService._quote_frozen`, and three equal consecutive stored levels in the straddle analysis: `spot_frozen`) AND more than 30 points from the options' level. A quote that moves is always used as sent. The stored snapshots of 19 Nov 09:15-09:47 keep their (wrong) spot, which is labelled by `spot_note`; nothing was deleted or rewritten. Also: `index_ticks_minute` now reports silent minutes as 0 ticks (15:16-15:19 had no line) and the feed counts every SDK message it does not turn into an event by type (`dropped_by_kind`, logged with the tick account; the per-stock closing-auction messages `cas_change` with reference price and imbalance are among them: counted, still not used), the stream status gains `closing_auction` from the status codes (phase transition, order_entry, orders_restricted, ended), and the closing note on the chart now says closing auction. Tests: the real 09:20 capture (five strikes, hand-calculated parity 25,160.6 against a 25,197.3 quote, replaced only if frozen), the freeze detector on the 15:15-15:28 and the morning series, the silent-minute account from the real 15:15-15:20 pattern, the counters on the SDK's own message types, the status codes. Not done: using the auction reference prices (an indicative closing level), the heavyweight flow trackers still read the 15:21 counter restart as a new counter run, and the stored 09:15-09:47 spot, ATM strike and straddle opening values are not recomputed.

## D206 (19 Nov 2026): the auction's index values are indicative closing values (Kotak's documents), kept and labelled; the index is requested on demand while it is silent
Research, from Kotak's own documents: (1) Kotak Neo's closing-auction explainer says the auction runs 15:15-15:35 (reference price calculated 15:15-15:20 with no order entry, order entry from 15:20, matching 15:30-15:35), the last traded price of F&O stocks "pauses from 3:15 to 3:20", from 15:20 "an indicative closing price updates as orders come in", and "index values (NIFTY, BANKNIFTY, SENSEX and BANKEX) keep updating with an indicative closing price throughout CAS"; F&O trading continues to 15:40. (2) The Kotak Neo market-data WebSocket developer guide (the SDK's own protocol document): the index message (7207) has no field that tells an indicative value from a traded one (the header's auction byte is "reserved, auction is not currently enabled"); the touchline message carries an `_indicative_close` that the SDK marks "present, unused"; message 104 carries each eligible stock's reference price and imbalance; `cas_status` in the subscribe acknowledgement marks the stocks eligible for CAS; a SNAPSHOT request (`snapshotIndices`) returns the current state once on the live feed. (3) Nothing in Kotak's documents says the index stream stops: a silent index in 15:15-15:20 matches "last traded prices pause" (no trade moves the index), and the values from 15:20 are the documented indicative closing values. So 19 Nov's 15:20 reads (25,560-25,596 against a future-implied 25,517, settling by 15:23, close 25,514.3) are exchange output of a thin auction book, not a feed glitch, and D192/D193/D194's reading of them as glitches was wrong. Kite's forum shows the same thing on another broker (the indicative close is a separate quote field, not on the WebSocket).
Changes: bars of 15:20-15:30 with enough reads are marked `ind` (indicative closing value) and are never flagged `off`, so no stand-in replaces them; the stand-ins remain for the silent minutes 15:15-15:19 (the cash index has no value to show then). The bar builder no longer holds back the first read after more than 120 s of silence as a "lone far read" (the old level is stale). The chart note says what the candles are and counts them (`indicativeMinutes`). While the pushed index is silent (15:15-15:21 IST) the stream asks the broker for it every 20 s with `snapshotIndices` (`KotakFeed.snapshot_index`, `StreamManager._index_snapshots`, counted as `snapshots_asked` in the per-minute account): the reply is an ordinary index message, so if the broker answers with a moving value it is stored like any other. NOT known until the 20 Nov session: whether the broker answers those requests with a new value at all, and whether a snapshot request briefly withholds the live ticks (the SDK holds incoming messages while a snapshot acknowledgement is outstanding, normally a moment; it is restricted to six minutes a day for that reason). Tests: the 19 Nov auction bars (`ind`, no `off`, stand-ins only for 15:15-15:19, a thin bar not vouched for), the 15:14:59/15:20:02 read sequence through the bar builder, the request loop (only in the window, index token only, a failing request survived), the front-end candles. The 18 Nov stand-in test is changed accordingly: its 15:20 bar is now an indicative value, not replaced. Not done: using the stocks' `_indicative_close` (the SDK drops it) or the per-stock reference prices and imbalances (counted, not stored).

## D207 (19 Nov 2026): the closing schedule is by date; from Monday 7 Dec 2026 regular trading runs to 15:30, then the closing auction to 15:40, and F&O to 15:45
Owner instruction, from a screenshot of a summary table that matches Kotak Neo's own page (kotakneo.com, "NSE new closing auction and F&O trading rules from 7 December"; NSE's own circular not read). Until Fri 4 Dec 2026: continuous cash trading ends 15:15, closing auction (CAS) 15:15-15:35, index and stock F&O close 15:40 (the schedule observed live on 19 Nov), expiry settlement from the auction price. From Mon 7 Dec 2026: continuous cash trading to 15:30, CAS 15:30-15:40 (reference price 15:30-15:31, order entry 15:31-15:40, matching 15:40-15:45), index and stock F&O to 15:45 (index contracts expiring that day stop at 15:30), expiry settlement back to the 15:00-15:30 VWAP. Nothing in the code may carry a closing clock time of its own any more: `app/domain/session.py` holds `regime(day)`, `default_close(day)` (15:30, 15:40, 15:45) and `closing_schedule(day)` (cash end / indicative values from / index end / tick-account window / snapshot window: 15:15, 15:20, 15:30, 15:10-15:35, to 15:21 until 4 Dec; 15:30, 15:31, 15:40, 15:25-15:45, to 15:32 from 7 Dec). The frontend mirrors it in `lib/session.ts` (`closingSchedule`, `defaultCloseFor`, `CAS_SECOND_DAY`).
What now follows the date: the session close in force (`session_close()`: a close confirmed by the exchange feed holds only inside the schedule it was seen in, so Friday's 15:40 does not keep the sampler at 15:40 on Monday, which would have cut five minutes of F&O samples; `same_regime` filters the observations and 15:45 joins `KNOWN_CLOSES`), the stored in-session sample window (`session_sample_counts`, `last_session_by_expiry`: was a fixed 15:40:59), the straddle recorder's hours (was a fixed 15:40), the server-side auto-close rule (`strategy/automation.hold_problem`, was 15:40) and the front-end limit of the auto-close time, the window in which a fresh chain is fetched before streaming (close + 5 minutes), the end-of-day run (15:55, was 15:50), the global-markets polling window (to 15:50), the index bars' indicative and suspect windows (`index_check`), the per-minute tick account and the snapshot requests (`index_watch`), and the chart note and the closing-value stall rule (`widgets.tsx`, `optionTools.ts`: the close value is printed in the last minute before the index end, 15:29 then and 15:39 from 7 Dec). ASSUMPTIONS, to be checked on 7 Dec from the tick account and the status messages: that the index behaves in the new auction as it did in the 15:15 one (silent for the reference-price minute 15:30-15:31, indicative values to 15:40); the feed will show it through `nse_cm` status codes 8/10/12 as it did on 19 Nov. Not changed: the paper trader's 15:20 exits, the Yahoo daily-bar completeness times, the GIFT Nifty hours, expiry-day option expiry at 15:30 (`spot_check.EXPIRY_CLOSE`). Not done: expiry settlement modelling (nothing in the code settled to the auction price). Tests: `test_session_schedule.py` (regimes, windows, the close not carried over from Friday, observations filtered, a 7 Dec bar set with ordinary bars to 15:29, a silent 15:30 and indicative 15:31-15:33, the tick account and snapshot window by date, the auto-close rule, the market-hours window, the stored-session window) and `session-schedule.test.ts`.

## D208 (19 Nov 2026): the pre-open price is held until the market opens instead of jumping back to the previous close
Owner report: the app shows the pre-market NIFTY price while the pre-open session runs (09:00-09:08), then goes back to the previous day's close once pre-open ends, instead of holding the pre-market price until the live market opens at 09:15. Cause, in `useLiveSpot`: the level counted as live only while the stream was "streaming" (fresh); with no index ticks between about 09:08 and 09:15 the stream is "stale", so every screen fell back to the latest STORED snapshot, and before the open that snapshot is yesterday's last one (the samplers only store from 09:15), i.e. the previous close. Fix: `useSpotDisplay` in `lib/useLive.ts` returns live / held / snapshot. When the stream is quiet, the last live index tick is HELD for as long as it is newer than the stored fallback (receipt time against the snapshot's time); it is labelled "Held HH:MM IST" with a tooltip, never "Live", and live takes over again at the first fresh tick. Used for the top-bar NIFTY (`SpotBadge`), the Analysis spot box, the Levels widget and the Command Center header; the screens that compute from the stored chain (Greeks, IV smile, Strategy Builder) keep their rule that a stale value is not live. A tick older than the stored snapshot is not held (the snapshot is the newer value; the evening and the next morning behave as before). The change is shown against the previous close the tick carries. Not covered: a server restart before 09:15 empties the server's tick book, so there is nothing to hold until the first new tick; the backend's stored samples and the stream status are unchanged. Tests: `spot.test.tsx` (held after 09:08 with yesterday's snapshot, live again at 09:15:02, an older tick not held).

## D209 (19 Nov 2026): safeguards after the day's findings (deploy guard, override ledger, after-close audit, front-door check, clock ratchet, data-state labels)
Five weaknesses the 19 Nov session exposed, each with a test; recommended by the assistant, authorised by the owner ("do one by one in auto").
1. **Deploy guard** (`deploy/deploy-guard.sh`, called by `deploy/update.sh` after the cheap "nothing new" check): a new commit is HELD on weekdays 08:55-15:50 IST (not on weekends or exchange holidays from the app's own calendar) because a deploy restarts the live stream (about a minute of lost samples). It goes by itself after 15:50; `touch /opt/nse-universe/state/allow-market-hours-deploy` lets one through once (the update script removes the file); `NSE_DEPLOY_GUARD=0` switches it off. The guard is installed at bootstrap and refreshed after every healthy deploy, so it becomes active from the deploy AFTER the one that carries it. Until then the old script deploys any push at once.
2. **Override ledger** (`app/domain/overrides.py`): every replacement of the broker's index quote is recorded (and failed index snapshot requests); five or more an hour raises a warning in `/api/ops/alerts`, which the app already shows. The 19 Nov false positives (32 in 33 minutes) had only been a log line. In memory; counts per IST day and the last 200 events; the log keeps the history.
3. **After-close audit** (`app/lifecycle/day_audit.py`, run inside the end-of-day pipeline, stored as `audit` in the day's manifest, shown through the same alert list for three days): the stored index level of the chain captures against the live index bar of the same minute (inside the bar's low-high within 15 points; bars of fewer than 10 reads and the closing auction skipped; three or more minutes outside is a warning). On the real 19 Nov rows it finds 27 of 30 minutes outside (worst -46.1 at 09:17) and none with the broker's own quotes. Read-only; nothing is repaired.
4. **Front-door check** (`deploy/check-front-door.sh`): OPTIONS requests without a login; every data and control path must answer 401, only the install files are open, and the backend must be bound to loopback. Run on 19 Nov: all closed, one login in the Caddy users file, backend on 127.0.0.1:8010. The backend's control endpoints trust the proxy, so this is the only gate: run it after any Caddy change.
5. **Clock ratchet** (`tests/unit/test_no_new_clock_literals.py`): counts 15:xx clock literals per backend file; a new one fails the test, so the next schedule change is made in `app/domain/session.py`. Also: `ruff check .` passes again (long lines in the one-off scripts are ignored; the rest is still checked), so `scripts\check.ps1` no longer stops at its first step.
6. **Data-state labels** (frontend glossary and Info buttons): one vocabulary (live price, held price, stored snapshot, stand-in candle, indicative closing value, closing auction) with the same help wherever it appears; the score chips' tooltip says which straddle and window the scores read (rolling ATM, last 60 minutes; the opening strike is not scored).
Not done, with reasons: replay of any page at any minute (large; the straddle page already has "as of"), score calibration against outcomes (the straddle validation endpoint exists, more sessions are needed first), push notifications (needs an outside channel and credentials), expiry-day page, compact mode, daily off-server backups (the laptop must be on), a single synchronised working tree (`S:\Nseuniverse` is a stale copy of the code without git and holds `.env` and data: converting it needs the owner's decision).

## D210 (19 Nov 2026): the Entry Lab
Owner instruction after the audit of the straddle intelligence (docs/straddle-intelligence-audit.md): the next step is a way to JUDGE entry rules instead of guessing them. The Entry Lab replays a short ATM straddle rule on every recorded session and shows what it would have made.
**What it is:** `app/intelligence/entry_lab.py` (pure: `Rule`, `Day`, `simulate`, `summarise`, `sweep`, `presets`), `entry_lab_service.py` (loads the finished sessions of the nearest expiry from the stored chain samples, one day at a time in a background thread, into compact in-memory series; reports "N of M days loaded"; a day that cannot be read is listed, not hidden), the endpoint `GET /api/intelligence/entry-lab` (entry, exit, stop, target, re-strike distance, cost; `presets=true` adds the standard sweeps) and the page `/market-intelligence/entry-lab` (linked from the straddle page): controls, summary, a bar per day, a day-by-day table, and three comparison tables (entry time, re-strike distance, stop) whose rows can be clicked to apply.
**Booking:** a short straddle at the strike nearest the index when opened; profit = premium at entry minus premium at exit from the stored last traded prices (call plus put), costs assumed (2.0 points for the entry and the final exit, 2.0 per re-strike: 0.5 per leg per trade); a stop or target is measured against the current position's entry premium and ends the day (no re-entry); a minute without a price repeats the last one (never zero); no bid/ask, no slippage beyond the cost, no taxes.
**Guardrails:** every number carries its days; below 20 days the page says it describes those days and is not evidence of an edge; a 95% range of the mean and the median from resampling the DAYS (deterministic); leave-one-day-out (how far the mean moves when any day is dropped, and which day matters most); a split by days to expiry (expiry day, 1-2, 3+); the number of variants tried is shown beside the comparison tables (more variants make a lucky one more likely) and the page asks for a range of neighbouring values that work, not a single best one.
**Tests:** the simulator reproduces, on the real recorded 6, 17 and 19 Nov sessions (a fixture of the chains), the numbers an independent study script gave for a fixed straddle opened at 09:30 and 09:45 (19 Nov -215.2 and -106.6 points, 6 Nov -214.8 and -182.6, 17 Nov -15.7 and +19.0); stops, targets, re-strikes, carried prices, skips, the statistics and the loader are checked on small hand-built days; the endpoint and the page have tests.
**Not done:** strangles and other structures, intraday triggers (IV falling, opening-range break), bid/ask fills (needs recorded bid/ask), walk-forward evaluation, a live "similar states" card, and the daily scheduled refresh (the page loads the finished days when first opened). The first full study (15 sessions) found no reliable entry edge: see the audit.

## D211 (19 Nov 2026): range-expansion signals, tested in the Entry Lab before they join any score
Follows the audit (docs/straddle-intelligence-audit.md: a short straddle's result is decided by how far NIFTY moves against the premium; no cheap filter had been shown to work). The signals describe what the day has done so far; the Entry Lab judges whether any of them has mattered. Nothing here changes the buyer or seller scores.
**Signals** (`app/intelligence/range_signals.py`, pure, point in time, only minutes up to the entry are read; a value that cannot be formed is None, never zero): opening gap (points, percent of the previous close, and as a multiple of the premium), range so far (and as a multiple of the straddle premium), break of the opening 30-minute range (10-point margin), path efficiency over the last 30 minutes by the clock (|net move| / path), acceleration (range of the last 15 minutes / the 15 before), premium as a percent of the index.
**In the Entry Lab:** every day's result now carries its signals; a rule can carry GATES (skip when the gap, the range so far or the 30-minute efficiency is above a limit, or the opening range is broken; a missing signal never stops an entry); a signal-against-result study (rank and plain correlation, mean and worst of the days at or below each signal's median against those above) and gate sweeps that show, next to what a gate traded, what the days it skipped would have made. Variants tried are counted (35 now). The page says below 20 days that a correlation describes those days and must not be traded on.
**Live:** `GET /api/intelligence/range-signals` (15 s cache) and a card "Range so far today" on the straddle page, from the analysis the page already computes: read-only description, with the as-of time, a stored-session and stale warning, and a link to the lab.
**Data correction found on the way:** the chain's stored index level of 18 and 19 Nov mornings was replaced by an option-implied level (the D205 bug, 31 and 32 replacements), 10 to 66 points off, so the lab's first study chose the wrong strike on those mornings (19 Nov 09:30: 25150 instead of 25100/25200 by the clean level; gross -215.2 became -174.45; 09:45 -106.6 became -73.85). The lab now takes the index level of each minute from the live index feed's one-minute bar (open of the minute's bar, 10+ reads; the stored chain level only where there is no bar: 29 Oct to 4 Nov) and the previous close from the broker's own quote; each day says where its levels came from. The numbers of the audit document that used the stored levels for 18 and 19 Nov are superseded by the lab's.
**Tests:** signals by hand on a rising session (gap +50, range 40 = 0.2 of the premium, efficiency 1.0, acceleration 1.0 and 2.0) and on the three real sessions; gates with their reasons and missing signals; the study on six hand-built trending days (rank correlation exactly -1, calm half better); gate sweeps showing what was avoided; the real-session replays re-derived with an independent script on the corrected series (19 Nov -174.45 and -73.85, 6 Nov -214.85 and -182.6, 17 Nov -15.7 and +19.0); the live function, endpoint and card.
**Not done:** event calendar (high-impact release inside the hold) and the overnight gap forecast from GIFT Nifty and the US close (both are collected, neither is joined to the lab yet: the point-in-time history is short), intraday triggers, using any signal in a score. A signal only earns a place in a score after it separates days in the lab with enough days.

## D212 (19 Nov 2026): overnight context and the event calendar, kept permanently and tested in the Entry Lab
**Why:** the next two candidates for "will the range stay under the premium" were the overnight gap forecast (GIFT Nifty and the US close) and the high-importance calendar. Both were collected but forgotten: one-minute bars after 7 days (`BARS_KEEP_D`), calendar events 2 days after their date (`EVENT_KEEP_D`), so the lab had nothing to join to old sessions.
**What:** a small permanent ledger (`context_ledger.sqlite3`, a few hundred bytes a day): (1) high-importance events only (country, name, clock time, forecast, the latest actual), written from every calendar poll; (2) once a day from 09:14 IST (and for any recent weekday that has none while its bars are still held) the overnight move of GIFT Nifty, S&P 500 and Nasdaq futures, Brent, the dollar index and USD/INR between the previous NIFTY close (15:30) and 09:14; a price older than 30 minutes at either time is not used, the first record of a day stands. Nothing reads it except the lab and a description card; it feeds no score.
**Lab features (point in time, read at the entry minute):** GIFT and S&P overnight move, the gap GIFT did not foresee (actual gap minus GIFT move x previous close), high-importance events overnight, high-importance events inside the hold (entry to exit). Three new gates (GIFT move above X%, skip if an event is in the hold, skip if an event came overnight), three new sweeps (variants tried 35 -> 40), five new study rows. A day with no recorded context or outside the event record has these features **unknown (a dash), never zero, and is never gated**; the study states how many days each signal had.
**Live:** `/intelligence/range-signals` carries `context` (today's recorded moves, today's timed events, overnight events) and the card shows it with an explicit "not recorded yet" / "calendar not covered" state.
**Backfill:** `python -m app.intelligence.context_backfill --data-dir PATH --from 2026-10-28 --to 2026-11-17` fills the events of the past sessions from the same third-party calendar (high importance, all countries, week by week). Overnight moves cannot be backfilled: bars before about 12 Nov are gone, so the context starts with the days still held plus every day from now on.
**Limits (stated up front):** with about 6 days of context and 15 of events the lab cannot say anything about these signals yet; they are collected so that the answer exists in a few months. The calendar is a third-party feed (MQL5 through biquote): a release's clock time is as published. Weekday logic does not know NSE holidays: after a holiday the move is measured from the last weekday's 15:30, which for a closed market is the last quote, so such a day's move is larger than the true overnight move.
**Tests:** moves, a stale price left out and the weekend by hand; the features and the event windows on a hand-built calendar (overnight, hold, medium and all-day ignored, after the exit ignored); an unknown calendar stays unknown and a known empty one is zero; the ledger (high only, latest outcome, first context stands); the service recording once and not before 09:14; the gates with their reasons and never gating unknown days; the backfill with the stored real biquote response; the live endpoint and card.

## D213 (19 Nov 2026): the recorded days beside the live decision; the pressure reading demoted
**Why:** the audit found no entry signal with validated skill, so the honest help today is to show the size of the typical and of the bad day before the entry, and to stop a reading without skill from moving a risk number.
**What:** (1) `/intelligence/entry-lab/reference` and a card at the top of the straddle page: the short ATM straddle sold at the latest lab entry time at or before now and held to the close, plain and with a 20% premium stop, as median, mean, share won and worst day (points and rupees a lot) over the recorded days, with the day count, "at least 20 days before this is a finding" and "a description, not a forecast and not an edge". It states loading and empty states. (2) The straddle page's call-side and put-side selling risk no longer average in the "directional pressure" reading (futures CVD proxy, VWAP, heavyweight CVD, last 30 minutes): it was one of up to five parts; the risk now comes from the IV change, realised-vol acceleration, gamma set-up and (puts) skew. The pressure is still shown on the tile as "unvalidated, not part of the risk score". The fused sentiment score already did not enter the straddle scores.
**Not done:** the Market Intelligence verdict still shows the fused score; its validation page carries the hit rate (52-53%, no skill) but the verdict does not show it beside the score yet. The risk-number change has no validation either way: the pressure reading was never validated, and the remaining parts are not validated either (stated, not fixed).
**Tests:** the reference by hand on the three real sessions (09:45: -184.6, +17.0, -75.85; mean -81.15, median -75.85, won 33.3%), the slot choice before the first and after the last time, 503 without the lab; the side risk by hand (call IV +0.5 and acceleration 1.3x give 75 whatever the pressure is; pressure alone gives no score); the card, its loading and error states.
**Correction found when the backfill ran on the server (19 Nov, 16:16 IST):** 51 events were written and the ledger held 212 events, because the calendar also lists far dates (central-bank meetings months ahead); the earliest event date therefore said nothing about where the record begins, and the lab would have called days before the backfill "known". The ledger now stores an explicit `covered_from` day (set by a backfill to its start and by the live calendar poll to two days back; the earliest stands), and `coverage()` is None without it. After deploying this fix, run the backfill once more (it is idempotent) to set `covered_from` to 2026-10-28. The daily context job was seen working on the server: contexts for 16 to 19 Nov (16 Nov has no GIFT move: no GIFT price at the previous close).

## D214 (19 Nov 2026): the decay-probability engine (core, calibration, endpoints; no page yet)
**Why:** the owner's goal is an engine that studies the straddle, the Greeks and the structure and says how likely the decay is to pay, with the odds of a stop or a target. The hand-weighted seller/buyer scores do not separate outcomes, and the Entry Lab is the test bench, not the product.
**What (backend only):** `intelligence/decay_probability.py`. The straddle at the strike nearest the forward, its premium the sum of the two last traded prices; the volatility that reproduces that premium (solved); the forward as a lognormal martingale; variance spent by trading minutes (the variance clock), the straddle repriced along the path with Black-76 on the variance still to come, so the premium is a martingale in the model; the distribution computed exactly on a grid (no simulation noise). Outputs: the probability the sale pays at the exit, the mean and the 5/25/50/75/95th percentile of the net result, the break-evens and the chance the index touches one, and for a stop (premium up X%) and a target (premium down Y%) the odds of each and of leaving at the exit. `vol_scale` (default 1 = the market's own volatility, expected result zero before cost) is the one parameter, left for the calibration to inform.
**Checks:** the grid matches the exact lognormal probability at the expiry (within 0.01), the expected result is about zero at the market's own volatility (|mean| < 0.5 points on a 35-point and a 290-point premium), the cost is taken once, a lower realised volatility pays more, a stop caps the bad day and the three outcomes add to one, break-even touch is at least the chance of ending beyond them, a chain priced at a flat 15% gives 15% back.
**Calibration:** `intelligence/decay_calibration.py` runs the engine at 9 entry times per recorded day exactly as live and compares with what the straddle then did: reliability by predicted band, Brier score against always saying the base rate, how often the result fell under the predicted 5/50/95th percentile, mean predicted against mean actual, for vol scales 0.7-1.1 (variants tried: 5). Entries of one day share its trend, so the evidence is nearer the number of days; under 20 days it says "a description, not a verdict".
**Endpoints:** `/intelligence/decay-probability?stop=&target=&exit=&cost=&vol_scale=` (newest stored chain, says its age and when it is stale or too close to the exit) and `/intelligence/decay-calibration` (computed in the background by the lab service, kept until the number of loaded days changes).
**Honest limits:** the model has constant volatility, no jumps, no events, no vol-of-vol, no bid/ask; the premium is a last traded price; monitoring is every 10 minutes so a spike between two steps is missed; exchange holidays count as trading days in the variance clock; on non-expiry days a 30% target is unreachable by decay alone (theta is small), which the engine shows. The first calibration run on three real days (27 entries) at scale 1 had a mean predicted result of -2.0 (the cost) against -56 actual, driven by 6 Nov and 19 Nov: three days say nothing; the server's 15 days are the first real reading.
**Not done:** the market-structure inputs (GEX regime, OI walls, VWAP, range so far, events) do not yet move the volatility; they will be added one at a time and kept only if they improve the calibration. No page card yet: the numbers are served and tested, shown after the calibration on the server has been read.

## D215 (19 Nov 2026): first calibration of the decay engine (15 days); structure candidates under test; the Decay odds card
**First server calibration (15 days, 135 entries, 9 entry times a day, exit 15:15, net of 2 points):**
- *Level and shape are right.* At the market's own volatility (scale 1) the engine predicted a mean result of -2.0 points (the cost) and the straddles made -3.5. The actual results fell below the predicted 5th / 50th / 95th percentile in 3% / 50% / 94% of entries (ideal 5 / 50 / 95). Every other volatility scale was worse: 0.7 to 0.9 predicted +25 to +79 points against -3.5 actual, 1.1 predicted -29. So on these days the market's implied volatility was about the right amount; the distribution of the straddle's result, the typical day and the bad day are credible numbers.
- *The win probability itself does not discriminate.* It sat between 55% and 75% for every entry (a short ATM straddle sold at the market's own volatility pays about 60-65% of the time whenever it is sold), the base rate was 65%, and the Brier score (0.234) did not beat quoting the base rate (0.227). The 55-65% band paid 79% (52 entries, 14 days) and the 65-75% band 57% (83 entries, 12 days): no ordering. Read the probability as "about 6 in 10, as always", and use the stop and target odds and the bad-day figure, which carry the real information.
- *Why:* with the market's own volatility the probability is almost the same at every entry. Information has to come from where the true volatility differs from the market's, which is what the structure candidates test.
**Structure candidates (`decay_structure.py`, `decay_calibration.structure_study`):** realised-volatility blend, range so far above half the premium, a straight 30 minutes, acceleration, an event in the hold, a big GIFT move, a wall of open interest within half a premium, modelled net gamma exposure sign. Thresholds and multipliers fixed before looking at the days; each is applied on top of the best constant scale and compared with it by Brier score overall and on each half of the days; kept only if it improves BOTH halves and at least 20 days stand behind it (`KEPT` is empty: nothing changes the live engine). Open interest is now kept in the lab days every 15 minutes (a few MB). VWAP is not testable (the index has no volume in the stored data). Not yet run on the server: it ships with this change.
**Card:** "Decay odds" at the top of the straddle page: probability, typical day, one day in twenty, break-evens and the chance of touching one, expected move, stop/target/exit odds for a stop, target and exit time chosen on the page, the model's plain sentences, and under them how the model did on the recorded days for the probability band shown. A stale chain, a chain too close to the exit, a loading check and an error each have their own state.

**Structure study, first result (19 Nov 2026, 15 days, 135 entries, base scale 1.0, base Brier 0.2344; a positive change is worse) and two bugs it exposed in my own code.**
Bugs found when reading the server's first run: (1) the open-interest slots are keyed by IST clock time but the lab days carry UTC times, so the OI wall and gamma candidates read nothing (all 135 entries unreadable); (2) the realised-to-implied ratio compared a trading-minute-clock volatility with a calendar-clock one, which overstates the ratio by a factor that depends on days to expiry (about 1.2 at four days, 2.4 on the expiry day). Both fixed with tests (the ratio is now the realised volatility over iv x sqrt(t0 x 94,500 / trading minutes to expiry), by hand 12.46% at four days and 6.36% on the expiry day for a 15% implied volatility).
Corrected result, run on this PC from a copy of the server's data (it reproduced the server's calibration table to the digit): realised-volatility blend +0.075 (117 entries changed, worse on both halves), range so far above half the premium +0.133 (89), a straight 30 minutes +0.007 (2), acceleration +0.049 (30), event in the hold +0.057 (36; first half +0.025, second +0.094), GIFT move +0.049 (9 entries, 3 days), OI wall within half a premium +0.018 (99 entries; first half -0.004, second +0.043), modelled gamma sign +0.034 (30). **No input improved the forecast on both halves; most made it worse; nothing is kept (`KEPT` stays empty).** With 15 days this does not prove the inputs are useless, it says that simple multiplicative versions of them did not beat the market's own volatility on these days. The realised-volatility candidate runs hotter than the implied volatility on minute data (a one-minute index series carries noise), so a cleaner estimator would be a different candidate, not a rerun of this one.
**Method note:** the study is also runnable off the server: copy consistent snapshots of observations, market, index and context_ledger (sqlite `.backup`) and run the lab loader and `decay_calibration` on them; the heavy part (about five minutes locally, more than ten on the 1 GB server) should not be a request-time cost.

## D216 (19 Nov 2026): the Entry Lab and the three straddle-page cards are removed from the app (owner's decision)
**What the owner saw and decided:** on the live straddle page the "Decay odds" card showed "Not available" after the close, "What the recorded days say" and "Range so far today" described past days and the day so far; none of it helped the trading decision, and the Entry Lab was a test bench. The owner asked to remove the recent implementation, the Entry Lab and the range card from the app.
**Removed (frontend only):** the Entry Lab page and its route and menu link, the cards "Decay odds", "What the recorded days say" and "Range so far today" (with the overnight-context line), their API calls, types and tests (10 files). The straddle page is as it was before D210 except for the demoted pressure reading below.
**Kept, deliberately:** (1) the backend (D210-D215: the lab replays, range signals, the decay-probability core and calibration, the event and overnight ledger, their endpoints): read-only, request-driven and cheap when unused, so the research can be resumed without rebuilding; the context ledger keeps collecting (one small job, a few rows a day) so the history exists; the owner can ask for the backend to be removed as well. (2) D213's second part: the call and put side selling-risk no longer average in the unvalidated "directional pressure" reading (it is shown as context on the tile). (3) The findings: the audit document and D210-D215 stay as the record of what was tried and what the 15 recorded days showed (the market's own volatility was the right level; the win probability does not discriminate; no structure input helped).
**Not done / for later:** if a probability-of-decay display is wanted again, the honest basis is the typical-day, bad-day and stop/target numbers of the decay-probability core, not the headline win percentage.

Removed: `entry_lab`, `entry_lab_service`, `range_signals`, `context_signals`, `context_ledger`, `context_backfill`, `decay_probability`, `decay_calibration`, `decay_structure`; the endpoints `/intelligence/entry-lab`, `/entry-lab/reference`, `/range-signals`, `/decay-probability`, `/decay-calibration`; the lab and ledger wiring in `main.py`; the ledger hooks, the daily overnight-context job and the calendar-to-ledger writes in the global-markets service; `StraddleDecay.range_signals` and `.decay_probability`; their tests, the lab fixtures, the lint and clock-literal exceptions. Nothing writes to it any more.

## D218 (19 Nov 2026): convexity and premium-expansion caps on the seller class, and a Pre-Flight Entry Radar on the straddle page (owner's specification)
**Why:** on a trend day the seller environment read 64 (Favourable) while the realised gamma cost was far above theta; and a manual seller wants one answer in seconds: is the premium decaying, is volatility cooling, is it safe to sell the at-the-money straddle.
**Flags (`straddle_env.flags`):** `convexity_blowout` when the realised gamma cost of the recent window is more than 1.5 x its expected theta decay (read from the window's decay-efficiency causes, so it needs the window's theta to be at least the existing 3-point floor): hard-caps the seller class at "High risk" (the straddle sub-score is held at 40, as for a directional flag). `premium_expansion` when the decay efficiency of that window is negative (the premium rose): caps at "Unfavourable". The cap logic now understands "High risk". The number shown stays the raw reading; the class is the capped one.
**Radar (`straddle_env.evaluate_preflight`, in the analysis as `preflight`, live session only):** four gates, each PASS / FAIL / WARN / UNKNOWN: roll-adjusted at-the-money premium over the last 15 minutes (a rise fails, flat or falling passes); ATM IV over the last 15 minutes (a rise above 0.2 vol points fails); spot against the modelled gamma flip (up to 15 points below still passes, further below warns); gamma cost against theta. RED blocks (a gate failed or the premium is expanding), YELLOW (only the structure warns), GREEN (all four pass). Card above the seller/buyer gauges on the Overview tab: verdict badge, the plain sentence, the four gates, and reference levels (strike, total premium in points and rupees a lot, a hard stop at +18% with its loss a lot, re-strike distance 35 points).
**Departures from the specification, on purpose:** (1) A gate whose input does not exist is UNKNOWN ("no data"), never a pass or a fail: the specification would have shown "Above gamma flip (calm)" when no flip exists and "Spiking" when 15 minutes of premium are not stored. Nothing fails but something unknown gives a GREY "no all-clear", never GREEN. (2) A fourth gate (convexity) is shown, because the specified RED reason "convexity overwhelming theta" is otherwise invisible; the red title names what actually failed (premium, volatility, gamma) instead of always saying premium expansion. (3) A stored or stale session gets no verdict ("no live reading"). (4) Premium is in points; rupees a lot (x 65) are shown beside it. (5) The levels are labelled reference levels, provisional, and dimmed with "the radar says do not sell" when red.
**Honest limits:** every threshold (1.5 x theta, 15-minute premium rise above 0, 0.2 vol points, 15 points of flip buffer, +18% stop, 35-point re-strike) is a working value, not validated: the Entry Lab study of 15 recorded days (D210-D215) found that a +20% stop helped early entries and hurt later ones, that re-strikes at 50 points over-traded (about seven a day) and only wider distances (100 points) gave tail protection, and that no simple gate separated good days from bad. The premium gate fails on any rise, so a 1.75-point (0.6%) rise over 15 minutes blocks (as on the 19 Nov 13:43 read); a tolerance is one constant (`RADAR_PREM_MAX_PTS`). The gamma flip is a modelled level under an assumed dealer sign convention.
**Verification:** hand-built analyses for every verdict and boundary (exactly 1.5 x, 0.2 vol points, 15 points below the flip), the cap on the seller class, the radar on the real 19 Nov 2026 13:43 premium series (BLOCKED for a +1.75-point rise over 15 minutes; its gamma, theta, IV and flip are not in that reduced fixture, so the convexity reason could not be shown from it), the card for each verdict, and its place on the page.

## D219 (20 Nov 2026): live regime and squeeze detectors, four alerts, and the pre-flight radar reworked around them (owner's specification, extends D218)
**What:** `intelligence/live_regime.py` reads four things from the stored minutes: the opening 30-minute range against the straddle's implied one-day standard deviation (above 0.65 = IB_EXPANSION_TREND, below 0.35 = IB_COMPRESSION_DECAY; needs the first 30 minutes stored from the open, otherwise "forming" or "unknown"); a short squeeze (index up more than 0.20% in 15 minutes while the future's open interest shed more than 35,000 units, about 540 lots); the put floor (puts added near the money more than 2.5 times calls added while the index rises 0.10%); and VWAP detachment (more than 35 minutes on one side of the future's VWAP and more than 0.22% away). `straddle_watch.detect` raises four alerts from them: "Short squeeze detected" (high, bearish for sellers), "Opening range expansion (wide IB)" (high), "Put floor advancing aggressively" (medium), "VWAP trend runaway" (high); the squeeze, put-floor and VWAP alerts need two minutes in a row and fire once (the existing persistence and cooldown rules), the opening-range alert fires when its flag first appears. `evaluate_preflight(a, env, live_regime)` now returns the specified shape (`headline`, four `gates`: premium_decay, volatility_shock, regime_squeeze, structure_flip, and `trade_setup`) with `reasons` and `regime`; the volatility limit is 0.1 vol points. The card shows the traffic-light banner, four badges (premium momentum, volatility velocity, 09:45 regime / squeeze, gamma flip distance), the regime flags and an action box (ATM strike, total premium, hard stop at +18%, re-strike alert at +-35 points).
**Departures from the specification, and why:** (1) The inputs the specification reads from `mi` do not exist there: `mi["futures"]` has only the session-cumulative open-interest change versus the previous close, there are no 15-minute option additions and no VWAP history. The detectors read per-minute values the service now writes into each stored snapshot (`track`: futures open-interest change, call and put open interest within 2.5% of the index, VWAP side and distance), so every 15-minute or 35-minute window is the difference of stored minutes: it survives a restart and a window with no stored minute within 3 minutes of its start is UNKNOWN, never zero. A VWAP run that reaches the oldest stored minute is shown as "at least". (2) Open interest is in units (the project convention, one lot is 65 units); -35,000 units is about 540 lots, far smaller than if the specification's "contracts" meant lots. (3) The opening range is the first 30 minutes by clock (not the first 30 list entries) and only counts when the first stored minute is within 5 minutes of the open. (4) Put floor: calls unwinding count as zero added and the added put interest must be at least 0.5% of the put interest near the money (otherwise a few contracts on an empty side would fire it). (5) A gate with no data is UNKNOWN and never a pass; nothing failing with something unknown is a grey "no all-clear", never green. Convexity (gamma cost above 1.5 x theta) and a negative decay efficiency remain blocking reasons named in the headline but are no longer a fourth badge.
**Bug found by the end-to-end test:** the D218 radar read `live` and `stale` from the top of the analysis; the analysis keeps them under `quality`, so on real data it would always have said "no live reading". Fixed, with the unit-test inputs corrected (they had made the same assumption).
**Honest limits:** none of the thresholds is validated. The Entry Lab study of 15 recorded days (D210-D217) found no simple signal that separated trend days from decay days, and that a +20% stop helped early entries and hurt later ones. The 19 Nov 2026 13:43 fixture holds only the premium series: the radar blocks it for a premium that rose 1.75 points in 15 minutes (a rise of 0.6% already fails the premium gate); its squeeze, VWAP, opening-range and convexity reasons cannot be shown from it, so the claim that the card flags the 19 Nov short-covering trend day through those detectors has not been verified on data. The squeeze and put-floor detectors need the futures and option open-interest history to have been recorded: they report "unknown" until 15 minutes of snapshots with the new `track` exist.

## D220 (20 Nov 2026): the index-feed cross-check compares like with like (fix of the false "NIFTY index feed problem" at the open)
**What happened:** the Smart OI page said "1 one-minute bar (09:18) ... 44 points or more away from where the NIFTY future says the index was" and left the 09:18 candle out. The stored bar was sound: 60 reads, opened 1.05 points from the previous close, closed within 2.4 points of the next open. **Cause:** `index_check` compared the future's price (sampled within seconds of the START of each minute) with the index bar's CLOSE (the END of the minute). When NIFTY moves 40 points inside a minute the two are up to a minute apart: future minus close swung from -87 to +3 minute to minute while future minus open stayed between -12 and -35. The 09:18 close-based gap was -64 against a neighbour median of about -25. The flag also moved as neighbours accumulated (at the time 09:18, later 09:25, then none), the sign of a timing artefact. **Fix:** the neighbours' reference basis is future minus the bar's OPEN (same moment); a bar's close is compared with the NEXT minute's future price (the end of this minute; the newest bar has none yet and is not judged); the high and low keep the start-of-minute future and the 80-point allowance. Tolerances (40 and 80) unchanged. **Checked on the 12 stored days (5 to 20 Nov):** the old rule flagged 09 Nov 08:46 UTC and 20 Nov 03:55 UTC, the new rule flags neither; the flags of 11 Nov 10:00 and 12 Nov 09:59 UTC remain; replaying today up to 09:27 IST the old rule flags 09:18 (-41.4), the new rule nothing. **Not shown on stored data:** neither rule flags anything on the stored 17 Nov minutes (the frozen feed of 15:15 to 15:27 IST is handled by the closing-session proxy bars, which I did not re-run), so detection of a real fault rests on the unit test with a 90-point jump at a close, not on a real event. Test added from the real 20 Nov 09:15 to 09:26 values; it fails on the old rule.

## D221 (20 Nov 2026): the score-history chart names the minutes that have no score
**What:** on 20 Nov the buyer line on "How the scores moved today" started at 09:19, two minutes after the seller line. The stored minutes show why: buyer coverage was 0.48 at 09:17 and 09:18 (below the 0.5 minimum, `MIN_COVERAGE`), 0.59 from 09:19, so the buyer score was null, shown as "Insufficient evidence", and the chart left it blank. Several buyer inputs need windows (30-minute IV change, realised-vol acceleration and direction, the premium path and skew history) that do not exist in the first minutes. Which input became available at 09:19 was not isolated (inferred, not checked). The behaviour is the rule (missing stays missing, never counted as neutral), so the minimum was not lowered; the fix is that the page says so: a line under the chart names each blank span ("Buyer: no score for 2 minutes (09:17 to 09:18 IST)") and why. Test from the real 20 Nov values.

## D222 (20 Nov 2026): IV-momentum, GEX-history and premium-basket charts draw an IST time axis; the smile compares with no earlier session until one is picked
**What:** (1) The IV Momentum chart labelled its x axis 03:45 to 04:19 for a session that opened at 09:15 IST. The API sends UTC times (`2026-11-20T03:45:05+00:00`) and the chart cut characters 11 to 16 of the text. The GEX-history and premium-basket charts used the same cut on the same kind of times (checked on the server: both return UTC), so they showed the same error; all three now use `hhmmIST` (`lib/format.ts`), which converts the instant. Other `slice(11, 16)` uses in the code add the 5 h 30 min first and were left alone. (2) The Volatility Skew smile drew the previous stored session (19 Nov, dashed) under today's live smile by default, and the "Compare with session" box was pre-set to it. An earlier day's curves on today's chart read as part of today's chart; the default is now none, and picking a session still draws it and fills the comparison table. Tests: the axis test (UTC to 09:15) and the default test both fail on the old code.

## D223 (20 Nov 2026): the VWAP runaway flag must hold for 3 minutes; the opening range is the clock window; the message names the future
**What the live card showed:** DO NOT SELL for a VWAP runaway at 0.23% from the future's VWAP, the only failing gate, while premium was decaying (-9 points in 15 minutes), IV cooling and spot above the gamma flip. The stored distances that hour were 0.232, 0.14, 0.108, 0.206, 0.266 (limit 0.22): the flag flipped on and off as the reading crossed the limit. Replayed over the day's 76 stored minutes, the old rule turned the flag on in 9 scattered minutes and changed state 10 times; the new rule is on in 1 minute (the latest) and changed twice. **Changes:** (1) `VWAP_TREND_DETACHMENT` also needs the distance beyond 0.22% in each stored minute of the last 3 (`vwap_hold_min`), on the same side; minutes stored before the distance was recorded count as not confirmed. (2) The opening range is 09:15 to 09:45 by the clock; it was 30 minutes from the first stored sample (09:16 to 09:46 on 20 Nov: 123.6 points and a ratio of 0.58 instead of 119.45 and 0.56; no flag changed). (3) The message says "The future has stayed above its VWAP" (the reading is the future against its own VWAP, not the index). The 0.22%, 35-minute and 3-minute values are provisional and unvalidated; whether a flag this close to its limit should turn the whole card red is a threshold question for the owner (the other three gates were green).

## D224 (20 Nov 2026): a lone VWAP runaway or wide opening range makes the radar YELLOW, not RED (owner: "fix all issues")
**Why:** on 20 Nov at 10:30 IST the card said DO NOT SELL because of one flag, the VWAP runaway at 0.23% (limit 0.22%), while premium was decaying 9 points in 15 minutes, IV was cooling and spot was above the gamma flip. The VWAP and opening-range thresholds are provisional and unvalidated (D219, D223), so a single such flag now warns instead of blocks. **Rule:** `regime_squeeze` is FAIL (RED) when a short squeeze is on, or when two of (squeeze, VWAP runaway, wide opening range) are on together; WARN (YELLOW, "SELECTIVE / CAUTION", the flag's message in the summary) when the VWAP runaway or the wide opening range is the only trend flag; other gates (rising premium, IV spike, convexity) still block on their own and a warning never hides them. **Not changed:** the 0.22% / 35 min / 3 min VWAP values and the 0.65 / 0.35 opening-range ratios stay provisional until there are recorded sessions with the stored `track` data to test them; the owner's original specification treated each flag as a block, this is a deliberate softening, reversible by changing `hard` in `evaluate_preflight`. Whether today's flag was right can only be judged at the close.

## D225 (20 Nov 2026): the live stream sends only the fields the browser reads
**Measured** on the server (20 Nov 10:47 IST, one client for 30 s): 5,019 ticks and 2.73 MB of tick messages (avg 23 kB a message, about 545 bytes a tick, 170 instruments), about 5.5 MB a minute to each open page before compression; in the Caddy log the stream was 223 MB of the 242 MB served in 88 minutes. **Change:** `wire_tick` leaves out ten fields the browser's `LiveTick` type does not have and no page reads: bid, ask, bid quantity, ask quantity, total buy and sell quantity, last trade quantity, average price, and the provider's two raw time integers. On the real NIFTY 25550 CE tick the message goes from 607 to 379 bytes (0.62). The book and everything on the server (flow, market service) still hold the full tick. **Not done:** a bigger saving would come from sending only changed fields or a slower batch interval (now 250 ms); neither was changed because it alters what the page shows. Not measured after the change until it is deployed.

## D226 (20 Nov 2026): the watchdog retries a refused call before it raises an alert
The "Heads up" strip with "/api/market/session failed (URLError)", "/api/prediction/history/status failed" and "/api/prediction/flows/status failed" (checked 03:11:48 IST) came from `deploy/watchdog.sh`, not from the page: the watchdog (every five minutes) fell inside the 03:11 deploy restart, got connection refused and left a warning on every page until its next run. Every call now retries up to three times, 12 s apart, when the connection is refused or reset or the answer is 502/503/504 (a deploy takes 10 to 25 s); a timeout or any other error is not retried. Tests: two 503s then the answer raises nothing; still down after three tries raises the alert; a plain 500 is raised at once; a refused port is a critical "backend" alert; the first test fails on the old script. A deploy during the five-minute check can still leave the alert for one cycle only if the backend is down longer than about 36 s.

## D227 (20 Nov 2026): history observations cache a digest of each snapshot, not an observation for one strike set
`/api/history/observations` took 450 ms at the median and 3.2 s at the slowest 5% (84 calls in 88 minutes). The cache was keyed by snapshot and the strike set (the newest snapshot's ATM +/- N strikes), so every time the ATM moved it was cold for the whole day: measured on the server, 28 to 52 ms warm against 2.74 s for a new strike count. The cache now holds nine numbers a strike per snapshot (`array('d')`, about 11 kB a snapshot, at most 1,500) and builds every observation from it. Equivalence: tests compare the digest result with `observe()` for several strike sets with missing prices, missing open interest and sides with no contract (missing stays missing, a missing price on a side with a contract is counted, a side without one is not); a test shows a new strike set rereads only the newest snapshot. The first request after a restart still reads every stored file once (about 2.7 s). The response is the same size as before (about 520 kB uncompressed for the default 1,000 observations); trimming it was not done.

(5) The unlinked "Expiry comparison" placeholder page and its test were removed (nothing in the app is labelled planned any more).
