## D229 (20 Nov 2026): Greeks and What-If engine, plan approved, checkpoint (a) pricing core (owner's specification)
**Approved plan (owner: "Approve defaults"):** a new `app/risk/` package, layers `pricing` -> `greeks` -> `ivsurface` -> `whatif` -> `portfolio` -> `service`/API, then a page in the Analyse menu. Answers: (1) implied volatility from the mid price where a live bid and ask exist (about 170 subscribed instruments in the stream), otherwise the last traded price, every strike carrying a visible source label (stored chain snapshots hold no bid or ask); (2) positions are the Strategy Builder legs, plus manual leg entry on the page; (3) NumPy as the only new runtime dependency (Brent and the SVI fit written in-house, no SciPy), Hypothesis as a test-only dependency; (4) the browser core is a TypeScript port, not WASM, with shared test vectors for parity; (5) the live surface rebuilds about once a second (measured first, then set); (6) polling once a second, no new WebSocket in v1; (7) surfaces live in memory only (bounded cache, version id returned in every response), nothing persisted; (8) once verified, the old Black-76 code (`intraday/volatility.py`, `optionModel.ts`) moves onto the new core as a separate tested step; (9) a dedicated page registered in the Analyse menu. Assumed unless corrected: rate default 6.5% (configurable), beta 1 for index options with a config hook, lot size read from the data (65), expiries and holidays from the app's own lists (the weekly expiry in the data is a Monday, and the expiry instant is the F&O close of the session schedule: 15:40 now, 15:45 from 7 Dec 2026, not a fixed 15:30), BankNifty out of scope, spread threshold 15% of mid (configurable), theta per calendar day and per trading hour.
**Checkpoint (a):** `app/risk/pricing.py`: Black-76 on a forward, vectorised with NumPy (broadcasting over strikes, expiries and scenario axes), pure functions with a frozen `PricingConfig` (T floor 1 s, sigma 1e-4 to 5.0, ACT/365). Guards: T below the floor or not positive, or sigma below 1e-4: discounted intrinsic value; sigma above 5.0 clamped; invalid or missing inputs: NaN (never zero); N(x) from `erfc` so deep out-of-the-money options underflow to 0 and never to NaN; the result held inside the no-arbitrage bounds (rounding noise only). `prepare()` returns the shared d1, d2 and masks that the Greeks will reuse. Validation: 21 tests, hand values worked out independently, put-call parity to 1e-8 over a 101-strike chain and to 1e-12 relative over 20,000 random inputs, agreement with the app's existing scalar pricer to 1e-9, the edge cases (1 s, sigma 0.01, deep ITM/OTM, NaN, infinite, expired), and Hypothesis properties (bounds, monotone in volatility, strike and forward, convex in strike, parity). Speed on the development PC (median): 10-leg scenario 0.07 ms, 122-option chain 0.19 ms, 402-option chain 0.27 ms, 21 x 21 x 10-leg grid (4,410 prices) 1.3 ms (targets 5 ms and 200 ms). Not measured: the same on the 1 vCPU server (NumPy is not installed there until the next deploy) and the memory NumPy adds to the backend. **Dependency change:** `numpy>=1.26,<3` added to `requirements.txt` and `pyproject.toml` (already pinned in `requirements.lock`); `hypothesis` added to the dev requirements. Nothing is wired into the application yet, so the live app does not import the module.

## D230 (20 Nov 2026): Greeks and What-If engine, checkpoint (b) Greeks engine
`app/risk/greeks.py`. **Analytic** first and second order for Black-76 with respect to the forward F (a one-point move of the index at an unchanged basis is a one-point move of F): delta, gamma, vega, theta (dV/dt, calendar time), rho (dV/dr = -T V, not zero even at r = 0), vanna, charm (d delta/dt), vomma, veta (d vega/dt); formulas and sources are in the module docstring. **Third order** (speed, color, zomma, ultima) is NOT analytic: it is a central difference of the analytic second-order Greeks (4 reprices per Greek), the step a fraction (2%) of the parameter (of F sigma sqrt T for F, so it follows the width of the Greeks' variation), checked against a step twice as large and combined by Richardson extrapolation; an element is flagged `noisy` when estimated rounding exceeds 0.01% of the result or the plain differences at the two steps disagree by more than 5%; where the time or volatility bump would cross the expiry floor (about 1.04 x one second) or the volatility floor or cap the value is NaN with `unavailable` set; an underflowed wing is exactly zero, not noise. **Units:** `scale()` gives per point (delta, gamma, speed), per volatility point (vega /100, vanna /100, zomma /100, vomma /1e4, ultima /1e6), per calendar day (theta, charm, color, veta), per calendar hour (theta /24) and per trading hour (theta per day / trading hours per day, 6.4167 for 09:15 to 15:40 until the service sets it from the session schedule: a variance-clock reading, labelled as such), rho per 1% rate. `in_rupees()` multiplies by lot size and signed quantity (a short position is negative) and adds the 1% convexity P&L 0.5 gamma (0.01 F)^2. **Guards** as in `pricing`: intrinsic elements get step-function deltas (+-df in the money, 0 out, +-df/2 exactly at the money) and zero curvature; invalid or missing inputs give NaN for every Greek.
**Validation (13 tests, plus the 21 of checkpoint (a): 34 pass; ruff and mypy clean):** every analytic Greek against a separately written Richardson finite difference of the price (or of the analytic first-order Greek for the second-order ones) over 24 at-the-money cases (tolerance 1e-6, worst observed 6.4e-7 for charm) and 72 wing cases at 2 to 3 standard deviations (tolerance 1e-4, worst observed 1.1e-5 for ultima); cross-derivatives both ways (vanna as d delta/d sigma and d vega/dF, gamma as the second difference of price and the difference of delta); the put-call relations (delta difference df, theta difference r df (F-K), rho difference -T df (F-K), charm difference r df, gamma/vega/vanna/vomma/veta equal); third order against the textbook closed forms used only as an oracle in the test (worst observed at the money: speed 1.9e-7, color 3.2e-7, zomma 6.4e-7, ultima 3.9e-11; in the wings 1.4e-7 to 1.1e-5); the guards, NaN handling, deep wings, units and a short two-lot position in rupees by hand; Hypothesis properties (signs, bounds, finiteness, put-call relations over random inputs). **Speed** on the development PC (median): analytic Greeks for 122 options 0.34 ms, for 402 options 0.46 ms; third order for 122 options 6 ms, for 402 options 7.7 ms; every Greek with units and rupees for 61 calls 3.0 ms; a 10-leg position with every Greek in rupees 2.8 ms. **Not measured:** the same on the server, and the third-order accuracy right at the expiry floor (it is flagged or NaN there by design). Nothing is wired into the application yet.

## D231 (20 Nov 2026): Greeks and What-If engine, checkpoint (c) implied-volatility surface
Three modules. `ivsolve.py`: implied volatility of one price by the Brenner-Subrahmanyam seed (sqrt(2 pi / T) V / (df (F + K) / 2), V the out-of-the-money price) placing a bracket, then Brent's method (written in-house, scalar, `math` only); the solver says why there is none (no time value, above the bound, invalid, below the expiry floor). `svi.py`: raw SVI total-variance smile, fitted by the Zeliade quasi-explicit method (linear least squares for a, b s, b s rho at fixed (m, s), projected onto the constraints; Nelder-Mead on (m, s), written in-house, no SciPy) with the no-arbitrage constraints held: minimum variance not negative, Lee's moment bound b (1 + |rho|) <= 2, the butterfly density g(k) >= 0 on a dense grid (penalty during the fit, minimum reported) and the calendar condition against the nearer expiry (penalty, violation reported). `ivsurface.py`: quotes to an immutable, versioned surface.
**Quote rules (owner's specification plus the approved last-price fallback):** a book is judged as it is: no bid, no ask, a crossed book, a spread above 15% of the mid (configurable) or a price below one tick are REJECTED with the reason; the price of a sound book is the mid, never the last price; a quote with no book at all (the stored chain snapshots) may use its last traded price, labelled `ltp` with fit weight 0.5, and a last price older than 600 s is rejected as stale; a broken book is never repaired by a stale last price. One volatility per strike is kept, the out-of-the-money side; the in-the-money side is used only when the chain has no out-of-the-money quote at that strike at all (an in-the-money price is nearly all intrinsic value); when the out-of-the-money quote exists but was rejected the strike is dropped. The forward is supplied or estimated by put-call parity (median of the three strikes where call and put prices are closest).
**Arbitrage is flagged, never smoothed away:** the kept prices, as calls by parity, are tested for vertical-spread bounds (non-increasing in strike, slope between -df and 0) and butterfly convexity with a one-tick slack; a strike that fails is listed as `flagged` with its rule and left out of the fit, and its residual against the fit is still shown. An expiry is marked `degraded` (never hidden) when the RMS residual exceeds 0.5 volatility points, any residual exceeds 2.0, the density is negative on the check grid, the calendar condition fails, or fewer than 7 points remain (the specification gives only "residual > 0.5 vol points"; the RMS and the maximum are both reported, and the 2.0 maximum is my addition). **Versioning:** `version_id = v<sequence>-<12 hex of a SHA-256 of the inputs and the configuration>`; the time of the build is not in the hash, so the same quotes always give the same hash; snapshots are frozen dataclasses of tuples; `as_of_ist` is the IST time. **Debounce:** `RebuildScheduler` (rebuild when at least N strikes changed, or the maximum interval passed with something changed, never without a change) and `QuoteBook` (counts only real changes); the interval is configuration (the specification's 300 ms or the approved 1 s, to be set from the measurements below).
**Validation (34 new tests, 68 in the risk package; ruff and mypy clean):** 1,500 random prices inverted back (price recovered to 1e-9, volatility to 1e-7 wherever the price carries information; every one solved); the seed worked by hand (0.199916 for 0.2); every rejection reason; the parity forward recovered to 1e-6; each arbitrage rule triggered on a built violation; the SVI fit recovering known parameters to 1e-5 relative with a minimum density of 0.29 and Lee's bound held; the known butterfly-arbitrage smile of Gatheral and Jacquier (Vogt) detected as negative; constraints held under 2% volatility noise; surfaces built from known smiles recovering forward (to 5 paise), parameters (3%) and residuals (below 0.1 vol points); a mis-priced 25,700 call flagged as a butterfly violation, listed with its residual and kept out of the fit; noise, too few points and a calendar violation marking the expiry degraded; last-price-only and stale chains, a hole in the chain, a crossed book, a calls-only chain (forward supplied or the expiry reported with no forward) and an expired contract; version hashes deterministic, sensitive to one paisa and to the configuration, immutable; the debounce with a fake clock. **Speed** on the development PC (median): 400 inversions 8.4 ms; SVI fit of 41 points 43 ms cold and 12 ms warm-started; a two-expiry surface of 164 quotes 98 ms cold and 34 ms warm. On the 1 vCPU server expect several times this (not measured: NumPy is not installed there yet), so a rebuild every 300 ms could use most of a core; one rebuild a second with warm starts is the recommendation (approved default), to be confirmed on the server. Not done in this checkpoint: the sticky-delta lookup and the interpolation between expiries (the What-If engine uses the surface of the same expiry); a feed adapter from the application's chain snapshots and live ticks (service layer, checkpoint f).

## D232 (21 Nov 2026): Greeks and What-If engine, checkpoint (d) What-If engine
`app/risk/whatif.py`. A `Scenario` (spot shock in points, volatility shock in volatility points, elapsed hours, sticky rule, rate shock) is applied to a list of `Leg`s (expiry, strike, call/put, signed lots, lot size, optional entry price) on a fitted surface. Pipeline: new forward F + shock (the basis unchanged; the rate shock moves the discount rate only, the forward is not re-carried); new time to expiry measured to the exchange expiry INSTANT (`expiry_instant(date, (hour, minute))`, the F&O close from the session schedule: 15:40 now, 15:45 from 7 Dec 2026, never midnight, and not a fixed 15:30; below one second the pricing core returns intrinsic); the leg's volatility by the sticky rule from its own expiry's smile plus the parallel shock; Black-76 re-pricing; rupee P&L = signed quantity x lot size x (new value - base value). **The base value is the MODEL value now (Black-76 at the surface's own volatility), not the market or entry price,** so a scenario P&L is the change of the theoretical value; P&L against an entry price is reported beside it. **Zero scenario:** inputs equal to the base return the base value by construction (a mask, not a recomputation, because NumPy may differ in the last bit between array shapes), so the P&L is exactly 0.0; a one-nanopoint shock gives a vanishing P&L (continuity checked).
**Sticky rules:** `moneyness` (default; configurable): the smile holds as a function of ln(K/F). `strike`: each strike keeps its volatility. `delta`: sigma' = sigma_surface(k*) with k* solving N(d1_base(k*)) = N(d1) of the shocked option at the scenario's time to expiry, by Newton's method started from the moneyness solution and held inside the fitted strike range widened by 0.02 (flat beyond: the volatility never reads outside the data). Two things learned while building it, both now tests: with no time elapsed sticky delta IS sticky moneyness (the same maturity and volatility give the same moneyness), so the rules differ only as time passes (strikes drift in delta terms and read the wings); and measuring the base delta at the shortened maturity, or interpolating the smile from a table sorted by delta, gave wrong volatilities (an early version returned the table edge; found by a check that the delta rule satisfies its own defining equation). Under moneyness and strike, elapsed time changes value through T alone (the annualised smile is unchanged); under delta it also moves the volatilities a little.
**Grids and presets:** `grid_spot_iv` (default 21 x 21, spot axis +-2 standard deviations of the nearest expiry, volatility axis +-10 points), `grid_spot_time` (21 steps from now to the nearest expiry instant), `payoff_at_expiry` (P&L against the current value if the index ends at each level at the NEAREST expiry; later expiries are valued then on the same rule). `presets_for` / `engine.presets()`: gap down 2% of the forward, IV spike +5 points, one and two standard deviation bands (forward x ATM volatility x sqrt(T) either way), and expiry-day crush (a convention, configurable: the clock moved to one hour before the expiry instant with four volatility points lost). A position with a leg that cannot be valued (expiry not on the surface, no expiry instant, no fitted smile) has NO total: the total and every grid cell are NaN, `partial_pnl` holds the sum of the others, and the warnings say which leg and why.
**Validation (19 new tests, 87 in the risk package; ruff and mypy clean):** the zero scenario exactly zero under all three rules and on the grids; every scenario equal to an independent re-pricing from the formulas and the fitted parameters (to 1e-9); small shocks against the Greeks (delta and gamma, vega and vomma, theta, rho); signs and scaling; the rules by their definitions (sticky strike keeps each volatility exactly, moneyness equals the smile at the new moneyness, delta satisfies its defining equation at several shocks and with time elapsed, and is flat beyond the data); the expiry instant (4 h 40 min to 15:40 is 16,800 s, 13 h to a midnight expiry, 4 h 45 min to 15:45) and the last second (intrinsic at the instant and after, 1.5 s before it a small premium remains); grids against single scenarios; a short straddle earning decay to the expiry; a vertical spread's payoff at expiry by hand; presets by hand (gap down 510 points, one standard deviation 346.8 points); missing legs; determinism. **Speed** on the development PC (median): a 10-leg scenario with the per-leg breakdown 0.19 ms (sticky strike 0.15, sticky delta 1.1); the 21 x 21 spot x volatility grid 1.4 ms (delta 1.6); the spot x time grid under sticky delta 32 ms median, up to about 100 ms; payoff at expiry 1.2 ms; the 21 x 21 grid with 50 legs 20 ms; building the engine 0.5 ms. Targets: 5 ms and 200 ms. Not measured: the server (several times slower, still far inside the targets). Not in this checkpoint: portfolio limits and beta (checkpoint e), the service that feeds the engine from the application's chain and stream (f).

## D233 (21 Nov 2026): Greeks and What-If engine, checkpoint (e) portfolio aggregation
`app/risk/portfolio.py` and `cache.py`. A `PortfolioRiskEngine` takes the legs, the surface, the expiry instants and an optional as-of time, and returns per-leg, per-expiry and net Greeks, the beta-weighted delta, limit breaches and warnings, stamped with `surface_version_id` and `as_of_ist`. It reuses the What-If engine's base state (forward, time to the expiry instant, model volatility), so Greeks and scenario P&L describe the same position at the same volatility. **Units:** `raw` is the model's units times lot size times signed quantity (delta in units of the forward, which is rupees per point; gamma per point; vega per 1.00; theta and rho per year); `rupee` is the conventional reading (per point, per volatility point, per calendar day, per calendar hour and per trading hour, rho per 1% rate, higher orders in the matching units, plus the convexity P&L of a 1% move). Third-order nets are NaN, never partial, when any leg's third order is unavailable or the option is switched off.
**Beta weighting:** beta x delta x (price of the underlying / price of NIFTY); for NIFTY options both factors are 1 (documented). Beta comes from the configuration (`beta`, default {NIFTY: 1.0}), or from a provider function (the hook for a future source); an underlying with no beta falls back to `default_beta` with a warning, and the source is reported (`provider`, `config`, `default`). **Buckets:** legs grouped by expiry, labelled front (nearest), next and later, with hours to the expiry instant; bucket nets add up to the portfolio net (tested). **Limits are configuration** (`PortfolioConfig`, `LimitSet`, `from_mapping` for JSON or settings): absolute maxima per leg, per expiry bucket and for the portfolio on delta, gamma and vega (rupee terms), lots per leg, the beta-weighted delta, and a floor on theta (the largest decay paid per day tolerated; decay earned is never limited). A value at or above 80% of its limit (configurable) is a warning, above it a breach, listed worst first with value, limit and utilisation. Unknown keys, negative or non-finite limits and a bad warn fraction are errors, so a typo cannot silently switch a limit off. **A position with a leg that cannot be valued** has no complete total (every net NaN, the partial sum beside it, the warning naming the leg and why); limits are checked on the legs that could be valued and flagged `partial`.
**Cache:** per-unit Greeks (and their third-order flags) are kept in an LRU cache keyed by (surface version, as-of time, expiry, strike, side), so a rebuilt surface never meets a stale entry, a different quantity of the same contract shares its entry, and an as-of time other than the surface's gets its own (tested, with the cache's hit and miss counts in the result). The numbers are identical with and without the cache. **Validation (12 new tests, 99 in the risk package; ruff and mypy clean):** a short call and a synthetic forward worked by hand from closed forms (delta m x df for call minus put, curvature and vega cancelling, theta of a forward r df (F - K)); the net equal to the sum of the legs and of the buckets for every field; beta by configuration, provider and fallback; every limit rule including the 80% warning, the exact-limit case, the theta floor and the beta-weighted limit; the configuration validation; a leg that cannot be valued; the cache; and three deliberately broken versions of the code (a theta limit that counts earned decay, beta ignored, a cache key without the surface version), each caught by a test. **Speed** on the development PC (median): 10 legs 3.7 ms cold and 0.7 ms with a warm cache; 50 legs 4.7 and 1.2 ms; 200 legs 8.8 and 2.9 ms. Not measured: the server. Not in this checkpoint: the service that feeds it from the application's positions, chain and stream, and the API (f).

## D234 (21 Nov 2026): Greeks and What-If engine, checkpoint (f) service and REST API
**Service** (`app/risk/service.py`): reads the newest stored chain snapshot of each requested expiry and, where the live stream holds a fresh tick (within `stream_stale_after_seconds`) with a bid and ask for the same contract, uses that book instead of the stored last price (stored snapshots carry last prices only, so most quotes are labelled `ltp` with half weight, as approved; contracts with a live book are priced at the mid). The surface is rebuilt only when due: when `greeks_rebuild_min_changed` (10) quotes changed, or `greeks_rebuild_interval_s` (1.0 s, the approved default instead of the specification's 300 ms) passed with something changed; polling faster costs nothing. The surface for every expiry asked for so far is kept in one snapshot with the next sequence number. `as_of` is now when any live book was used, otherwise the time of the oldest stored chain (prices and clock stay consistent for a stored session; the response says `source: stored` and `stale: true` when the chain is older than `snapshot_stale_after_seconds`). Expiry instants come from the session schedule (`default_close`, or the exchange-confirmed `session_close` when the expiry is today); lot sizes from the chain snapshot (an expiry without a lot size is not valued). A bad limits configuration does not stop the application: the engine answers 503 `config_error` with the reason until it is fixed.
**Endpoints** (`app/api/risk_routes.py`, all under `/api/risk`, read-only, no control endpoints, no orders): `GET /status`; `GET /surface?expiry=` (per expiry the SVI parameters, forward, fit quality, arbitrage checks, degraded flag with reason; per strike the market volatility, the fit, the residual in volatility points and whether the quote was used, rejected or flagged, with the reason); `GET /chain-greeks?expiry=&strikes=all|N&third_order=` (every strike, call and put, at the surface's volatility: per unit and in rupees for one long lot; third order optional); `POST /scenario`, `POST /grid` (kinds `spot_iv`, `spot_time`, `payoff`; custom axes up to 101 values), `POST /portfolio`, `POST /presets` (legs as the Strategy Builder holds them: expiry, strike, CE/PE, BUY/SELL, whole lots, optional entry premium). **Every response carries `surface_version_id`, `as_of_ist`, `source`, `stale` and per-expiry `data`** (snapshot id and time, age, quotes priced from a live book, lot size, expiry instant). A missing number is JSON null (never zero, never the invalid token NaN: tests parse every body strictly). Inputs are validated at the boundary (strike and lots ranges, side and rule enums, shock bounds, at most `greeks_max_legs` legs, at most 101 axis values); unavailable data is an error with a code: 503 `no_surface`, 503 `config_error`, 409 `expiry_unavailable` (a surface exists but not for that expiry), 409 `no_fit`, 400, 404 `disabled`, 422 for bad input. Configuration (`GREEKS_*` in the environment and `.env.example`): enabled, spread threshold, last-price fallback, rebuild interval and count, default sticky rule, maximum legs, and the limits as one JSON object (`GREEKS_LIMITS_JSON`); the rate is the application's `RISK_FREE_RATE`.
**Decisions kept from the plan:** polling about once a second instead of a new WebSocket (approved); positions are the Strategy Builder legs posted by the page, with the lot size taken from the chain; surfaces live in memory only and nothing is persisted. **Validation (23 new tests, 122 in the risk package; ruff and mypy clean):** version and time stamps on every response; no rebuild when polled again and a rebuild when more than ten quotes change; stale chains flagged; the engine switched off (404) and a typo in the limits answered with 503 while the application starts; the surface endpoint's fits, residuals and rejection reasons; a live book preferred and a two-minute-old tick ignored; chain Greeks equal to the closed forms from the returned volatility and forward (delta difference df, gamma and vega of calls and puts equal, vega per volatility point); a scenario over HTTP equal to the engine called directly (to 1e-12, all three rules, zero scenario exactly 0.0); grids equal to the engine's, custom axes, the three kinds; portfolio and presets; limits from configuration with breaches worst first; nine kinds of bad input refused with 422; leg, axis and missing-data limits; a degraded expiry reporting null numbers with its reason; and 3 ms-scale endpoint latency when the surface is current. **Not measured:** the endpoints on the server (they need the new NumPy dependency installed by the next deploy; NumPy's memory on the server is also unmeasured). Not in this checkpoint: the Analyse-menu page and its charts (g).

## D235 (21 Nov 2026): Greeks and What-If engine, checkpoint (g) the page in the Analyse menu
**Where:** a new tool "Greeks & What-If" in the Analyse menu (group Risk, route `/greeks-whatif`, its own lazily loaded chunk of about 12 kB gzipped); registered in `lib/tools.ts` so the menu, the phone menu and the home page list it. The page shows what the backend computed and does no financial arithmetic of its own (it formats, plots and finds the zero crossings of a returned curve for the breakeven marks). Files: `pages/GreeksWhatIf.tsx`, `components/risk/*` (legs editor, scenario panel with presets and the per-leg result, risk matrix and payoff charts, Greeks dashboard, surface viewer), `lib/risk.ts` (types, API calls, formatting), `lib/riskCharts.ts` (pure ECharts option builders).
**What it shows:** the position (the Strategy Builder's enabled legs on one tap, or legs typed here; kept in the browser between visits; lot size from the chain); the scenario controls (spot move in points, volatility move in volatility points, time in hours, rate move in percentage points, and which sticky rule the volatility follows, with a one-line explanation of each) and the standard presets as buttons that show their own P&L and apply themselves; the change in value in rupees in total and per leg (volatility and value before and after, and against the entry price when one was entered); the risk matrix (spot against volatility, or against time: a heat map, red below zero and green above, symmetrical, with the forward and the shock on the axis); the payoff chart (at the nearest expiry and today, the one and two standard deviation bands, breakeven diamonds); the Greeks dashboard (delta, gamma, vega, theta in rupees or raw units, the beta-weighted delta and the delta of a 1% move, all fifteen Greeks on request, expiry buckets, every leg collapsible, the limits that are near or over with their utilisation); the volatility surface (an expiry at a time: forward, quotes used, rejected and flagged, RMS and largest fit residual, the arbitrage checks, the smile with its fit, residual bars, and every quote left out with its reason). **The stamp** "Greeks as of HH:MM:SS IST, surface vN" (full id on hover) heads the page and every Greeks and matrix panel; a stored chain is labelled "Stored chain" and a stale one "stale"; a degraded expiry is announced on top and in the viewer with the engine's reason.
**Polling, not a WebSocket (approved):** the portfolio every second, the scenario, grids and payoff every two, the surface and presets every five; the server rebuilds the surface only when quotes changed, so this costs little; hidden tabs pause. **States:** no legs (a message, no calls made), no stored chain (503, in plain words in every panel), an expiry with no chain, no fitted smile, invalid input, the engine switched off, a configuration error, an incomplete position (totals unavailable, the legs that could be valued shown) are each spelled out; a missing number is a dash, never zero. On a phone the page has no sideways overflow, each leg is a labelled card (the first table version cut the dropdown text), and tap targets are 36 px.
**Validation:** 28 new frontend tests (1,058 pass in all; type check and build clean) run against fixtures that are real responses of the risk API on exact synthetic chains (`src/test/fixtures/risk`, captured by running the backend; they are engine output, not hand-made numbers): the stamp text and the version in the title; the legs editor and its persistence; the scenario result against the fixture to the rupee; presets applying themselves to the controls and the request (including percentage points becoming an absolute rate); the matrix and payoff options (cells, symmetrical colour scale, labels, bands, breakevens by hand, gaps for missing cells); the smile and residual builders; the Greeks dashboard in rupees and raw units with the breaches worst first; the collapsible legs; the surface viewer's tabs and degraded, stale and problem-quote states; and each empty and error state. **Looked at in a browser** against a local backend with exact synthetic chains at desktop and phone width (matrix, payoff, dashboard and surface render; no overflow inside the page at 375 px; a first version overlapped the legend with the axis titles and cramped the legs table on a phone, both fixed). **Not done:** a look at the page on live market data (the engine is not deployed; it needs NumPy installed by the next deploy), and the Strategy Builder's own page does not yet link to this one. The header of the app overflows by about 30 px at a 1,024 px window when the status chips are wide (a pre-existing layout matter, not changed here).

## D236 (21 Nov 2026): no live book while the market is closed (found on the server after the first deploy of the Greeks engine)
At 00:47 IST on a Saturday `/api/risk/surface` said `source: live` and `as_of` now for a chain stored at 15:39 the day before (age 9 hours, stale). Cause: with the market closed the feed repeats the last quotes and each repeat renews a tick's receive time (`LiveBook.update`), so ticks passed the 30-second freshness test for hours. **Fix:** the service is given no live book while `stream.market.state` is "closed" (`create_risk`); an unreported state does not hide a fresh book, whose own age still decides. Test added for closed, open and unknown. **First look at real data** (the stored chain of 20 Nov 15:39 IST, weekly expiry 23 Nov): last prices only, so the surface labels every quote `ltp`; the parity forward is about 25,750, the same at every strike (call minus put prices are consistent); 25 quotes were flagged (butterfly and vertical-spread violations: last prices taken at different moments) and the RMS fit residual was 0.44 volatility points, just under the 0.5 that marks an expiry degraded; the endpoints answered in 12 to 100 ms; the backend's memory was 273 MB against 270 MB before the deploy. Not yet seen: a live session (live books, flagged counts with mids).

## D239 (22 Nov 2026): Greeks page: gamma map, since-open gamma and the sudden-move ("gamma blast") reading
**Owner's request:** institutional-style gamma analysis on the Greeks page; "gamma blast is nothing but a sudden movement". **What exists already and is reused:** the server's GEX engine (`intraday/gex_history.py`: per-strike gamma exposure from stored prices and open interest, gamma flip, walls, near-the-money net) and its 5-minute stored-snapshot history; nothing was recomputed a second way. **New:** `intraday/gamma_map.py` (pure), `IntradayService.gamma_map`, route `GET /api/intraday/gamma-map?expiry&universe`, `lib/gammaMap.ts`, `components/risk/GammaMapPanel.tsx` (a section on the Greeks page, chosen expiry and "near the money / full chain").
**1 Gamma map:** calls (+), puts (−) and net by strike (₹ crore per 1% move) for the 31 strikes nearest spot at the latest stored snapshot, with the spot and flip strikes marked; flip, walls. **2 Since open:** near-the-money net, absolute exposure and its session average as lines, NIFTY with the flip and walls, tiles for now / average / ratio / range / peak time and the share of the session spent short gamma. **3 Sudden-move reading:** a 0 to 100 score from five components (weights 25 dealer gamma near spot short, 20 spot near the flip, 15 gamma building against its session mean, 15 hours left to expiry, 25 the last 15 minutes against the session's typical 15 minutes), each shown with its value and a one-line reason; a component with no input is left out and the coverage reported, never zero-filled; levels calm / building (25) / elevated (50) / high (75). Plus a list of the sudden steps of the session (a 5-minute step of at least three times the typical step and at least 0.15% of spot) with the direction as it happened and whether near-the-money gamma was short before it. It never says which way a move will go.
**Honesty:** gamma exposure is modelled (assumed sign convention, not dealer positions); the weights and thresholds are working values and have not been tested against outcomes, and the page says so. **Tests:** hand-worked components (flip 180 points away = 0.6, 15.75 h left = 0.5, 125 against a 103.125 mean = 0.4242, score 67.8 over 75% coverage), event detection on a constructed path, missing values left out of the mean, the service on the stored synthetic session, and 10 frontend tests against that real service output (`fixtures/risk/gamma_map.json`). **Not done:** looked at in a browser; run on a live or a real stored session of the server; the weights checked against how often sudden moves actually followed high readings (that needs several stored expiry days).

## D240 (22 Nov 2026): audit of the gamma exposure, vega and Greeks and What-If numbers against the real stored session
**Method:** the 20 Nov 2026 15:39 IST chain of the 23 Nov expiry on the server (81 strikes, 162 quotes), recomputed by separate scripts that import nothing from the app (plain Black-76: bisection implied volatility, parity forward, closed-form gamma, central differences of an independent pricer), read-only against the server's database and API. **Gamma exposure (server `gamma-map`, 0 differences):** forward 25,751.35 (parity, five strikes); every one of the 31 profile strikes (call, put, net, absolute) within 0.01% to rounding of the solved vol; net, absolute, near-the-money net, call and put walls, peak and the gamma flip for both universes (dynamic ±10: net −1,279.6, abs 103,320, flip 25,744.69; full chain: net −9,656.8, abs 119,178.7, flip 25,781.62); average, now and the short-gamma share recomputed from the points. **The browser's Gamma Exposure and Vega Analysis calculations (0 differences, 49 contracts):** implied vol, vega per volatility point, gamma, vega exposure (vega × OI), gamma exposure, totals, flip 25,764.03 (25 strikes) and walls match; the page for the full chain showed net −9.66K, flip 25,782, walls 25,500 / 26,000, the same as the server. **Greeks and What-If (0 real differences):** a three-leg position's value, delta, gamma, gamma P&L of 1%, vega, theta (day, hour, trading hour), rho, vanna, vomma, charm and veta agree with finite differences of an independent pricer to 1e-5 or better; net totals are the sums; the scenario (spot −100, vol +3, 24 h) was repriced by hand from the fitted smile for the moneyness and strike rules (new vol, forward 25,651.75, time, value, P&L −2,705.95 to the paisa); the spot × volatility and spot × time grid cells equal the scenario's; the payoff curve equals intrinsic value minus today's value at all 21 points; the ±2σ range is 563.7 points. **Charts looked at on the real data in the browser:** the risk matrix (short volatility is green, a move either way is red), the payoff (breakevens, today's curve), the gamma by strike bars with the spot line, the since-open lines and the flip and walls through the session; the strike bars agree with the independent per-strike values (e.g. 25,500 net −5.6K, 26,000 +8.0K).
**Found and fixed (display only, no number was wrong):** the sudden-move list said "three times the typical step" while the 0.15% floor was what applied (it now says the larger of the two and shows the typical step); the spot and flip marks fell on one strike and one label hid the other (one line now carries both values); the time-to-expiry component says its hours are calendar hours (a Friday read of a Monday expiry shows 72 h and scores 0, as the option model counts time). **Not checked:** the Vega Analysis page itself did not finish loading through the SSH tunnel used for the check (its numbers were checked as above; its charts were not looked at); live (market-hours) behaviour of everything above.

## D241 (22 Nov 2026): audit of the Vega Analysis page on the real stored session
**Method as D240:** the 20 Nov 2026 session of the 23 Nov expiry (389 one-minute samples; strike window 25,750 / 25,800 / 25,850), recomputed by a separate script (own implied vol, own parity forward from the five strikes nearest spot, own vega = e^(-rT) F phi(d1) sqrt(T) / 100), compared with what the page showed in the browser. **Matches:** call vega 9.099 and put vega 9.099 (independent 9.0989 / 9.0991), average and skew −0.000; the percentile 86% (independent 85.9 over the same 999 history samples) and the regime "High Vega" (rank above two thirds); the tooltip at 12:44:04 (call minus put 0.001, independent 0.0006); the candle statistics over 77 candles (call average 7.885, range 5.112 to 9.307; put 7.895, 4.509 to 9.306, all identical); the change of the anchored band (open ATM 25,400; call strikes 25,400 to 26,400 and put strikes 24,400 to 25,400, 21 each): call +36.45 and put −56.57, identical. The price line, call and put vega lines, the CE minus PE bars and the band-change chart render with the right axes and values.
**Found and fixed:** the "Change of the band" sentence said the put side "gained the larger share of sensitivity" when the put side's vega had fallen by 56.57 (the larger of the two moves was a loss). It now says whether each side's vega rose or fell and which side changed more; a test reads the sentence against the printed signs.
**Method notes, not changed (they are choices, now written down):** (1) the percentile reference is the newest 999 samples of the window's strikes (about 2.6 sessions) because the history request is capped at 1,000 points; the server holds 17 sessions (about 6,000 samples) for this expiry, so "position in the stored history" means the last few sessions. (2) The reference series forwards come from the three window strikes alone, the day series from the five strikes nearest spot: they differ by about 0.4 index points at the end of the session, which moves vega by about 0.002 (0.03%), far below the rank's resolution. (3) Vega falls with the square root of time to expiry, so the rank mostly says where in the expiry cycle the session is; it is not a volatility-regime signal. **Not checked:** live market-hours behaviour.

## D244 (22 Nov 2026): the releases on the server stopped growing: hard-linked built files, 7-day keep, stable library chunks
**Found:** between 6 and 21 Nov the server's disk use rose 748 MB; 417 MB of it was the three kept releases, which went from about 11 MB each to about 150 MB each. 139 MB of a release is `frontend/dist/assets`: `deploy/update.sh` copied every built page file of the release before it into the new one (so a browser tab opened before a deploy can still load its pages) and kept them 14 days. Almost every build renames about 25 files (the entry file lists the names of all lazy pages, so its name changes with every build, and every page that imports a shared module is renamed with it): about 2.3 MB per deploy, and 74 deploys in 10 days. Each of the three releases held its own copy (3 x 139 MB).
**Fixed:** (1) `keep_assets`: the old files are hard links (`cp -aln`, same disk; a plain copy only if linking fails), so a file exists once however many releases hold it; (2) kept 7 days instead of 14 (`NSE_ASSET_KEEP_DAYS`), dropped from the new release only, never from the running one; (3) `dedupe_assets`: after a healthy deploy, releases that still hold separate copies of identical files are made to share one, so the existing 449 MB collapses without waiting for the old releases to rotate out (dry run on a copy of the live releases: 407 MB to 141 MB); (4) the library code (React, the router, the query library, ECharts) is built into stable vendor chunks (`chunkFor` in `vite.config.ts`), so the 692 KB chart file is no longer re-issued under a new name with every build (a one-line change used to rename 25 files, 2.3 MB; it now renames about 19 to 26 files, 1.1 to 1.4 MB, and the first page load is unchanged at 543 KB); (5) `git gc` on the deploy clone after a healthy deploy (it holds objects of every earlier commit: 23 MB to 60 MB in two weeks). **Tests:** `test_deploy_assets.py` runs the two functions in bash (links not copies, a new build's own file never replaced, the 7-day rule only on the new release, a missing old release, de-duplication and its idempotence); the built bundle was opened in a browser against the live API (pages render, charts draw, no failed requests). **Not done:** a deploy of this script on the server (it takes effect with the next deploy that carries it); the entry file still changes its name on every build, so most page files are still renamed on each deploy, by design of the tool.

Cost about 140 MB more (about 220 MB in all; up to 440 MB on two-expiry days). **Already lost before this change:** the raw files of 13 Nov (older than 7 days and not in the PC copy, which was last pulled on 13 Nov and holds days up to 12 Nov); the normalized files of 13 Nov are still on the server. **Plan written, not built:** `docs/data-library-plan.md` (library page, zip bundles downloaded from the app with a checksum confirmation, pruning that waits for the export, and a temporary restore into a scratch store with a coverage check before every backtest). Finding that shapes it: the observation database and the archive hold all the research data forever, so no backtest needs a restore today.


## D246 (22 Nov 2026): the data library is built (automatic bundles, confirmed downloads, temporary restore, pruning gate, coverage check)
**Built from the plan in `docs/data-library-plan.md`, with the owner's approvals** (bundle contents: snapshot files, archive, manifests; deletion waits for the bundle; a scratch store on the server up to 1 GB for 24 hours; automatic bundles). New: `app/library/` (`bundles.py`: build, verify, restore with path guards; `store.py`: its own small database; `service.py`: the automatic step, confirmation, restores, inventory, coverage), `app/api/library_routes.py`, the Data page's "Data library" panel, `data_coverage` on the signal backtest, and a bundle step in `scripts/pull-backups.ps1`. Changed: `prune_snapshots` takes the bundled days (a day waits for its bundle except at the hard stop or low disk), the end-of-day run bundles before it prunes, the manual clean-up waits the same way, and `SnapshotStore.load` reads a missing file from an active restore or restores the bundle holding that day on demand. **Why it is safe:** a bundle counts only after it was written to a temporary name, fsynced, read back member by member against its manifest and renamed; the gate re-checks that the file exists with its recorded size; an unconfirmed bundle is never removed; a restore verifies everything first, refuses paths outside `snapshots/`, `archive/`, `manifests/`, never overwrites and is capped. **Tests:** 34 backend tests (bundle contents and checksums, tamper detection, forged paths, never overwriting, size limits, the automatic batching rules, the gate and its hard stop, confirm/remove rules, the database being lost, restore expiry, on-demand restore through the snapshot reader, coverage, the API incl. a streamed upload and local-only control) and 11 frontend tests (lists, download with checksum, a damaged download not saved or confirmed, remove rules, restores, coverage note). **Not done / not verified:** it has not run on the server (deploy first; the first automatic bundle appears when snapshot days reach 25 days of age, around 3 Dec 2026, or on a manual build), the PowerShell step has been parsed but not run against the server, and restoring observation rows from the archive (needed only if row pruning is ever enabled) is not built: no backtest needs it today.

## D247 (22 Nov 2026): the data library follows one simple rule: every 30 days a bundle, a heads-up, delete after the download
**Owner's instruction:** "after 30 days the system has to make the 30 days into a zip file, tell me in the heads-up, and after the download delete it, and this goes on forever." **Replaced:** the 25-day trigger, the 7-day batch, the 60-day hard stop and the 90-day keep. **Now:** the oldest snapshot day in no bundle starts a 30-calendar-day block; the day after it ends the system builds one verified bundle after the close; the heads-up strip announces it (`/api/headsup/trades` carries a `library` alert, shown as "Data library", linking to the Data page, returning weekly, red when the disk is getting full) until it is downloaded; when the download is confirmed by checksum the server deletes the bundle and the bundle's snapshot files (only a file whose size matches the bundle's record and whose day has its verified-archive manifest; never the archive, the manifests or the database) and the next block starts. The nightly time-based pruning no longer deletes while the library is on (`prune: handled_by the data library`); the manual clean-up page removes only days whose bundle was downloaded. The only deletion without a download: under 6 GB free, the snapshot files of a bundle that exists on the server go (the bundle holds them) so the disk cannot fill. The Data page lost its Remove button (the server removes the bundle itself) and says how much a download freed. **Tests:** 39 backend tests (the block and its readiness, block length setting, empty server, deletion after a confirmed download and not after a refused one, a file that no longer matches or a day without its archive being kept, an unconfirmed bundle surviving any wait, the full-disk case, the heads-up lines and their weekly return, the end-of-day run building once a day and leaving the pruning to the library, the API incl. the heads-up feed) and 16 frontend tests. **Left over from the test on the server:** the small bundle I made by hand on 22 Nov (28 Oct to 1 Nov) is on the server unconfirmed; it will show in the heads-up strip, and downloading it is the first real run of the rule (it then frees 3 days of snapshot files).

## D248 (22 Nov 2026): the SSH hardening is applied
**Owner's instruction:** complete the SSH hardening. **State before:** password and root login both on, no firewall, no fail2ban, about 9,500 failed logins a day (`btmp` 104 MB). **Found:** the existing `/etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/99-ctrl.conf` set `PermitRootLogin yes` and `PasswordAuthentication yes`, and sshd keeps the FIRST value it reads (files in name order), so `deploy/harden.sh` as written (`99-nse-hardening.conf`, read after `99-ctrl`) would have left passwords on. **Fixed in the script:** the file is now `00-nse-hardening.conf`, and the script checks the effective values with `sshd -T` and removes its file and refuses if anything still overrides them; fail2ban never bans the address the script is run from. **Applied safely:** the key login was proved first (the laptop connects with `id_ed25519`, the server holds that one key), a second SSH session was kept open, a systemd timer was armed to undo everything after 10 minutes unless cancelled, the script ran, a NEW connection with the key worked, a password-only attempt was refused ("Permission denied (publickey)"), then the timer was cancelled. **Now:** passwords off, root only with a key (`prohibit-password`), 3 tries per connection; ufw on with 22, 80 and 443 only (everything else denied; the backend on 8010 and Caddy's admin on 2019 listen on loopback only); fail2ban bans an address for an hour after 4 failed logins in 10 minutes (2 addresses banned within the first minutes). The app (backend 200) and the site (Caddy login 401) are unaffected. `99-ctrl.conf` was left in place (copy in `/var/backups`), now overridden. **Consequences for you:** any other device that logs in with a password (a phone SSH app, another PC) no longer can: add its public key to `/root/.ssh/authorized_keys` first (from a session that works). If you are ever locked out, the hosting provider's web console can run `rm /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/00-nse-hardening.conf && ufw disable && systemctl reload ssh`. **Still open from the owner's list:** the pending kernel reboot, the 2027 holiday list, the Caddy Content-Security-Policy header.

## D249 (22 Nov 2026): Content-Security-Policy header on the public site
**What it is:** a response header that tells the browser which places the page may load scripts, styles, images, fonts and connections from, and refuse everything else. It limits the damage if someone ever managed to put hostile text or a script into a page (cross-site scripting): the browser would not run a script from anywhere but our own site, would not send data to another site, and would not let another site frame the app. **The policy (enforcing):** `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: blob:; font-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self' wss://nseuniverse.duckdns.org; worker-src 'self'; manifest-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'`. Scripts, fonts, images and connections are the site's own only; no inline script and no eval is allowed. Inline styles are allowed because the charts and the loading screen set styles directly (they cannot run code). **Checked before it was applied:** the app has no external script, font, image, frame or connection (searched in the source; the built files contain no `eval` or `new Function`); the built site was served with this exact header and 6 pages opened (home, Greeks, Gamma Exposure, option chain, Intraday analysis with 14 charts, Settings, Market Intelligence): all rendered, all charts drew, the service worker registered, no violation in the console or in `securitypolicyviolation` events. **Applied:** `/etc/caddy/Caddyfile` (backup `/var/backups/Caddyfile.before-csp`), validated, reloaded (not restarted), the header is on the public files and the login still answers 401 on protected paths. Also in `deploy/Caddyfile.template`. **Not seen:** the site after login in a real browser session (it needs your login: open it and look for a blank page; if anything is blocked, the console says "Refused to ..."). **Undo:** `cp /var/backups/Caddyfile.before-csp /etc/caddy/Caddyfile && systemctl reload caddy`. If a future feature needs an outside source (a map, a chart library from a CDN, an embedded video) its address must be added to the policy first.

## D250 (22 Nov 2026): a trade appeared in Closed on a Sunday: automation acts only inside a real session, and a closed trade is never filed twice
**What happened:** the "straddle" (CE and PE 25800, expiry 24 Nov) was really closed on 20 Nov (CE stop 209.85, PE clock close 40.10, +Rs 422.5). Its legs later came back as running legs in the server's running strategy (an older browser copy written back). On Sunday 22 Nov at 08:20:21 IST the backend restarted (my deploy); the broker replays Friday's closing quotes on subscribe, the stream counted as live, and the server executed the stop (CE 262.30) and the intraday clock close (PE 41.35) at those stale prices, with nobody present and the market shut. A browser then filed it at 09:01 IST as a second closed trade (-Rs 3,068). **Two faults:** (1) the server automation asked only "is the stream live", never "is the market open": any stored price could trigger a stop or a clock close on a holiday, a weekend, before the open or after the close. (2) The page filed a finished running strategy as a new closed trade unless it matched an existing one by identical exit prices; the same legs with other exit prices passed. **Fix:** `StrategyAutomation.market_open()` (exchange holiday calendar, weekday when the calendar has no entry, 09:15 to the session close) gates every check; wired in `main.py` with the intraday calendar. In the page, `isRefiled` recognises a closed trade by its leg ids and entry premiums (any exit prices) and shows that trade instead of filing a new one, for the open strategy and for saved ones that finished on the server. **Tests:** Sunday, holiday, before the open and after the close do nothing; a stop at 09:30 on a trading day still executes; the real leg ids and premiums are recognised as already closed, other ids or another premium are a new trade. **Not fixed / owner decision:** the wrong record `leg-mwra63ro-3c` (-Rs 3,068, 22 Nov) is stored data and was not deleted; the real result is `leg-mwouykdh-0` (+Rs 422.5), which the portfolio statistics currently double count. Not yet known: which client wrote the old legs back as running (the guard stops the harm either way). **Deploy:** not yet.

## D251 (22 Nov 2026): one page-wide timeframe on the Gamma Exposure and Vega pages
The 1m / 3m / 5m / 15m switch used to sit inside one chart (and again inside another section) and changed only that chart. It is now one bar at the top of both pages (`components/dashboard/Timeframe.tsx`, one shared choice kept on the device, default 5m) and every time chart and table on the page follows it: on Gamma, the session structure chart, the intraday GEX chart with its flip, migration and flip-time figures, and the GEX-price divergence table (it was fixed at 5-minute candles; windows shorter than the chosen candle are hidden because one candle cannot show them); on Vega, the level chart, the vega-change charts and the selected-strike line. The per-section buttons were removed (no two controls for one thing). The strike-profile, density and strike-by-strike sections are a single moment, not a series, so they have no timeframe. **Tests:** Vega page: 1m shows all 40 stored samples, 15m at most 4 candles, the choice is remembered; Gamma page keeps its timeframe group; full frontend suite 1091 passed. **Not seen:** the page on a phone after login; live data in market hours.

## D252 (22 Nov 2026): the NIFTY future as a strategy leg (for delta-neutral strategies)
**What:** the Strategy Builder takes a NIFTY index future next to the option legs (buttons "Buy future" / "Sell future", also on the empty page so a future can be the first leg). A future is a leg with `type: "FUT"`, strike 0 and the entry price in `premium`; one lot is 65 units like an option lot. **Contract:** the backend resolves it (`app/market/future_quote.py`, `GET /api/futures/contracts`): the current contract follows the monthly option expiry the app already resolves, which moves to the next month once the month's last expiry has passed, so "current month, the next month after expiry" is the existing rule; the next-month contract is offered for rolling. A price older than 180 s or missing is shown as missing, never zero. **Roll:** a button on the leg ("Roll to <next expiry> contract") closes the old contract at its live price (the P&L is booked into `realised`) and opens the same position in the next contract at its live price, stop and target keeping their percentage distance (`rollFutureLeg`). It is a manual action: a position left open at expiry is closed by the hold rule like an option; there is no automatic roll. **Payoff (exact):** at expiry a future is worth the index (`intrinsic` = S), so the P&L is sign x (S - entry) x qty; a future adds no kink; above the highest strike the slope is the calls plus the futures, so a short future against a long call has slope zero (limited loss) and a long future makes the profit unlimited. Same-expiry strategies use the exact analysis; weekly options with the monthly future use the date curve (model estimate, as for any multi-expiry strategy). **Model (date curve, Greeks):** value = S x e^(carry x t) (not discounted: a future carries no premium); delta = e^(carry x t) (about 1.005 for a month, the same spot-based convention as the option deltas, so they add up on one chart axis); gamma 0, vega 0; theta = the carry running off in one day (about -4.26 per unit at 25 800, 6 % carry, 30 days; it is basis convergence, not option decay). The carry is the one the future's own live price implies when that quote is fresh and from the same moment as the spot (so the model passes through the price on screen), else the option chain's. **Net premium** counts options only (a future has no premium; its entry price is a notional). **Server:** stops, targets and the hold rule work on a future like on an option; this needed a fix: the server read prices only from the browser feed and the futures are server-side contracts (`LiveBook.get_internal`, only a tick under 180 s old counts). **Not included, said on screen:** (1) the Greeks & What-If page (backend surface engine) prices options only: the future is left out of "Use builder legs" and a note says so; (2) the price-through-the-day chart is drawn from option-strike samples, so it leaves the future out and says so. **Tests:** hand-calculated payoffs (protected long future, short future + long straddle: flat -200 points above the strike, 2(K-S)-200 below, breakeven K-100, max profit at S=0), Greeks and value (25927.5471, delta 1.0049437, theta -4.2617), roll booking (+6 500 for 25 800 to 25 900 on 65), validation, contract helpers, the page (add, hedged-straddle payoff, roll, no contract), server stop/target booking (-7 150 and +13 650), the quote module. **Not seen:** the page in a browser with the live backend (the contract endpoint needs the running market service), a real roll across an expiry.

## D253 (22 Nov 2026): MTM tab in the Strategy Builder
A new tab "MTM" next to Positions / Price chart / Greeks / Scenarios draws the strategy's mark-to-market through time: booked + open P&L (before costs) at every stored one-minute sample, from the moment the last leg was entered (`lib/strategyMtm.ts`, `components/StrategyMtm.tsx`). The Price chart tab already had a "P&L" mode, but it measures the whole day's samples against the entry prices; this tab starts at the real entry, follows exits and re-entries, includes the future, and shows peak, trough and maximum drawdown. **Rules:** a leg not yet entered is not part of the book, so the line starts when the last leg is in; an open leg = earlier booked + sign x (stored price - entry) x lots x lot size; an exited leg stays at what it booked after its exit time (a leg exited at a sample counts as out at that sample); a minute without a stored price for a needed leg is a gap, counted and said, never zero or carried forward; a future uses the stored future series only when it is the same contract (a position in the other month has no stored series and shows gaps); legs with no recorded entry time (built before the time was kept) or no entry price get an explanation instead of a line. The builder's live total is shown beside the stored "Now" so they can be compared (the stored line lags by up to a minute). Expiries the sampler does not collect have no stored prices and say so. **Tests:** hand sums with lot 65 (short straddle + long future: 0, -650, -1950, +845; exit at a sample; re-entry after a booked cycle; gaps; every refusal) and the tab with stubbed stored series. **Not seen:** the tab on a phone or with a real session's samples.

## D254 (22 Nov 2026): global-markets chart sheet opens on the screen, closed hours no longer leave gaps
**Position:** the chart sheet (View chart) used `position: fixed`, but an ancestor on the page (the page-enter motion added in D242 puts a transform on it) makes `fixed` relative to that ancestor, so on a PC the sheet opened at the top of the page and the owner had to scroll up to see it. It is now rendered into the page body (`createPortal`), so it is always centred on the screen. A test checks that the dialog sits directly in the body. **Gaps:** a US index or a future leaves hours with no prices (closed market); on a time axis that is flat empty space, so a 7-day chart of the S&P 500 was five small fragments. The axis is now the stored samples in order (`lib/globalChart.ts`): the line is continuous, nothing is added, interpolated or carried over a closure, each closure longer than 90 minutes is marked with a dotted separator (labelled with how long it lasted when it is 3 hours or more), and the axis labels the first sample of each IST day (several days) or of each two-hour block (one day). **Reading aids:** change over the range with percent, high, low and the number of closed periods; the line is green or red by the range's direction; the tooltip shows the value, the time and the change against the previous close. **Tests:** hand counts for closures, labels, formatting and range statistics, and the sheet with a real three-sample series. **Not seen:** the new chart drawn in a browser (the unit tests stub the chart); check 1D, 3D and 7D on gold and the S&P 500 and look for crowded labels. The small card sparklines were not changed.

## D255 (22 Nov 2026): one chart look, readable type, a new Home page, offline strip, request defaults
**Charts:** `lib/chartTheme.ts` is applied to every chart in `EChart` before the layout fitting, so the 40-odd chart builders are not edited one by one. It only adds defaults or lifts a too-small size; a value a builder sets always wins. Axis, legend and label text under 12 px becomes 12 px on a chart wider than 560 px (a narrow chart keeps its small text for the room); soft dashed value-axis grid; tooltip cards with the theme surface, a clear edge, rounded corners and a dashed crosshair; rounded bar tops; a line without a width gets 2; a line that only coloured its stroke now also colours its legend swatch (the swatch showed a palette colour that did not match the line); an eight-colour colour-blind-safe series palette for charts that pick no colours (day and night variants). **Type:** headings, `th`, `dd` and `strong` are bold, `dt` medium, and the 10 and 11 px utility sizes are 11 and 12 px everywhere. **Home:** header with a bigger title, "Jump back in" (the pages opened last, kept on the device by `lib/recent.ts`, recorded in the shell), four start cards with icons, tool list with a filter per menu and a search box (`/` focuses it). **Robustness:** `OfflineStrip` says when the device is offline and refreshes every query when the connection returns. **Latency:** query defaults now keep data fresh for 3 s (a burst of identical requests when widgets mount or the window regains focus is shared) and retry a failed read after a growing pause. The existing caching was already in place and is unchanged (zstd/gzip, immutable hashed assets, route splitting, idle prefetch); no timing was measured, so no speed-up is claimed. **Checked by eye:** a throwaway gallery with three charts and the Home page, day and night, at 800 and 1280 px wide. **Not seen:** every real page: the 12 px text may crowd a dense chart or table somewhere (the global 11 to 12 px size change touches many small labels); look through Option Chain, the Greeks pages and the Strategy Builder after deploy and say which page looks tight.

## D256 (22 Nov 2026): global chart fixes; GEX and Vega calculations re-verified; a defect from D251 corrected
**Global charts (seen on a phone, owner's screenshot):** the closure label ("closed 17h 10m") was drawn rotated at the left edge over the price-axis numbers when a closure came right after the first samples, and the line joined the last price before a closure to the first one after it with a steep stroke. Now each stretch of trading is its own line (nothing is joined across a closure; a lone sample is a dot), the label appears only on a screen at least 640 px wide and only between 12 % and 90 % of the way across the chart, the first axis label is left-aligned so "20:00" is not cut, and the stats show "Vs previous close" (the same figure as the quote at the top) next to "First to last" (the range). **Defect I introduced in D251 and corrected:** the GEX-price divergence table used the new timeframe bar's candles. Its windows (5, 15, 30, 60 minutes) look for the sample that many real minutes before the newest one within a tolerance of a quarter of the window, so with 15-minute candles and the newest candle only partly formed the 15-minute window found nothing ("no stored sample about 15 minutes earlier"). It is back on fixed 5-minute candles, independent of the bar, and the section says so. A test shows the case. **Verification (independent recompute, not shared code):** on the real captured chain of 29 Oct 2026 (expiry 3 Nov, 81 strikes), a separate Python Black-76 (implied volatility by bisection, gamma, vega, delta, GEX = gamma x OI x spot^2 x 1 % / 1e7) was compared strike by strike with the app's TypeScript on 140 contracts: relative differences at most 1.0e-7 for IV, delta, gamma, vega and call and put GEX once the expiry close is 15:40 (the app's value for that date); with a 15:30 close the differences are about 5e-3, which shows how much the close time matters. Levels: peak 22,700, call wall 23,000, put wall 22,500, gamma flip 22,672.76 and net GEX 1,069.33 agree with the server's own rebuild (flip 22,672.76, net 1,069.32; the server totals agree with the independent script to better than 1e-5). The existing frontend and backend parity tests pass. Candle alignment for every timeframe on the bar (1, 3, 5, 15 minutes from 09:15) is covered by a hand-counted test. **Conventions to keep in mind (not errors):** net GEX assumes calls positive and puts negative (the market long calls, short puts), which is a convention, not an observed position; gamma flip uses each contract's own implied volatility held fixed; the Greeks use the carry implied by put-call parity at the nearest strikes. **Not re-verified here:** live stored one-minute series in market hours (the series builders were hand-checked in D240 and D241 and are unchanged).

## D257 (23 Nov 2026): Smart OI divergence indicators (eight), 3 and 5 minute candles
**What:** a panel under the Smart OI chart (`components/dashboard/SmartOiDivergence.tsx`, logic in `lib/smartOiDivergence.ts`, plan in `docs/smart-oi-divergence-plan.md`). Seven indicators and a composite: (1) flow divergence: price against the day's cumulative Smart OI at confirmed swing highs and lows (regular and hidden; a swing is known only after 3 candles on 3-minute, 2 on 5-minute candles; failed when price goes beyond the swing); (2) writing or buying: each ATM +-10 strike's OI change read with its premium change after taking out delta x the index move (OI up with the premium down is writing, up is buying, OI down with the premium up is covering, down is unwinding), against the direction the Smart OI label gives, two candles in a row; (3) conviction: new OI per option contract traded against the day's usual level read from the candles before the window (churn: a move of 0.15 % or more on at most half the usual; absorption: positions at 1.5 times the usual with no move); (4) PCR: OI PCR against volume PCR and against price over 30 minutes; (5) the NIFTY future's build-up against the direction of Smart OI; (6) zone: put OI added below spot against call OI added above, against price; (7) momentum: the smoothed speed of Smart OI against price, and readings unusual for the day (robust z-score from the earlier candles); (8) composite: a direction needs three active indicators and a lead of two. An evidence grade (A, B, C) drops when gap, strike-change, stand-in-price or stalled-feed flags sit in the window. Nothing looks ahead; a missing input leaves an indicator silent and says why. **Shown for 3 and 5 minute candles only** (the owner's choice), the same on expiry day as on other days. **Tests:** 36 hand-counted cases (each indicator, each refusal, no look-ahead, the strike classification from premiums) plus the panel. **Research (owner approved a read-only look at the stored sessions):** `scripts/smart-oi-export.py` reads the app's own API on the server (GET only) and `frontend/src/test/smart-oi-research.test.ts` measures the forward NIFTY move after each signal against the move after any candle, on 7 stored sessions (first 5 to fit, last 2 to judge), Wilson 95 % intervals, verdict only with 30 or more judging cases and the interval above the baseline. **Result in `docs/smart-oi-research.md`: no indicator is supported yet** (most have too few cases; writing or buying has about 35 to 40 cases per direction and a hit rate level with the baseline). So the panel says "indicator, not validated" and carries no weight in the intelligence summary or alerts. Re-run the research as sessions accumulate (about 5 a week). **Limits:** the totals use the whole chain, the per-strike indicators use ATM +-10 of the day's opening strike; candle changes are differences of two one-minute samples; cases inside one session are not independent. **Not seen:** the panel in a browser against live data.

## D258 (23 Nov 2026): bars rounded on the outer end, GEX by strike drawn on one side
**Defect from D255 (owner's screenshots):** the shared chart look rounded the tops of every bar with one fixed radius, so a bar below zero (or to the left of it) was rounded at the zero axis and looked upside down. It now rounds the end away from the axis, per bar: up for a positive vertical bar, down for a negative one, right and left for sideways bars (`barRadius` in `lib/chartTheme.ts`); a stacked series is left square, and a radius the chart set itself (on the series or on a bar) is kept. **GEX by strike:** the absolute-size bar was drawn to the right while the net bar of a negative strike went to the left, so one strike showed two bars on opposite sides; the absolute bar is now drawn on the same side as the net bar, the net bar sits inside it, and its tooltip still shows the size without a sign. **GEX profile:** the absolute line was the same blue as the positive bars (two legend entries in one colour); it is now a dark slate. **Tests:** the radius for positive, negative, sideways, pair-shaped, stacked and self-chosen cases. **Seen:** a three-chart gallery on a phone-sized screen. **Not seen:** every real page (the other bar charts follow the same rule automatically).

## D259 (23 Nov 2026): the header's "Held" time was the time of a replayed quote, so it changed with every restart
**Cause:** after the close the header showed "Held 23:15:21 IST" (and earlier "Held 15:20:40 IST"): different times for the same price. The broker sends the last known quote again whenever the backend restarts or the feed reconnects, and the browser stamped that tick with the time it was received. Each deploy or reconnect therefore produced a new "held" time for a price that had not changed since the Friday close. **Fix:** a held price is shown only for a tick received on a weekday between 09:00 and 15:45 IST while the exchange does not say the market is closed (`isLiveTickTime`, `useSpotDisplay`): the pre-open hold at 09:08 to 09:15 still works. Outside that, and on a holiday, the header shows the stored snapshot with its own time, and its date when it is not from today ("20 Nov, 15:39:04 IST"), so a Friday price is not read as today's. **Tests:** the Sunday-night replay, the holiday case, the boundaries of the hours, and the existing pre-open tests unchanged. **Not seen:** the header after the next restart.

## D261 (23 Nov 2026): Reset clears the builder, two-colour MTM, the running strategy pinned on Home
**Strategy Builder:** the New button is gone. Reset now removes every leg on the page, including legs only being checked and not saved, the name and the what-if controls, and shows the default start page; saved and closed strategies are not touched (a saved strategy is unloaded, not deleted). It is disabled when there is nothing to clear. (It used to undo edits; that is no longer offered.) **MTM chart:** green area above the zero line, red below it, and the line itself in the same two colours. Between two stored samples on opposite sides of zero the crossing is interpolated and added as a point at exactly zero (`splitAtZero`), so green and red meet where the line crosses, not at the next sample; a missing minute stays a gap, no crossing is invented across it. The value axis always includes zero and ends on round numbers. **Home:** while a strategy is running (at least one ticked leg open with an entry price) a card sits under the market summary with its name, open legs, P&L now (large, green or red), booked and open parts, and the same MTM chart; a link opens the builder. The P&L is the server's own (`GET /api/strategies/running/pnl`, `StrategyAutomation.pnl_now`): booked (earlier exits and cycles) plus open legs marked at the server's live prices times the lot multiplier, before costs, so it is right with no strategy page open and includes a future; a leg without a live price leaves the total empty and is named, nothing is guessed; outside the session it says the prices are the last ones. Nothing is shown when no strategy is running. **Tests:** Reset (clears unsaved legs, no New, saved strategy kept), crossing points by hand, the P&L by hand (-1,000 booked, +650, -650, +3,250 open = 2,250; lot multiplier 2; missing price; closed), the pinned card (green, red, missing price, market closed, absent). **Seen:** the two-colour chart from the real 20 Nov session, day and night. **Not seen:** the pinned card on the live Home page with a real running strategy.

## D263 (23 Nov 2026): the OI bars of the Strategy Builder chain
The bars were thick faint pills with no number, scaled against the larger of the two sides, so a column's biggest put or call could look small and a missing value looked like a small bar. `components/OiBar.tsx` now draws, per side, the open interest as a number above a thin bar on a faint track. The Option Chain page's table shows OI as numbers only and was not changed.

## D264 (23 Nov 2026): centred candles on the Smart OI chart; one marker per run of divergence signals
**Candles:** the NIFTY candles (and the Smart OI bars) used ECharts' built-in candlestick, which snaps the body edges and the wick to whole pixels separately: with an odd body width the wick was half a pixel off the middle of the body, which on a phone (candles a few pixels wide) read as off-centre wicks. Both are now a custom series (`candleSeries` in `lib/chartOptions.ts`, `CustomChart` registered): the wick is drawn on the exact centre of the category and the body is centred on it, at any zoom or screen; a doji keeps a 1 px body; a missing candle draws nothing; the grey outlined stand-in candle of the closing session is kept; the Smart OI bar is the body only. **Divergence chart:** a rolling indicator fires on every candle while its condition lasts, so the marker chart showed over a hundred triangles piled on the price line and the list repeated the same sentence every 3 minutes. `collapseBursts` makes one entry for a run of the same indicator and direction (signals within 3 candles of each other): its start, length, last candle and strongest reading; the report keeps every signal, collapsing is only for drawing and listing. Markers are smaller, bullish ones sit just under the price line and bearish ones just above it, the list shows the time range and a count (x3), and a caption says how to read the chart. **Tests:** candle geometry for bands from 3 to 12 px (wick centre = body centre, exact), colours, doji, missing, outline, body-only, the series is drawn; bursts by hand. **Seen:** the real 20 Nov session on a phone-size screen. **Not seen:** the live page at 3 and 5 minutes with today's data.

## D265 (23 Nov 2026): the implied-volatility smile on the Greeks page, explained
The smile chart (Greeks and What-If, Volatility surface) had no explanation, a legend that said little ("Other side (not used)"), red diamonds that covered the market dots, and stray quotes printing 100 % or more that squashed the curve into the lower third of the chart. **Now:** an "In plain words" box above the chart, built by `smileReading` from the surface itself: the at-the-money volatility (read from the fitted line at the forward), the line 5 % below and above the forward, which side is richer (a difference above one point either way; below that, level), what the fit gap means (under 0.5 vol points a tight fit, under 1.5 fair, above that loose), whether the butterfly and calendar checks pass, what the grey dots are and how many, and how many quotes are flagged or rejected. A heading and one sentence explain each of the two charts. **Chart:** the legend says what each marker is (market quote used in the fit, flagged because it breaks a no-arbitrage rule, not used because far or on the other side); flagged quotes are small hollow diamonds; the fitted line sits above the dots; the y axis starts at 0 and stops at 1.6 times the highest fitted volatility (rounded up to 5), and the number of quotes above the top is stated under the chart instead of being drawn; the forward line is labelled. **Tests:** interpolation and the top of the axis by hand, and every sentence of the reading for a hand-built smile (richer puts, level, richer calls, loose fit, failed check, no forward). **Seen:** a synthetic smile shaped like the owner's on a phone-size screen. **Not seen:** the live surface.
