If the card is still missing on a page after pressing "Show chart info", tell me which page.

## D267 (23 Nov 2026): the what-if moved into the Strategy Builder; the Greeks page is Advanced Greeks
**Owner's request:** the Strategy Builder already had a spot target, a date and a volatility shift and a spot-by-date grid; the Greeks and What-If page had the smile-based version. They are merged in the builder and the page is renamed. **Builder:** the Scenarios tab now carries `BuilderWhatIf` under its grid: the engine's scenario (index, volatility, time and rate moves, the volatility rule: moneyness, strike or delta), the presets, the risk matrix (spot by volatility, spot by time) and the payoff, computed on the surface fitted to the stored chain for the strategy's open option legs (lots as shown, multiplier applied). **Futures:** the engine prices options only, so the NIFTY future is added on top: rupees per index point = signed lots x multiplier x lot size (`futureUnits`), added to every matrix and payoff cell as units x the row's index shock, and to the scenario and each preset as units x its spot shock. A future moves one for one with the index (the engine's convention: the forward moves by the points at an unchanged basis); time, volatility and rate moves do not change it. A strategy with only a future has no option legs to ask the engine about and says so. **Advanced Greeks:** the page (`/advanced-greeks`, the old address redirects) keeps the legs editor, the rupee Greeks by leg, expiry and limit, the gamma map and the volatility surface; the scenario panel, the matrix and the payoff are gone from it, and it points to the Builder's Scenarios tab. The Builder's link and the tools menu use the new name. **Tests:** the moved tests (scenario result, presets, matrix, payoff, error messages) run against `BuilderWhatIf`; the futures units, grid, scenario and preset adjustments by hand (a sold lot of 65 is -65 per point; gap down 2 %: engine -25,801 plus -65 x -510 = +7,349); the Builder's Scenarios tab; the route and the menu name. **Plan for institutional additions:** `docs/advanced-greeks-plan.md` (ladders, delta hedge sheet, gamma-theta breakeven, higher-order Greeks, vega by smile factor, attribution, smile analytics, implied against realised, VaR and stress), in four phases, awaiting the owner's order. **Not seen:** the new Scenarios tab on the live page.

## D268 (23 Nov 2026): Advanced Greeks built, all four phases, on the Strategy Builder's strategy only
**Owner's order:** build every item of `docs/advanced-greeks-plan.md` one after another; analyse only the Builder's strategy (not legs typed on the page); include a one-day VaR and expected shortfall, labelled a model estimate.
**Scope:** the page no longer has its own legs editor. Its position is the Builder's running strategy (`/api/strategies/running`): open, ticked legs, futures included, quantity = signed lots x multiplier x lot size (`lib/builderPosition.ts`). Each option keeps the volatility its own stored price implies (sticky strike); the carry is the chain's. The engine's panels (rupee Greeks, gamma map, surface) stay below and still price options only.
**Maths (`lib/advancedGreeks.ts`, pure, Black-76):** closed forms for vanna (-k phi(d1) d2 / sigma), volga (vega d1 d2 / sigma) and charm (-(d delta / dT) per calendar day, d delta / dT = (c-r) delta + k phi(d1) d(d1)/dT); each is checked in the tests against a finite difference of the Black-76 price (delta, gamma 1e-6, vega, vanna, volga, charm, calls and puts) and by put-call parity. Ladder (-5 % to +5 %), delta hedge sheet (futures lots = -delta / lot size, now and at +-1 %, +-2 %, no cost estimated), gamma-theta breakeven m = sqrt(2|theta|/|gamma|) against the implied daily move S sigma / sqrt(365) and the realised one, vega by expiry, by strike zone and by three defined smile shapes (level, skew, curvature: shapes defined in the page text, not fitted parameters), P&L by Greek since entry with the residual shown (a leg needs an entry price and a stored index level at the entry time, so today's entries only; others are listed as skipped), 25-delta risk reversal and butterfly by expiry (linear in delta), forward volatility, implied against close-to-close realised volatility over 5, 10, 20 sessions (no percentile rank until more sessions are stored), vanna and charm exposure by strike of the market (calls +, puts -, an assumption stated on screen).
**Risk figures:** one-day VaR and ES at 95 % and 99 % by seeded Monte Carlo full revaluation (5,000 lognormal paths, mulberry32 + Box-Muller, sticky strike, one day of decay, sigma = the nearest leg expiry's ATM implied volatility) with the delta-only closed form beside it as a check; stress grid (+-1, 2, 3 one-day sd x volatility -5, 0, +5 points), the three largest falls and rises in the stored closes replayed, and reverse stress (smallest move down and up losing 25,000, 50,000, 100,000, bisection, searched to +-15 %). All labelled estimates on a normal distribution that understates gaps.
**Not available, listed on the page:** cost of unwinding (no depth stored), margin and SPAN, funding, live broker P&L.
**Tests:** `advanced-greeks.test.ts` (19: finite differences, parity, hand-calculated breakeven 316.23, forward volatility, 25-delta quotes by hand, future attribution 65 x 100 = 6,500, attribution identity, VaR delta-normal = 1.645 x 65 x S sigma / sqrt(252) with Monte Carlo within 4 %, reverse stress -0.4 % at 25,000, realised volatility by hand), `advanced-panels.test.tsx` (position from a synthetic chain priced by Black-76, every section renders, empty and no-index states), `greeks-page.test.tsx` updated. **Not verified:** the page against a live market-hours chain; the realised-volatility closes come from `/api/intraday/iv-chart`.

## D269 (23 Nov 2026): Writing-or-buying read a fast fall as bullish; fixed
**Owner's report:** the index was down 0.7 % to 0.8 % and the indicator said bullish at 09:24 ("calls added faster, but the premiums say calls were bought"). **Cause (mine):** the flow classifier took the premium change left over after delta x index move. In a fast move that remainder holds the curvature (1/2 gamma x move^2, positive for every option) and the rise in volatility, both of which lift calls and puts alike. So calls with OI added looked bought (bullish) and puts with OI added looked bought (bearish), and with more call OI than put OI added the net came out bullish: an artefact of the model, not of the market. **Fix (`buildStrikeFlow`):** the explained part now includes the gamma term, and the median relative repricing across all contracts of the candle (the common volatility move, needs six or more contracts) is removed before a contract is called written or bought. **Test:** 100-point fall, volatility up 2 points, every strike repriced by Black-76, three times as much call OI added as put OI: the old logic gave a bias above the 0.25 threshold (the test fails on it), the new one leaves it below. **Not verified:** the stored 20 and 23 Nov sessions have not been re-run with the new rule, so the other indicators' hit rates are unchanged and still unvalidated.

## D270 (23 Nov 2026): divergence indicators audited against the day's stored strikes; three faults fixed
**Owner's report (09:50 IST):** the index was down 0.6 %, Smart OI candles red, yet Writing or buying and Momentum showed bullish, and the chart showed two opposite arrows on one candle. **Audit:** I fetched today's stored per-strike series (expiry day, 40 samples, 41 strikes) and replayed the classifier. **Fault 1, Writing or buying (mine, second part of D269):** after D269 the residual still mixed in a drift that is common to the whole chain: option prices follow a forward slightly different from the index we read (calls up, puts down on every strike) and a common volatility move. Calls with OI added then read as bought and puts as written at once. Also the old residual ignored the time decay of the candle (large on expiry day) and used a delta-gamma approximation that fails on a 100-point move. Now each premium is re-priced exactly for the new index level and the time passed (Black-76 at the volatility it had), then a forward shift and a common volatility move are fitted across every contract of the candle (least squares on delta and vega, outliers trimmed once, needs eight contracts) and removed; only what a strike did on its own is read as writing or buying. On today's data (3-minute candles) the 09:21 lean fell from +1.00 (delta-gamma version) to +0.31, and the 09:42 lean from -1.00 to -0.20; the leans of the twelve candles now run from -0.20 to +0.58, ten of them inside +-0.35, with 09:18 (+0.58) and 09:24 (+0.56) still bullish: the data still shows call OI added alongside call premiums that held up better than the chain did, which I cannot tell from real call buying. The minimum lean for the signal went from 0.25 to 0.5 because 8 of 12 neutral candles had |bias| up to 0.35. **Fault 2, Momentum divergence:** it looked at the slope of the smoothed Smart OI only, so Smart OI going from -3 Cr to -1 Cr (calls still being added faster than puts, only less so) in a fall was called bullish. It now needs the smoothed level on the side of the signal (above zero to be bullish, below to be bearish). **Fault 3, markers:** signals starting on the same candle each drew an arrow, so opposite arrows appeared together. One marker per candle now: the shared direction, or a neutral dot when they disagree; the tooltip lists all. **Wording:** each reading says "bullish divergence", and a line explains that a divergence is a disagreement with price (bullish in a fall means something under the fall is turning up), a warning and not a forecast. **Tests:** three regression tests built from Black-76 prices (100-point fall with volatility up and more call OI added; a forward 20 points above the index; momentum below and above zero), each of which fails on the old logic; marker grouping. **Not verified:** whether any indicator predicts anything (still none validated); the other indicators were read for design faults only, no change was needed for Flow, Conviction, PCR, Future against options and Zone beyond the clearer wording; the page was not looked at after the change.

## D272 (23 Nov 2026): MTM graph is live, tick by tick, with four plain tiles
**Owner's request:** simple live MTM, max high, max low, drawdown; the graph on live ticks. The old tiles (Now (stored), Live now, Peak, Trough, Max drawdown) disagreed in a new position: one stored sample read -65 while the live value was +65, and peak, trough and now were that one sample. **Now:** the line is the stored one-minute history before the page was opened, then every change of the live value seen on the page (a point is added only when the value changes; thinned past 6,000 points; kept per strategy in the browser's session storage so leaving the page keeps the day). A position entered after the last stored sample is drawn from live ticks alone. The tiles are Live MTM, Max high, Max low and Drawdown (largest fall from a running high to a later low, with times to the second), all read from the drawn line. **Known limit:** where the stored history meets the first live point the two can differ slightly (the stored price is the last trade at the sampling second), and that step counts in high, low and drawdown; points seen only while the page is closed are not live ticks (the server-side running strategy has no tick history). Stored P&L at the server (Home pinned card) uses the same component. **Tests:** appendTick, joinLive and mtmStats by hand (0, 300, 100, 250, -50, 80 gives high 300, low -50, drawdown 350); a fresh position drawn from live ticks (65, -130, +200, -20: high +200, low -130, drawdown 220). **Not seen:** the live page.

## D273 (23 Nov 2026): the closing auction's indicative index is no longer shown as the NIFTY price
**Owner's report:** around the closing auction the app showed NIFTY down about 950 points, the broker about 430. **Finding (stored logs and bars, 23 Nov):** continuous cash trading ended 15:15 IST; the traded level was 25,366 (-430 on the previous close 25,796). From 15:20 the exchange's index feed carried its indicative closing value: 25,073, a low of 24,847 at 15:20-15:21 (-950), then a climb to 25,332 by 15:26 and 25,349 by 15:29 as the order book filled. The app's own guards worked (the feed-drop counter, `ind`-flagged hollow bars, the option-implied replacement of the stale snapshot index 25,366 -> 25,398), but the header, and every screen that read the live index, showed the indicative value as the price. It is real exchange output, not a feed glitch (the same behaviour as 19 Nov, D-numbers in `app/market/index_check.py`); the broker app keeps the last traded value in the window. **Fix:** in the indicative window (15:20-15:30 IST until 4 Dec 2026, 15:31-15:40 from 7 Dec) the displayed index stays the last traded level seen in the continuous session; the auction value is shown beside it in a labelled chip (`Closing auction, indicative X`, with a tooltip saying it is not a traded price). If the page was opened inside the window with no traded level seen, the stored snapshot is shown instead. `useLiveSpot` (strategy builder, live Greeks) is not live in the window, so they fall back to the snapshot rather than price off the auction estimate. **Not changed:** the stored one-minute index bars and Smart OI candles still draw the indicative bars hollow, as before; the server's own `_live_price` and the day-audit already skip the window. **Tests:** window edges by date (15:19 no, 15:20 yes, 15:30 no; 8 Dec 15:30 no, 15:31 yes; Sunday no), badge holds 25,366.40 while the auction prints 24,855.85, snapshot when opened inside the window, normal again after it. **Not seen:** the live page at the next closing auction (24 Nov 15:20).

## D274 (23 Nov 2026): the NIFTY index is not collected after cash trading ends; OI and volume go on to the F&O close
**Owner's decision** (after D273): stop the NIFTY spot charts at 15:15 and from 7 Dec at 15:30; show the auction price at the top only, do not collect it; keep OI, volume and the rest to the real F&O close (15:40, 15:45 from 7 Dec), not 15:30; for screens that need a spot after the cut, the last traded level frozen at the cut. **Rule (`spot_end(day)` in `domain/session.py`, `spotEndFor` in `lib/session.ts`):** continuous cash trading end, 15:15 until 4 Dec 2026 and 15:30 from 7 Dec, none before 3 Aug 2026. **Backend:** `observe_index` makes no one-minute index bar from that time (the minute before it is flushed and kept); `index_bars()` no longer adds stand-in bars from the future and does not serve the indicative bars stored before this change (they stay on disk, nothing deleted); the chain snapshot service keeps the last level seen during cash trading for the day and, from the cut, stores it as the capture's index level with a note ("Index not collected in the closing auction ... last traded one, X at 15:14") and the broker's value in `underlying_ltp_broker`; option prices, OI and volume are stored exactly as before; a capture in the auction with no earlier level that day (a restart) keeps the existing parity check. **Frontend:** the Smart OI builder reads no spot (bar or stored sample) from the cut, so the held flat level is not read as a stalled feed and no price candle is drawn; the OI and volume candles continue to the F&O close; the quality grade does not count the missing price after the cut as a fault; the premium-flow indicator (writing or buying) is silent without a price for the candle, so the price-based indicators go quiet after the cut and the OI and volume ones continue; the closing-auction note on the chart now says the price stops at the cut. The header still shows the last traded level with the indicative value in its labelled chip (D273). **Checked on the stored 23 Nov data:** 129 candles, from 15:15 to 15:39 no price, OI bars present at every candle to 15:39, no stall, jump, stand-in or indicative bar reported. **Tests:** backend `test_spot_auction.py` (cut by date, 15:15 and 15:30; no bar or stand-in from the cut; old auction bars kept on disk and not served; held level with the note; no held level after a restart; level of another day not carried; 7 Dec cut 15:30), the flush test moved to 15:14; frontend tests for the Smart OI cut, quality and flow; five old tests that asserted the auction-window behaviours (stall in the window, stand-in minutes, indicative minutes, closing constant) were rewritten to the new contract, not weakened: they now assert that nothing is read from the cut and that a frozen run before the cut is still a stall. **Not done:** the Gamma and Vega pages and the IV pages take the held level from the snapshots without a visible label (their stamps show the snapshot time); the stored history from before today keeps its auction values on disk. **Not seen:** the next closing auction live (24 Nov 15:15). Deploy after the close (a restart in the auction would lose the held level for that day).

## D275 (23 Nov 2026): a closed strategy came back in the builder; the server's running strategy is now the truth
**Owner's report:** a short straddle (25600 CE and PE sold 10:36 IST) with an extra 25500 CE sold 13:02; the PE stopped at 13:24; the extra CE exited by hand 14:19; the strategy closed at 15:20 and was filed under Closed at 15:20:54; later the builder showed another strategy. **Stored record (UTC):** saved copy revisions 05:07, 07:33 (extra CE), 07:54 (PE stop), 08:49 (extra CE out), 09:50 (CE closed by the 15:20 rule), closed record 09:50:54, saved copy deleted by the same step. The closed record and its P&L are right (+5,086 - 5,694 + 1,284 = +676 before costs). At 11:57:02 UTC (17:27 IST) the running document was overwritten by the straddle of 20 Nov (`straddle with s57`, 25550 CE and PE, no exits, rearm time of 20 Nov), with the server's current revision. **Cause:** two holes. (1) On opening, the page took the server's running strategy only when it had legs; with none (closed and reset) it kept the working copy of this browser and sent it to the server on its first change, with the server's current revision, so the conflict check passed. That working copy refreshes its own date on every visit, so the three-day limit never retires it; a browser that holds an old straddle writes it back each time the server's strategy is empty (the same straddle was re-created on 22 Nov, a Sunday, and valued on old prices). (2) When the server's running strategy is empty it has nothing to compare with (`keep_exits` returns at once), so an old copy is accepted. **Which device sent it is not in the log** (PUT requests are not logged); the page-side reproduction below is the same path. **Fix, page:** with a server document present and no usable running strategy (none, no legs, or its expiry has passed) the page takes that as the truth, drops its own working copy and does not send it; the working copy now only seeds a server that holds nothing at all. **Fix, server:** a PUT that carries an open leg identical to a leg of a Closed strategy (contract, side, entry price and entry time) is refused (409, the page then takes the server's state); a re-entry or a new trade has a new entry time and passes; the server's own current legs are exempt. **Also:** `pnl_now` reports a strategy past its last expiry as not running (the automation checks already skipped it), so the home page no longer shows an expired straddle's P&L. **Tests:** page test reproduces the case (empty server strategy, an old working copy in the browser: nothing shown, nothing sent; it fails without the fix) and the seeding case; backend: closed legs cannot return, new entry time passes, own legs pass, expired strategy not running. **Left as it is, to decide:** the stale `straddle with s57` document is still in the server's running slot until Reset is pressed (it is skipped by the checks and now by the home card, and the page clears it on the next open); not changed: opening a closed trade for viewing writes its (exited) legs to the running slot like any edit; the leg ids of a saved strategy are renewed on each save (matching then falls back to contract and entry price); the move to Closed happens only when a page is open (54 s after the close today). **Not verified:** the next live day; which device wrote the stale copy.

## D276 (23 Nov 2026): more strategy-builder faults found in the same audit, fixed
**Opening a closed trade replaced the live strategy (found by a test; it failed on the old code).** The Strategy portfolio page hands a closed trade to the builder; the page then sent those (exited) legs to the server as the running strategy with the current revision, so a running strategy's legs, stops and rules on the server were overwritten, and pressing Reset to leave the view then sent an empty strategy. **Fix:** a closed trade opened for viewing is never sent to the server; leaving the view (Reset) brings the server's running strategy back (or an empty page when there is none) and sends nothing. **Update button:** when the strategy differs from its saved copy, or is not saved yet, the button is now amber, ringed and says "unsaved" or "not saved" (before, a dot only); the owner saw no sign after adding the extra 25500 call. **Reviewed and left as designed:** a saved strategy gets new leg ids each time it is loaded (a new running instance; the server matches on contract and entry price when ids differ); the move to Closed of a strategy the server closed while no page was open is done by the page the next time the saved list loads (the existing `auto-` filing), not by the server, to avoid two writers filing the same trade. **Tests:** the closed-trade test (viewing sends nothing, Reset restores the live strategy) fails without the fix; the full frontend suite (1,279) and the build pass. **SSH:** the connection to the server timed out once around 18:30 IST and worked on every retry a few minutes later (uptime 1 day, load 0.1, no ban in fail2ban: 0 banned now, 9 ever; sshd allows 10 unauthenticated connections at a time and the server is hit by internet scans of user names every few minutes); the cause of the single timeout is not identified from the server side (likely a network blip between this machine and the host).

## D277 (23 Nov 2026): gaps in the fixed straddle line (Market Intelligence, premium change since the open)
**Owner's question:** dashes and gaps in the fixed and managed lines from about 13:20 to 15:25. **Cause:** the fixed strike was 25900 (nearest spot at 09:15). On expiry day the index fell 430 to 530 points, so the 25900 put was deep in the money. Such a contract trades rarely: in 22 of 387 stored samples its last traded price was at or below its own intrinsic value (for example 427.0 against 25900 minus 25467.95 = 432.05 at 13:57; 507.65 against 533.6 at 15:18). No Black-76 implied volatility exists for a price at or below intrinsic, so the model built no leg for it, and the premium series took the model's leg price: missing. The raw stored price existed in every one of the 387 samples (checked on the server's stored series: no missing put or call price at 25900). The gap was the model's, not the data's. **Fix (`intelligence/straddle.py`, `leg_series`):** where the model has no leg, the stored last traded price is used (before, only for samples too close to expiry); the premium and straddle series run unbroken, the volatility and Greeks of those samples stay empty (never filled), and `quality.price_only_samples_fixed` counts them. Attribution and efficiency are unchanged (they skip a step without a model on both ends, as before). **Limit, stated:** such a deep in-the-money price can lag the index by a few points, so the fixed line in those minutes carries that lag; the strike has also gone stale as a reference (the page already says the opening strike is 'mostly direction, not decay' once it is 100 points from spot). **Test:** a put priced 195 against an intrinsic of 200 keeps its price and straddle, has no volatility, and is counted; it fails without the fix. **Not seen:** the page after the fix (the live server still shows the gaps until deployed).

## D278 (23 Nov 2026): the MTM graph is saved with a closed strategy and opened from the Strategy portfolio page
**Owner's request:** the strategy closed at 15:20 and the MTM graph was missed; save the graph with the strategy when it closes and give the portfolio page an MTM graph button. **Record:** a Closed record now carries `mtm` (time and P&L points, thinned to 900 keeping first, last, highest and lowest, rounded to paise; `complete`, `source`, `savedAt`). **At closing** the page stores the live ticks it drew (kept in this browser's session storage under a key made from the first entry and the leg ids, so exits no longer change it). **Completion:** a closed record whose graph is not complete (closed on a page with no ticks, filed by the page after a server close, or older than this change) is built once from the stored one-minute history from the entry to a minute past the close (strike series per expiry, the future's series), joined to the live ticks before it, and saved with the record on the server (the builder does it for up to two records per pass, the portfolio page when its graph is opened). The history is kept 30 days; a record older than 35 days is not tried, and with no stored prices the page says so. **Portfolio page:** each closed row has an `MTM graph` button that opens the graph under the row (green above zero, red below, exit marks, tiles: At the close, Max high, Max low, Drawdown, and the source). **Graph rule changed (also the live MTM tab):** the line starts at the FIRST entry and each leg counts from its own entry (before, it started when the last leg went in, so a strategy with an extra leg added at 13:02 showed nothing before 13:02). A leg closed by Exit all books its result at the closing time. **Checked on the real record of today (Straddle based s60, 3 legs):** built from the server's stored prices, 285 points from 10:37 to 15:20; the last point is +₹676.00, equal to the booked P&L on the record; high +1,917.5 at 11:37, low -1,537.25 at 13:53 (drawdown 3,454.75). **Tests:** hand sums for the exit-all booking, the stored-plus-live join, the thinning, the saved-graph view, the on-demand build, the button on the portfolio page, the live ticks kept at closing; the existing MTM tests were updated to the new start rule (first entry). **Not seen:** the portfolio page in the browser. **Limit:** a graph built from stored samples has one point a minute and no ticks between; for strategies closed from now on, the ticks the page saw are in it.

## D279 (23 Nov 2026): a scorecard of the day's divergence signals on the Smart OI page
**Why:** on 23 Nov (NIFTY -2.0 %) the markers showed about 16 bullish divergences; replayed on the stored data the bullish ones were mostly wrong 15 minutes later (future/bullish 0 of 2, PCR/bullish 0 of 2, momentum/bullish 2 of 3 with an average move of zero, zone/bullish 2 of 6), the bearish ones mostly right (zone 2 of 2, future 3 of 3). A divergence points against price, so a one-way day repeats the opposite signal; nothing in the calculation was wrong. **Scorecard (`scoreBursts`, shown under the price markers):** each burst (the start of a run of one signal) is judged once, by the index 15 minutes after the candle it started on (5 candles of 3 minutes, 3 of 5), against that candle's own close: right if the index moved the way the signal pointed, wrong the other way, flat if unchanged; a signal with no index price at either end (after the cash close there is none) or too recent is counted as not judged yet, never as a move. Rows for bullish and bearish with count, right, wrong, not judged and the average move in the signal's direction; an expandable list by indicator; neutral dots only counted. The note on the page says a handful of signals proves nothing and that on a trending day the opposite signal is wrong again and again. **No threshold was changed:** 8 stored sessions are too few to tune on. **Tests:** by hand (bullish 100 to 99 wrong; bearish 101 to 98 right at +2.97 %; bullish 102 to 97 wrong at -4.90 %; one too recent; one neutral), missing prices, a flat index, and the panel shows it. **Not seen:** the page in the browser.

## D280 (23 Nov 2026): divergence signals in the Smart OI chart, on 1, 3 and 5 minute candles
**Owner's request:** put the divergence marker graph with the Smart OI graphs in place of the volume panel, and make it follow the timeframe (1, 3 and 5 minute signals), done sensibly. **Chart:** the lower panel of the Smart OI chart (price candles, Smart OI bars, then volume) now shows, by default, the NIFTY close with one marker per candle that started a signal (bullish under the line, bearish above it, neutral on it, hollow once a flow divergence failed, a dashed line between the two swings of a flow divergence), on the same candle axis as the two panels above: one crosshair, one zoom, the tooltip of a candle lists the signals that started on it. A `Divergence signals | Volume` switch above the chart brings the volume bars back (the volume is also in the lower-right chart); on 15 minute candles the indicators are not run and the panel stays volume. The panel is taller (price scale) and the chart 800 px. After the cash close there is no index price, so the line stops at 15:15 (15:30 from 7 Dec) while the OI bars go on; a signal on such a candle has nowhere to sit and is in the list below (23 Nov: the future indicator at 15:21 to 15:39). **One data source:** `useDivergence` feeds both the chart and the cards (indicators, scorecard, signals in words, notes) below it, so they cannot disagree; the cards no longer draw their own chart. **1 minute:** the indicators now run on 1 minute candles with values that mean the same stretch of time as the 3 minute ones they were built with (`TFP`): a swing needs 8 candles each side (3 min: 3 candles, 9 min), two swings 9 candles apart, the momentum average 15 candles (5), writing or buying must hold 6 candles in a row (2 = 6 minutes), conviction needs 24 candles of history (8); the 30 minute window, the 180 minute swing limit and the percentage thresholds were already in minutes. 3 and 5 minute behaviour is unchanged (same numbers as before). **On 23 Nov's stored data:** 1 minute 385 candles and 46 signal groups, 3 minute 129 and 24, 5 minute 77 and 21; the same pattern on all three, bearish signals right 18 of 22, 8 of 10 and 8 of 10 (15 minutes later) on a day NIFTY fell 2 %, bullish ones wrong 15 of 24, 9 of 14 and 6 of 11: the scorecard shows this on the page; no threshold was tuned. **Tests:** the time-equivalence of the 1 minute values, momentum on 1 minute candles, no flow divergence from 3 candle wiggles on 1 minute candles (the same series is one on 5 minutes), the lower panel (volume unchanged without signals; with them: close line, markers per direction, hollow failed one, a candle with no price not drawn, one axis, tooltip, link line). **Seen:** the chart rendered in the browser on the stored 23 Nov data at 1, 3 and 5 minutes (panels aligned, markers readable); the widget itself (switch, live data) not seen.

## D281 (23 Nov 2026): the Closed list no longer reorders when a record is saved again
**Owner's report:** clicking MTM graph on a closed strategy moved that strategy to the top of the list (the three opened that day sat first, in the order they were opened). **Cause (mine, D278):** opening a graph that had to be built saves the record again; the server lists strategies by the time of the last write, so the record moved to the front. **Fix:** the portfolio page and the builder sort Closed newest first by `closedAt`, whatever order the server returns them in (`sortClosed`). **Test:** records returned by the server oldest first are shown newest first (it fails without the fix). The cumulative column was already computed in order of closing and does not change.

## D282 (23 Nov 2026): Save layout and Reset on the Smart OI widget
**Owner's request:** a Save layout option on Smart OI: change the candle length or switch a graph off, click Save layout and the same view comes back; Reset returns it to normal. **What a layout holds** (`lib/smartOiLayout.ts`, kept in this browser under `nse.smartoi.layout.v1`, the view only, never data): the candle length (1, 3, 5, 15), the strikes summed, the lower panel of the main chart (divergence signals or volume), the lower-right chart (PCR or volume), and the graphs switched off in each of the three charts (legend entries, by name). **Buttons** next to the strike choice: Save layout (amber with a dot while the view differs from the saved one or the standard one; "Layout saved ✓" and disabled when it matches; disabled on the standard view) and Reset (standard view: 3 minute candles, the whole chain, signals and PCR panels, every graph on; it also forgets the saved layout, so the next visit is standard; disabled when there is nothing to reset). **Mechanics:** the chart wrapper takes `legendOff` (entries to keep switched off, re-applied after every refresh; an entry taken out of the list is switched on again) and `onLegendChange` (what the legend holds after a click); a legend change keeps the entries that are off in the other panel mode (Put volume switched off, then looking at the signals, is not forgotten). A stored layout is checked field by field when read; an unusable field falls back to the standard value. **Tests:** the parse, compare and merge functions; the widget: standard view has nothing to save or reset, a changed candle length lights Save, saving survives reopening the page, a graph switched off plus the lower panel are saved and come back, Reset restores the standard view and removes the saved layout. **Seen in a browser** on the real chart and the stored 23 Nov data: a graph in the saved list is off after load (Put volume greyed, only the call bars drawn), taking it out of the list brings the bars back, and a real legend click is reported (Call volume off). The widget with its buttons on the page was not seen. **Not saved:** zoom, and the divergence cards' open or closed notes.

## D283 (23 Nov 2026): Save layout and Reset on the IV pages, Gamma Exposure and Vega Analysis
**Owner's request:** the Smart OI layout option (D282) on the IV pages (IV Momentum, Volatility Skew, IV / HV / IVP Chart, IV - HV, IV - Intraday), Gamma Exposure and Vega Analysis. **Mechanism (`lib/viewLayout.ts`, `components/LayoutBar.tsx`):** a page asks `layout.use(name, standard)` where it used `useState(standard)`; the layout starts from what was saved or the standard value, and `layout.legend(chart)` gives an EChart the graphs switched off and remembers a click on its legend (new `legendOff` and `onLegendChange` on the chart wrapper from D282). `Save layout` (amber with a dot while the view differs from the saved one or the standard one, `Layout saved ✓` when it matches) and `Reset` (standard view, forgets the saved layout) are the same on every page. Saved in this browser under `nse.layout.v1.<page>`; a saved entry that does not fit the page's choice (another type, a missing field) is ignored. **Saved per page:** IV Momentum: candle length and delta bucket; Volatility Skew: view (smile, term, table); IV / HV / IVP and IV - HV: HV window and range; IV - Intraday: candle length and view; Gamma Exposure: strike range, display type, level lines, absolute GEX line, the density chart, candle size; Vega Analysis: strikes each side, strikes per side, normalized, selected strike line, synthetic future, which series are shown, candle size; each chart's switched-off graphs. **Not saved, on purpose:** anything that names a day or a strike of the day (an expiry, a session, the strike picked, the baseline): it would be wrong tomorrow. **Candle size on Gamma and Vega:** it is a device-wide choice that was already remembered (`nse.timeframe.v1`) and shared by both pages; it is now also part of each page's layout: with no layout saved the device's choice is shown as before (and counts as a change from the standard 5 minutes, so Save lights up); a saved layout wins on that page and sets the shared choice; Reset sets it to 5 minutes for the tools that read the shared choice too. An edited saved value that is not a candle size is ignored. Smart OI keeps its own implementation (D282). **Tests:** the hook (standard view disables both buttons, a change lights Save, saved choices and switched-off graphs come back, Reset restores and forgets, a choice that starts elsewhere, tampered storage ignored, pages keep separate layouts), Volatility Skew and IV - Intraday end to end (no day saved), Vega Analysis end to end (candle size and Normalized saved and restored, Reset to 5 minutes). **Not exercised in a test or the browser:** the Gamma page, IV Momentum and the two IV / HV pages (they use the same hook the same way as the ones tested); the buttons on the live pages.

## D284 (23 Nov 2026): why the Volatility Skew page showed nothing, and an audit of the IV pages' calculations from scratch
**Why the page said "not computed: less than 15 minutes to expiry (or expired)":** both listed expiries, Weekly and Monthly, were Mon 23 Nov (the Tuesday expiry moved to Monday because Tuesday 24 Nov is a holiday), and the page was read after the close. The newest stored chain of that expiry was at 15:39, past its close, and the model refuses to solve a chain with under 15 minutes left (theta and gamma explode and prices are mostly noise). It is correct that it could not solve that chain; it was wrong to show nothing: the same expiry has solvable chains earlier in the session. **Fix:** the skew (`iv_skew`) shows the last chain of the session that can be solved, with the time and a note; the momentum table reads from the last snapshot that has volatility (`momentum_table`); with nothing solvable the page says the expiry is over and to choose a later one. Nothing is filled or carried: a skipped chain is skipped. **Audit, independent of the app's code (stored data of the server, 23 Nov):** (1) HV and RV: recomputed from the raw daily NIFTY bars in `market_history.sqlite3` for 10, 21 and 63 day windows on all 750 plotted days: maximum difference 0.0050, the 2-decimal rounding of the API (HV = population standard deviation of daily log returns x sqrt(252); RV = root mean square of the same returns x sqrt(252), no mean taken out). (2) IV Rank and IV Percentile: recomputed from the plotted India VIX series; the page's `rankAt` gives the same numbers (23 Nov: 63 days IVR 90.97, IVP 96.83; 126 days 90.97 and 98.41; 252 days 45.62 and 80.56, from a 9.15 low and 27.89 high); IVR = (IV - low) / (high - low), IVP = share of the days in the range below today's, days without an IV skipped. (3) IV - HV and IV - RV: plain differences of the rounded figures on the page (23 Nov: IV 17.70, HV 21 14.91, RV 21 17.25: +2.79 and +0.45). (4) Volatility skew: one stored chain (expiry 23 Nov as of 20 Nov 15:39) solved again with my own Black-76 and bisection: forward 25,751.35 (median of put-call parity at the five strikes nearest spot), ATM strike, ATM IV 12.301 (page 12.302), all 31 smile points within 0.0005 volatility points, deltas within 0.00005, 25 delta call 12.471 and put 12.639, skew 0.168, 10 delta 12.513 and 14.883, skew 2.370, butterfly 0.253: all equal to the page. **Data checks:** the 750 plotted days have no unexplained gap (the missing weekdays are exchange holidays); the largest daily move is the 4 Jun 2024 election day (-6.11 %); India VIX runs 9.15 to 27.89, no day without it. Five real trading days since Nov 2023 (1 Jan 2024, 19 Feb 2024, 1 Jan 2025, the Saturday budget session of 1 Feb 2025, 1 Jan 2026) have a NIFTY bar and no India VIX bar in the source; they are not plotted but their returns ARE in HV and RV (correct: those days traded), which is why a recomputation from the plotted days alone differs on a few days. **Conventions, not errors (decide):** HV divides by n (population); the usual textbook estimator divides by n-1, which is 5.4 % higher at 10 days, 2.5 % at 21 and 0.8 % at 63 (HV 21 on 23 Nov would be 15.28 instead of 14.91 and IV - HV 2.42 instead of 2.79); the pages say which is used. IV is India VIX (30 calendar day, NSE), HV is 252 trading days: comparable, not the same basis; stated on the page. Special sessions (the Sunday Muhurat session of 8 Nov 2026, the Saturday budget session) count as a full day in a window of returns: a one-hour session's small return enters the average and lowers HV slightly while it is in the window. Today's provisional close (before the daily history is published) is the last stored index level, which since D274 is the 15:14 level, not the official close (the average of the last half hour); it is flagged provisional and replaced by the published bar. **Tests:** backend: momentum from the last solvable snapshot, skew from the last solvable chain with a note, nothing invented when none is solvable, a normal read has no fallback; frontend: the note and the expiry-is-over message. **Not changed:** the HV estimator (population). **Not seen on the live page:** the fallback (the server still runs the old code until deployed).

## D285 (23 Nov 2026): the four open choices of D284 settled
**Owner's instruction:** fix all four in an institutional and sensible way. **1. HV estimator:** HV is now the sample standard deviation (divide by n - 1) of daily log returns x sqrt(252): the mean is estimated from the same returns, so the unbiased variance divides by n - 1 (Hull; the usual vendor definition). RV stays the root mean square (divide by n, no mean taken out), which is a sum-of-squared-returns measure by definition. Effect on 23 Nov (with item 3): HV 21 14.91 -> 15.47, RV 21 17.25 -> 17.27, IV - HV 21 +2.79 -> +2.23. **2. Time basis:** kept (252 trading days for HV and RV; India VIX and the model's IV are on calendar time), because the realised measure of a market that trades on business days is conventionally annualised on trading time and a 21-trading-day window is the 30 calendar days of the VIX. Measured and stated instead of hidden: for n trading days spanning D calendar days the two bases differ by sqrt(365 n / (252 D)) in volatility (`basis_ratio`): +0.7 % for 21 over 30, -2.5 % for 21 over 32 (holiday weeks); on the pages' notes and in the metric registry. **3. Special sessions:** the one-hour Diwali Muhurat session is not a day. Its bar is dropped, so the return runs between the two regular closes (a 20 to 23 Oct 2025 return of three calendar days, one observation); built-in dates 2020-11-14, 2021-11-04, 2022-10-24, 2023-11-12, 2024-11-01, 2025-10-21, 2026-11-08, plus any special session named Muhurat in the trading calendar (Settings), so a future year is picked up when entered. A full special session (the Saturday budget day, 1 Feb 2025) is a day and kept. The Muhurat date is also off the chart's days (its India VIX is a one-hour figure). **4. Provisional close:** it is no longer part of any statistic: the day appears on the NIFTY price line only (flagged), with no HV, RV, rank or percentile, because a statistic should be made of final closes (its India VIX is missing anyway, so IV - HV cannot be made for it); the HV figures stop at the last published day and gain today's the morning the official close is published. The cards read the newest day that has a figure. **Checks:** the new code run on the server's raw daily bars (NIFTY 50 and India VIX, 2023 on) matches a separate recomputation (regular sessions, sample standard deviation, root mean square) to the 2-decimal rounding on 750 dates for 10, 21 and 63 days; hand-calculated tests (closes 100, 102, 101 give HV 33.29 and RV 24.83; the Muhurat return; the basis ratios 1.0069 and 0.9749; the provisional day is price-only and leaves the others unchanged). Page texts and the metric registry updated. **Not changed:** IVR and IVP (they use the India VIX series; the Muhurat day is no longer in it). **Not seen on the live page.**

## D286 (23 Nov 2026): trend / range indicators on the Analyse menu (descriptive, price only)
**Owner's request:** institutional trend or sideways indicators, every one in the Analyse section. **Built (menu group "Trend & Regime", one tool per view, routes `/trend/<id>`):** Market Regime (the label, its confidence and reasons, shaded behind the NIFTY candles), Trend Strength (efficiency ratio, ADX with +DI / -DI, R-squared), Choppiness & Variance Ratio, Session Structure (TWAP and the future's VWAP), Opening Range, Range Used vs Implied Move, Gamma Regime, Daily Regime. Library `lib/trendStats.ts` (pure, missing stays null): efficiency ratio (Kaufman), Wilder ADX / DMI, regression slope and R-squared, Lo-MacKinlay variance ratio with the homoskedastic z, Choppiness Index (Dreiss), running mean and volume-weighted price, structure (share above, crossings, longest stay, distance), opening range (breaks and failed breaks), range used = (H - L) / (1.5958 x spot x IV / sqrt(252)). Every one has a hand calculation in `trend-stats.test.ts` (efficiency ratio 0.5; Wilder n = 2 ADX 60 then 64.6154; R-squared 0.81; variance ratio 1.625 with z 1.5309; choppiness 58.496; VWAP 101.5 then 102.3333). **Classifier** (`lib/trendRegime.ts`): four readings vote per bar (efficiency ratio 0.35 / 0.20, ADX 25 / 20, R-squared 0.60 / 0.30, choppiness 38.2 / 61.8); 3 or more trend votes = Trending (up or down by the regression slope, which +DI against -DI must not contradict), 3 or more range votes = Range-bound, else Unclear; confidence = share of evaluable readings that agree x share evaluable; a shown label changes only after the new one holds 3 bars (hysteresis). Thresholds are textbook values, none tuned. **The intraday label is descriptive and NOT validated** and says so on every page; the Market Regime page adds a stored-session look (the next 12 bars' efficiency ratio by label across the stored sessions, overlapping windows, no significance claimed). The Smart OI chart shades the regime behind its candles ("Regime" legend entry, so the saved layout keeps it on or off). Range used and Gamma Regime use today's live chain (nearest weekly) and say so; they are for the newest session only. **Backend:** `/api/market/index-bars` takes an optional `day` and returns the stored `days` (the page's session picker and the stored-session look). **Checks:** frontend 1348 passed, backend 1688 passed, tsc, ruff, mypy, build. **Not seen live** (market closed).

## D287 (23 Nov 2026): the daily regime and its validation study: NOT validated
**Study fixed before running** (`app/intraday/regime.py`, `/api/intraday/regime-daily`): the same four-vote label on a 20-day window of the stored regular-session daily bars of NIFTY 50 (4,685 bars, 17 Sep 2007 to 28 Oct 2026), causal, same dwell. Claim: after a day labelled Trending the next 10 days travel in straighter lines (efficiency ratio of the next 10 closes) than after Range-bound. Every 10th day only (463 samples), one-sided permutation test (5,000, fixed seed), and the difference must also be positive in each chronological third. Direction (Trending up against down, next-10-day return) and forward realised volatility are information only. **Result: failed.** Trending minus Range-bound forward efficiency ratio -0.074 (0.324 against 0.398), p = 0.979, by period -0.119, -0.107, +0.009. Thresholds were not touched afterwards: tuning on the data that just failed the test would be a new, invalid test. The page shows the verdict as it came out ("Not validated ... shown as a description of the last 20 days only"). **Information, not claims:** after Trending down the next 10 days' realised volatility averaged 32.3 % against 13.7 % after Trending up and 14.5 % after Range-bound (28 and 64 and 71 samples); Trending up minus down next-10-day return +1.81 %, p = 0.058. These are the kind of thing a later, separately pre-stated study could test. **Python hand calculations** in `test_daily_regime.py` match the frontend's values for the same inputs (a cross-check of the two implementations). **Open:** a straddle-by-regime relevance test (does the regime change what a short straddle earns) is not built; it needs its own pre-stated criterion.

## D288 (23 Nov 2026): GIFT Nifty rolls on expiry day; portfolio period filter
**GIFT Nifty:** the quote took the nearest contract whose expiry was today or later, so on expiry day the expired November contract (last trade 15:30) stayed on the card as "Stale" for the rest of the day. It now rolls to the next contract as soon as that contract has traded more than 10 minutes after the nearest one's last trade (`ROLL_GAP` in `app/globalmkts/nseix.py`); a feed holding only the expired contract still shows it, stale. The other global futures (ES, NQ, YM, RTY, ZN, DX, GC, SI, BZ) are Yahoo continuous symbols that Yahoo rolls itself; not checked live. **Portfolio period filter:** Overall / Year / Month dropdown (`lib/portfolioPeriod.ts`) by the IST calendar date of closing (a close at 00:10 IST on 1 Dec is December, 23:50 on 30 Nov is November); it drives the closed tile, the performance figures, the cumulative curve, the closed list and the "Delete all" button (which names the period and count). Running strategies are not a period and are labelled "not filtered". The choice is kept with Save layout / Reset; a saved period with no data falls back to its year, then to Overall. Tests `portfolio-period.test.tsx` (hand sums 277 overall, 230 for November, 287 for 2026), `test_globalmkts.py`. Not seen live.

## D289 (24 Nov 2026): audit and rebuild of the intraday trend indicators
**Owner's question:** why so much "Unclear" and a 50 % confidence on 23 Nov, and is the analysis precise? **Audit findings:** (1) the 3-of-4 vote was a cliff: at the close ER 0.32 and choppiness 38.7 were within 0.03 and 0.5 of their lines while ADX 47.9 and R-squared 0.89 were far past theirs, so a day that fell 498 points was "Unclear" (40 of 72 bars Unclear, 4 label changes); (2) the confidence of an "Unclear" label (2 of 4 readings "not pointing anywhere" = 50 %) was not a confidence in anything; (3) one 12-bar look-back at every bar length (12 minutes at 1-minute bars is noise, 3 hours at 15); (4) a 17-bar warm-up (ADX needs 27 bars, the others a full window), so the largest move of the day, the fall after the open, was never labelled; (5) the variance ratio z assumed constant variance, which intraday returns do not have; (6) no range or distance measures on the opening range, no bands on the session mean, no time profile for the range used. **Changes:** each reading is now a 0-1 ramp between a "no trend" and a "full trend" level (efficiency ratio 0.15 to 0.45, ADX 15 to 35, R-squared 0.20 to 0.80, choppiness 61.8 to 38.2: conventional, none fitted) and the trend score is their mean; classes Range-bound below 0.30, Mixed 0.30-0.45, Weak trend 0.45-0.65, Trending above 0.65, with a 0.05 margin to cross a boundary (floating-point safe, tested) and the 3-bar dwell on top; direction from the regression slope, +DI against -DI contradicting it makes the bar Mixed; the look-back follows the bar length (30, 20, 12, 8 bars at 1, 3, 5, 15 minutes, about an hour); the look-back is the bars there are from 6 bars on, so the first label comes after about 30 minutes; "confidence" is gone: the card shows the trend score, an agreement count (readings on the label's side of the midpoint, "a count, not a probability"), and the session's own efficiency ratio and net move. New in the library: heteroskedasticity-robust variance-ratio z* (Lo-MacKinlay; hand value 3.603 for the test series against the plain 1.531), Welford running standard deviation (TWAP band), volume-weighted VWAP band, session trend, running range, opening-range extension / first break / furthest excursion. **Charts:** one stacked time axis per page (shared crosshair, zoom and slider), the regime shaded behind every panel (weak and mixed faded), value zones shaded where a reading means something, panels for price with session mean and opening range, trend score, ADX with +DI / -DI; efficiency ratio, R-squared and slope; choppiness and a variance-ratio-by-horizon bar chart coloured only when |z*| reaches 1.96; TWAP and VWAP with bands and distance in ATRs; opening range with distance beyond it; range so far against the implied random-walk range path with a pace line; net GEX by strike with spot and the flip; the daily page's forward-efficiency-ratio bars by label. **On the four stored days** (5-minute bars): 23 Nov now ends Trending down (score 0.88) with 33 Trending-down bars (was 15) and 8 warm-up bars (was 17); 19 Nov ends Trending up; 20 Nov Weak uptrend; 18 Nov (a 67-bar partial session) Range-bound. **Still true:** the intraday label is descriptive and NOT validated, and so is the new scoring (not tested on 18 years like the daily label, which failed its test); the daily page keeps the four-vote rule that the study actually tested and says so. 6-7 label changes a day remain; a longer dwell would trade responsiveness for calm and was left alone. Tests: `trend-stats` (16), `trend-regime` (12), `trend-charts` (3), `trend-page` (7), full suite 1366 passed, build.

## D290 (24 Nov 2026): preferences follow the owner, not the browser; chart cards on the Trend & Regime pages
**Problem:** saved layouts (Save layout), candle lengths, theme, the capital figure, read marks and recent tools lived in one browser's localStorage, so a second PC opened the app with defaults. **Fix (server-side, no page changes):** `app/storage/preferences.py` (`preferences.sqlite3`: key, value exactly as the app wrote it, server-stamped time; a removal is a dated tombstone, never a delete; at most 400 keys, 64 KB each; every write kept in a history table; backed up with the other databases), `GET /api/prefs`, `PUT` / `DELETE /api/prefs/{key}`. Frontend `lib/prefs.ts`: before the app is loaded (`main.tsx` imports `App` only after it) the server's preferences are brought into localStorage; afterwards every write of a shared key is mirrored to the server (0.6 s later, retried every 20 s while the server is away). Shared: keys starting `nse.` or `nse-universe.` and `newsSeenAt`, except the strategies' own copies and the builder's working copy (they have their own server store), the module's bookkeeping and the storage probe. Conflicts: the server's clock only, never the devices'; a key changed here and not yet sent wins and is sent; otherwise the server's newer copy replaces this browser's; the first time a browser meets the server, what it holds and the server lacks is uploaded, and where the server has a copy the server's wins. With no answer in 2.5 s the app opens with the browser's own values and keeps its unsent changes. **Checked:** unit tests with a fake server and "devices" (a layout saved on the laptop appears on the other PC; a change and a removal reach the other device and are not resurrected; first-use upload; unsent change wins; offline then later send; a change during sending stays unsent; strategies never sent); backend store and API tests; in the real app (local build against a mock of the endpoints) a layout saved with Save layout reached the server, and after clearing the browser's storage the page opened with the 15-minute candles again. **Still per browser by design:** the open tab's live-tick buffer for the P&L graph (sessionStorage; the closed strategy's graph is built from the server's stored prices), and the builder's unsent working copy until it is saved. **Chart cards:** the Trend & Regime pages had no "Hide / Show chart info" button and a thin card; they now have the button, and every chart's card shows the time, every series (a candle as open, high, low, close), a dash for a missing value, and the regime, trend score, agreement and the four readings at that bar. Panel titles no longer sit on the legend and the guide labels (OR high / OR low) fit. Tests `prefs-sync` (9), `trend-charts` (5), `test_preferences` (4).

## D291 (24 Nov 2026): a Learn page in the System menu
**Request:** a page that teaches how to read every tool, written like a professor with clear examples, nothing missed. **Decision:** `/learn` (System > Learn) holds a seven-lesson course on the ideas the tools share (option, OI, IV, Greeks, straddle, futures basis, reading numbers honestly) and one lesson for every tool in the FNO Tools and Analyse menus (51 tools, the eight Trend & Regime tabs included), each with the idea, how to read it, a worked example and common mistakes. **Examples are round numbers to follow by hand and are labelled as not market data**; formulas used in them were checked by hand (max pain, contribution in points, IV move, Greeks in rupees, efficiency ratio, R², range used). **Guard:** `learn.test.tsx` fails if any tool in `lib/tools.ts` has no lesson or a lesson lacks an example, so a new tool cannot ship without one. Text lives in `frontend/src/lib/learn.ts`; no backend change. **Limits:** lessons describe what each page shows and how to interpret it; they do not claim any signal predicts, and statements about participant behaviour are hedged because OI cannot show who is long or short.

## D292 (24 Nov 2026): the header price time is the market time, not the fetch time
**Problem:** on the 24 Nov holiday the header showed `10:32:38 IST` beside 25,349.65: every restart (each deploy) stores a fresh snapshot of the unchanged chain, so the badge showed the restart time and changed after each deploy although the prices were Monday's close. **Decision:** the badge shows when the prices are as of, using `marketClockMs` (the fetch time during a session, otherwise the close of the last trading day, weekends and exchange holidays skipped): `23 Nov 15:40:00 IST` all day on the holiday and stable across restarts. The tooltip says the market is closed and gives the real fetch time. **Not changed:** pages that say 'retrieved' keep showing the fetch time, which is what they claim. **Known limits:** the time is the F&O close (15:40), not the index's last tick (15:30); the frontend's holiday list comes from the backend calendar, so a day missing from it would show the fetch time. Each restart still stores a snapshot on non-trading days; that is a storage question, not changed here.
**Follow-up (same day):** the badge's second part was the chain's expiry (`12-01`), which read like a date of the prices. It now shows the trading date of the prices instead (`15:40:00 IST · 23 Nov`); the expiry is only in the tooltip.

## D293 (24 Nov 2026): the Strategy Builder and portfolio no longer depend on the browser
**Request:** the builder must work from the server so any device shows the same, with no browser-specific bugs. **Audit:** saved and closed strategies, the running strategy and its stops/targets/re-entries were already on the server (D186-D281); what still came from this browser: (1) the builder started from this browser's working copy, so a second device briefly showed (and could act on) a stale strategy; (2) the Strategy portfolio page took its 'Current strategy (not saved)' row from the local working copy, so another device showed none; (3) 'Open in builder' from the portfolio handed the strategy over through localStorage/sessionStorage, which a second tab or device cannot read, and a running strategy on the server replaced it; (4) a strategy set aside because its expiry had gone was kept only in this browser's storage, and nothing read it. **Changes:** the builder starts empty and takes the running strategy from the server; this browser's copy is read once and used only when the server cannot be reached (and is not overwritten before the server has answered); an empty page with nothing on the server sends nothing; the portfolio reads the running strategy from the server (local copy only if the server is unreachable); opening a saved or closed strategy travels in the address (`/strategy?open=NAME`, `/strategy?closed=ID`, removed once opened); an expired strategy is filed in the Saved list on the server as `NAME (expired DATE)`. **Tests:** `strategy-device.test.tsx` and new cases in `strategy-server.test.tsx` (new device, server empty, server away, hand-over by address, expired copy). **Still per browser, by design:** the live P&L tick buffer behind the MTM graph (per tab; the closed record keeps the stored history), temporary views of forward-test positions (this tab), and display choices (now synced by D290). **Not verified:** a real two-device run against the server.

## D294 (24 Nov 2026): the NIFTY future is a live tick in the browser
**Problem:** the NIFTY future in the Strategy Builder (its quote, the price a new leg enters at, a held leg's P&L) moved only every 5 seconds, unlike the index. **Cause:** the future's ticks were 'internal' on the server (read by the server's own analysis and stop/target checks, never sent to browsers); the page polled `/api/futures/contracts`. **Fix:** the server now also publishes the ticks of the current and next-month NIFTY future to browsers (`LiveBook.publish_internal`, `StreamManager.public_internal`, `MarketService.is_nifty_future_key`): they ride the normal 'ticks' broadcast and a new client's first message, are kept apart from the option feed (never counted as option-feed activity or instruments with data), and stock futures stay server-side. The page overlays the streamed price on the polled contract (`overlayLiveFutures`: only while streaming, only when fresh within 3 minutes and not older than the poll; otherwise the polled quote). Used by the Strategy Builder, the Strategy portfolio and Advanced Greeks. **Tests:** backend book/manager/market-service tests, `futures-live.test.ts`, and a builder page test that follows two ticks and enters a new future at the live price. **Not verified:** live in market hours (the next session is Wed 25 Nov); deploy restarts the stream, so it should go out outside market hours.

## D295 (25 Nov 2026): Market Intelligence redesigned around the day: a trend-and-regime group, sentiment through the day and an evidence-based outlook
**Request:** bring the Trend & Regime indicators into Market Intelligence and show market sentiment and the expected outcome through the day. **Quant-manager decisions:**
1. **A sixth evidence group, F "Trend and regime"** (`intelligence/trend_engine.py`, a server port of the page's rules, parity-tested on the real 23 Nov bars: same score, readings, label counts). Three terms: regime direction x strength (strength = trend score above the 0.30 Mixed floor, rescaled), close against the session mean in ATRs, close against the 15-minute opening range in ATRs; the last two read index price (also group D) so they share one family at weight 0.5. Weights re-set to sum 100 (positioning 25, orderflow 17, volatility 8, structure 17, participation 17, trend 16), proportions of the five older groups kept within about 2 %. **The layout id (ARCH 2) is deliberately not bumped:** stored rows keep their record; history is re-scored with today's weights (a group missing from an old row is left out and the rest renormalised, as the live fusion does), so old and new rows share one definition. Each recorded minute now also stores the trend reading (`metrics.trend`), so after a few sessions the verdict with and without the group can be compared on the same rows.
2. **"Expected outcome" is not a forecast** (D148 removed the prediction engine). It is the priced move (spot x ATM IV x sqrt(minutes / (252 x 375)), one standard deviation, a band with no direction), the lean (a call only outside +-15 with evidence quality at least 35), the levels that confirm or invalidate it, what followed each verdict class in the stored sessions (share up/down/flat with a Wilson interval, sessions shown because snapshots overlap), today's scorecard of 15-minute calls against the next 30 minutes, and a plain grade of the verdict's out-of-sample record (**Early** until 20 test sessions; today six sessions, 5 of 6 above 50 %, pooled 30-minute 67 % but per-session 26 % to 85 %).
3. **Page:** the first tab is now the Day board: four answers (sentiment, session regime, lean, next-30-minutes band), the reading in sentences, the day chart (NIFTY, the fused sentiment with the trend group, evidence quality; trend regime shaded where recorded), five phases (opening hour, morning, midday, afternoon, closing: finished ones with verdict and move, ahead ones priced), the expected-outcome table with levels, and the track record. The old verdict card, turn watch, engine bars, straddle summary and key numbers follow unchanged. New endpoint `GET /api/intelligence/outlook`.
**Verified:** backend and frontend tests on real stored rows (20 and 23 Nov 2026) with independent hand counts; the page rendered in a browser against the real outlook of 23 Nov. **Not verified:** a live session (the first is Wed 25 Nov); the trend group has no history in the validation until it has been recorded for some sessions; the weights are provisional and not calibrated.

## D296 · 25 Nov 2026 · One Market Intelligence overview, group F on the Evidence tab, no stale trend evidence
**Problem seen on the phone at 08:14 before the open:** the Evidence tab listed groups A to E only (the frontend `ENGINE_ORDER` still had five names, so group F never rendered); the Day board and the old verdict card each led with their own sentiment, regime and trust answer (two dashboards); and group F scored the 23 Nov bars as today's evidence (-89) while every other group had no data.
**Fix:** (1) `ENGINE_ORDER` has the sixth group. (2) One overview: the Day board header is the only lead (sentiment, session regime, lean, priced move); the old card is now "Why, and how far to trust it" (headline, trust, what changed, what points up and down, what to watch), then the turn watch and the evidence bars, all passed into the Day board so they also show if the outlook request fails; the big duplicate label and gauge are gone. (3) `wiring.trend()` marks the reading `is_today`; group F is unavailable ("the newest stored bars are of <day>, not today's session") when the bars are of an earlier day. The Day board still shows the earlier session's regime on a closed day, labelled as that session.
**Verified:** backend 1,722 and frontend 1,414 tests, ruff, mypy and build pass; new tests for F on the Evidence tab, a single verdict lead, and the earlier-session rule. **Not verified:** a live session.

## D297 · 25 Nov 2026 · Audit of the Market Intelligence overview
**Found and fixed:** (1) `/api/intelligence/outlook` recomputed the history table on every request (0.6 s CPU on 30 sessions, polled every 15 s per open page; 3 s cold) while the summary takes 6 ms: it is now computed once per 5-minute cache life. (2) A closed or pre-open page mixed provenance: tiles read today's empty live summary ("No verdict") beside the chart of the last session; it now reads the last recorded minute of the shown session and says so, and the levels measure from that session's last spot. (3) The label of group F kept its letter (`plainName` stripped A to E). (4) No stale state: a live page now warns when the newest recorded minute is 3 or more minutes old. (5) The evidence sections remounted when the outlook request resolved (a click could be lost): fixed slots. (6) "To the close (0 min)" row on a closed page, and a missing figure printed as 0 % in the reading sentence: removed. (7) The track record now says it was measured on the five older groups.
**Known, not changed:** the turn watch still reads five groups (it is a reversal detector built on engine changes; F is not in it); F rests on the regime term alone until the 14-bar ATR exists (about 70 minutes after the open) and is flagged PARTIAL; the priced band uses 252 x 375 trading-minute scaling of the quoted IV (a convention, not verified against the broker's IV time basis); the server's own weight setting was not inspected (the file with credentials is not read), so if it overrides the weights the sum may differ from 100 (the fusion renormalises).
**Verified:** backend 1,724 and frontend 1,418 tests, ruff, mypy, tsc. **Not verified:** a live session.

## D298 · 25 Nov 2026 · The Day board follows the live NIFTY tick
**Request:** "live, no delay". **Measured delays before:** every figure on the Day board came from the server's read, re-requested every 15 s (summary cache 5 s; inputs refresh every 5 to 15 s: market 5 s, chain 15 s), and the chart holds one recorded point a minute. The browser's live NIFTY tick was not used.
**Now:** the outlook is re-read every 5 s (cheap since D297: about 0.05 s); the live NIFTY tick (same source and rules as the header badge: streaming, traded not indicative) re-prices the 15/30/60-minute and to-the-close bands (spot x server IV x sqrt(minutes / 94,500)), the distances to the levels and the last point of the chart, and a line under the tiles says whether a tick is arriving and when it was received.
**Not live by nature:** the verdict, trend group and evidence are computed from the option chain and the flow samples on the server, so they follow the data within 5 to 15 s, not tick by tick; the sentiment line and its track record are one recorded point a minute. Stated on the page.
**Verified:** pure re-pricing tested with a hand calculation (25,400 x 16.92 % x sqrt(30 / 94,500) = 76.6 points). **Not verified:** a real tick in a live session.
